# Retrospective review of EP-0 … EP-12 — findings ledger (2026-08-18)

Machine-rendered record of the adversarial retrospective review run on 2026-08-18 (owner-directed 'ghost' mega-session between EP-12 and EP-13; ten review lenses → one adversarial verifier per material finding → completeness critic; 81 agents). Every entry below is the reviewers' and verifiers' own text (evidence, impact, proposed fix, verifier notes) — nothing was rewritten, so the record is high-fidelity but not edited for style. **Verdict** is the independent verifier's call after re-reading the cited files/lines and running read-only probes; **severity** is the verifier's corrected severity. Low/info findings were carried without a verifier pass. Nothing in this file was implemented in the review session itself; the implementing episodes are EP-165 … EP-170 (see `README.md`). Owner decisions taken on 2026-08-18 are recorded in `../mimicwarehouse/DECISIONS.md` (D-43) and in each retro brief's *Decisions* section.

Each finding is assigned to the retro episode that implements it in the `EP` column of the index (EP-165 governance/session hardening · EP-166 docs consolidation · EP-167 CLI/config · EP-168 test infrastructure · EP-169 contract + fixture regeneration · EP-170 P2/P3 brief reconciliation · re-plan = hand to EP-16 or the phase re-plan · owner = owner action).

Guard note: identifiers in this file are review artefacts (finding ids, file paths, line numbers); id bands are written as `1xxxxxxx` / `10_000_000` style on purpose (`mwh guard` G4).

## Lens summaries

### settings, CLI contract and host diagnostics (config.py / cli.py / doctor.py / .env.example / .streamlit / tests ep02-ep03-ep164) (18 findings)

The Settings/CLI/doctor foundation is solid and well tested (mwh doctor 8/1/0/5, paths and doctor JSON well-formed, help ~0.5 s, duckdb_settings() accepted verbatim by duckdb 1.5.5), but five things will trip P2 briefs unless consolidated now: (1) the "unknown MWH_* keys are rejected" safety claim is false for shell environment variables (verified: MWH_DATA_ROOTT is silently ignored) and is mis-stated in .env.example, README, tests/README and DESIGN §20; (2) there is no single DuckDB connection factory (EP-17 item 1 assumes one may exist) — two ad-hoc openers already exist and DuckDB 1.5.5 does not create a missing temp_directory parent (verified IOException on first spill) while doctor reports that case as pass; (3) `--data-root` is not propagated to child processes (verify's pytest child, future spawn/background jobs) because configure() only sets an in-process override; (4) `mwh <validated-command> --help` exits 2 on an unsafe root or a .env typo because the group callback validates before click parses the sub-command's --help; (5) console encoding is handled three different ways (verify._console_safe, doctor glyph switch, nothing elsewhere), piped output is cp1252 while every consumer decodes UTF-8, and JSON outputs end in \r\r\n. The rest is doc drift, dead members, triplicated doctor test mocks, decimal-vs-binary GB semantics, and settings-namespace inconsistencies across future briefs (MWH_ROLE vs MWH_APP_ROLE, MWH_APP_MEMORY_LIMIT vs duckdb_app_memory_limit) that the P1 re-plan should settle. Nothing here reads data; all evidence comes from code, docs and the doctor/paths/help commands themselves.

### governance enforcement layers (deny rules, mwh guard, .gitignore/.gitattributes, hooks, GOVERNANCE text vs reality) (17 findings)

The four shipped layers (.claude deny rules, mwh guard G1-G5 + pre-commit, .gitignore/.gitattributes, GOVERNANCE/CLAUDE prose) are installed and working as documented: hook installed at .git/hooks/pre-commit, core.hooksPath unset, 67 deny rules parse and fire on sub-commands of compound Bash commands (verified with a data-free probe), and every completed EP was hook-guarded. The weaknesses are structural rather than bugs: the Bash/PowerShell deny list is a prefix blocklist that python -c / uv run python / cp-to-scratchpad / nested sh -c bypass (the nested-shell probe was caught only by the auto-mode classifier, which is not a project control), the data root is protected only when it is literally C:\mimicdata, G4 misses float-rendered ids (2xxxxxxx.0), underscore-prefixed ids in file names, and comma-grouped ids, and G1 lacks .tsv/.xlsx/.zip/.sqlite/.csv.zst/.parquet.gz. Two concrete downstream traps: .gitignore's unanchored `data/` will silently drop EP-39's `src/mimicwarehouse/data/item_units.yaml` from commits, and the `!mimicwarehouse/tests/fixtures/**` negation un-ignores every extension under fixtures. GOVERNANCE §2/§4 no longer describe the endpoint-security reality recorded in D-38/D-42 (Malwarebytes exclusions include `source material/` inside the repository; telemetry off) and overstate the reach of the `*.csv` deny rule. A small PreToolUse command-string hook (parked GOV-1) is now the cheapest way to close the python/cp/nested-shell class of bypass and is worth an owner decision.

### schema-contract (EP-9) as the spine of later briefs (12 findings)

The EP-9 contract is in good shape as a *type/nullability/key* description: `mwh schema check` is clean against mimic-code 8bcbd190 (41 tables, no drift), the three recorded deviations (spec_type_desc/drug nullable, ed.vitalsign.resprate DOUBLE) are correct and visible to the drift oracle, keys.yaml matches constraint.sql (25 PKs / 51 FKs, verified by test), EP-10 confirmed all 41 real headers equal the contract order, and the load metadata is internally consistent (load_class = exactly the 13 files >= ~0.9 GB, partitioned == subject_keyed everywhere). The weaknesses are on the *loader-facing* side and in ripple effects: (1) the contract carries no CSV parsing hints and silently discards upstream precision (nine TIMESTAMP(3) columns in pharmacy/prescriptions/outputevents) while the CSV dialect (timestampformat/dateformat/quote/escape/nullstr/quoted-null policy) is already duplicated in inventory.py and fixtures/write.py and will be hard-coded a third time by EP-17; (2) `content_hash()` is documentation-sensitive and is asserted equal to the committed fixture manifest by test_ep11/test_ep12, so any comment/flag edit (EP-29 will make many) breaks the suite until fixtures are regenerated; (3) several downstream briefs (EP-17 item 3, EP-22 items 2-3, EP-29 item 1) describe a contract that does not match what shipped (identity map / `added_in_3_1` naming, `comment` not `description`, dims without PK). Lower-severity items: REAL->FLOAT contradicts the resprate "one numeric type" rationale, sort_keys conventions differ between ED and ICU event tables, the generic ColumnMap model has version-specific field names, dataset/directory knowledge lives in four places, a small DDL-parser word-boundary bug, and `mwh schema list` is unreadable at the 80-column width autonomous sessions see.

### raw inventory / provenance module (EP-10) (17 findings)

inventory.py is solid for what it does: streaming SHA-256, BOM/CRLF-tolerant header check, DuckDB row count with a serial fallback, per-file atomic JSONL rewrite, a deterministic order-independent snapshot id, and a guard-clean docs page (verified: `mwh guard` clean on the page/module/tests, 28 tests pass in 5 s, README Risk 1 / D-26 addendum / docs page all agree on 41 files, 34 match, 7 no-expectation, id 8209301d…). The gaps are mostly at the seams the next EPs will lean on: (1) a no-op resume `build` overwrites the snapshot's started/finished/git_sha/contract_hash, which breaks EP-16's own verification recipe and the "timestamp-only diff" promise; (2) resume never re-checks headers when the contract changes, so `header ok` can silently go stale under a new `contract_hash`; (3) the meaning of `source_manifest_id` is contradictory between the D-26 addendum/DESIGN (raw_snapshot_id) and the EP-17 brief (per-file sha256), and there is no table→record/path lookup helper, so EP-17/20 will re-derive the `<dataset-dir>/<csv_path>` key that is already duplicated four times; (4) FILES_EXPECTED=41 and DATASET_DIRS are hard-coded beside a contract that already knows datasets and csv paths. Lower-severity items: docs `Generated:` uses wall-clock so every `reconcile` dirties git, `--no-resume` is advertised but not accepted, stale manifest records are never pruned, no manifest schema version, and several test-coverage gaps (interrupted-run resume, retry path, same-size/same-tick rewrite blind spot measured at 137/200). Nothing here requires touching the plan; most fixes are XS–S and belong in the EP-16 re-plan or the first loader EP.

### fixture-generator (EP-11/12 synthetic fixture as the test substrate for every later brief) (17 findings)

The EP-11/12 generator is in good shape as a substrate: build_frames() takes 0.74 s, test_ep11 (43 tests) runs in 4 s and test_ep12 (32) in 6 s, the in-memory catalog loads 31 typed tables in 0.63 s without touching the data root, NULLs round-trip as empty fields with no empty strings anywhere, every real itemid I checked in d_items.yaml / d_labitems.yaml is correct, and the per-table child RNG design does keep icu bytes from moving hosp bytes. The weaknesses are structural rather than bugs: (1) every id space (subject/hadm/stay/labevent/transfer/orderid/caregiver) starts at the same 90_000_000, so a wrong-key join still matches on the fixture, which silently weakens every concept/phenotype/loader test that follows; (2) fixture bytes depend on unpinned numpy/polars streams and on the full contract hash, and the GENERATOR_VERSION / regeneration protocol is undocumented and already inconsistent (icu added, version unchanged); (3) several vendored concepts are empty or degraded on the fixture (bg_art via 52033, crrt via chartevents settings, kdigo stage 3, T2DM lab/med branch), which EP-37/41/42 will hit; (4) two forward briefs (EP-19 fixture lake root, EP-21 re-pointing fixture_catalog at a 20-table lake) conflict with the fixture layout/tests as they stand. Because every fixture change forces a full regeneration plus test-count churn, the practical recommendation is one bundled regeneration (distinct id floors, seconds in timestamps, 52033/A1c/metformin/T1DM/CRRT-settings additions, a second ICU segment and short-gap readmissions) with a GENERATOR_VERSION bump to 0.2.0 before EP-17, and a written fixture-change protocol.

### verification tooling & test infrastructure (verify.py, roadmap_check, conftest tiers, tests/ep/*, pyproject test/lint config) (15 findings)

The verify/roadmap tooling is sound and its checks pass today (mwh verify --roadmap --strict: 165 rows / 165 briefs / 14 done / 0 errors / 0 warnings; test_ep06+test_ep12: 79 passed in 13.5 s; xdist trial -n 4 on four modules: 139 passed in 7.2 s). The biggest forward-looking problem is semantic: the EP-12 dev-tier gate skips every tier("dev") test until <data_root>/warehouse/dev.duckdb exists (EP-21), while EP-17..EP-20 and EP-23..25 briefs define dev tests that need raw CSVs / a lake / a dev_ready status, so their "--tier dev green" acceptance would be vacuously green for four EPs. Cross-EP churn is real and documented in the git history: test_ep06 was edited by every one of the six code EPs after it (rolling "EP-17" probe, hash-count pin) and test_ep11 by EP-12 (22 -> 31 file counts); both have cheap mechanical fixes. Runtime is dominated by fresh-interpreter/CLI tests (the EP-2 end-to-end verify alone is 3.2 s; five near-identical "import stays light" subprocess tests; roadmap_check spawns two git processes per ☑ hash and will grow ~10x by roadmap end). Remaining items are low-severity but the owner wanted them consolidated: exit-code overlap (verify EXIT_USAGE = pytest INTERRUPTED = 2), a private _console_safe helper that DESIGN and Risk 13 treat as a house rule, marker range 0..199 with 35 slots left for re-plan insertions, hypothesis import cost with zero property tests, duplicated helper code across nine test modules, and README/pyproject/CLAUDE.md wording drift.

### documentation drift and internal contradictions across all prose (README/CLAUDE/NOTICE/DESIGN/DECISIONS/GOVERNANCE/tests+fixtures+brand READMEs/raw-inventory/source-material README/roadmap README+final-roadmap/completion notes) (18 findings)

The dated-note discipline worked: DESIGN §15 notes, DECISIONS addenda and the brief completion notes are accurate and mutually consistent in almost every checked fact (D-19 pin, raw snapshot id, doctor 8/1/0/5, guard G1-G5, 14 checks, 165 briefs, 31 fixture CSVs; all 176 markdown files' relative links resolve except one placeholder in EP-6). The drift is concentrated in the *status* prose that a cold session reads first: the root README still says "Planning complete; no code yet" and "mwh CLI arrives with EP-2", the workspace README says "no data code yet ... from EP-8 on" and lists only EP-1..EP-5, DESIGN's intro/§15 header still say "nothing exists as code yet / (all planned)", four places say "D-1 … D-41" and three say "164 briefs". The single highest-cost gap is CLAUDE.md: it never absorbed D-42 (no heredocs / no `python -` stdin / no burst scratch loops), never says that `uv` is not on either tool shell's PATH while bare `python` there is the system 3.14, writes the marker as `ep_<NN>` (zero-padded markers select nothing), and points to `safe_query` which does not exist until EP-30. Two internal contradictions exist (DESIGN §2 note claims §21 "now reads Defender + Malwarebytes" while §21 still reads "Defender/NTFS"; DESIGN/EP-12 record 5,370,673 fixture bytes vs the committed manifest's 5,370,674). GOVERNANCE §2 ("excluded from Windows Defender ... at the owner's discretion; the repository is not") is overtaken by D-38 addenda/D-42 (two products, `source material\` and the workspace `.venv` inside the repo are Malwarebytes-excluded as a disclosure control) and needs an owner-approved edit. There is no single "what exists today" page: the Malwarebytes/D-42 story is retold in seven places and the raw-snapshot facts in five; I recommend a re-plan-maintained "State of the workspace" section in mimicwarehouse/README.md plus a one-line status pointer in the root README, with DESIGN §15's tree allowed to be refreshed (tree = map, notes = history).

### forward-compat: pending briefs (EP-13..EP-56) vs shipped P0/P1 code (30 findings)

The pending P1 docs briefs (EP-13/14/15/16) are in good shape thanks to the EP-7 amendments; their remaining drift is small (docs/resources/ now exists without a README, EP-16 items 1-2 are already done by EP-10, Risk 14 is resolved so --strict is usable). P2 is where the plan and the shipped code diverge in ways that will trip sessions: (1) the EP-12 pytest tier ladder skips every tier("dev")/tier("full") test until <data_root>/warehouse/dev.duckdb exists, which EP-21 builds, so the dev/full acceptance of EP-17..EP-20 is vacuous as written, and EP-22 wants a `demo` test tier that EP-12 explicitly ruled out; (2) the EP-9 contract's sort_keys/load_class disagree with the tie-break sort keys and size classes EP-20/23/24/25/26/27 expect, and every contract YAML edit re-hashes `content_hash()` which `tests/fixtures/manifest.json` pins, so each per-brief contract fix forces a fixture regeneration commit; (3) identifier lists and `free_text`/`identifier` column flags that EP-23..30 assume do not exist (Column is `extra="forbid"`, keys.yaml has no identifiers section); (4) several concrete mechanics are wrong on Windows/this host: `os.replace(dir.new -> dir)` fails when the target exists (verified), the EP-19 fallback recipe writes to the drive root when MWH_DATA_ROOT is unset, `uv` is not on the tool PATH, psutil is not a dependency; (5) EP-21's plan to re-point `fixture_catalog` at a runner-built catalog will break test_ep12 while stage.yaml only covers 20 of 31 tables. Depends-on columns are insufficient for EP-23..27 (open_catalog, EP-21), EP-37 (EP-34/35), EP-40 (EP-30) and wrong for EP-42 (uses EP-43's `mwh disclose check`). Several briefs duplicate shipped work (EP-29 descriptions: all 421 columns already carry `comment`; EP-21 `catalog_path`; EP-17 connection factory vs `inventory.open_connection`). Most fixes are XS-S doc amendments best batched into EP-16's pickup notes plus one contract/fixture commit before EP-17.

### Local development / session environment as it really is vs. what the roadmap assumed (10 findings)

The environment is in better shape than the docs and the persistent memory notes suggest, but three session-level frictions recur and none of them is written into the repo: (1) "uv is not on the tool PATH" is a stale-process artefact, not a machine fact — the persistent User PATH already ends with %LOCALAPPDATA%\Microsoft\WinGet\Links, but the VS Code process that hosts every Claude session (pid 44932, started 2026-08-17 16:46) predates the uv install (17:30), so every tool shell and every git hook it spawns inherits the old PATH; a VS Code restart fixes it. (2) The cp1252 problem (Risk 13) is specifically the Bash tool: the harness already gives the PowerShell tool PYTHONIOENCODING=utf-8:surrogateescape and chcp 65001, while under Bash `uv run python` reports utf8_mode 0 / cp1252 and prints U+2265 as a traceback and U+00B7 as a replacement glyph; a project-level `.claude/settings.json` "env" block (PYTHONUTF8=1) would make both tools behave alike (owner decision per CLAUDE.md §6). (3) The D-42 I/O rules and the tooling gotchas (heredoc ban, `python -` hang, `git commit -F`, bare `python` = system 3.14, PATH prefix) live only in machine-local auto-memory and completion notes, not in CLAUDE.md, so the auto-mode harness prompt that recommends heredocs currently outranks nothing in the repo. Verified fine and needing no action: `uv build --wheel` (1.1 s) packages 13 YAML + 142 SQL + VENDOR.json + vendored LICENSE; the cu130 index is `explicit=true` and never consulted; uv cache and .venv share C: (hardlink default, nothing to pin); LongPathsEnabled=1 + core.longpaths=true; `.gitattributes` `* text=auto eol=lf` neutralises the system-wide core.autocrlf=true (418/421 tracked files LF in index and worktree); 414.5 GB free; %TEMP%\mimic-code still present at the pinned sha; Malwarebytes coverage is process-based so the un-listed burst-write paths (uv cache, scratchpad, .git/objects, .pytest_cache/.ruff_cache) are written by already-trusted binaries. Small doc drift remains (README "D-1 … D-41", stale pyproject comment, `.claude/settings.json` worktree CRLF, no LICENSE file for a `license = "MIT"` package).

### data architecture parity/uniformity (lake / catalog / tier / DAG design vs EP-3/9/10/11/12 code) (16 findings)

The shipped foundation (Settings.layout/catalog_path/duckdb_settings/dev_buckets, the EP-9 contract metadata, EP-10 raw_snapshot_id, EP-11/12 fixtures + in-memory catalog) is internally sound, but the P2 briefs and DESIGN sections 3-6/11 do not yet agree with it in several load-bearing places. Two are design gaps rather than drift: (1) the ".new + os.replace" swap that DESIGN section 6 calls atomic fails on Windows while any READ_ONLY handle is open (probed: WinError 5 for both a .duckdb file and a non-empty lake directory), and EP-57 caches READ_ONLY connections for the app's lifetime, so catalog and runs.duckdb rebuilds will fail whenever the app is up; (2) the fixture (and demo) tiers have no defined lake root - Settings.catalog_path("fixture") points into the real data root, EP-21 says "temp root", EP-19's default lake root is the credentialed lake/core, and GOVERNANCE section 6 needs a persistent fixture catalog for app work. Beyond that, the staging briefs EP-20/23-27 all expect tie-breaker sort_keys and one load_class the contract does not carry (six briefs would each edit the contract), the sha256-based snapshot id cannot be stable across rebuilds without a total sort order, provenance identifiers are named inconsistently (source_manifest_id per file vs raw_snapshot_id per D-26), EP-22 wants a "demo" pytest tier that EP-12's tests explicitly refuse, DEV_BUCKETS constant vs Settings.dev_buckets, and EP-30's free-text heuristic would refuse the dictionary tables GOVERNANCE explicitly allows. Time-semantics/type decisions (naive TIMESTAMP, DATE for dod/chartdate, DOUBLE for NUMERIC, no DECIMAL) are consistent between contract and EP-34; only DATE-grain time columns need a rule before the timeline/spine briefs. Memory/temp budgets are coherent for one process at a time but not for a background 36 GB build plus dev-tier tests plus the app.

### Completeness critic

The merged list is thorough on code/doc mechanics but thin on three things: (1) owner decisions that the completion notes explicitly deferred ("owner may revert", "table change the owner may make", "re-confirm at hand-off", EP-10's five review points) and that nobody has recorded a verdict for; (2) the meta-question of how this consolidation itself lands (EP-165 slot vs hotfix commits vs EP-16, commit-tag/verify conventions, ordering vs EP-13..15, CLAUDE.md §6 "ask before" gates on .gitignore/settings.json/GOVERNANCE), without which none of the 150+ fixes can be committed cleanly; and (3) an unexamined egress path — MCP connectors (Gmail, Calendar, Hugging Face authenticated as the owner, PubMed/bioRxiv/ICD-10, Context7) are live in sessions and mentioned only in machine-local memory, not in GOVERNANCE §4 or the deny list. Two systemic patterns deserve one structural fix each rather than the many local pickup notes already proposed: a brief-notation glossary in roadmap/README (`%MWH_DATA_ROOT%` appears in 89 briefs, `roadmap_check.py` in 18, "Command forms" was pasted into 9 amended briefs) and a rewritten "earlier tests unchanged" acceptance clause (7 of 8 code EPs edited an earlier test module). Remaining gaps are small: no `git` doctor probe, a bash-only env-var example in tests/README, a hard-coded "EP-21 builds it" skip literal.

## Verified findings — detail

### DOC-1 — CLAUDE.md never absorbed D-42 and the tool-shell environment realities (heredocs, `python -`, uv PATH, system python 3.14, cp1252)

**Severity (verified):** high · **Verdict:** confirmed-with-corrections (high confidence) · **Kind:** docs-drift · **When:** now-must · **Effort:** S · **Retro episode:** EP-165 · **Lens:** documentation drift and internal contradictions across all prose (README/CLAUDE/NOTICE/DESIGN/DECISIONS/GOVERNANCE/tests+fixtures+brand READMEs/raw-inventory/source-material README/roadmap README+final-roadmap/completion notes)

**Files:** `CLAUDE.md:35`, `CLAUDE.md:18`, `mimicwarehouse/DECISIONS.md:498`, `roadmap/EP-12-fixtures-icu-markers.md:171`, `roadmap/EP-8-mimic-code-vendoring.md:122`

**Evidence.** CLAUDE.md §3 line 35: "Run everything as `uv run --project mimicwarehouse --group <group> mwh <cmd>`" — verified this session: `which uv` in the Bash tool → "no uv in (...)"; `Get-Command uv` in the PowerShell tool → nothing; `Get-Command python` → `C:\Python314\python.exe`; `%LOCALAPPDATA%\Microsoft\WinGet\Links\uv.exe` exists but is not on either PATH. D-42 (DECISIONS.md:498-520): "(1) sessions write files with the Write/Edit tools, **never** shell heredocs (Defender killed three `bash -c … <<'EOF'` command lines ...); (2) no burst copy / `sed -i` / delete loops ..." and EP-12 completion note: "one accidental `python -` stdin invocation hung for two minutes". EP-8 note: "`uv` was reached via `%LOCALAPPDATA%\Microsoft\WinGet\Links`". Roadmap Risk 13 / DESIGN §2 EP-7 note: "CLI output must stay ASCII or pass through `verify._console_safe`". None of these sentences appear in CLAUDE.md, which is the one file every session reads first; the harness's own auto-mode text actively suggests heredocs.

**Impact.** Every cold session re-discovers these by failure: a heredoc gets the shell process killed by Defender (Trojan:Win32/ClickFix heuristic), `python -` hangs, `uv run` reports command-not-found so a session may conclude the toolchain is missing (or `pip install` into 3.14, which CLAUDE.md forbids elsewhere), `python scripts/roadmap_check.py` runs under 3.14 without the package, and `git commit` from the Bash tool fails its pre-commit hook (`entry: uv run --project mimicwarehouse ...`) with uv absent from PATH. This will hit EP-13 on its first command.

**Proposed fix (reviewer).** Add to CLAUDE.md §3 (Environment) five bullets: (a) `uv` is NOT on the tool shells' PATH — Bash: `export PATH="$LOCALAPPDATA/Microsoft/WinGet/Links:$PATH"`; PowerShell: `$env:PATH = "$env:LOCALAPPDATA\Microsoft\WinGet\Links;$env:PATH"` — before any `uv`, `poe`, `pre-commit` or `git commit` (the hook shells out to `uv run`); (b) bare `python` in the tool shells is the system 3.14 — always `uv run python …`; (c) D-42 verbatim: write files with Write/Edit, never bash heredocs, never `python -`/stdin scripts, no burst copy/`sed -i`/delete loops over many files in scratch dirs; if a process vanishes mid-command check Malwarebytes quarantine + `mbamservice.log` first; (d) console is cp1252 — CLI strings ASCII or through `_console_safe`; JSON unaffected; (e) `default-groups=["dev"]` so `uv run mwh` ≡ `uv run --group dev mwh`. Cross-reference D-42 / Risk 12 / Risk 13.

**Corrected fix (verifier).** Add to CLAUDE.md §3 (Environment & commands), immediately after the "Workspace" bullet at lines 35-39, a block titled "Tool-shell realities (D-42, roadmap Risks 12/13)":
(a) `uv` is NOT on the Bash-tool or PowerShell-tool PATH (only `%LOCALAPPDATA%\Microsoft\WinGet\Links\uv.exe` exists). Bash: `export PATH="$LOCALAPPDATA/Microsoft/WinGet/Links:$PATH"`; PowerShell: `$env:PATH = "$env:LOCALAPPDATA\Microsoft\WinGet\Links;$env:PATH"` — required before any `uv`, `poe`, `pre-commit` AND before `git commit`: the installed `.git/hooks/pre-commit` runs pre-commit from the workspace `.venv` directly, but the three local hooks in `.pre-commit-config.yaml` are `entry: uv run --project mimicwarehouse --group dev …` / `language: system`, so the commit fails with "uv: command not found" without the export.
(b) bare `python` in both tool shells is the system CPython 3.14 (`C:\Python314`); never run scripts/tests with it — always `uv run python …` / `uv run pytest …` (e.g. `uv run poe roadmap-check`, not `python scripts/roadmap_check.py`).
(c) D-42 verbatim consequences: write files only with the Write/Edit tools — never `bash -c … <<'EOF'` heredocs (Defender ClickFix heuristic kills the shell), never `python -` / stdin scripts (EP-12: hung 2 min), no burst `cp -r`/`sed -i`/`rm -rf` loops over many files in scratch dirs (Malwarebytes ARW quarantined bash.exe); commit messages via `git commit -F <file>` written with Write; if a process vanishes / "access denied" mid-command, check Malwarebytes Quarantine + `C:\ProgramData\Malwarebytes\MBAMService\logs\mbamservice.log` before Defender. State explicitly that the harness auto-mode text recommending heredocs/sed is overridden by this rule.
(d) console is cp1252 in the tool shells: any new CLI table/log string stays ASCII or goes through `verify._console_safe`; JSON outputs unaffected (Risk 13).
(e) `[tool.uv] default-groups = ["dev"]` (pyproject.toml:64) so `uv run mwh …` == `uv run --group dev mwh …`; the `--group ui` isolation still applies.
Cross-reference D-42 (DECISIONS.md:498-520), Risk 12/13 (roadmap/README.md:394-395), DESIGN §2 EP-7 note (DESIGN.md:53-66). Effort S (~15 min); must fix now, before EP-13. Line-number correction to the finding: the EP-12 `python -` hang is at roadmap/EP-12-fixtures-icu-markers.md:197-198, not :171.

**Verifier notes.** Checked: CLAUDE.md lines 1-79 in full — §3 (lines 33-46) contains none of: uv PATH export, WinGet Links location, D-42/heredoc/`python -` prohibition, cp1252/_console_safe, default-groups; `git log -- CLAUDE.md` shows its only commit is the planning commit cd67743 (2026-08-16), i.e. it predates every EP and every hiccup. Verified environment live: Bash `which uv` → "no uv in (…)" (exit 1) and `python --version` → 3.14.7 at /c/Python314; PowerShell `Get-Command uv` → null, `Get-Command python` → C:\Python314\python.exe, no WinGet entry in $env:PATH; `$LOCALAPPDATA/Microsoft/WinGet/Links/` contains uv.exe/uvw.exe/uvx.exe. Verified D-42 text at DECISIONS.md:498-520 (heredocs killed as Trojan:Win32/ClickFix.FFQ!MTB, no burst loops, quarantine-check order), Risk 12/13 at roadmap/README.md:394-395 (cp1252 → `_console_safe`), DESIGN.md:53-66 EP-7 note, EP-8 completion note line 122 ("uv was reached via %LOCALAPPDATA%\Microsoft\WinGet\Links"), EP-12 completion note lines 197-198 ("one accidental `python -` stdin invocation hung for two minutes") — the finding's cited line 171 is wrong, content is at 197-198. Verified pyproject.toml:64 `default-groups = ["dev"]` and D-15 addendum (DECISIONS.md:86-92) already record it. Verified .pre-commit-config.yaml: three local hooks use `entry: uv run --project mimicwarehouse …` with `language: system`, and .git/hooks/pre-commit exec's the .venv python for pre-commit itself — so the finding's claim "git commit fails with uv absent from PATH" holds via the local hook entries (nuance added to fix). Also observed in this very session that the harness auto-mode instruction recommends heredocs/sed for file changes, confirming the contradiction the finding describes. The auto-memory file partly mitigates for this owner's account, but memory is neither repo-authoritative nor read by other tooling/agents; CLAUDE.md is the file every session reads first. Impact is real and recurrent (toolchain-missing misdiagnosis, pip into 3.14, killed shells, failed commits), effort S — severity high stands; verdict is confirmed-with-corrections only for the wrong EP-12 line number and the pre-commit mechanism detail.

### GOV-1 — Bash/PowerShell deny list is a prefix blocklist: python -c, uv run python, cp/tar/7z to scratchpad, and nested `sh -c` all bypass it

**Severity (verified):** high · **Verdict:** confirmed-with-corrections (high confidence) · **Kind:** design · **When:** owner-decision · **Effort:** XS · **Retro episode:** EP-165 · **Lens:** governance enforcement layers (deny rules, mwh guard, .gitignore/.gitattributes, hooks, GOVERNANCE text vs reality)

**Files:** `.claude/settings.json:19-70`, `mimicwarehouse/GOVERNANCE.md:76-80`, `roadmap/final-roadmap.md:142`

**Evidence.** Rules are of the form `Bash(cat *source material*)`, `Bash(head *mimicdata*)`, `Bash(cat *.csv)`, `PowerShell(Get-Content *.csv*)`; the only wildcard-prefixed rule is `Bash(*duckdb.exe*)`. Observed in this session: `uv run python -c "from mimicwarehouse.guard import ..."` ran unchallenged (so `uv run python -c "open('C:/mimicdata/...').read()"` would too); `echo probe-first && strings --version` was denied (deny rules DO apply to compound sub-commands), but `sh -c 'strings --version'` was NOT matched by any deny rule and was stopped only by the auto-mode classifier ("Blocked by classifier") — a layer that does not exist in plan/manual modes. No rule covers `cp`/`Copy-Item`/`robocopy`/`tar`/`7z`/`diff`/`od`/`nl`/`paste`/`perl`/`node`/`[IO.File]::ReadAllText`; and per EP-0's finding `Read(**/*.csv)` is project-relative, so a copy placed in the scratchpad is then readable with the Read tool.

**Impact.** A careless session that decides to 'copy a small sample to the scratchpad and inspect it', or to check a loader with `uv run python -c` against C:/mimicdata (EP-17/EP-18 loader work is exactly where this temptation appears), transmits row-level data to Anthropic with no project-side control firing. GOVERNANCE §4(3) presents the deny rules as an enforcement layer; today they are friction only.

**Proposed fix (reviewer).** (a) Owner-decision, XS: add literal-string deny rules for the obvious two-step paths — `Bash(cp *source material*)`, `Bash(cp *mimicdata*)`, `Bash(mv *mimicdata*)`, `Bash(python* *mimicdata*)`, `Bash(python* *source material*)`, `Bash(uv run python* *mimicdata*)`, `Bash(uv run python* *source material*)`, `Bash(sh -c *)`, `Bash(bash -c *)`, `Bash(perl *)`, `Bash(node -e *)`, `Bash(diff *.csv*)`, `PowerShell(Copy-Item *mimicdata*)`, `PowerShell(Copy-Item *source material*)`, `PowerShell(*ReadAllText*)`, `PowerShell(*ReadAllLines*)`, `PowerShell(gc *.csv*)`, `PowerShell(cat *.csv*)`, `PowerShell(type *.csv*)`, `Read(//C:/**/*.csv)`, `Read(//C:/**/*.csv.gz)`, `Read(//C:/**/*.parquet)` (probe with EP-0's synthetic two-line probe.csv in the scratchpad before recording). (b) See GOV-2 for the structural fix (PreToolUse hook). Record the change in GOVERNANCE §4(3), DECISIONS D-39 addendum, and roadmap Risk 8.

**Corrected fix (verifier).** (a) Owner-decision, XS-S (about 30 min incl. probing each new rule with a NONEXISTENT path such as C:/nonexistent_dir/mimicdata_probe/x.csv, which exercises the matcher without touching data): extend .claude/settings.json deny list with, at minimum: Bash(cp *mimicdata*), Bash(cp *source material*), Bash(mv *mimicdata*), Bash(mv *source material*), Bash(python* *mimicdata*), Bash(python* *source material*), Bash(uv run *python* *mimicdata*), Bash(uv run *python* *source material*) [note: the finding's `uv run python* *mimicdata*` misses `uv run --project mimicwarehouse python -c ...` because options precede `python`; do NOT use the broader `Bash(uv run *mimicdata*)`, it would deny legitimate `uv run mwh build --data-root C:\mimicdata` invocations], Bash(sh -c *), Bash(bash -c *), Bash(pwsh *), Bash(powershell *), Bash(cmd /c *), Bash(perl *), Bash(node -e *), Bash(nl *.csv*), Bash(od *), Bash(diff *.csv*), Bash(cat *.duckdb*), PowerShell(Copy-Item *mimicdata*), PowerShell(Copy-Item *source material*), PowerShell(cp *mimicdata*), PowerShell(cp *source material*), PowerShell(*ReadAllText*), PowerShell(*ReadAllLines*), PowerShell(*ReadAllBytes*), PowerShell(*OpenRead*), PowerShell(ipcsv *) [Import-Csv alias is NOT alias-resolved: `ipcsv C:/nonexistent_dir/probe_dir/x.txt` ran while `Import-Csv` is denied], PowerShell(bash *), PowerShell(sh *), PowerShell(cmd /c *), PowerShell(Invoke-Expression *), PowerShell(iex *), Read(//C:/**/*.csv), Read(//C:/**/*.csv.gz), Read(//C:/**/*.parquet), Read(//C:/**/*.duckdb). DROP from the finding's list: PowerShell(gc *.csv*), PowerShell(cat *.csv*), PowerShell(type *.csv*) — probes show all three are already denied today (the PowerShell tool evidently normalizes cat/gc/type to Get-Content, matching `PowerShell(Get-Content *.csv*)`); adding them is harmless but not a gap. (b) Treat this finding as the trigger condition of D-39 / final-roadmap.md:142 ("if deny rules + wrapper prove insufficient") having fired: unpark GOV-1 as a PreToolUse command-string hook (see GOV-2) — the static list stays a blocklist (script files that hard-code the path, `$R=$(uv run mwh paths ...)`-style indirection, and any command not enumerated still pass). Record in GOVERNANCE.md §4(3) (lines 80-83: state explicitly that the deny rules are a blocklist of common readers, not a data-path firewall), a D-39 addendum in DECISIONS.md, and roadmap/README.md Risk 8. Also note in Risk 8 that the auto-mode classifier is NOT a dependable layer: in this verification `sh -c 'cat "C:/nonexistent_dir/mimicdata_probe/x.csv"'` executed with no deny and no classifier block.

**Verifier notes.** Checked .claude/settings.json (all 67 rules, lines 5-71): Bash rules are command-name-anchored globs (cat/head/tail/less/more/sed/awk/grep/rg/cut/sort/zcat/gunzip/gzip/strings/xxd/hexdump; only `*duckdb.exe*` is name-agnostic); no rule names cp/mv/python/uv/sh/bash/perl/node/nl/od/diff, and no PowerShell rule names Copy-Item/.NET IO/Invoke-Expression/ipcsv. Read `mimicwarehouse/GOVERNANCE.md` 66-90 (§4(3) at lines 80-83 lists the deny rules as an enforcement layer; the finding's cited 76-80 is off by a few lines), `mimicwarehouse/DECISIONS.md` 317-321 and 396-428 (D-39 + EP-7 addendum: hook parked, `Read(**/*.csv)` project-relative), `roadmap/EP-0-baseline.md` 37-39 and 103-109 (probe.csv smoke test), `roadmap/README.md` Risk 8 (line 390), `roadmap/final-roadmap.md:142` (GOV-1 parked with trigger "if deny rules + wrapper prove insufficient"). Empirical read-only probes with NONEXISTENT paths (no data touched): Bash `cat "C:/nonexistent_dir/mimicdata_probe/x.csv"` -> denied by rule (matcher works, mid-string wildcards work); Bash `cp <same> <scratchpad>` -> NOT denied (ran, ENOENT); Bash `python -c "os.path.exists('...mimicdata_probe/x.csv')"` -> NOT denied; Bash `sh -c 'cat "...mimicdata_probe/x.csv"'` -> NOT denied and NOT classifier-blocked (worse than the finding reports); Bash `nl ".../x.csv"` -> NOT denied; PowerShell `Copy-Item <mimicdata path> <scratchpad>` -> NOT denied; PowerShell `[IO.File]::Exists(<mimicdata path>)` -> NOT denied; PowerShell `ipcsv x.txt` -> NOT denied (alias not resolved) whereas PowerShell `cat/gc/type ".../x.csv"` -> all DENIED (so those three proposed rules are redundant); PowerShell `hexdump x.txt` -> not denied (Bash rules do not apply cross-tool). `MWH_DATA_ROOT` is unset in the tool shell (`echo` returned empty), so env-var indirection is not live today but `mwh paths` output could supply it. Verdict: core claim (prefix/blocklist bypassable by cp/python -c/nested shells/PowerShell .NET) fully holds and impact is real (license obligation; EP-17/18 loader work is where a session would reach for `python -c` against the data root; plan/manual modes lack the classifier and even auto mode did not block the nested-shell probe). Corrections: three PowerShell alias rules already effective; `uv run python*` pattern misses `--project` form; add ipcsv/.NET read APIs/cross-shell nesting; cite exact GOVERNANCE lines 80-83. Severity kept high because the docs (GOVERNANCE §4(3), D-39) present deny rules as an enforcement layer and their own parked-hook trigger has now demonstrably fired; the owner could reasonably call it medium on the grounds that D-39/Risk 8 already admit the rules are partial and CLAUDE.md prose plus safe_query are the primary controls.

**Owner question (as posed by the reviewer).** Extend the static deny list with the ~20 rules above now (cheap, still a blocklist) and/or unpark GOV-1 as a PreToolUse command-string hook (GOV-2)? Recommendation: do both — the static rules cost nothing, and the hook is the only way to express 'any command mentioning mimicdata/source material/.csv that is not `uv run mwh …`'.

### ARCH-1 — Catalog '.new + os.replace' swap cannot succeed on Windows while a READ_ONLY reader is open; DESIGN section 6 calls it atomic and EP-57 holds cached READ_ONLY connections for the app's lifetime

**Severity (verified):** medium · **Verdict:** confirmed-with-corrections (high confidence) · **Kind:** design · **When:** owner-decision · **Effort:** M · **Retro episode:** EP-166 · **Lens:** data architecture parity/uniformity (lake / catalog / tier / DAG design vs EP-3/9/10/11/12 code)

**Files:** `mimicwarehouse/DESIGN.md:191-210`, `roadmap/README.md:99`, `roadmap/EP-21-stage-catalog-builder.md:33-40`, `roadmap/EP-30-safe-query-audit.md:59-64`, `roadmap/EP-35-run-ledger.md:50-52`, `roadmap/EP-57-ui-app-shell.md:7-10,55`, `mimicwarehouse/src/mimicwarehouse/config.py:622-624`

**Evidence.** DESIGN section 6: "`mwh build` is the only writer; it writes `<tier>.duckdb.new` and atomically swaps; the app, notebooks and analyses open `access_mode='READ_ONLY'`." Roadmap P2 standing decision repeats "build-to-`.new`-and-swap catalogs opened READ_ONLY". Probe run in the scratchpad with duckdb 1.5.5 (no data touched): a `duckdb.connect(path, read_only=True)` handle open -> `os.replace(t.duckdb.new, t.duckdb)` raises `PermissionError [WinError 5] Access is denied`; after `reader.close()` the same replace succeeds. EP-21 item 1 already concedes "On Windows the swap fails while a reader holds the file — raise a clear message", but EP-57 says "a READ_ONLY catalog connection cached per tier ... (`@st.cache_resource`)", i.e. held for the whole Streamlit process; EP-30/35 use the same swap for `warehouse/runs.duckdb` (`mwh runs refresh`, called while the app's Runs page has it open).

**Impact.** Every `mwh build --tier dev --select catalog`, every `mwh runs refresh` and every EP-34/EP-55 catalog extension will fail with the EP-21 message whenever the Lab app (or a notebook) is running - which is the normal state from P4 on. Owner workflow becomes 'quit the app to rebuild anything', contradicting the multi-reader promise the single-writer rule is built on; ⏱ background jobs that end with a catalog step (EP-23..27 -> EP-28 `catalog-full-p2`) will fail at their last step if the app is open.

**Proposed fix (reviewer).** Decide the reader/writer protocol before EP-21 and record it in DESIGN section 6 + roadmap README P2 standing decision. Recommended: versioned catalog files (`warehouse/<tier>/<build_id>.duckdb` + a tiny `warehouse/<tier>.current` pointer written atomically; `open_catalog` resolves the pointer; `catalog_path(tier)` returns the pointer target; stale versions deleted with retry when no handle holds them). Same scheme for `runs.duckdb`. Cheaper alternative: keep the swap, but the app opens/closes a connection per query (DuckDB open is milliseconds; only Streamlit-side caching of results) and EP-21's refusal stays as the safety net.

**Corrected fix (verifier).** (1) Now, S (~10 min, docs only): soften the wording in mimicwarehouse/DESIGN.md:201 ("writes `<tier>.duckdb.new` and atomically swaps") and roadmap/EP-21-stage-catalog-builder.md:12 ("atomically swapped") to state the real Windows contract: the swap is a rename that fails with PermissionError while any process holds `<tier>.duckdb` (or, for `runs.duckdb`, has it ATTACHed), so readers must be closed or the swap must use the rename-aside protocol below; add the same sentence to the roadmap README P2 standing decision (roadmap/README.md:99). (2) Owner decision before EP-21 (record in DESIGN §6 + DECISIONS addendum): choose the reader/writer protocol. Verified cheap option D: two-step rename-aside — `os.rename(<tier>.duckdb -> <tier>.duckdb.old)` succeeds while READ_ONLY duckdb 1.5.5 handles are open (DuckDB opens with FILE_SHARE_DELETE), then `os.replace(.new -> <tier>.duckdb)` succeeds, and `os.remove(.old)` succeeds (delete-pending) with the reader still alive; open readers keep serving the old snapshot. Caveats to write into EP-21 item 1/2: (a) not atomic — a sub-ms window with no `<tier>.duckdb`, so `open_catalog` should retry on FileNotFoundError; (b) DuckDB's in-process instance cache is keyed on path, so a second `duckdb.connect(same path)` in a process that still holds an old connection returns the OLD instance (probe: new in-process reader still saw the old table) — EP-57's `get_conn` must close + `st.cache_resource.clear()` when `meta.catalog_info.build_id`/mtime changes, and EP-30's per-call safe_query opener is unaffected across processes. Option A (versioned files + `.current` pointer) avoids caveat (b) by construction but is more code (~M). Option C (keep 'close the app to rebuild', which EP-21:42-44, EP-26:37 and EP-34:59 already assume) is acceptable if the owner tolerates it; then it should be stated once in DESIGN §6 rather than as scattered brief remarks. Recommendation: D (S–M) decided now so EP-21/EP-30/EP-35/EP-57 implement it once; add a fixture-tier test in EP-21 that swaps with a READ_ONLY handle open.

**Verifier notes.** Checked: DESIGN.md:191-210 (line 201 says "atomically swaps"; §6 also promises readers open READ_ONLY); roadmap/README.md:99 (P2 standing decision "build-to-`.new`-and-swap"); EP-21:33-46 (swap via os.replace; item 1 already concedes Windows failure with a 'close the app/notebooks' message and old catalog intact); EP-30:44-49 (safe_query ATTACHes runs.duckdb read-only; build_runs_db swaps runs.duckdb.new); EP-35:50-52 (runs refresh = build-to-.new-and-swap); EP-57:7-10,53-62 (`get_conn` @st.cache_resource held for the process, passed as conn= to safe_query, hence catalog AND attached runs.duckdb held for the app's lifetime); config.py:622-624; EP-26:37 and EP-34:59 (both already say 'close readers first on Windows'); EP-37:50 (concept builds require `--select catalog` reruns, so rebuilds are frequent from P3 on). Ran two scratchpad probes with the project venv duckdb 1.5.5 on throwaway temp DBs (no data root touched): os.replace(new->cur) with a read_only handle open -> PermissionError WinError 5 (finding's evidence reproduces); after close -> OK. Additional evidence the finding lacked: os.rename of the OPEN file succeeds, os.replace into the vacated name succeeds, os.remove of the renamed-aside open file succeeds; the old reader keeps reading the old data; but a new duckdb.connect on the same path inside the same process returned the OLD instance (DuckDB instance cache) — relevant to any fix. Corrections: severity is medium not high — the roadmap already plans this failure mode with a clear message and intact old catalog (workflow friction, no data loss, no silent corruption); the claim that ⏱ background jobs EP-23..27 'fail at their last step' does not hold (grep shows those jobs have no catalog step; EP-28's `catalog-full-p2` is a separate job and the app defaults to the dev tier, so it usually is not blocked); the 'contradicts the multi-reader promise' framing is overstated — many concurrent READ_ONLY readers plus one writer on `.new` still works, only the swap instant is blocked. Real drift confirmed: DESIGN §6/EP-21 'atomically' is inaccurate on Windows, EP-57's cached connection + attached runs.duckdb makes `mwh build --select catalog` and `mwh runs refresh` fail whenever the app is up, and the protocol is stated inconsistently across DESIGN §6 vs EP-21/26/34 remarks. Owner decision still required (options A/C/D), with D now the evidenced cheap default.

**Owner question (as posed by the reviewer).** How should readers coexist with catalog rebuilds on Windows? Options: (A) versioned catalog files + `.current` pointer (robust, small change to `catalog_path`/`open_catalog`, adds GC); (B) app/notebooks open per query and never cache connections (simplest, small latency cost, still fails for a notebook left open); (C) accept 'close the app to rebuild' and document it. Recommendation: A, decided now so EP-21/EP-30/EP-35/EP-57 build the same protocol once.

### ARCH-10 — EP-30's free-text heuristic (any VARCHAR value > 64 chars refused) contradicts GOVERNANCE 4.1 (dictionary tables allowed) and EP-29's meta.* dictionary

**Severity (verified):** medium · **Verdict:** confirmed-with-corrections (high confidence) · **Kind:** design · **When:** now-should · **Effort:** S · **Retro episode:** EP-170 · **Lens:** data architecture parity/uniformity (lake / catalog / tier / DAG design vs EP-3/9/10/11/12 code)

**Files:** `roadmap/EP-30-safe-query-audit.md:44-52`, `mimicwarehouse/GOVERNANCE.md:71-76`, `mimicwarehouse/DESIGN.md:325-334`, `roadmap/EP-29-catalog-data-dictionary.md:39-54`, `mimicwarehouse/src/mimicwarehouse/schema/contract.py:250-254`

**Evidence.** EP-30 item 1(e): "refuse if ... any VARCHAR column has a value longer than 64 characters or containing a newline (free-text heuristic; contract `free_text` columns are refused by name too)". GOVERNANCE section 4.1: sessions may receive "schemas, dimension/dictionary tables, counts, statistics". `d_icd_diagnoses.long_title`, `d_icd_procedures.long_title`, `d_hcpcs.long_description`, `d_labitems.label`+`fluid`+`category` and EP-29's `meta.columns.description` (the contract comments are sentences) routinely exceed 64 characters; the contract already exposes `Table.is_dim`.

**Impact.** `mwh sql "SELECT icd_code, long_title FROM mimiciv_hosp.d_icd_diagnoses WHERE ..."` and `mwh sql --describe` through safe_query (EP-30 item 4 routes them through it) will be refused as 'free text', so the one thing GOVERNANCE explicitly allows a session to read becomes unreachable, and EP-39/40 (itemid curation, code sets) which need dictionary text will work around the wrapper.

**Proposed fix (reviewer).** In EP-30 item 1(e): apply the length/newline heuristic only to columns of subject-keyed tables; for `is_dim` tables and `meta.*` rely on the contract `free_text` flag (none of the dims carry it) and keep the row cap; state the rule in DESIGN section 12.

**Corrected fix (verifier).** In roadmap/EP-30-safe-query-audit.md item 1(e) (lines 45-48): scope the ">64 chars / newline" heuristic to VARCHAR output columns of subject-keyed tables (contract `Table.subject_keyed`), and exempt (i) `d_*` dims (`Table.is_dim` — note `provider`/`caregiver` are also `is_dim`; they stay covered by the identifier-name refusal, so the exemption is only from the length heuristic), (ii) `meta.*` and `information_schema`, and (iii) an explicit contract allow-list for public dictionary text living in subject-keyed tables (at least `drgcodes.description` — the fixture DRG vocab already has a 78-char title — and `hcpcsevents.short_description`); refuse by contract `free_text: true` flag (to be introduced by EP-23/25/29 — the flag does not exist in schema/contract.py today, so EP-30 must state which flag name it consumes, e.g. `free_text` per EP-23 line 29). Make it consistent with EP-43 item 2(c) which already carries a "dictionary allow-list / --allow-text" carve-out (roadmap/EP-43-disclose-primitives.md:40-42). Add one allowed test to EP-30 item 6: `SELECT icd_code, long_title FROM mimiciv_hosp.d_icd_diagnoses WHERE icd_version = 10 AND icd_code LIKE 'I21%'` (the synthetic fixture icd.yaml already has 18 titles > 64 chars, so the fixture test will catch a regression) and `SELECT "schema", "table", description FROM meta.tables` (37 of 41 contract table comments exceed 64 chars). Record the rule in DESIGN.md §12 (lines 325-334) as a dated note when EP-30 ships. Effort S (brief edit ~20 min; no code exists yet).

**Verifier notes.** Checked: roadmap/EP-30-safe-query-audit.md lines 28-90 — item 1(e) applies the ">64 chars or newline" VARCHAR heuristic to every result with no dictionary carve-out, while item 1(c) (line 43) explicitly exempts `meta.*`, `d_*` dims and `information_schema` from the count-family requirement, i.e. the brief itself intends dictionary reads to be allowed — internal inconsistency confirmed. GOVERNANCE.md:73-74 allows "dimension/dictionary tables". EP-43 item 2(c) (roadmap/EP-43-disclose-primitives.md:40-42) uses a dictionary allow-list / `--allow-text` — cross-brief inconsistency confirmed. Verified with `uv run python` (contract loader): 7 `is_dim` tables (d_hcpcs, d_icd_diagnoses, d_icd_procedures, d_labitems, provider, caregiver, d_items); 37/41 table comments and 40/421 column comments exceed 64 chars (none contain newlines) → `SELECT ... description FROM meta.tables/meta.columns` (EP-29 line 43-45) would be refused. Verified the synthetic fixture vocab (src/mimicwarehouse/fixtures/vocab/*.yaml, YAML not CSV): icd.yaml has 18 titles > 64 chars (max 116, e.g. ICD-9 diabetes titles 91-106 chars, ICD-10-PCS 75-80), d_hcpcs.yaml 9 long_descriptions > 64 (max 168), categories.yaml drg_hcfa description 78 chars, d_items one label 87 — so the refusal is reproducible on the fixture tier, not just full. No `free_text` flag exists in schema/contract.py (grep: only comments mention it; EP-23/25/29 briefs add it) — the finding's phrase "contract free_text flag (none of the dims carry it)" is technically about a not-yet-existing flag. Corrections: (1) `mwh sql --describe`/`DESCRIBE` is NOT affected — DESCRIBE output columns are short and the brief lists `DESCRIBE mimiciv_hosp.patients` as an allowed test; drop that claim. (2) `d_labitems.label/fluid/category` do not "routinely" exceed 64 chars (fixture max ≤ 64; real labels are short) — the real offenders are d_icd_diagnoses/d_icd_procedures.long_title, d_hcpcs.long_description, meta.* descriptions and drgcodes.description (subject-keyed, so the proposed subject-keyed scoping alone would still refuse it). (3) EP-39/40 impact is partial: their `mwh codeset validate/expand` and `units.report` outputs come from module code, not safe_query; the concrete tripping points are EP-39 line 33-34 ("verified with `mwh sql` against d_items/d_labitems labels") and any session lookup of ICD titles via `mwh sql`. Severity medium stands: it is a plan-level defect in a pending brief, cheap to fix now, but would make GOVERNANCE-permitted dictionary reads unreachable once EP-30 ships.

### ARCH-2 — Lake directory swap is not atomic on Windows when the table already exists, and dev.duckdb views point at the same files a full-tier ⏱ restage rewrites

**Severity (verified):** medium · **Verdict:** confirmed-with-corrections (high confidence) · **Kind:** design · **When:** now-should · **Effort:** S · **Retro episode:** EP-166 · **Lens:** data architecture parity/uniformity (lake / catalog / tier / DAG design vs EP-3/9/10/11/12 code)

**Files:** `roadmap/EP-17-stage-loader-core.md:37-44`, `roadmap/EP-18-stage-loader-buckets.md:33-47`, `roadmap/EP-21-stage-catalog-builder.md:24-33`, `mimicwarehouse/DESIGN.md:125-135`

**Evidence.** EP-17 item 3: "then swaps `dest_dir.new` -> `dest_dir` atomically (`os.replace`; a stale `.new` is deleted first)"; EP-18 item 3: "A later **full** request on a table whose status is `tier_complete = "dev"` restages the whole table". Probe: `os.replace(dir.new, dir)` over an existing non-empty `dir` raises `PermissionError [WinError 5]` on Windows (MoveFileEx cannot replace a directory). DESIGN section 4: dev is "a partition filter over the same lake"; EP-21 builds dev views as `read_parquet('<abs glob>', hive_partitioning=true) WHERE subject_bucket IN (0,1,2,3,4)` over `lake/core/...` - the directory EP-18 pass 2 rewrites in place (`raw_*` deleted, `_sorting.tmp` -> `part-0.parquet`).

**Impact.** First-time stages work (dest absent). A restage (dev->full promotion, `--force`, a contract fix) must `rmtree(dest)` first, so there is a window with no table and a query from an open dev/full catalog view raises 'file not found' or the `os.replace` of `part-0.parquet` fails with WinError 5 if a reader has that Parquet file open. Nothing in DESIGN says a table under (re)stage is unavailable in every tier, and `partition_glob` is not pinned to `part-*.parquet`, so `raw_*.parquet` files of an in-progress large table are visible to the glob.

**Proposed fix (reviewer).** Add to DESIGN section 5/6 (and EP-17/18 acceptance) the rule: 'a table being (re)staged is unavailable in all tiers; restage = rename old dir to `<table>.old-<build_id>` (fast, same volume) -> rename `.new` -> delete `.old` with retry; catalogs list it as `missing` while `status.json` says in-progress'; pin `partition_glob` to `subject_bucket=*/part-*.parquet` so `raw_*` files are never matched; document that dev.duckdb over a table under full restage is transiently broken.

**Corrected fix (verifier).** Keep the finding's rule but correct the mechanism and add the concrete trip points. (1) Spec gap is real and un-mitigated: roadmap/EP-17-stage-loader-core.md:53-55 says only "swaps `dest_dir.new` -> `dest_dir` atomically (`os.replace`; a stale `.new` is deleted first)" and EP-18:31-32/37 repeat "swap `.new` -> `dest`"; neither brief nor DESIGN.md sec 5/6 (lines 174-205) says what happens when `dest` already exists. Verified by probe on this machine (CPython 3.13.15, NTFS): `os.replace(dir.new, dir)` raises PermissionError WinError 5 whether the existing dir is non-empty OR empty; `os.replace(_sorting.tmp, part-0.parquet)` raises WinError 5 while a reader holds the file; renaming a directory that contains an open file ALSO raises WinError 5; `shutil.rmtree` on it raises WinError 32. (2) Concrete downstream trips, not hypothetical: EP-19:78-79 fixture test "a second run skips completed steps and `--force` reruns" (dest exists -> WinError 5); EP-19:81-84 dev smoke `mwh build --tier dev --select stage.mimiciv_hosp.patients` then `--tier full` on the same table = the EP-18:50-52 dev->full promotion restage over an existing dest; EP-18:44-46 resume: `_progress.json` lives INSIDE `<dest>` so it can only be written after the swap; a crash between swap and progress-write leaves dest present with no progress file, and the rule "pass1_done false -> redo pass 1 into `.new`" then swaps into an existing dest -> WinError 5. (3) Corrected fix text for DESIGN sec 5 + EP-17 item 3 + EP-18 items 2-3: "restage = if `dest` exists rename it to `<table>.old-<build_id>` (same volume, instant), then `os.replace(.new -> dest)`, then `rmtree(.old)` with retry; a stale `.old-*` is removed at start; a table under (re)stage is unavailable in every tier and readers must be closed (the EP-19 build lock + EP-26:37 pre-flight already require app/notebooks closed for the catalog swap -- extend that sentence to the lake), because on Windows even the rename fails while any handle is open inside the directory (Python-style opens; DuckDB share mode not verified). Write pass-1 progress into `<dest>.new/_progress.json` before the swap so it travels with the directory (closes the crash window)." (4) Pin `partition_glob` (EP-18:55) to `subject_bucket=*/part-*.parquet` -- cheap hardening; note that the dev-view-over-raw_* concern is mostly theoretical (dev views require dev_ready = buckets 0-4 already sorted, hive pruning drops bucket>=5 files, large tables are never dev-only per EP-18:52), so it belongs in the same paragraph as a should-fix, not as the headline. Effort S (doc + brief edits now; ~20 lines of loader code when EP-17/18 are implemented).

**Verifier notes.** Checked: roadmap/EP-17-stage-loader-core.md:25-80 (item 3 swap wording, item 4 status.json atomic write), roadmap/EP-18-stage-loader-buckets.md:1-100 (small/large path swaps, resume rules incl. `_progress.json` inside `<dest>`, dev->full restage sentence, `partition_glob` unpinned), roadmap/EP-19-stage-dag-runner.md:36-100 (`--force` rerun test and dev->full patients smoke), roadmap/EP-20:20-50, EP-21:15-95 (dev views via `read_parquet_sql`, catalog swap message), EP-26:30-42 (pre-flight requires readers closed for the catalog swap only), mimicwarehouse/DESIGN.md:110-215 (sec 4-6: no restage/unavailability rule), grep of DESIGN/DECISIONS/roadmap for atomic/restage/.old/rmtree (nothing covers directory replacement). Ran a scratchpad probe (uv run python probe_replace.py, temp dir only): dest-absent OK; dest-exists non-empty -> PermissionError 5; dest-exists empty -> PermissionError 5; file replace with open reader -> PermissionError 5; dir rename with open file inside -> PermissionError 5; rmtree with open file -> WinError 32. Existing code precedent: src/mimicwarehouse/inventory.py:503-515 already retries os.replace for the same Windows reader problem (EP-10), which corroborates the concern. Verdict: the core claim (spec never addresses restage over an existing dir; os.replace fails; catalogs/design have no unavailability rule) fully holds and will trip EP-19's own tests; corrections needed because the proposed `.old` rename also fails while a reader holds a file (so "readers closed" must be the primary rule, rename is only the fast-path), the `_progress.json` placement creates an additional crash window the finding missed, and the raw_*-visible-to-dev-view part is over-weighted (hive pruning + dev_ready ordering make it near-theoretical). Severity medium stands; when = now-should (brief/DESIGN edits before EP-17 starts).

### ARCH-3 — Fixture (and demo) tiers have no defined lake root; Settings.catalog_path('fixture') points into the real data root while EP-21 says 'temp root' and EP-19's default lake root is lake/core; Settings.layout lacks lake_demo / lake_fixture / lake_rejects

**Severity (verified):** medium · **Verdict:** confirmed-with-corrections (high confidence) · **Kind:** design · **When:** owner-decision · **Effort:** S · **Retro episode:** EP-167 · **Lens:** data architecture parity/uniformity (lake / catalog / tier / DAG design vs EP-3/9/10/11/12 code)

**Files:** `mimicwarehouse/src/mimicwarehouse/config.py:74-90,600-624`, `roadmap/EP-19-stage-dag-runner.md:31-38`, `roadmap/EP-21-stage-catalog-builder.md:8-9,24-33,60-64`, `roadmap/EP-22-stage-demo-tier.md:37-44`, `roadmap/EP-17-stage-loader-core.md:52-56`, `mimicwarehouse/tests/ep/test_ep03.py:194,391`, `mimicwarehouse/GOVERNANCE.md:97-103`, `roadmap/EP-57-ui-app-shell.md:28`, `mimicwarehouse/DESIGN.md:87-110`

**Evidence.** `config.py` `Tier = Literal["fixture","demo","dev","full"]` and `catalog_path(tier) -> self.layout["warehouse"] / f"{tier}.duckdb"` (so `fixture` -> `C:\mimicdata\warehouse\fixture.duckdb`). EP-21 context: "one `.duckdb` file per tier under `C:\mimicdata\warehouse\` (`fixture` under a temp root, `demo`, `dev`, `full`)" and item 1: "`fixture` and `demo` require complete on their own lake roots" - but no brief defines the fixture lake root; EP-19 item 2 resolves only the raw root per tier (`fixture` -> `tests/fixtures/`) and takes `data_root=None`, so `mwh build --tier fixture` without `--data-root` would write synthetic Parquet into `lake/core/<schema>/<table>` - the same directories as dev/full - and stamp `status.json` for the real lake. EP-22 item 2 introduces `lake\demo\` and EP-17 item 5 `lake/rejects/...`; neither is in `LAYOUT_KEYS` (15 keys, pinned by test_ep03 lines 194/391 and `mwh paths --create`). GOVERNANCE section 6: row views "are developed and screenshotted only against `fixture`/`demo` tiers", yet EP-57 offers `mwh app [--tier demo|dev|full]` (no fixture) and no persisted fixture catalog exists anywhere.

**Impact.** EP-19's fixture-tier smoke run or any owner `mwh build --tier fixture` pollutes the credentialed lake and `status.json` (`mimiciv_hosp.patients` marked complete with 120 synthetic subjects, later merged into full snapshot ids); EP-21/EP-22 each invent a lake root and a layout key ad hoc (two 15->16->17 pin bumps and two `mwh paths` doc edits); the app cannot be developed on the fixture tier as GOVERNANCE requires.

**Proposed fix (reviewer).** Before EP-17: add `Settings.lake_root(tier)` (`lake` for dev/full, `lake/demo`, `lake/fixture`), layout keys `lake_demo`, `lake_fixture`, `lake_rejects` (bump the pin once, update DESIGN section 3 tree and `mwh paths`), make `mwh build --tier fixture|demo` default to those roots (tests keep `--data-root <tmp>`), keep `catalog_path('fixture')` under the data root so `mwh app --tier fixture` / `mwh sql --tier fixture` work without a temp root, and add `fixture` to EP-57's tier switcher.

**Corrected fix (verifier).** Decide the fixture/demo lake roots once, before EP-17 starts (owner decision; recommend option A):

1. `config.py`: add `Settings.lake_root(tier)` -> `layout["lake"]` for `dev`/`full`, `layout["lake_demo"]` (`lake/demo`) for `demo`, `layout["lake_fixture"]` (`lake/fixture`) for `fixture`; add layout keys `lake_demo`, `lake_fixture` (and `lake_rejects` = `lake/rejects`, or specify that rejects live under `<lake_root(tier)>/rejects/` so demo/fixture rejects never mix with the credentialed ones). One pin bump 15 -> 18 in `tests/ep/test_ep03.py:194,391`, the `layout` docstring / `LAYOUT_KEYS` comment (`config.py:12,77,601`), the DESIGN §3 tree (`DESIGN.md:96-110`, and change the `warehouse` line `{demo,dev,full}.duckdb` to `{fixture,demo,dev,full}.duckdb`), and `mwh paths` output.
2. EP-19 brief item 2: state that `StepContext.lake_root = settings.lake_root(tier)` and add a hard refusal (not just a convention) that a `fixture`/`demo` build never resolves `lake_root == layout["lake"]`; keep tests on `--data-root tmp_path` as written.
3. EP-17/EP-18 `status.json`: `tier_complete: null|dev|full` has no value for `fixture`/`demo`; either give each non-credentialed lake root its own `manifests/status.json` with `tier_complete = "<tier>"` (what EP-21's "complete on their own lake roots" already implies) or extend the vocabulary — say which in EP-17 item 4 and EP-21 item 1.
4. EP-22 item 2 / EP-17 item 5: reference the new layout keys instead of inventing `lake\demo\` and `lake/rejects/` ad hoc.
5. `catalog_path` needs no change (it is data-root-relative, so `--data-root <tmp>` already yields a temp-root fixture catalog); under option A `mwh build --tier fixture` with the default data root writes `warehouse/fixture.duckdb` and EP-57's `mwh app --tier` list (`EP-57-ui-app-shell.md:28`) gains `fixture`; under option B make `lake_root("fixture")` raise unless `data_root` was overridden and reword GOVERNANCE §6 to "demo tier (or a temp-root fixture catalog)".
Effort S (config + docs + one pin bump), plus 4 one-line brief edits.

**Verifier notes.** Checked: `mimicwarehouse/src/mimicwarehouse/config.py:67` (`Tier` includes `fixture`), `:77-94` (15 `LAYOUT_KEYS`, no lake_demo/lake_fixture/lake_rejects), `:600-624` (`layout` and `catalog_path(tier) = warehouse/<tier>.duckdb`; no per-tier lake-root helper; `raw`/`source_root` exists but no tier mapping); `tests/ep/test_ep03.py:194,391` pin `len(layout)==15` and exactly 15 dirs; `DESIGN.md:96-110` §3 tree lists warehouse as `{demo,dev,full}.duckdb` (fixture absent) and no `lake/demo`, `lake/fixture`, `lake/rejects`; `DESIGN.md:129` fixture tier "Where = repo"; `roadmap/EP-19-stage-dag-runner.md:41-52` runner takes `data_root=None`, resolves only the raw root per tier and passes an unspecified `lake_root` in `StepContext`; item 6 builds fixture into `tmp_path` (so the brief's own tests do not hit the real lake); `EP-21:8-9,31-33,60-64` says "fixture under a temp root" and "fixture and demo require complete on their own lake roots" but no brief defines the fixture lake root; `EP-22:37-44` invents `lake\demo\`; `EP-17:71-73` invents `lake/rejects/<schema>/<table>/`; `EP-17:64-68`/`EP-18:50` `tier_complete: null|dev|full` has no fixture/demo value, so "complete on their own lake roots" is undefined; `GOVERNANCE.md:97-103` row views developed on fixture/demo; `EP-57:28` `mwh app [--tier demo|dev|full]`; root `tests/conftest.py` does not set `MWH_DATA_ROOT` (only test_ep03's own `data_root` fixture does, line 98-102), so any future CliRunner test or ad-hoc `mwh build --tier fixture` that omits `--data-root` resolves to the real root. Verdict: the design gap is real and touches EP-17/19/21/22/57, and the layout-key pin will be bumped ad hoc twice without a decision. Corrections: (a) `catalog_path('fixture')` is not a contradiction - it is data-root-relative, so under `--data-root <tmp>` it lands in the temp root exactly as EP-21 says; both owner options are compatible with today's code; (b) the "pollutes the credentialed lake" impact is not on the paved path (EP-19/EP-21 tests use temp roots) - it needs a manual/ad-hoc `mwh build --tier fixture` without `--data-root`, and EP-17's `os.replace` swap onto an existing non-empty dir fails on Windows rather than silently clobbering, though `status.json` entries and manifests would still be written; hence medium, not high; (c) GOVERNANCE §6 is satisfiable by the demo tier alone, so the EP-57 point is a low-severity inconsistency, not a blocker; (d) the fix should also cover the `status.json` tier vocabulary and a runtime refusal in the runner, which the original fix omits.

**Owner question (as posed by the reviewer).** Where does the fixture tier live when built for keeps? Options: (A) `<data_root>/lake/fixture` + `warehouse/fixture.duckdb` (uniform `catalog_path`, app/screenshots on fixture possible, tests still use tmp roots); (B) fixture is temp-only and `catalog_path('fixture')` should raise. Recommendation: A - and decide it now because EP-19, EP-21, EP-22 and EP-57 all touch it.

### ARCH-4 — Contract sort_keys (and one load_class) disagree with what EP-20/23/24/25/26/27 each expect; six briefs would edit the contract piecemeal

**Severity (verified):** medium · **Verdict:** confirmed-with-corrections (high confidence) · **Kind:** design · **When:** now-should · **Effort:** S · **Retro episode:** EP-169 · **Lens:** data architecture parity/uniformity (lake / catalog / tier / DAG design vs EP-3/9/10/11/12 code)

**Files:** `mimicwarehouse/src/mimicwarehouse/schema/tables/mimiciv_hosp.yaml`, `mimicwarehouse/src/mimicwarehouse/schema/tables/mimiciv_icu.yaml`, `roadmap/EP-20-stage-small-tables.md:24-35`, `roadmap/EP-23-stage-labevents.md:26-27`, `roadmap/EP-24-stage-emar.md:24-27`, `roadmap/EP-25-stage-hosp-rest.md:22-30`, `roadmap/EP-26-stage-chartevents.md:29-30`, `roadmap/EP-27-stage-icu-events.md:22-27`, `roadmap/EP-9-schema-registry.md:59-61`, `mimicwarehouse/DESIGN.md:174-190`

**Evidence.** Contract as shipped: `transfers [subject_id, intime]`, `procedures_icd [subject_id, hadm_id, seq_num]`, `hcpcsevents [subject_id, chartdate]`, `procedureevents [subject_id, starttime]`, `outputevents [subject_id, charttime]`, `labevents [subject_id, charttime]`, `chartevents [subject_id, charttime]`, `emar [subject_id, charttime]`, `emar_detail [subject_id, emar_id, parent_field_ordinal]`, `pharmacy/prescriptions [subject_id, starttime]`, `poe [subject_id, ordertime]`, `microbiologyevents [subject_id, chartdate, charttime]` + `load_class: large`, `inputevents/ingredientevents [subject_id, starttime]`, `datetimeevents [subject_id, charttime]`.
