### ARCH-5 — Snapshot id = hash of manifest lines including Parquet sha256 is not stable across rebuilds (non-total sort orders + preserve_insertion_order=false), and the dev snapshot changes when full pass 2 finishes

**Severity (verified):** medium · **Verdict:** confirmed-with-corrections (high confidence) · **Kind:** design · **When:** owner-decision · **Effort:** S · **Retro episode:** EP-166 · **Lens:** data architecture parity/uniformity (lake / catalog / tier / DAG design vs EP-3/9/10/11/12 code)

**Files:** `mimicwarehouse/DESIGN.md:174-190,310-323`, `roadmap/EP-17-stage-loader-core.md:45-52,80-82`, `roadmap/EP-18-stage-loader-buckets.md:37-47,80`, `roadmap/EP-19-stage-dag-runner.md:44-48`, `roadmap/EP-47-cohort-compiler-attrition.md:39`, `roadmap/README.md:398-400`

**Evidence.** DESIGN section 5: "Every Parquet file has a manifest line (`sha256, bytes, rows, schema_hash, ...`) ... the **snapshot id** of a layer is the hash of its manifest"; EP-19 item 3: `layer_snapshot(...) = sha256 over the sorted manifest lines of every table whose status is complete for the tier (dev_ready suffices for dev)`. EP-18 pass 1 is `COPY ... PARTITION_BY` with `preserve_insertion_order=false`, pass 2 sorts by `(subject_id, <sort_by>)`; with the contract's `[subject_id, charttime]` (and even the briefs' `[.., itemid]`) chartevents/labevents have many tied rows whose relative order after a parallel scan is not deterministic, so file bytes and sha256 differ between two identical rebuilds. EP-17/18 assert 'sha256 identical across two runs' only on `d_labitems`, `patients`, fixture `admissions` (unique keys / no ties). EP-47: "Rebuild with the same spec + snapshot ids reproduces a byte-identical" cohort. For dev, `layer_snapshot(dev)` includes every manifest line of a small table (all 100 buckets) and, for large tables, whatever buckets are sorted so far - so the dev snapshot id changes when buckets 5-99 finish although dev's data did not.

**Impact.** A snapshot id will identify a build, not a data state: two full rebuilds from unchanged raw + code yield different `core_snapshot_id`s, run manifests and cohort registries cannot recognise 'same data', and the promise in roadmap README 'Derived layers carry manifests whose hash is their snapshot id ... reproduce from them' is only nominally met. Dev-tier runs cite ids that move underneath them during ⏱ jobs.

**Proposed fix (reviewer).** Define in DESIGN section 11 (and EP-17/19) two identifiers: a physical per-file `sha256` (integrity only) and a *logical* layer snapshot id = sha256 over sorted `(schema, table, path, rows, schema_hash, source_sha256/raw_snapshot_id, sort_keys, writer_version)` - stable when raw + contract + code are unchanged; for `dev`, hash only lines whose path lies in `dev_buckets` plus unpartitioned tables. Where a natural unique key exists (labevent_id, emar_id/emar_seq, poe_seq, orderid, transfer_id) also adopt it as the last sort key (ARCH-4) so bytes are reproducible too.

**Corrected fix (verifier).** Keep the finding's recommendation A, with these corrections/additions. (1) DESIGN section 5 (l.180-183) and section 11 (l.323) plus EP-19 item 3 (actual lines 53-56, not 44-48) should define two identifiers: the per-file `sha256` in the manifest line is integrity-only; the layer *snapshot id* is a logical hash = sha256 over the sorted JSON of `(schema, table, path, rows, schema_hash, source_manifest_id/raw_snapshot_id, sort_keys, writer_version)` per file, exactly the pattern EP-10 already uses for `raw_snapshot_id` (DESIGN l.606-608: sha256 of the sorted `(rel_path, bytes, sha256, rows)` tuples) - cite that precedent so EP-19 does not invent a third scheme. For `tier="dev"` hash only lines whose `path` lies under `subject_bucket=<n>` with n in `DEV_BUCKETS`, plus unpartitioned tables; state explicitly that the dev id must not move when buckets 5-99 finish or when a dev-only small table is restaged for full. (2) Correct the evidence: the two-pass path was NOT shown to be non-deterministic - two identical runs (duckdb 1.5.5, 12 threads, CSV source, 20_000_000 tie-heavy synthetic rows, one raw file per bucket) gave byte-identical part-0.parquet in 100/100 buckets; the risk at 40 GB (several raw files per bucket, spills) is real but unproven, so phrase it as "not guaranteed", not "differ". (3) The EP-18 *small* path is the demonstrably broken one and needs its own must-fix at EP-18: `COPY (... ORDER BY subject_bucket, subject_id, <sort_by>) TO ... (PARTITION_BY (subject_bucket))` with `preserve_insertion_order=true` on duckdb 1.5.5 produced partition files that (a) differ across two identical runs in 59/100 buckets and (b) are NOT sorted in 79/100 buckets - exactly one out-of-order seam per file at a vector-aligned position (e.g. row 154240, 2560 of 200000), while a per-bucket `COPY (SELECT ... WHERE subject_id % 100 = n ORDER BY subject_id, <sort_by>) TO <one file>` loop was sorted in 10/10 and byte-stable. EP-18 item 1 (brief l.30-34) must therefore write small subject-keyed tables per bucket (or reuse the pass-2 per-bucket sort for every partitioned table) instead of relying on ORDER BY + PARTITION_BY, and the acceptance line "sorted row groups" (l.80) needs a sortedness assertion at dev scale (`tier("dev")` admissions/transfers, via `read_parquet(..., file_row_number=true)` lag check) because the fixture (single batch per partition) cannot expose the seam. (4) Where a natural unique key exists (labevent_id, emar_id+parent_field_ordinal, poe_id, orderid+itemid, transfer_id, microevent_id) append it as the last `sort_keys` entry in the EP-9 YAML (ARCH-4) so bytes are reproducible where cheap; chartevents/prescriptions/outputevents keep a non-total order and rely on the logical id. Effort: S for the doc/brief edits; the EP-18 small-path change is inside EP-18's own budget. Owner decision stays as stated (A recommended); the EP-47/README "reproduce" wording is fine under A because cohort.parquet is sorted by unique keys and only needs logical snapshot equality.

**Verifier notes.** Checked: mimicwarehouse/DESIGN.md l.174-190 (section 5: 'the snapshot id of a layer is the hash of its manifest'; two-pass with preserve_insertion_order=false), l.310-323 (section 11 'Snapshot id = hash of the layer manifest'), l.125-140 (dev = partition filter over the same lake), l.604-612 (EP-10 raw_snapshot_id = hash of sorted tuples, i.e. a logical id precedent); roadmap/EP-17 l.40-82 (manifest line fields; determinism test only on d_labitems/patients); EP-18 l.30-80 (small path = ORDER BY + PARTITION_BY 'so each partition file is sorted'; pass 2 sorts by subject_id,<sort_by>; determinism test only on fixture admissions); EP-19 l.53-56 (layer_snapshot = sha256 over sorted manifest lines of every table complete for the tier, dev_ready suffices for dev - no bucket filter, so lines for buckets 5-99 and full restages of dev-only small tables move the dev id); EP-47 l.39-40 (byte-identical cohort.parquet given same spec + snapshot ids); roadmap/README l.398-400; schema/tables/mimiciv_{hosp,icu}.yaml sort_keys (chartevents/labevents/emar/outputevents/prescriptions etc. = [subject_id, charttime|starttime], non-total) and keys.yaml (labevent_id, emar_id, orderid+itemid, transfer_id, microevent_id available as unique keys; chartevents has upstream duplicates). Ran five scratch DuckDB 1.5.5 experiments (synthetic ids >= 90_000_000 only, scratchpad, deleted after): two-pass emulation (COPY PARTITION_BY preserve_insertion_order=false, then per-bucket ORDER BY COPY) at 6_000_000 and 20_000_000 rows from table and CSV sources with 8/12 threads -> 0/100 buckets differ in bytes or row order across two runs (finding's flat non-determinism claim for the large path not reproduced at this scale; one raw file per bucket each time). Small-path emulation (single COPY ... ORDER BY subject_bucket, subject_id, charttime ... PARTITION_BY, preserve_insertion_order=true) at 20_000_000 rows -> row order differs across two identical runs in 59/100 buckets, and with a robust file_row_number-based lag check 79/100 partition files are unsorted (one seam each; controls: single-file COPY ORDER BY and per-bucket WHERE+ORDER BY loop are sorted and stable). Conclusion: the design point (physical sha256 as snapshot id = build identity, dev id drifting during full pass 2) holds; the evidence should be re-attributed to the small path, whose brief text is additionally wrong about sortedness; severity medium is right for the id semantics, with the EP-18 sortedness seam flagged as a separate must-fix.

**Owner question (as posed by the reviewer).** Should the snapshot id be physical (file bytes; changes on every rebuild unless every table has a total sort order) or logical (rows/schema/source; stable)? Options: (A) logical id + physical sha256 kept for integrity (recommended); (B) physical id, and every table gets a total-order sort key (chartevents has upstream duplicate rows, so 'sort by all columns' - expensive); (C) accept build-identity semantics and drop the 'reproduces byte-identical' claims from EP-47/README.

### ARCH-6 — Provenance identifiers are named/defined inconsistently: source_manifest_id (per-file sha256, EP-17) vs 'raw_snapshot_id is the source manifest id every lake manifest cites' (D-26 addendum) vs snapshot_id / core_snapshot_id / snapshot_ids

**Severity (verified):** medium · **Verdict:** confirmed-with-corrections (high confidence) · **Kind:** docs-drift · **When:** now-should · **Effort:** S · **Retro episode:** EP-166 · **Lens:** data architecture parity/uniformity (lake / catalog / tier / DAG design vs EP-3/9/10/11/12 code)

**Files:** `mimicwarehouse/DECISIONS.md:196-208`, `mimicwarehouse/DESIGN.md:174-182,310-323`, `roadmap/EP-17-stage-loader-core.md:45-52`, `roadmap/EP-19-stage-dag-runner.md:44-48`, `roadmap/EP-21-stage-catalog-builder.md:31-33`, `roadmap/EP-30-safe-query-audit.md:27,59`, `roadmap/EP-35-run-ledger.md:26-27`, `roadmap/EP-31-tracer-bullet.md:48`, `mimicwarehouse/src/mimicwarehouse/inventory.py:76-90,560-580`

**Evidence.** D-26 addendum: "**`raw_snapshot_id = 8209301d...`** ... This id is the `source manifest id` every lake manifest (EP-17+) cites". EP-17 item 4: "`source_manifest_id` (the sha256 EP-10 recorded for the source file in `lake/manifests/raw/<dataset>.jsonl`, or `"fixture"`)" - a per-file hash, not the snapshot id. DESIGN section 5 lists "source manifest id" (singular, undefined). EP-21 stores `core_snapshot_id`; EP-30 audits a bare `snapshot_id`; EP-35 records `snapshot_ids` (layer -> id); EP-31 cites `core_snapshot_id`; EP-19 writes `snapshots.json` entries `{layer, tier, snapshot_id, build_id, ts}`. `inventory.py` also computes `raw_snapshot_id` only when all 41 files (incl. Note) are present (`FILES_EXPECTED = 41`).

**Impact.** EP-17 will implement one meaning, EP-35/EP-47 will look for another; the raw -> lake -> catalog -> run chain cannot be walked mechanically (which id in a run manifest tells you which raw file version was read?).

**Proposed fix (reviewer).** Add a short identifier glossary to DESIGN section 11: `raw_snapshot_id` (EP-10, all 41 files), per-file `source_sha256` (EP-10 record), `build_id` (EP-19), layer `snapshot_id` per `{core, derived, marts, notes}` (EP-19), catalog `build_id` + `core_snapshot_id` (EP-21), `run_id`, `audit_id`, protocol hash. Make EP-17's `ManifestLine` carry both `source_sha256` and `raw_snapshot_id`; rename the D-26 sentence accordingly; use `snapshot_ids` (dict) everywhere a run/audit cites layers.

**Corrected fix (verifier).** Narrow the finding to the one genuine contradiction and fix it in the EP-16 re-plan (or now, S effort): (1) EP-17 brief roadmap/EP-17-stage-loader-core.md:50,61-63 (and EP-18 :26) define `source_manifest_id` as the per-file sha256 from lake/manifests/raw/<dataset>.jsonl (or "fixture"), whereas D-26 addendum DECISIONS.md:204-205, DESIGN.md:635 (EP-10 note: "EP-17/18/19 cite `raw_snapshot_id()` as the `source manifest id`"), the EP-10 brief :34 and :174, and EP-16 :35 all say the source manifest id IS the 41-file `raw_snapshot_id`; DESIGN.md:181 (section 5) leaves "source manifest id" undefined. Amend EP-17 item 4 so `ManifestLine` carries BOTH `source_sha256` (per-file, or null for fixture) and `raw_snapshot_id` (from `inventory.raw_snapshot_id()`, or "fixture"); update the `stage_unpartitioned`/`stage_partitioned` keyword accordingly in EP-17:50 and EP-18:26; reword D-26 addendum, DESIGN.md:181 and :635, EP-10:34/174 to name both fields. (2) Add a short identifier glossary to DESIGN section 11 (raw_snapshot_id, source_sha256, build_id, layer snapshot_id, core_snapshot_id, snapshot_ids dict, run_id, audit_id) and state in EP-30 that its bare `snapshot_id` is the queried catalog's `core_snapshot_id` from `meta.catalog_info`. (3) Drop the claim that snapshot_id / core_snapshot_id / snapshot_ids are inconsistent: EP-19:52-57 defines `layer_snapshot(...)` per {layer, tier}, EP-21:40-41 stores it as `core_snapshot_id`, EP-31:48 reads that same field, EP-35:27 records the dict `snapshot_ids` (layer -> id) via EP-19 helpers - one definition, consistently scoped; only EP-30's unlabeled `snapshot_id` needs the one-line clarification. Note EP-16 (roadmap/EP-16-replan-p1.md:35-38) already lists `raw_snapshot_id()` (EP-10) among the P2 names to amend, so it is the natural vehicle.

**Verifier notes.** Checked: DECISIONS.md:196-208 (D-26 addendum, quote verified: "This id is the `source manifest id` every lake manifest (EP-17+) cites"); DESIGN.md:174-182 (section 5 manifest line lists undefined "source manifest id"), :310-323 (section 11: "Snapshot id = hash of the layer manifest"), :606-635 (EP-10 note asserting EP-17/18/19 cite raw_snapshot_id() as the source manifest id); EP-17:45-63 (source_manifest_id = per-file sha256 from raw/<dataset>.jsonl or "fixture") and EP-18:26 (same kwarg); EP-10 brief :30-34, :171-174 (raw snapshot id = the source manifest id); EP-16:30-38; EP-19:44-57 (layer_snapshot -> snapshots.json {layer,tier,snapshot_id,build_id,ts}; "EP-21 stores it in meta.catalog_info; EP-35 cites it"); EP-21:31-44 (core_snapshot_id in meta.catalog_info); EP-30:25-27,57-59 (bare snapshot_id); EP-35:22-30,84 (snapshot_ids dict); EP-31:45-50; EP-47:77; inventory.py:73 (FILES_EXPECTED = 41), :564-579 (compute_snapshot_id returns None below 41 files, raw_snapshot_id()). git log: EP-17 brief unchanged since cd67743 (2026-08-16), i.e. written before EP-10 (8418a53, 2026-08-17) redefined the term - explains the drift. Verdict: the source_manifest_id contradiction is real and multi-sourced (two authoritative docs vs the brief that will be implemented), so medium docs-drift is fair; but the layer-snapshot part of the finding is over-stated - the EP-19 -> EP-21 -> EP-31/EP-35 chain uses one definition consistently, with only EP-30's field lacking a scope label. Impact is real but bounded: EP-17 will implement the per-file meaning per CLAUDE.md brief-first rule, leaving D-26/DESIGN wording wrong rather than breaking EP-35 (which uses EP-19 helpers, not source_manifest_id).

### ARCH-7 — EP-22 wants '--tier demo' in the pytest ladder, but EP-12 shipped and tests the opposite (demo is 'never a test tier'; --tier demo is a usage error)

**Severity (verified):** medium · **Verdict:** confirmed-with-corrections (high confidence) · **Kind:** design · **When:** owner-decision · **Effort:** S · **Retro episode:** EP-168 · **Lens:** data architecture parity/uniformity (lake / catalog / tier / DAG design vs EP-3/9/10/11/12 code)

**Files:** `roadmap/EP-22-stage-demo-tier.md:59-64`, `mimicwarehouse/tests/conftest.py:1-60`, `mimicwarehouse/tests/ep/test_ep12.py:810-815,849`, `mimicwarehouse/tests/README.md:32-38`, `mimicwarehouse/DESIGN.md:823-838`

**Evidence.** EP-22 item 5: "Extend EP-12's tier vocabulary with `demo` (`--tier demo` selects fixture + demo; skipped with a reason when `warehouse\demo.duckdb` is absent). `tier("demo")`-marked (opt-in ...)". conftest: `TIERS = ("fixture", "dev", "full")` ... "the ladder is deliberately the three-step subset ... (`demo` is a data tier for EP-22, never a test tier)"; test_ep12 line 810-815 asserts `PYTEST_TIER=demo` and `--tier demo` are refused with "is not a test tier"; DESIGN section 20 note says the same.

**Impact.** EP-22 either breaks test_ep12 (and rewrites a documented EP-12 decision without a decision record) or silently drops its demo tests; a linear 'maximum tier' ladder cannot express an orthogonal tier anyway (demo is not 'above' fixture and 'below' dev).

**Proposed fix (reviewer).** Amend EP-22 item 5: keep the ladder; add an orthogonal opt-in `@pytest.mark.demo` marker + `--with-demo` option (or `PYTEST_DEMO=1`), skipped with a reason when `catalog_path('demo')` is missing; note it in tests/README.md and DESIGN section 20.

**Corrected fix (verifier).** At the EP-16 re-plan (already in EP-16's In-scope: "amend the next phase's briefs ... the tier-marker vocabulary (EP-12)"), take the owner decision and add an "Amended at EP-16 re-plan" header to roadmap/EP-22-stage-demo-tier.md that rewrites item 5 (lines 59-64) AND the acceptance line 71. Recommended option A: keep `TIERS = ("fixture","dev","full")` and the maximum-tier semantics; add an orthogonal opt-in marker `@pytest.mark.demo` (registered in `pytest_configure`) plus `--with-demo` option (fallback env `PYTEST_DEMO=1`, deliberately not `MWH_`-prefixed for the same extra="forbid" reason); demo-marked tests are deselected unless opted in and skipped with a reason when `get_settings().catalog_path("demo")` is missing (reuse `catalog_status`, which already accepts any `config.Tier`); update the conftest docstring, tests/README.md ladder paragraph, DESIGN section 20 (dated note) and test_ep12's `test_poe_tasks_and_docs` needle list; `mwh verify EP-22 -- --with-demo` passes through via EP-6's `--`. Option B (fixture < demo < dev < full ladder) requires rewriting conftest TIERS/choices, three test_ep12 assertions, tests/README.md, DESIGN section 20 and a DECISIONS addendum superseding the EP-12 header note — not recommended because demo is not "below" dev in data terms. Option C (no demo-marked pytest tests; EP-22 verifies demo via `mwh build --tier demo` / `mwh verify` CLI only) needs only the brief amendment. Effort: S (brief text at EP-16) + S (conftest/docs if A or B, inside EP-22).

**Verifier notes.** Checked: roadmap/EP-22-stage-demo-tier.md lines 54-64 (item 5: "Extend EP-12's tier vocabulary with `demo` (`--tier demo` selects fixture + demo; skipped with a reason when `warehouse\demo.duckdb` is absent). `tier("demo")`-marked (opt-in ...)") and line 71 acceptance ("`tier("demo")`-marked tests green after `mwh demo fetch`"); mimicwarehouse/tests/conftest.py lines 1-20 (docstring: ladder is "the three-step subset fixture < dev < full ... (`demo` is a data tier for EP-22, never a test tier)"), line 46 `TIERS = ("fixture", "dev", "full")`, lines 63-70 `--tier` `choices=list(TIERS)`, lines 82-86 UsageError "is not a test tier", lines 98-103 marker name outside TIERS -> UsageError "unknown tier"; tests/ep/test_ep12.py lines 810-816 (asserts `PYTEST_TIER=demo` and `--tier demo` are USAGE_ERROR), line 837 (`getoption("--tier") in (None,"fixture","dev","full")`), lines 849-850 (asserts tests/README.md contains "demo" and "fixture < dev < full"); tests/README.md lines 30-38 ("`demo` is a *data* tier ... never a *test* tier"); DESIGN.md section 20 note lines 823-838 (same wording); EP-12 brief header amendment (lines 11-13) and completion note (line 146) both record the decision. Also config.py line 67 `Tier = Literal["fixture","demo","dev","full"]` and line 622 `catalog_path(tier)` accepts "demo", so `catalog_status("demo")`-style skipping is trivially implementable outside the ladder. The contradiction is real and literal: EP-22 as written would either fail test_ep12 (three assertions) or require rewriting conftest, tests/README.md, DESIGN section 20 and the EP-12 completion note. Corrections: (1) The finding's "must be an ad-hoc fix" framing is too strong — roadmap/EP-16-replan-p1.md lines 30-37 already scopes exactly this: "amend the next phase's briefs where this phase changed a fact ... the tier-marker vocabulary (EP-12)". So the venue is the EP-16 re-plan (already planned, S effort), not an out-of-band edit; the owner decision A/B/C should be taken there and written as an "Amended at EP-16 re-plan" header on EP-22 (EP-7 precedent on EP-12). (2) The fix must also amend the EP-22 acceptance line 71 ("`tier("demo")`-marked tests green"), not only item 5, and should add a sentence to conftest/tests/README.md/DESIGN section 20 only if option A or B is chosen (option C needs no code). (3) Note that other briefs' `--tier demo` mentions (EP-37/38 build, EP-57/60/146/157/158/159/162 app/init/screenshot) are CLI data-tier flags, not pytest, and are consistent with the code — the collision is confined to EP-22 item 5 + acceptance. Severity stays medium: nothing is broken today, but EP-22 is on the P2 critical path (blocks EP-28/33/37/158) and a session executing the brief literally would trip test_ep12.

**Owner question (as posed by the reviewer).** Demo tests: (A) orthogonal opt-in marker/flag outside the ladder (recommended, keeps EP-12 tests and the 'maximum tier' semantics); (B) change the ladder to fixture < demo < dev < full and rewrite test_ep12/DESIGN section 20; (C) no demo-specific tests (EP-22 verifies by CLI only).

### ARCH-8 — DEV_BUCKETS 'constant in mimicwarehouse.config' (EP-18/19/21, hard-coded IN (0,1,2,3,4) in EP-21) vs the configurable Settings.dev_buckets that EP-3 shipped

**Severity (verified):** medium · **Verdict:** confirmed-with-corrections (high confidence) · **Kind:** design · **When:** now-should · **Effort:** S · **Retro episode:** EP-167 · **Lens:** data architecture parity/uniformity (lake / catalog / tier / DAG design vs EP-3/9/10/11/12 code)

**Files:** `mimicwarehouse/src/mimicwarehouse/config.py:523,579-584`, `roadmap/EP-18-stage-loader-buckets.md:44-46`, `roadmap/EP-19-stage-dag-runner.md:36-38`, `roadmap/EP-21-stage-catalog-builder.md:27-29`, `mimicwarehouse/DESIGN.md:125-135`, `mimicwarehouse/.env.example:43`

**Evidence.** config.py: `dev_buckets: list[int] = Field(default_factory=lambda: [0, 1, 2, 3, 4])` validated 0..99, overridable via `MWH_DEV_BUCKETS=[0,1,2,3,4]` (.env.example line 43). EP-18 item 3: "`DEV_BUCKETS = (0, 1, 2, 3, 4)` lives once in `mimicwarehouse.config` and is imported by the loader and the catalog builder (EP-21)"; EP-19: "bucket filter (`dev` -> `DEV_BUCKETS`, else all)"; EP-21: "`WHERE subject_bucket IN (0,1,2,3,4)` for `dev`". DESIGN section 4 fixes dev as `subject_id % 100 IN (0,1,2,3,4)`.

**Impact.** Two sources of truth: a session that follows EP-18 literally adds a constant next to the setting; if the setting is honoured, a changed `MWH_DEV_BUCKETS` produces a dev.duckdb whose views, `dev_ready` flags in status.json and dev snapshot ids silently disagree with previous builds and with the fixture 'dev keeps 10 subjects' assumption.

**Proposed fix (reviewer).** Pick one: keep `Settings.dev_buckets` as the single source (recommended - it already exists and is tested), have EP-18/19/21 import `get_settings().dev_buckets`, record `dev_buckets` in `meta.catalog_info` and in the `dev` snapshot entry, and have `open_catalog('dev')` warn when the recorded list differs from the current setting; or delete the setting and freeze the constant (D-18 addendum either way).

**Corrected fix (verifier).** Decide once (owner decision, options: A keep the setting as the single source; B freeze a constant and delete the setting). Recommended A: (i) edit roadmap/EP-18-stage-loader-buckets.md:52-53,65,72, roadmap/EP-19-stage-dag-runner.md:16,46 and roadmap/EP-21-stage-catalog-builder.md:15,29 to say "the dev bucket list is `get_settings().dev_buckets` (EP-3); no separate DEV_BUCKETS constant" and replace `WHERE subject_bucket IN (0,1,2,3,4)` with "IN (<settings.dev_buckets>) via read_parquet_sql(..., buckets=settings.dev_buckets)"; (ii) EP-21: record `dev_buckets` in `meta.catalog_info` and in the dev snapshot/status entry, and have open-catalog/`mwh doctor` warn when the recorded list differs from the current setting; (iii) EP-3 gap: add a test in tests/ep/test_ep03.py that `MWH_DEV_BUCKETS` round-trips through env/.env/toml and that the validator rejects 100 (currently untested), and note in EP-11 brief line 46 / fixtures/spec.py docstring / test_ep11.py:203 that the fixture's "10 subjects" assumption holds only for the default list (or compute it from `get_settings().dev_buckets`); (iv) DESIGN.md:131 wording "IN (0,1,2,3,4)" -> "IN (settings.dev_buckets, default 0-4)" and a D-18 addendum in DECISIONS.md stating the list is a setting recorded per build. If B is chosen: delete `dev_buckets` from config.py:523/579-584, .env.example:43, README.md:84 and add `DEV_BUCKETS: Final = (0,1,2,3,4)` in config.py with a D-18 addendum. Effort S either way (docs + ~20 lines).

**Verifier notes.** Checked: mimicwarehouse/src/mimicwarehouse/config.py:523 (`dev_buckets: list[int] = Field(default_factory=lambda: [0, 1, 2, 3, 4])`) and :579-584 (validator: 0..99, sorted/deduped); mimicwarehouse/.env.example:42-43 (`# MWH_DEV_BUCKETS=[0,1,2,3,4]`, "D-18"); roadmap/EP-3-config-data-root.md:35 (setting is in-scope of EP-3, shipped); roadmap/EP-18-stage-loader-buckets.md:52-53 (exact quote "`DEV_BUCKETS = (0, 1, 2, 3, 4)` lives once in `mimicwarehouse.config` and is imported by the loader and the catalog builder (EP-21)"), also :65 and :72 (`buckets=DEV_BUCKETS`); roadmap/EP-19-stage-dag-runner.md:16 and :46 ("bucket filter (`dev` -> `DEV_BUCKETS`, else all)"); roadmap/EP-21-stage-catalog-builder.md:15 and :29 ("`WHERE subject_bucket IN (0,1,2,3,4)` for `dev`"); mimicwarehouse/DESIGN.md:131 (dev = `subject_id % 100 IN (0,1,2,3,4)`, "cannot drift from full"); mimicwarehouse/DECISIONS.md:138-141 (D-18 text says nothing about buckets) and :491 (planning addendum: "dev = buckets 0-4"). All quoted lines are as the finding states, so the two-sources-of-truth conflict between the pending briefs and the shipped setting is real, and D-18 itself never fixes the bucket list. Corrections: (1) grep of mimicwarehouse/tests/ for `dev_buckets|DEV_BUCKETS` returns nothing - the setting is NOT tested (only the validator exists), and grep of src/ shows it is not consumed by any module (config.py only, no cli/inventory/verify use), so the finding's rationale "it already exists and is tested" is wrong; the setting is dead configuration today. (2) The hard-coding is wider than the three briefs: roadmap/EP-11-fixtures-hosp.md:46, src/mimicwarehouse/fixtures/spec.py:5-6 and tests/ep/test_ep11.py:203 all encode the dev filter as `% 100 < 5` / "keeps 10 subjects" literally, and DESIGN.md:131 states the list as a fact. Impact is real but conditional: nothing breaks unless someone sets MWH_DEV_BUCKETS; the concrete tripping point is a session implementing EP-18 literally (adds a module constant next to the field, then EP-21 hard-codes the IN list) - after which the config field silently lies and `mwh doctor`/`.env.example` advertise a knob that does nothing. Medium severity stands (should fix now, S effort, brief-text edit + one line in DECISIONS).

### CFG-1 — extra="forbid" does not cover shell environment variables: a typo'd MWH_* var is silently ignored, contrary to .env.example/README/tests-README/DESIGN

**Severity (verified):** medium · **Verdict:** confirmed-with-corrections (high confidence) · **Kind:** docs-drift · **When:** now-should · **Effort:** S · **Retro episode:** EP-167 · **Lens:** settings, CLI contract and host diagnostics (config.py / cli.py / doctor.py / .env.example / .streamlit / tests ep02-ep03-ep164)

**Files:** `mimicwarehouse/.env.example:6`, `mimicwarehouse/README.md:84`, `mimicwarehouse/tests/README.md:34`, `mimicwarehouse/DESIGN.md:826`, `mimicwarehouse/src/mimicwarehouse/config.py:493`, `mimicwarehouse/src/mimicwarehouse/doctor.py:385`, `roadmap/EP-3-config-data-root.md:110`

**Evidence.** Observed: `MWH_DATA_ROOTT='Q:\typo' uv run mwh paths` -> `data_root C:\mimicdata  (from default)`, exit 0; `MWH_FOO=secret uv run mwh paths` -> exit 0. pydantic-settings' EnvSettingsSource only reads variables that match fields; extra="forbid" applies to the .env file and mwh.toml (test_ep03::test_unknown_keys_and_bad_toml_are_rejected only exercises .env). .env.example:6-7: "unknown MWH_* keys are rejected (extra="forbid") so a typo fails loudly"; README.md:84-85 "Unknown keys are rejected"; tests/README.md:35-36 "a stray MWH_TEST_TIER would break every Settings() construction"; DESIGN.md:826 same reasoning for PYTEST_TIER. config.py:10-11 is the only precise wording ("a typo in .env fails loudly").

**Impact.** The exact hazard the design says it prevents (mis-set MWH_DATA_ROOT / MWH_DUCKDB_MEMORY_LIMIT via a PowerShell profile or the EP-19 background-job command line) silently falls back to defaults; a full build could target C:\mimicdata with 36GB when the owner believed a different root/limit applied. Docs mislead the next brief author (EP-12 chose PYTEST_TIER on a false premise).

**Proposed fix (reviewer).** (a) Add `config.unknown_env_keys() -> list[str]` (names only: MWH_* keys not in {MWH_<FIELD>}) and have doctor `check_settings` warn on them and the CLI callback print a one-line stderr warning; (b) correct the four doc sentences to "unknown keys in .env / mwh.toml are rejected; unknown MWH_* environment variables are reported by mwh doctor"; (c) add a test that sets an unknown MWH_* env var and asserts the doctor warning.

**Corrected fix (verifier).** (a) config.py: add `def unknown_env_keys() -> list[str]` returning sorted names (never values) of os.environ keys whose upper() starts with "MWH_" and whose suffix.lower() is not in Settings.model_fields; doctor.check_settings -> status "warn" with `unknown_env: [...]` in value when non-empty; cli callback prints one stderr line "warning: unknown MWH_* variables ignored: NAME1, NAME2 (see mwh doctor)". (b) Reword .env.example:6-7, README.md:84-85 to: "unknown keys in .env (any name) and in mwh.toml [settings] are rejected (extra="forbid"); MWH_* environment variables that match no setting are ignored by pydantic-settings and reported by mwh doctor". (c) Reword the PYTEST_TIER rationale in tests/README.md:34-36, DESIGN.md:826-827 (dated note, do not rewrite the 2026-08-18 note — append a correction line), tests/conftest.py:13-14: "not MWH_-prefixed to keep the test knob out of the Settings namespace and the .env.example parity test; note an unknown env var of either name is ignored by Settings, whereas any unknown line in .env — MWH_TEST_TIER or PYTEST_TIER alike — is rejected". Append a one-line correction to roadmap/EP-12-fixtures-icu-markers.md (header amendment + item 5) and roadmap/EP-7-replan-p0.md:213 rather than editing history. (d) test_ep03: new test using the `workspace` fixture: monkeypatch.setenv("MWH_DATA_ROOTT", "Q:/typo"); assert Settings() succeeds with data_root from default, assert config.unknown_env_keys() == ["MWH_DATA_ROOTT"], assert doctor.check_settings(...) status == "warn"; also assert a `.env` line `PYTEST_TIER=dev` raises ValidationError (documents the nuance). Effort S (about 45 min incl. docs).

**Verifier notes.** Reproduced everything the finding claims and found one more nuance that changes the doc wording. Checked: (1) `MWH_DATA_ROOTT='Q:\typo' uv run mwh paths` -> `data_root C:\mimicdata (from default)`, exit 0; `MWH_FOO=secret uv run mwh paths` exit 0; `MWH_DUCKDB_MEMORY_LIMT=1GB uv run mwh doctor` -> settings row `info: sources: defaults only`, exit 0 (doctor.py check_settings, lines 388-400, only reports fields per source; it has no notion of unmatched MWH_* vars). (2) Scratch script (isolated workspace via monkeypatched config.workspace_root, all MWH_* vars removed, pydantic-settings 2.15.0): env `MWH_DATA_ROOTT` and lowercase `mwh_data_roott` -> Settings() OK, data_root from default; `.env` line `MWH_DATA_ROOTT=` -> ValidationError extra_forbidden (mwh_data_roott); `mwh.toml [settings] data_roott` -> ValidationError extra_forbidden; and, new: a `.env` line `PYTEST_TIER=dev` (no MWH_ prefix) ALSO raises extra_forbidden (pytest_tier). So the true rule is: EnvSettingsSource only reads variables matching fields; DotEnvSettingsSource feeds every unknown key (prefixed or not) into the forbid check; TomlConfigSettingsSource likewise. (3) config.py:493-500 model_config (env_prefix MWH_, extra=forbid), settings_customise_sources 534-543, named_sources 478-487 confirm no custom env handling; config.py:10-11 docstring ("a typo in .env fails loudly") is the only accurate wording. (4) tests/ep/test_ep03.py:167-173 test_unknown_keys_and_bad_toml_are_rejected only exercises .env (MWH_DATA_ROOTT) and a toml without [settings]; the `workspace` fixture (79-91) deletes all MWH_* vars, so a new test can monkeypatch.setenv safely. (5) Stale sentences verified verbatim: .env.example:6-7 "unknown MWH_* keys are rejected (extra="forbid") so a typo fails loudly"; README.md:84-85 "Unknown keys are rejected"; tests/README.md:34-36 "deliberately not MWH_-prefixed, because MWH_* is the Settings env prefix and Settings is extra="forbid" (a stray MWH_TEST_TIER would break every Settings() construction ...)"; DESIGN.md:826-827 same; also tests/conftest.py:13-14 ("the option is not MWH_-prefixed on purpose (Settings is extra="forbid" on that prefix)"), roadmap/EP-12-fixtures-icu-markers.md:5-9 and :76, roadmap/EP-7-replan-p0.md:213 carry the same rationale. Corrections to the finding: (a) the PYTEST_TIER rationale is wrong in BOTH directions, not just one: an `MWH_TEST_TIER` environment variable would NOT break Settings(), while a `PYTEST_TIER=dev` line in .env WOULD (only a `.env` line of any name trips forbid; the EP-12 header's "a stray MWH_TEST_TIER= line in .env would make every Settings() construction raise" is literally true but the prefix is irrelevant). The PYTEST_TIER choice remains sensible (namespace hygiene + the .env.example parity test at test_ep03:580-589), so the fix is to reword the reason, not the choice; add tests/conftest.py:13-14 and the two roadmap briefs to the file list (briefs: append a one-line correction, do not rewrite). (b) Corrected doc sentence: "Unknown keys in `.env` (any name) and under `[settings]` in `mwh.toml` are rejected (extra="forbid"); `MWH_*` environment variables that match no setting are ignored by pydantic-settings — `mwh doctor` warns about them" (once (c) lands). (c) The `unknown_env_keys()` helper is a 3-line comprehension over os.environ (`k.upper().startswith("MWH_") and k[4:].lower() not in Settings.model_fields`; env matching is case-insensitive by default, my lowercase probe confirms); wire it into doctor.check_settings as `warn` and a stderr line in the cli callback; test in test_ep03 with the workspace fixture + monkeypatch.setenv. Effort S holds. (d) Severity: medium is fair — provenance is visible (`mwh paths` prints "(from default)", doctor lists sources) but only to someone who looks, and the docs affirmatively promise the opposite; the EP-19 background full-build scenario (typo'd MWH_DUCKDB_MEMORY_LIMIT/MWH_DATA_ROOT in a job command line silently defaulting to 36GB / C:\mimicdata) is real. Owner question stands; "warn" recommendation is right: .pre-commit-config.yaml:16 runs `uv run ... mwh guard --staged` and cli.py:85-114 builds Settings in the callback for every command, so a refusing policy would make a stray MWH_* var block every commit.

**Owner question (as posed by the reviewer).** Should an unknown MWH_* environment variable make every mwh command refuse (exit 2, strict namespace) or only warn (doctor + one stderr line)? Options: refuse / warn / ignore-and-document. Recommendation: warn now (refusing would break `mwh guard` in pre-commit on any stray variable), reconsider at EP-16.

### CFG-5 — `mwh <validated-command> --help` exits 2 on an unsafe root or a broken .env; DIAGNOSTIC_COMMANDS allow-list has inverted (6 of 7 commands are diagnostic)

**Severity (verified):** medium · **Verdict:** confirmed-with-corrections (high confidence) · **Kind:** design · **When:** now-should · **Effort:** S · **Retro episode:** EP-167 · **Lens:** settings, CLI contract and host diagnostics (config.py / cli.py / doctor.py / .env.example / .streamlit / tests ep02-ep03-ep164)

**Files:** `mimicwarehouse/src/mimicwarehouse/cli.py:51`, `mimicwarehouse/src/mimicwarehouse/cli.py:109`, `mimicwarehouse/README.md:82`

**Evidence.** Observed: `uv run mwh --data-root 'Q:\nowhere' inventory --help` -> "mwh: Q:\nowhere: volume Q:\ is DRIVE_NO_ROOT_DIR, not a local fixed disk ... (D-29, GOVERNANCE §2)", exit 2 (same with MWH_DATA_ROOT set); `mwh --help` works because the group callback never runs. click runs the group callback before `cmd.make_context` parses the sub-command's eager --help. cli.py:51-53 DIAGNOSTIC_COMMANDS = {doctor, paths, guard, verify, schema, fixtures} — only `inventory` receives validated settings; README.md:82-83 still says "`doctor` and `paths` run anyway".

**Impact.** Help is unavailable exactly when the user is debugging a settings problem; a `.env` typo (ValidationError) blocks `mwh build --help` in P2. The allow-list grows with every brief and each brief re-litigates membership (EP-9/EP-11 amendments).

**Proposed fix (reviewer).** Short term: in `main`, skip settings loading when any of `ctx.help_option_names` is in `ctx.args + ctx.protected_args` (or set `ctx.resilient_parsing`) and add a test `mwh inventory --help` with an unsafe root -> exit 0. Structural (re-plan): make `CliState.settings` a lazy property (unchecked instance loaded in the callback; `state.checked_settings` runs require_safe on first access) so commands validate at the moment they touch the data root and DIAGNOSTIC_COMMANDS disappears; update README.md:82 either way.

**Corrected fix (verifier).** Short term (S): the finding's proposed check of `ctx.args + ctx.protected_args` in the callback does NOT work — typer's `Group.invoke` (mimicwarehouse/.venv/Lib/site-packages/typer/core.py:1112-1114) copies the remaining args into a local and sets `ctx.args = []; ctx._protected_args = []` BEFORE `super().invoke(ctx)` runs the `main` callback (line 1122), and `ctx.resilient_parsing` is only true during shell completion. Working options: (a) in `main`, skip settings loading when `any(h in sys.argv[1:] for h in ctx.help_option_names)` (cheap; test via subprocess or by monkeypatching `sys.argv`, since `CliRunner.invoke(app, args)` does not touch `sys.argv`); or (b) keep loading `load_settings(checked=False)` in the callback for every command and, on `ConfigError`, store the pending error in `CliState` and let a `state.settings` property re-raise as `typer.Exit(2)` on first access — this handles `--help`, `--version`, and `no_args_is_help` on sub-typers (`mwh --data-root Q:\nowhere inventory` also exits 2 today) without an argv heuristic. Either way add tests: `mwh inventory --help` and `mwh inventory scan --help` with an unsafe root -> exit 0 and usage printed. Structural (re-plan, effort M not S): lazy `CliState.settings` (validated on first access via `config.get_settings()`, `state.unchecked_settings` for doctor/paths) retires `DIAGNOSTIC_COMMANDS`; note this requires rewriting `tests/ep/test_ep03.py:457-476` (`test_non_diagnostic_commands_refuse_unsafe_root_in_the_callback`, whose docstring pins "the callback exits 2 before running") and touching DESIGN.md:488/575/699 + DECISIONS.md:241 which enumerate the set. Also correct the doc citation: the stale sentence "`doctor` and `paths` run anyway and *report* it" is at mimicwarehouse/README.md:87 (not :82); DECISIONS.md:241 says the same ("only `doctor` and `paths` run against an unsafe root") — both should read "the `DIAGNOSTIC_COMMANDS` (`doctor paths guard verify schema fixtures`)". Additional evidence to fold in: the exemption covers only location refusals (`ConfigError`), not `ValidationError` — reproduced `MWH_DUCKDB_THREADS=abc uv run mwh doctor` -> exit 2, `mwh paths` -> exit 2, `mwh doctor --help` -> exit 2; i.e. a `.env` typo (extra="forbid", config.py:499) blocks the very commands meant to diagnose settings, so the "doctor reports settings problems" claim (README.md:89-90 `settings` check) is only true for location problems. Whether that is intended ("fails loudly", config.py:11) is an owner question; a lazy design would let doctor still print its `settings` row with the pydantic message.

**Verifier notes.** Checked: mimicwarehouse/src/mimicwarehouse/cli.py:44-53 (DIAGNOSTIC_COMMANDS = {doctor, paths, guard, verify, schema, fixtures}; 7 commands attached at lines 121-127, so 6/7 is accurate) and 107-117 (callback loads settings; ConfigError|ValidationError -> exit 2). Reproduced with uv on the workspace: `mwh --data-root 'Q:\nowhere' inventory --help` -> D-29 refusal, exit 2; `... inventory scan --help` -> exit 2; `... inventory` (no args) -> exit 2; `MWH_DATA_ROOT='Q:\nowhere' mwh inventory --help` -> exit 2; `mwh --data-root 'Q:\nowhere' doctor --help` -> help printed (the traceback in my run was only a BrokenPipe from `| head`, not a bug). Also reproduced the ValidationError path with `MWH_DUCKDB_THREADS=abc`: `mwh inventory --help`, `mwh doctor --help`, `mwh doctor`, `mwh paths` all exit 2 -> the diagnostic exemption does not cover typos, stronger than the finding states. Verified click ordering in vendored typer/core.py:1094-1125: `ctx.args`/`ctx._protected_args` are emptied before the group callback runs, so the finding's proposed `ctx.args + ctx.protected_args` guard would never fire; `resilient_parsing` (typer/_click/core.py:149,231) is completion-only. Doc drift confirmed at mimicwarehouse/README.md:87 ("`doctor` and `paths` run anyway and *report* it"; finding cited :82) and DECISIONS.md:241 (same wording, though that is a dated EP-3 note and later notes at DESIGN.md:452/478/488/575/699 track the growth). tests/ep/test_ep03.py:457-476 pins callback-time refusal, tests/ep/test_ep164.py:391-392 only tests `doctor --help` (a diagnostic command), tests/ep/test_ep02.py:131 only tests group `--help` — no test covers sub-command --help under an unsafe root, so the regression is unguarded. Roadmap briefs EP-4/6/9/11 each amended the list (EP-9 lines 6-8, EP-11 line 19/135), confirming the per-brief re-litigation claim. Verdict: real, correctly medium (usability-only, no data risk, but hits exactly during settings debugging and the allow-list keeps growing into build/catalog/sql); fix text needed correction because the suggested mechanism does not work and the doc line number was off.

**Owner question (as posed by the reviewer).** Keep the DIAGNOSTIC_COMMANDS allow-list (explicit, but 6/7 commands are now on it) or switch to lazy validation at first data-root access (no list, but each data-touching command must call `state.checked_settings`)? Recommendation: fix --help now, decide the structural change at EP-16 before EP-17/19/21 add `build`, `catalog`, `sql`.

### CMP-1 — MCP connectors are a second egress path (Gmail, Calendar, Hugging Face authenticated as the owner, PubMed/bioRxiv/ICD-10, Context7) that GOVERNANCE §4, CLAUDE.md and .claude/settings.json never mention; the enabled set lives only in machine-local auto-memory and is already stale

**Severity (verified):** medium · **Verdict:** confirmed-with-corrections (high confidence) · **Kind:** governance · **When:** owner-decision · **Effort:** S · **Retro episode:** EP-165 · **Lens:** critic

**Files:** `mimicwarehouse/GOVERNANCE.md:67-87`, `.claude/settings.json:1-71`, `CLAUDE.md:16-31`, `mimicwarehouse/DECISIONS.md:402-428`

**Evidence.** GOVERNANCE §4 line 69-70: "Claude Code transmits tool results (file reads, shell output, search hits) to Anthropic. Therefore…" — the only egress it reasons about is Anthropic; §4(3) lists the enforcement layers as "this document → CLAUDE.md → .claude/settings.json deny rules … → the safe module". `.claude/settings.json` contains 67 deny rules, none of the form `mcp__<server>__<tool>`; no allow/deny for WebFetch/WebSearch either. The persistent memory preamble records "connectors enabled 2026-08-18: PubMed, bioRxiv, ICD-10 Codes (Google Drive deliberately off)", but this session's tool roster also exposes Gmail (send_message, forward, create_draft), Google Calendar (create/update_event), Hugging Face (`hf_fs`, `hf_whoami` → authenticated user 'willtfarrington'), Context7, and six more servers pending OAuth. None of this is written in the repository.

**Impact.** A session that (mis)uses `mcp__claude_ai_Gmail__send_message` or `hf_fs` with a fixture/aggregate/notes-derived payload sends project content to a third-party service outside the PhysioNet-compliant boundary; the 'Google Drive deliberately off' decision (a real D-29-class control: Drive is the G: sync client the data root must never touch) evaporates when memory is lost or a collaborator machine is set up; EP-13/EP-15 (web research, PMID lookups) will use WebFetch/PubMed with no written rule on what may be sent in a query.

**Proposed fix (reviewer).** Owner decides the connector policy, then: (a) add a paragraph to GOVERNANCE §4 naming MCP connectors/WebFetch as egress paths — allowed only for public-reference lookups (PubMed, bioRxiv, ICD-10, Context7, docs), never with fixture rows, aggregates, ids or note text in the request; (b) mirror one line into CLAUDE.md §2; (c) deny write/send-capable tools in `.claude/settings.json` (`mcp__claude_ai_Gmail__send_message`, `…__forward`, `…__create_draft`, `mcp__claude_ai_Google_Calendar__create_event`/`update_event`, `mcp__claude_ai_Hugging_Face__hf_fs`) or disable those connectors for this project; (d) record the enabled/disabled connector list and the 'Google Drive off' decision as a D-39 addendum so it is not memory-only.

**Corrected fix (verifier).** Owner decides connector policy (recommend option 1), then: (a) add a short paragraph to GOVERNANCE §4 (after item 3, ~line 78) naming claude.ai MCP connectors, WebFetch and WebSearch as additional egress paths beyond Anthropic: allowed only for public-reference lookups (PubMed, bioRxiv, ICD-10 Codes, Context7, GitHub/DOI URLs — EP-13/EP-15 need WebFetch, so do NOT deny it) and never with fixture rows, aggregates, ids, run records or note text in a request body/query; generalise the per-brief wording that already exists in roadmap/EP-13-resources-repos.md:30 ("web research is allowed, no data is touched") and roadmap/EP-15-resources-reading-datasets.md:24 ("No data, no ids, no row-level examples") into repo policy; state that write/send-capable connector tools are out of scope for sessions because they bypass the D-40 disclosure gate (`mwh disclose check` only guards git). (b) Mirror one bullet into CLAUDE.md §2 and one line into CLAUDE.md §6 ("Ask before … sending anything via a connector"). (c) Add deny rules to .claude/settings.json for the write/send tools that exist in the current roster: `mcp__claude_ai_Gmail__send_message`, `__reply`, `__forward`, `__create_draft`, `__update_draft`, `mcp__claude_ai_Google_Calendar__create_event`, `__update_event`, `__delete_event`, `__respond_to_event`, `mcp__claude_ai_Hugging_Face__hf_fs`, `__dynamic_space` (or simply disable Gmail/Calendar/Hugging Face at claude.ai level, and say so in the repo); update the `_readme` string and the D-39 sentence in GOVERNANCE §4(3) that enumerates what settings.json denies. (d) Record as a D-39 addendum in DECISIONS.md: enabled connectors (PubMed, bioRxiv, ICD-10 Codes, Context7), the disabled/denied set, and "Google Drive connector deliberately not enabled" — but state the reason accurately: the claude.ai Drive connector is a cloud-content integration, not the G: GoogleDriveFS sync client that D-29/GOVERNANCE §2 forbid for the data root; the rationale is "no project content in Google-hosted storage / no second document store", not D-29 itself. (e) Add a one-line Risk to roadmap/README.md (connector roster is claude.ai-account state, invisible to git; re-check at each re-plan EP-16/…). Effort S (~30 min); parked GOV-1 (PreToolUse hook) remains the stronger long-term control.

**Verifier notes.** Checked: mimicwarehouse/GOVERNANCE.md lines 67-87 (§4 reasons only about "Claude Code transmits tool results … to Anthropic"; §4(3) enforcement chain = this doc → CLAUDE.md → settings.json deny rules on source material/data/duckdb → safe module; nothing on MCP/WebFetch); .claude/settings.json (67 deny rules, all Read/Grep/Glob/Bash/PowerShell patterns for data files; zero `mcp__*`, zero WebFetch/WebSearch entries; no settings.local.json in repo or ~/.claude); CLAUDE.md §2 and §6 (only MWH_ALLOW_REMOTE text-module remote calls are covered); DECISIONS.md D-31/D-32/D-39 + addendum (lines 271-282, 397-428) — no connector mention. `grep -rni "mcp|connector|WebFetch|WebSearch|PubMed|bioRxiv|Hugging"` across GOVERNANCE, CLAUDE.md, DECISIONS, DESIGN, roadmap/*.md, READMEs, docs returns only EP-11 completion note ("the ICD-10 connector was not needed") and EP-132's Hugging Face card format — so the enabled-connector list truly lives only in ~/.claude/projects/…/memory/project-mimicwarehouse-status.md:15 (PubMed, bioRxiv, ICD-10; Drive off, citing GOVERNANCE §2/D-29). The staleness claim holds: this session's tool roster exposes Gmail (send_message/reply/forward/create_draft…), Google Calendar (create/update/delete_event), Hugging Face (hf_fs, dynamic_space; server instructions say authenticated user 'willtfarrington') and Context7, none of which the memory note records, plus six servers pending OAuth. Corrections: (1) impact overstates "no written rule" for EP-13/15 — both briefs already say web research allowed / no data, no ids, no row-level examples (EP-13:30, EP-15:22-24) and GOVERNANCE §9:125-127 forbids note text in "any LLM prompt (local or remote)"; the gap is a general policy + settings-level control, not total silence. (2) The realistic license exposure is bounded because sessions only ever hold k-suppressed aggregates/schemas/code (D-31); the concrete hazard is a write-capable third-party path (hf_fs, Gmail send) that bypasses the D-40 `mwh disclose check` gate and the pre-commit guard, in autonomous sessions — a governance-completeness gap, which justifies medium rather than high. (3) "Google Drive connector = the G: sync client the data root must never touch" conflates the claude.ai Drive content connector with the GoogleDriveFS volume that D-29 refuses; the decision is worth recording but the stated rationale should be corrected. (4) The fix must keep WebFetch/WebSearch allowed (EP-13/15 acceptance criteria require every URL fetched in-session). Severity medium retained; owner decision required on the connector set.

**Owner question (as posed by the reviewer).** Which connectors stay enabled for mimicwarehouse sessions? Options: (1) keep everything, add a written 'reference lookups only' rule + deny the send/write tools in settings.json (recommended — cheap, auditable, keeps EP-13/15 research working); (2) disable all connectors except PubMed/bioRxiv/ICD-10/Context7 at the claude.ai level (strongest, but per-machine and invisible to git); (3) leave as is (not recommended: undocumented egress).

### CMP-2 — No decided vehicle for landing the consolidation: EP-165 (the pre-allocated 'toolchain remediation P2' slot) vs hotfix commits vs folding into EP-16 — commit-tag, verify and 'one brief per session' conventions all assume an EP number, and CLAUDE.md §6 gates most GOV-* fixes behind 'ask before'

**Severity (verified):** medium · **Verdict:** confirmed-with-corrections (high confidence) · **Kind:** process · **When:** owner-decision · **Effort:** S · **Retro episode:** EP-166 · **Lens:** critic

**Files:** `roadmap/EP-16-replan-p1.md:57-61`, `roadmap/EP-7-replan-p0.md:129-131`, `CLAUDE.md:11-12`, `CLAUDE.md:58-60`, `CLAUDE.md:74-79`, `roadmap/README.md:7-9`, `mimicwarehouse/src/mimicwarehouse/verify.py:383-400`

**Evidence.** EP-16 item 3: "decide whether the optional per-phase 'toolchain remediation' S slot is needed for P2 (write it as `roadmap/EP-165-toolchain-remediation-p2.md` — the next free number, EP-164 precedent; `EP-16a`-style names do not parse in `roadmap_check`/`mwh verify` — only if the owner agrees, insert its row before EP-17…)". CLAUDE.md §1: "One brief per session; do not start the next one"; §4.5: "Commit in two steps: `feat(mimicwarehouse): <what> (EP-<n>)` … Only commit when the owner asks or the brief's commit recipe says so"; §6: "Ask before … changing `.gitignore`, `.gitattributes`, `.claude/settings.json`, `GOVERNANCE.md`". `roadmap_check --strict` warns when a ☑-cell hash's subject lacks `(EP-n)` (verify.py:396-400). The merged list already carries ~150 findings, ~40 of them touching .gitignore/.gitattributes/settings.json/GOVERNANCE (GOV-1..GOV-17, ENV-2, ENV-5, DOC-6) — every one of them is behind the §6 gate; EP-0 set the precedent that additive tightening of .gitignore was done without asking (`*.duckdb.new/.tmp`, EP-0 note).

**Impact.** Without a decision, the next session either (a) writes fixes under a fake or reused EP tag (breaks the D-8/roadmap_check provenance), (b) commits untagged hotfixes that no ☑ cell cites and no `mwh verify` covers, or (c) stalls on §6 prompts fix by fix. Ordering also matters: EP-13/14/15 sessions read CLAUDE.md/README/DESIGN cold — landing the doc fixes (DOC-1..DOC-4, ENV-3) before them saves three sessions from the misreads DOC-18 describes; landing code fixes after EP-15 but before EP-16 lets EP-16's retro record them.

**Proposed fix (reviewer).** Owner picks the vehicle and ordering; then (i) allocate `EP-165-toolchain-remediation-p2.md` (or a `-consolidation` slug; must be numeric) with the accepted findings as its In-scope, insert its row (before EP-13 or before EP-17 per the ordering choice), commit `docs(roadmap): add EP-165 — …`; (ii) state in CLAUDE.md §6 that additive tightening of .gitignore/.gitattributes/settings.json (new deny/ignore rules) is allowed with a completion-note line, loosening still requires the owner; (iii) update the auto-memory 'next = EP-13' pointer once decided.

**Corrected fix (verifier).** Owner decision still required (vehicle + ordering), but the mechanics are already documented and need no new convention: (i) allocate `roadmap/EP-165-toolchain-remediation-p2.md` (or `EP-165-consolidation-p2.md`; numeric prefix mandatory — `_BRIEF_FILE = ^EP-(\d+)-.*\.md$`, verify.py:70) exactly as EP-164 was allocated at EP-7 (`docs(roadmap): add EP-165 — …`, then `feat(...) (EP-165)` / `docs(roadmap): record EP-165 commit hash`); if it runs before EP-13, insert its row after EP-12 in the P1 table with Depends-on EP-12 and amend EP-16 item 3 (`roadmap/EP-16-replan-p1.md:57-61`), which currently says "insert its row before EP-17" and "only if the owner agrees", so it does not point at an already-consumed slot. (ii) Instead of (or in addition to) editing CLAUDE.md §6, put the EP-0 pre-authorisation sentence into the EP-165 brief: EP-0's brief (`roadmap/EP-0-baseline.md:33-35`) reads "this brief is the owner's approval for additive data patterns, CLAUDE.md §6 … Never loosen a rule" — that is the established mechanism for clearing the §6 gate for a whole brief; a standing CLAUDE.md §6 amendment ("additive tightening of .gitignore/.gitattributes/.claude/settings.json allowed with a completion-note line; loosening and GOVERNANCE.md still ask") is optional and itself needs an owner OK. (iii) Multi-session execution is already supported: ☑ cells accept several hashes (EP-0 cell has two; verify.py:520-535 only warns per hash whose subject lacks `(EP-165)`), and CLAUDE.md §4.6 provides the `EP-<n>-completion-handoff.md` mechanism (excluded from parity by `_EXCLUDED_BRIEF_SUFFIXES`, verify.py:71). (iv) Update the auto-memory 'next = EP-13' pointer. Correct the citation: the hash-subject warning lives at verify.py:520-535, not 383-400 (those are `_run_git`/`commit_exists`/`commit_subject` helpers), and it is emitted regardless of `--strict`; `--strict` only makes warnings exit 1 (verify.py:22, 340-344).

**Verifier notes.** Checked: roadmap/EP-16-replan-p1.md:55-61 (item 3 quotes match exactly, incl. "only if the owner agrees, insert its row before EP-17"); CLAUDE.md:8-14, 55-62, 72-80 (one-brief-per-session, two-step tagged commits, §6 ask-before list — all quoted correctly); roadmap/README.md:7-9, 37, 52, 60, 71 (EP-164 allocation precedent, per-phase toolchain slot); verify.py:70-72 (numeric brief-file regex, handoff/report suffix exclusion), 340-344 (strict semantics), 405-535 (parity, header, hash checks — warning on subject lacking `(EP-n)`); roadmap/EP-7-replan-p0.md:214-224 (EP-165 named as next free number, "EP-16a-style names do not parse"); roadmap/EP-0-baseline.md:28-36; git log (no untagged/hotfix commits exist since cd67743 — every commit since carries an `(EP-n)` tag or is a `docs(roadmap): add/record` commit, so there is no precedent for out-of-band fixes; grep for hotfix/chore/out-of-band conventions in CLAUDE.md, README, DESIGN, DECISIONS: none). Verdict: the structural claim holds — every commit/verify/☑ convention assumes an EP number, ~all GOV-* items sit behind §6, and no document decides whether/when a consolidation EP is created, so the owner does have to choose vehicle and ordering. Two corrections: (1) the EP-0 evidence is wrong in spirit — EP-0 did not tighten .gitignore "without asking"; its brief explicitly carried the owner's §6 pre-approval, which is in fact the cleanest way to clear the gate for EP-165 without amending CLAUDE.md; (2) verify.py line numbers and the strict/non-strict distinction. Downgraded from high to medium: it is a required owner decision, not a defect — the recipe (EP-164 precedent, next free number EP-165, `docs(roadmap): add EP-165`) is already written down in EP-7 and EP-16, so nothing breaks or regresses; the only open items are timing (before EP-13 vs at EP-16) and whether §6 pre-approval lives in the brief or in CLAUDE.md. The "~150 findings / ~40 GOV-*" counts come from the merged review list and could not be verified from the repository.

**Owner question (as posed by the reviewer).** How should the consolidation land? Options: (1) one EP-165 brief split into 2–3 sessions (docs+CLAUDE.md first, then code, then settings.json/GOVERNANCE) executed BEFORE EP-13 — recommended: cold EP-13/14/15 sessions get correct docs, EP-16 retro records it, provenance stays clean; (2) EP-165 after EP-15, folded into EP-16's amend-P2 pass — fewer sessions but EP-13..15 run on stale CLAUDE.md; (3) untagged `chore(...)` hotfix commits now — fastest, but invisible to roadmap_check/mwh verify. Sub-decision: may sessions tighten .gitignore/.gitattributes/settings.json without asking (recommend yes, additive only, note it)?

### DOC-18 — What a cold EP-13 session will misread (readability assessment)

**Severity (verified):** medium · **Verdict:** confirmed-with-corrections (high confidence) · **Kind:** process · **When:** now-must · **Effort:** S · **Retro episode:** EP-166 · **Lens:** documentation drift and internal contradictions across all prose (README/CLAUDE/NOTICE/DESIGN/DECISIONS/GOVERNANCE/tests+fixtures+brand READMEs/raw-inventory/source-material README/roadmap README+final-roadmap/completion notes)

**Evidence.** Reading order per CLAUDE.md §1: GOVERNANCE (accurate except DOC-6), the EP-13 brief (amendment (4) stale, DOC-15), roadmap README §How to use (fine; headline counts stale DOC-7), DESIGN (§15 '(all planned)' DOC-4), DECISIONS (accurate; D-42 present but not surfaced in CLAUDE.md, DOC-1). Root/workspace READMEs (DOC-2/3) say no code exists. Verified this session: `uv` absent from both tool PATHs, bare `python` = 3.14.

**Impact.** Concrete misreads in likely order: (1) first `uv run` fails → 'toolchain missing' detour; (2) heredoc/`python -` attempts (harness suggests them) → killed/hung process; (3) 'no data code yet' → session assumes concepts/schema/inventory/fixtures are future and may not cite the D-19 pin or contract in repos.md; (4) `docs/resources/` exists → skips creating README.md → EP-16 item 2 dangles; (5) commits from the Bash tool fail the pre-commit hook for lack of `uv` on PATH.

**Proposed fix (reviewer).** Fix DOC-1, DOC-2, DOC-3, DOC-15 before handing out EP-13 (≈ 1 h total); the rest at EP-16. Add to CLAUDE.md §1 a fourth read-first item: 'mimicwarehouse/README.md § State of the workspace — what exists as of the last re-plan'.

**Corrected fix (verifier).** Keep the aggregate fix (land DOC-1/DOC-2/DOC-3/DOC-15 before handing out EP-13) but correct three details. (a) The proposed CLAUDE.md §1 pointer to "mimicwarehouse/README.md § State of the workspace" targets a section that does not exist (mimicwarehouse/README.md has only the intro paragraph, Install, Contributing, layout tree) — either create that section as part of DOC-3 or, cheaper, extend CLAUDE.md §1 item 2 to "roadmap/README.md § How to use *and the phase tables — ☑ hash = shipped*", which is already the accurate source of truth (roadmap/README.md:61-66 show EP-8..EP-12 ☑ with hashes, EP-13 ☐). (b) CLAUDE.md §3 must carry the two environment facts verbatim, not just a pointer: `export PATH="$LOCALAPPDATA/Microsoft/WinGet/Links:$PATH"` (needed for `uv` and for git commit, because .pre-commit-config.yaml local hooks are `language: system` with `entry: uv run --project mimicwarehouse …`) and the D-42 rule "Write/Edit only, no bash heredocs, no `python -` stdin". Verified: `which uv` → not found on the Bash tool PATH; bare `python` = 3.14.7; CLAUDE.md contains no occurrence of D-42/heredoc/PATH. Today these facts live only in the user-scoped auto-memory (~/.claude/projects/…/memory/MEMORY.md), which is not in the repo. (c) Drop impact (4): EP-13 brief In scope item 4 (roadmap/EP-13-resources-repos.md:61 "create mimicwarehouse/docs/resources/README.md if absent") and acceptance line 81 already force the README, so the stale amendment (4) at line 15 ("docs/resources/ does not exist yet") is low-severity drift (docs/resources/ now holds raw-inventory.md, no README.md), not an EP-16 item-2 breakage. Also soften impact (3): the brief itself cites D-19 "vendored by EP-8" and the phase table shows ☑, so a session is unlikely to conclude nothing shipped; the sharper defect is that mimicwarehouse/README.md is self-contradictory (lines 12-14 "no data code yet — that arrives with … EP-8" vs line 54 "tier(...) marker (selection from EP-12)"). Effort unchanged: S (~1 h across DOC-1/2/3/15 plus two CLAUDE.md lines). Timing: now-should (medium) rather than now-must, since on the owner's host the auto-memory already covers (1)(2)(5); it becomes must-fix for any fresh clone / memory reset / different Claude client.

CLAUDE.md: grep for D-42/heredoc/stdin/PATH → no hits; §3 (lines 33-45) has no PATH prefix or heredoc ban — confirmed. mimicwarehouse/DESIGN.md:353 "## 15. Package / module map (all planned)" — confirmed stale. roadmap/EP-13-resources-repos.md:15-16 amendment (4) says docs/resources/ does not exist; `ls mimicwarehouse/docs/resources` → raw-inventory.md present, no README.md — amendment stale, confirmed; BUT lines 61 and 81 of the same brief require creating README.md if absent, so the "EP-16 item 2 dangles" impact is refuted. roadmap/EP-16-replan-p1.md:51-53 item 2 references docs/resources/README.md — exists as claimed. Environment: `which uv` on Bash tool → not found (PATH lists C:/Python314 etc., no WinGet/Links); `python --version` → 3.14.7; .pre-commit-config.yaml local hooks use `language: system` + `entry: uv run --project mimicwarehouse --group dev …`, so a Bash-tool commit without the PATH export fails the hook — impacts (1),(2),(5) technically confirmed. Repo-wide grep for "WinGet/Links|not on PATH|PATH=" → no repo doc records the uv-PATH fact (only EP-1 completion note lines 170-171 saying no PATH change was needed); D-42 heredoc rule is recorded in DECISIONS.md:505ff, DESIGN.md:62, roadmap/README.md Risk 12 but not in CLAUDE.md. Auto-memory MEMORY.md (loaded in this session's context) does contain both facts, which mitigates on this host only. roadmap/README.md § How to use (line 7ff) and phase table lines 61-66 are accurate about what shipped, mitigating impact (3). Net: finding's evidence and core fix hold; impact (4) refuted, impact (3) overstated, proposed CLAUDE.md pointer targets a non-existent section, and severity/timing best expressed as medium / now-should with the repo-level gap called out.
