### DOC-6 — GOVERNANCE §2 endpoint-security sentence is overtaken by D-38 addenda / D-42 (Defender-only, 'owner's discretion', 'the repository is not')

**Severity (verified):** medium · **Verdict:** confirmed-with-corrections (high confidence) · **Kind:** governance · **When:** owner-decision · **Effort:** S · **Retro episode:** EP-165 · **Lens:** documentation drift and internal contradictions across all prose (README/CLAUDE/NOTICE/DESIGN/DECISIONS/GOVERNANCE/tests+fixtures+brand READMEs/raw-inventory/source-material README/roadmap README+final-roadmap/completion notes)

**Files:** `mimicwarehouse/GOVERNANCE.md:43`, `mimicwarehouse/GOVERNANCE.md:80`, `mimicwarehouse/DECISIONS.md:498`, `mimicwarehouse/DECISIONS.md:257`

**Evidence.** GOVERNANCE.md:43-45: "The data root is excluded from Windows Defender real-time scanning at the owner's discretion (D-38); the repository is not." D-42 (DECISIONS.md:498-520): two products both on; Malwarebytes exclusions for `C:\mimicdata`, `source material\` (inside the repo tree) and the workspace `.venv` (inside the repo tree); "the data-location exclusions are also a GOVERNANCE §2 disclosure control (a scanner that uploads detected objects must never have reason to look at MIMIC files)". D-29 EP-7 addendum (:257): "keeping the local copy 'secured' (GOVERNANCE §1) now also means the data root and `source material\` are excluded from **both** real-time products". GOVERNANCE §4 item 3 (:80-83) lists the enforcement layers without `mwh guard` (EP-4) or the EP-0 finding that `Read(**/*.csv)` is project-relative (D-39 addendum).

**Impact.** GOVERNANCE is declared to override everything, yet it now understates a control the decisions log calls mandatory ("disclosure control", not "discretion") and mis-scopes it (Defender only; repository 'not' excluded). A collaborator or a future re-plan reading GOVERNANCE alone would not add Malwarebytes exclusions on a new machine.

**Proposed fix (reviewer).** Owner-approved edit (CLAUDE.md §6 requires asking): replace GOVERNANCE.md:43-45 with "BitLocker on C: is required ... Both real-time endpoint products (Windows Defender and Malwarebytes) exclude the data root and `source material\`; this is a disclosure control (D-38 addenda, D-42), not a performance choice — a scanner that uploads detected objects must never have reason to open MIMIC files. Toolchain paths (Git, uv, uv's CPython, the workspace `.venv`, pre-commit venvs) are excluded from Malwarebytes so its ransomware heuristic does not kill long writers. Neither exclusion list is readable non-elevated; `mwh doctor antivirus` names the products." In §4 item 3 add `mwh guard` (EP-4) as the git-side layer and note the project-relative limit of `Read(**/*.csv)`.

**Corrected fix (verifier).** Owner-approved edit of mimicwarehouse/GOVERNANCE.md (CLAUDE.md §6 requires asking; precedent: commit 795a044 already amended GOVERNANCE §1 at the EP-0 follow-up, so the file is not frozen). (1) Replace lines 43-45 with a dated amendment: "BitLocker on C: is required (owner verified 2026-08-16; `mwh doctor` re-checks and records it per run). Endpoint security is two real-time products (Windows Defender and Malwarebytes 5.1 Premium, D-38 addenda / D-42). Defender excludes `C:\mimicdata`; Malwarebytes excludes `C:\mimicdata`, `source material\` and the toolchain (Git, uv, uv's CPython, the workspace `.venv`, pre-commit hook venvs). The two data-location exclusions are a disclosure control, part of keeping the local copy 'secured' (§1, D-29 addendum): a scanner that uploads detected objects must never have reason to open MIMIC files; both products' telemetry/sample submission are off. The rest of the repository is not excluded. Neither exclusion list is readable non-elevated; `mwh doctor` `antivirus` names the products and the paths but takes the lists on the owner's word. (amended 2026-08-18, D-42)". (2) In §4 item 3 do NOT add `mwh guard` — it is already the git-side layer in §3 line 58 and does not stop a session reading data; instead append one sentence to §4 item 3: "`Read(**/*.csv)`-style rules are project-relative (EP-0, D-38 addendum, Risk 8): a `*.csv` outside the repo and outside `C:\mimicdata` (e.g. the session scratchpad) is not covered, which is why `mwh guard` and `safe_query` remain necessary layers." (3) Optionally cross-link Risk 12 from §2. Effort S (15 min). Also correct the finding's impact framing: a "collaborator on a new machine" is barred by the DUA (§1: no sharing access), so the realistic beneficiaries are the owner rebuilding on a second machine (§11 recovery) and future sessions/re-plans that read GOVERNANCE as the top of the precedence chain.

**Verifier notes.** Checked GOVERNANCE.md lines 43-45 (exact text: "The data root is excluded from Windows Defender real-time scanning at the owner's discretion (D-38); the repository is not."), §3 line 58 (mwh guard already listed), §4 item 3 lines 80-83; DECISIONS.md D-29 EP-7 addendum (lines 263-266: "secured" now means both products exclude data root and source material), D-38 addenda (lines 334-397: Malwarebytes exclusions for `source material\` and `mimicwarehouse\.venv` — both inside the repo tree; "Governance reading (GOVERNANCE §2/§4): ... a **disclosure control**"), D-42 (lines 505-524: "also a GOVERNANCE §2 disclosure control"); roadmap/README.md Risk 8 (Read(**/*.csv) project-relative) and Risk 12; DESIGN.md lines 55-63; .claude/settings.json (deny rules unchanged, `**/*.csv` patterns without `//`); git log on GOVERNANCE.md (last edit 795a044, EP-0 follow-up, i.e. before the 2026-08-17 EP-6 Malwarebytes discovery); doctor.py lines 77, 604-649 (antivirus check names products and spells out D-38 paths, owner's word). Verdict: the core drift is real — GOVERNANCE overrides DECISIONS, yet it calls the exclusion "owner's discretion", names only Defender, and says the repository is not excluded while Malwarebytes excludes `source material\` and `.venv` inside the repo tree; three later records (D-29 addendum, D-38 addenda, D-42) call the data-location exclusions a mandatory disclosure control. Corrections: the `mwh guard`-in-§4 suggestion is misplaced (already in §3, and it is a git-side not a read-side layer); the "collaborator" scenario contradicts the DUA; practical impact is softened because `mwh doctor antivirus` warns with the seven paths on every run and CLAUDE.md §1 makes sessions read DECISIONS/DESIGN too. Severity stays medium only because GOVERNANCE is the top-of-precedence contract and the sentence inverts a control's status (discretionary vs mandatory); operationally it is low. Owner decision required: option (a) dated amendment recommended.

**Owner question (as posed by the reviewer).** Approve the GOVERNANCE §2/§4 wording change above? Options: (a) edit GOVERNANCE now with a dated '(amended 2026-08-18, D-42)' marker (recommended — GOVERNANCE is the contract others read); (b) leave GOVERNANCE frozen and add a §2 footnote pointing to D-42; (c) defer to EP-157 docs refresh (P11) — too late for a second machine or collaborator.

### ENV-2 — cp1252 (Risk 13) is a Bash-tool-only problem; the PowerShell tool already runs UTF-8 — fix it once with a settings.json env block instead of per-command _console_safe workarounds

**Severity (verified):** medium · **Verdict:** confirmed-with-corrections (high confidence) · **Kind:** environment · **When:** owner-decision · **Effort:** XS · **Retro episode:** EP-165 · **Lens:** Local development / session environment as it really is vs. what the roadmap assumed

**Files:** `roadmap/README.md:395`, `mimicwarehouse/DESIGN.md:65`, `.claude/settings.json:3`, `mimicwarehouse/src/mimicwarehouse/verify.py:673`

**Evidence.** Bash tool: `uv run python -c "import sys; print(sys.flags.utf8_mode, sys.stdout.encoding)"` -> `0 cp1252`; `print('geq ≥')` -> `UnicodeEncodeError: 'charmap' codec can't encode character '≥'`; `print('mid a·b')` -> `mid a�b` (U+00B7 is cp1252-encodable; the tool decodes stdout as UTF-8, so a lone 0xB7 byte becomes U+FFFD — the 'middle-dot replacement character' from the baseline). Same command with `PYTHONUTF8=1` -> `utf-8`, both glyphs print. PowerShell tool: `[Console]::OutputEncoding` = utf-8, `chcp` = 65001, process env `PYTHONIOENCODING=utf-8:surrogateescape` (not set at User/Machine scope — injected by the harness). Risk 13 says 'The Bash tool and some PowerShell hosts run `mwh` under code page 1252' and offers 'PYTHONIOENCODING=utf-8 in the profile' — `$PROFILE` does not exist (Test-Path False) and a PowerShell profile would not reach the Bash tool anyway.

**Impact.** Every P1+ CLI table/log line must keep threading through `_console_safe` (Risk 13 obligation) and any forgotten glyph crashes rich in a Bash-tool session (as at EP-3 and EP-6); doctor output already renders `·` as U+FFFD in Bash. Owner-side rich output in a real UTF-8 console is unaffected, so the divergence is invisible until a session hits it.

**Proposed fix (reviewer).** Owner decision (CLAUDE.md §6 covers `.claude/settings.json`): add `"env": {"PYTHONUTF8": "1"}` to the repo-shared `.claude/settings.json` (applies to Bash and PowerShell tool subprocesses; the code base already passes explicit encodings to every open()/read_text — grep shows only `tests/ep/test_ep03.py:140,180` write_text without encoding, ASCII content — so no behaviour change besides stdout/stderr). Alternatively `PYTHONIOENCODING=utf-8` (stdout only, mirrors what the harness sets for PowerShell). Then refine Risk 13 + DESIGN §2 note (2): 'the Claude Bash tool (MSYS bash, cp1252 stdio) is the affected host; the PowerShell tool is UTF-8 with PYTHONIOENCODING set by the harness; sessions get PYTHONUTF8=1 from .claude/settings.json; `_console_safe` stays as belt for owner consoles'.

**Corrected fix (verifier).** Owner decision (CLAUDE.md §6 covers .claude/settings.json). Preferred: add `"env": {"PYTHONUTF8": "1"}` to the repo-shared `.claude/settings.json` (currently only a `permissions.deny` block; no settings.local.json exists), plus a one-line D-39 addendum saying the env block is not a permission change. Two corrections to the proposed fix: (a) PYTHONUTF8=1 is NOT stdout-only — it also flips `subprocess.run(text=True)` decoding from cp1252 to strict UTF-8, and `doctor._run` (mimicwarehouse/src/mimicwarehouse/doctor.py:146-156) calls Windows PowerShell 5.1 / `powercfg` with `text=True` and no encoding/errors; those children emit OEM (cp437) bytes when piped, so a non-ASCII power-scheme or AV product name would raise UnicodeDecodeError (uncaught, not ProbeError) instead of decoding to a harmless wrong glyph. Harden `_run` with `errors="replace"` (XS) in the same change, or choose the zero-side-effect variant `PYTHONIOENCODING=utf-8` (stdio only, exactly what the harness already injects for the PowerShell tool as `utf-8:surrogateescape`). On this host doctor ran clean under PYTHONUTF8=1 (all output ASCII), so this is a robustness note, not a blocker. (b) The claim that only `tests/ep/test_ep03.py:140,180` write_text without encoding is wrong — both pass `encoding="utf-8"` on the continuation line; the codebase is uniformly explicit (read_text("utf-8") everywhere; only doctor._run relies on the locale). Then refine roadmap/README.md Risk 13 (line 395) and DESIGN.md §2 note (2) (line 65): the affected host is the Claude Bash tool (MSYS bash, Python stdio cp1252 because stdout is a pipe; `sys.flags.utf8_mode=0`); the PowerShell tool is UTF-8 (chcp 65001, [Console]::OutputEncoding utf-8, PYTHONIOENCODING set by the harness); owner interactive consoles are unaffected because CPython uses UTF-8 console I/O when attached to a real console; also correct Risk 13's glyph list — `·` (U+00B7) and `—` (U+2014) are cp1252-encodable and do NOT raise; their symptom is U+FFFD mojibake in the Bash tool (0xB7 byte decoded as UTF-8), which `_console_safe` cannot fix — only UTF-8 stdio can (verified: `mwh doctor` renders `·`/`—`/`✓`/box glyphs correctly under PYTHONUTF8=1 and shows `�` without it). Keep `_console_safe` and the ASCII discipline as belt-and-braces; drop the "some PowerShell hosts" / "$PROFILE" advice (no $PROFILE exists and it would not reach the Bash tool).

**Verifier notes.** Checked: roadmap/README.md:395 (Risk 13 text quoted), mimicwarehouse/DESIGN.md:65, .claude/settings.json (deny-only, no env block; no settings.local.json), verify.py:673-722 (_console_safe uses sys.stdout.encoding). Bash tool probes: PYTHONUTF8/PYTHONIOENCODING unset; `uv run python -c` -> utf8_mode 0, stdout/stderr cp1252; print('≥') -> UnicodeEncodeError; print('a·b') | od -c -> byte 267 (0xB7) i.e. cp1252-encoded, which the tool then shows as U+FFFD; with PYTHONUTF8=1 -> utf-8, both glyphs print. PowerShell tool probes: [Console]::OutputEncoding/InputEncoding/$OutputEncoding all utf-8, chcp 65001, process env PYTHONIOENCODING=utf-8:surrogateescape, User/Machine scope empty, $PROFILE and AllUsers profile Test-Path False, python -> utf-8 and glyphs print. Ran `uv run mwh doctor` in Bash with and without PYTHONUTF8=1: without -> `·` renders as `�` and `—` too; with -> full Unicode table renders correctly, 8 pass 1 warn 0 fail 5 info, exit 0, ~6 s. Grepped all read_text/write_text/open/subprocess calls under src/ and tests/: every file read/write passes an explicit encoding (test_ep03:140/180 do too — finding detail wrong); inventory._git_sha and verify._run_git pass encoding="utf-8"; only doctor._run (doctor.py:146-156, powershell/powercfg/uv/git probes) uses text=True without encoding/errors, so PYTHONUTF8=1 is not purely a stdio change there — hence the correction. Impact statement (per-brief _console_safe obligation, prior crashes at EP-3/EP-6, invisible to the owner's real console) holds; severity medium kept; verdict confirmed-with-corrections because the fix's "no behaviour change besides stdout/stderr" and the test_ep03 detail are inaccurate and Risk 13's glyph list needs correcting too.

**Owner question (as posed by the reviewer).** Add `env: {PYTHONUTF8: "1"}` to the shared .claude/settings.json (recommended — one line, project-scoped, keeps _console_safe as fallback), or to settings.local.json (owner-only, not versioned), or leave Risk 13 as a per-brief obligation? Recommendation: shared settings.json + a D-39 addendum sentence noting the env block is not a permission change.

### ENV-3 — CLAUDE.md §3 does not carry the D-42 session I/O rules or the tooling gotchas that currently live only in machine-local auto-memory and completion notes

**Severity (verified):** medium · **Verdict:** confirmed (high confidence) · **Kind:** docs-drift · **When:** now-should · **Effort:** S · **Retro episode:** EP-165 · **Lens:** Local development / session environment as it really is vs. what the roadmap assumed

**Files:** `CLAUDE.md:33`, `mimicwarehouse/DECISIONS.md:505`, `roadmap/EP-12-fixtures-icu-markers.md:197`, `roadmap/EP-7-replan-p0.md:129`

**Evidence.** CLAUDE.md §3 (lines 33–45) mentions uv groups, tiers, DuckDB limits and `__main__` guards only. D-42 (DECISIONS.md:505) says 'sessions write files with the Write/Edit tools, **never** shell heredocs' and 'no burst copy / sed -i / delete loops'; EP-12 note: 'one accidental `python -` stdin invocation hung for two minutes'; EP-7 retro: 'a Bash heredoc for this very note was refused by the shell'. The auto-mode harness prompt in this very session says 'make file changes with sed, heredocs, or short scripts'. Persistent memory (not in the repo) is the only place recording 'no bash heredocs / no `python -` stdin — Write/Edit files, `git commit -F <file>`', 'uv not on tool PATH', 'sleep blocked'. Also: `which python` in the Bash tool -> `/c/Python314/python` (3.14.7), i.e. bare `python`/`pip` = the system interpreter CLAUDE.md says never to touch; PowerShell tool likewise `C:\Python314\python.exe`.

**Impact.** A session on another machine, after a memory reset, or driven by the harness default will use a heredoc (Defender ClickFix kill, D-42 (1)), pipe `python -` (2-minute hang, no stdin in the tool), run `python scripts/roadmap_check.py` under 3.14 (ImportError, or worse a `pip install` into C:\Python314), or write a multi-line commit message inline. These are the exact incidents of EP-6/7/12 repeating.

**Proposed fix (reviewer).** Repo edit (S): add a 'Session tooling (D-42, Risk 12/13)' bullet list to CLAUDE.md §3: (a) never `bash -c ... <<EOF` heredocs or `python -` stdin — write files with Write/Edit, run scripts from the scratchpad, `python -c` one-liners are fine; (b) commit messages via `git commit -F <scratch file>`; (c) bare `python`/`pip` in the tool shells is C:\Python314 — always `uv run python`; (d) if `uv` is not found, PATH prefix (ENV-1) and restart VS Code; (e) foreground `sleep` is blocked in the Bash tool, foreground cap ~10 min (already there) — background jobs with logs; (f) no burst copy/sed -i/rm loops over hundreds of files in scratch (Malwarebytes ARW); (g) 'process killed / binary vanished' -> Malwarebytes quarantine first. Then retire the duplicate memory notes to 'see CLAUDE.md §3'.

**Verifier notes.** Checked: CLAUDE.md lines 33-46 (§3) verbatim — bullets cover uv groups, tiers/foreground cap, DuckDB limits, __main__ guard only; nothing on heredocs, `python -` stdin, commit -F, bare python = 3.14, uv PATH. DECISIONS.md:505-524 (D-42) does say 'sessions write files with the Write/Edit tools, **never** shell heredocs' and 'no burst copy / sed -i / delete loops', so the heredoc rule is in the repo — but only in DECISIONS.md and roadmap/README.md Risk 12 (line 394), neither of which CLAUDE.md §1 requires to be read in full (§1.3 says read 'the settled decisions the brief cites as D-n'), and `grep -l D-42 roadmap/*.md` shows the pending briefs EP-13, EP-14, EP-15, EP-17 do NOT cite D-42 (only EP-16 replan and completed briefs do). So a fresh EP-13 session following CLAUDE.md to the letter never meets D-42. EP-12 note line 197 ('one accidental `python -` stdin invocation hung for two minutes') and EP-7 retro line 129 ('a Bash heredoc for this very note was refused by the shell') confirmed verbatim. The `python -` stdin hazard, `git commit -F`, and 'uv not on tool PATH' exist nowhere in the repo (grep over CLAUDE.md, READMEs, DESIGN.md, GOVERNANCE.md, roadmap/*.md for heredoc|commit -F|WinGet/Links|not on PATH found only Risk 12 and DESIGN.md:41-42 recording the install path, not the gotcha); EP-1 note lines 170-171 even say 'no further PATH change was needed', which is misleading for the tool shells. Live check in this session: `which python` -> /c/Python314/python (3.14.7), `which uv` -> not found on the Bash tool PATH; after `export PATH="$LOCALAPPDATA/Microsoft/WinGet/Links:$PATH"` uv.exe/uvx.exe/uvw.exe are there. The auto-mode harness text in this very session again instructs 'make file changes with sed, heredocs, or short scripts', so the conflict is real and recurring. Impact (repeat of EP-6/7/12 incidents on a memory reset or another machine) is concrete, not imaginary; severity medium is right for docs drift with a demonstrated incident history. Fix as proposed (S: add a 'Session tooling (D-42, Risk 12/13)' bullet list to CLAUDE.md §3 and point memory notes at it) is appropriate; one small addition — also amend the EP-1 completion-note sentence at roadmap/EP-1-toolchain-bootstrap.md:170-171 or add a one-liner in §3 that uv is on the user PATH but not the Claude tool shells' PATH.

### FC-1 — Tier ladder skips dev/full tests until dev.duckdb exists — EP-17..EP-20 dev/full acceptance is vacuous

**Severity (verified):** medium · **Verdict:** confirmed-with-corrections (high confidence) · **Kind:** test · **When:** now-must · **Effort:** S · **Retro episode:** EP-168 · **Lens:** forward-compat: pending briefs (EP-13..EP-56) vs shipped P0/P1 code

**Files:** `mimicwarehouse/tests/conftest.py:118-131`, `mimicwarehouse/tests/conftest.py:167-181`, `mimicwarehouse/tests/ep/test_ep12.py:786`, `mimicwarehouse/tests/ep/test_ep12.py:798`, `roadmap/EP-17-stage-loader-core.md:84-91`, `roadmap/EP-18-stage-loader-buckets.md:70-73`, `roadmap/EP-19-stage-dag-runner.md:80-89`, `roadmap/EP-20-stage-small-tables.md:50-56`

**Evidence.** conftest.py: `catalog_status(tier)` returns `path.is_file()` of `get_settings().catalog_path(tier)` and `pytest_collection_modifyitems` adds `pytest.mark.skip(reason=f"{tier} tier: catalog not found ({where}); EP-21 builds it")`; test_ep12.py:786 pins `"*SKIP*dev tier: catalog not found*dev.duckdb*"`. EP-17: `@pytest.mark.tier("dev")` … stage real `mimiciv_hosp.d_labitems` … acceptance `uv run poe test -m ep_17 --tier dev green`; EP-19 tier("dev") test writes `lake/core/mimiciv_hosp/patients/subject_bucket=0…4/` in the real lake; EP-20 has `tier("full")`-marked reconciliation tests. EP-21 (which creates dev.duckdb/full.duckdb) runs after all four.

**Impact.** EP-17, EP-18, EP-19 and EP-20 sessions will run `--tier dev` / `--tier full`, see only SKIPs and report green; the only real-data checks in P2 before EP-21 never execute, and nobody re-runs them afterwards. The first real-data staging bug surfaces at EP-23's background job instead of EP-17's 12 MB test.

**Proposed fix (reviewer).** At EP-17 (first code brief that needs it) extend the tier machinery: `tier(name, needs="catalog"|"raw")` (default `catalog` keeps EP-12 semantics); `needs="raw"` skips only when `settings.source_root/'mimic-iv-3.1'` is absent. Mark EP-17/18/19/20 dev+full tests `needs="raw"`; update the two pinned reason strings in test_ep12 and tests/README.md; add a one-line EP-16 pickup note to EP-17..20. Alternative: EP-16 amends EP-17..20 to say 'dev/full tests are skipped until EP-21; EP-28 re-runs `poe test -m ep_17,18,19,20 --tier full`' (weaker).

**Corrected fix (verifier).** At EP-17 (code) with an EP-16 pickup note in EP-17..EP-20 (docs): (a) conftest.py — accept `tier(name, needs="catalog"|"raw")` (relax the kwargs check at :94-98; default `catalog` keeps EP-12 semantics); `needs="raw"` skips only when `get_settings().source_root / inventory.dataset_dir("mimic-iv-3.1")` is not a directory (optionally also when EP-10's `lake/manifests/raw/mimic-iv-3.1.jsonl` is missing, since EP-17 compares against it); reason string e.g. "dev tier: raw MIMIC-IV 3.1 not found (<path>)"; update the marker registration text (:124-130) and the `--tier` help (:66-70). (b) Mark EP-17/18 dev tests and EP-20 dev/full reconciliation tests `needs="raw"` (EP-20's full check reads lake status.json, so `needs="lake"` or `"raw"` — pick one and document). (c) Update pinned strings/tests: test_ep12.py:786, :798 and add one nested-pytester case for `needs="raw"`; tests/README.md:24-33 table; DESIGN.md §20 dated note; pyproject.toml:106-107 comments; amend the EP-12 hand-off sentence (EP-12 brief :202) via an EP-16 pickup note rather than rewriting it. (d) Alternative (weaker, docs-only): EP-16 states in EP-17..EP-20 that dev/full tests skip until EP-21 and adds an explicit `poe test -m "ep_17 or ep_18 or ep_19 or ep_20" --tier full` re-run to EP-28's acceptance. Effort S either way. Owner decision: (a) `needs=` marker at EP-17 (recommended) vs (d) accept skips + EP-28 re-run.

**Verifier notes.** Checked: mimicwarehouse/tests/conftest.py:107-118 (`catalog_status` = `get_settings().catalog_path(tier).is_file()`), :150-159 (adds `pytest.mark.skip(reason=f"{tier} tier: catalog not found ({where}); EP-21 builds it")`), :94-98 (`tier_of` raises UsageError on ANY marker kwargs — a `needs=` argument needs that check relaxed); config.py:622-624 (`catalog_path` = `<data_root>/warehouse/<tier>.duckdb`); tests/ep/test_ep12.py:786 and :798 pin the reason strings; tests/README.md:24-33; DESIGN.md:818-826 (EP-12 note); pyproject.toml:106-107 (`test-dev`/`test-full` comments say "skipped while dev.duckdb is missing"). Briefs: EP-17-stage-loader-core.md item 6 + acceptance ("`poe test -m ep_17 --tier dev` green") — its dev test needs the raw CSVs + EP-10 raw manifest, not a catalog; EP-18 item 5 tier("dev") stages real admissions into a temp lake; EP-19 item 6 tier("dev"); EP-20 item 4 tier("full")/tier("dev") reconciliation; EP-21 is the first brief to write dev.duckdb/full.duckdb (its acceptance line: "%MWH_DATA_ROOT%\warehouse\dev.duckdb and full.duckdb exist"). EP-28 acceptance/items 2 do NOT re-run ep_17..ep_20. So the core claim holds: with the shipped predicate every dev/full-marked test in EP-17..EP-20 is unconditionally skipped and each brief's "--tier dev green" acceptance is satisfied by skips. Worse, EP-12's own hand-off (roadmap/EP-12-fixtures-icu-markers.md:202: "the first `@pytest.mark.tier("dev")` tests go in `test_ep17.py` and skip cleanly until EP-21 writes `dev.duckdb`") explicitly tells the EP-17 session to expect and accept the skip; EP-16 item 6 (amend P2 briefs re `--tier` markers) says nothing about the predicate.

Corrections: (1) Impact is over-stated. EP-19's `ep19-smoke` build (acceptance requires `runs\jobs\ep19-smoke.json` = done) and EP-20 item 3's `stage-small-full` background job are session commands whose artifacts/counts are recorded in completion notes regardless of pytest, so the first real-data staging exercise still happens at EP-19/EP-20, not EP-23; and EP-28's tier("full") checks run after EP-21 and largely re-cover EP-20's count reconciliation. What is genuinely lost for good: EP-17's rows-vs-raw-manifest/rejects=0 assertion (12 MB), EP-18's five-partition dev assertion (90 MB), EP-20's dev-partition sum check, and honest acceptance lines. Hence medium, not high; still "fix now" because it is one small conftest change and the first brief that needs it (EP-17) is next-but-four. (2) EP-16 is docs-only, so the code fix cannot land before EP-17; EP-16 must add the pickup note. (3) Fix inventory is larger than the finding lists — see corrected_fix.

**Owner question (as posed by the reviewer).** Which fix: (a) `needs=` marker argument at EP-17 (recommended — keeps the ladder honest), or (b) accept the skips and add an explicit EP-28 re-run of EP-17..20 with --tier full?

### FC-11 — EP-21 item 4 re-points `fixture_catalog` at a runner-built catalog while stage.yaml covers only 20/31 tables — test_ep12 and any 31-table consumer break

**Severity (verified):** medium · **Verdict:** confirmed-with-corrections (high confidence) · **Kind:** test · **When:** now-should · **Effort:** S · **Retro episode:** EP-168 · **Lens:** forward-compat: pending briefs (EP-13..EP-56) vs shipped P0/P1 code

**Files:** `roadmap/EP-21-stage-catalog-builder.md:60-65`, `roadmap/EP-20-stage-small-tables.md:35-37`, `mimicwarehouse/tests/ep/test_ep12.py:571-604`, `mimicwarehouse/tests/conftest.py:118-121`

**Evidence.** EP-21 item 4: "build the fixture lake into a session temp root (`mwh build --tier fixture --tag small --data-root <tmp>`) … keep EP-8…EP-12's tests green (same 31 table names)". EP-20: "That is 15 hosp + 5 icu = 20 tables; with EP-23/24/25 (hosp) and EP-26/27 (icu) every table in the contract is covered" — the remaining 11 steps land only in EP-23..27. test_ep12 iterates `contract.by_schema("mimiciv_icu")` (9 tables incl. chartevents/inputevents/ingredientevents/datetimeevents) over `fixture_catalog`. conftest `catalog_status("fixture")` hard-codes "in-memory fixture catalog".

**Impact.** At EP-21 the re-pointed fixture catalog lacks 11 tables → test_ep12 (and EP-30's `mimiciv_hosp.labevents` free-text test) fail; also every `poe test` session gains a fixture build + catalog build at startup.

**Proposed fix (reviewer).** Have EP-20 add all 31 fixture-tier stage steps to stage.yaml (tiers `[fixture]` for the 11 event tables; EP-23..27 later add `dev/full/demo` to their tiers and own the ⏱ runs), so EP-21's fixture catalog is complete; update the conftest docstring/`catalog_status` text; keep `build_fixture_catalog()` as the documented fast fallback.

**Corrected fix (verifier).** Pick one of two brief-level fixes (owner decision; both S effort, docs-only now):

Option A (as proposed, with two corrections): EP-20 item 1 declares all 31 stage steps in stage.yaml (event tables with `tiers: [fixture]` only, tags per their owning brief); EP-23..27 item 1 becomes "extend tiers to dev/full/demo + own the ⏱ runs"; EP-20's coverage test becomes "every contract table has exactly one step". Correction 1: EP-21 item 4 must also drop `--tag small` from `mwh build --tier fixture --tag small --data-root <tmp>` (or add a common `fixture` tag) — otherwise the 11 event tables are still excluded even after EP-20 adds them. Correction 2: this makes EP-20 exercise the loader's `large` load path at fixture scale — acceptable, but note it in EP-20's context.

Option B (less churn, recommended): leave EP-20 at 20 steps; EP-21 item 4 keeps the existing in-memory `fixture_catalog` (31 tables) untouched and adds a *second* session fixture `fixture_lake_catalog` (real `fixture.duckdb` over whatever stage.yaml stages, via `open_catalog`) used by test_ep21/22/29/30; the "re-point `fixture_catalog` at the real catalog and retire the in-memory build to a fallback" step moves to EP-28 (Verify full staging — the first brief that depends on EP-23..27), which also updates conftest.py:108-110 `catalog_status("fixture")` text ("in-memory fixture catalog") and tests/README.md:26,55. Either way, EP-30 item 6 should say which fixture it uses (its `mimiciv_hosp.labevents` free-text test needs a 31-table catalog).

**Verifier notes.** Checked roadmap/EP-21-stage-catalog-builder.md:60-66 (item 4: "build the fixture lake … `--tier fixture --tag small` … then build_catalog('fixture') … keep EP-8…EP-12's tests green (same 31 table names)") against roadmap/EP-20-stage-small-tables.md:20-37 ("15 hosp + 5 icu = 20 tables; with EP-23/24/25 (hosp) and EP-26/27 (icu) every table … is covered") and :51-52 (event tables explicitly out of scope). EP-23..27 briefs each add their own stage steps with `tiers [fixture, dev, full, demo]` (EP-23:25-26, EP-24:23-24, EP-25:21, EP-26:28-29, EP-27:22), so at EP-21 the fixture-tier lake/catalog has 20 tables. Contradiction is real: EP-21's own item 4 also uses `--tag small`, which limits to EP-20's steps regardless.

Consumers verified: mimicwarehouse/tests/conftest.py:197-206 `fixture_catalog` (session, in-memory, 31 tables) is used only by tests/ep/test_ep12.py — test_duckdb_reads_all_9_typed_with_zero_rejects (571-602) loops `contract.by_schema("mimiciv_icu")` = 9 tables incl. chartevents/inputevents/ingredientevents/datetimeevents; test_catalog_icustays_transfers_and_windows (605-650) queries `mimiciv_icu.{chartevents,datetimeevents,inputevents,ingredientevents,…}` via check.ICU_EVENT_TIMES (fixtures/check.py:87-94) plus `mimiciv_hosp.microbiologyevents` and `mimiciv_hosp.prescriptions` (EP-25 tables). With a 20-table fixture catalog these fail → `poe test` and `mwh verify EP-12` (verify.py runs `pytest -m ep_12`) red from EP-21 until EP-27 lands (linear order roadmap/README.md:9: EP-21→22→23..27→28), i.e. ~6 sessions, unless the EP-21 session improvises. conftest.py:108-110 hard-codes "in-memory fixture catalog" and tests/README.md:26,55 say the same — doc drift once re-pointed.

Corrections to the finding: (1) the EP-30 `mimiciv_hosp.labevents` impact is moot under the linear order (EP-30 executes after EP-27, roadmap/README.md:85-92), so the real blast radius is test_ep12 + the EP-21..27 window; (2) the proposed fix is incomplete without removing `--tag small` from EP-21 item 4; (3) an alternative (keep in-memory `fixture_catalog`, add a separate lake-backed fixture, defer the re-point to EP-28) avoids pre-declaring 11 event steps in EP-20 and is arguably cleaner. Also `poe test` startup-cost concern is minor: the fixture is session-scoped and only test_ep12 requests it today. Severity medium stands: it is a brief inconsistency that will produce a red suite or an ad-hoc judgment call in EP-21.

### FC-15 — EP-18 wants a `DEV_BUCKETS` constant 'once in mimicwarehouse.config'; EP-3 shipped a configurable `Settings.dev_buckets`

**Severity (verified):** medium · **Verdict:** confirmed-with-corrections (high confidence) · **Kind:** design · **When:** now-should · **Effort:** XS · **Retro episode:** EP-167 · **Lens:** forward-compat: pending briefs (EP-13..EP-56) vs shipped P0/P1 code

**Files:** `roadmap/EP-18-stage-loader-buckets.md:52-54`, `mimicwarehouse/src/mimicwarehouse/config.py:506`, `mimicwarehouse/src/mimicwarehouse/config.py:551-556`, `mimicwarehouse/.env.example`

**Evidence.** EP-18: "`DEV_BUCKETS = (0, 1, 2, 3, 4)` lives once in `mimicwarehouse.config` and is imported by the loader and the catalog builder (EP-21)". config.py: `dev_buckets: list[int] = Field(default_factory=lambda: [0, 1, 2, 3, 4])` with validator (0..99, sorted set); .env.example `# MWH_DEV_BUCKETS=[0,1,2,3,4]`.

**Impact.** If the loader imports a constant while the catalog builder/conftest read the setting, a user override of MWH_DEV_BUCKETS makes dev.duckdb views disagree with the sorted-first buckets and `dev_ready` flags.

**Proposed fix (reviewer).** Amend EP-18/19/21: read `settings.dev_buckets` everywhere (no constant); record in `status.json`/`meta.catalog_info` which buckets 'dev' meant at build time so a later override is detectable.

**Corrected fix (verifier).** Owner decision required (D-18 says dev = buckets 0-4 as a settled decision; EP-3 shipped it as a user knob). Options: (A) FREEZE - keep D-18 literal: replace `Settings.dev_buckets` (config.py:523, validator :579-584) with a module constant `DEV_BUCKETS: tuple[int, ...] = (0, 1, 2, 3, 4)` in `mimicwarehouse.config` (or keep the field but mark it `Field(frozen=True)`/drop it from env parsing), delete `# MWH_DEV_BUCKETS=[0,1,2,3,4]` from `.env.example:42-43` and the `dev_buckets=[0..4]` clause from `mimicwarehouse/README.md:84`; briefs EP-18/19/21 then need no text change. (B) CONFIGURABLE - keep the field and amend the briefs: EP-18:52-54 (`DEV_BUCKETS ... lives once in mimicwarehouse.config` -> `settings.dev_buckets`), EP-18:48 (`when buckets 0-4 are sorted set dev_ready` -> `when every bucket in settings.dev_buckets is sorted`), EP-18:65,72 and EP-19:16,46 and EP-21:15,37 (`WHERE subject_bucket IN (0,1,2,3,4)` -> `IN (<settings.dev_buckets>)`); write `dev_buckets` into `_progress.json`/`status.json` (EP-18) and `meta.catalog_info` (EP-21) and have the catalog opener/`mwh verify` assert build-time buckets == current settings so a later override is detected instead of silently mismatching. Recommendation: A (D-18 already fixed the value; the fixture generator also hard-assumes it: `fixtures/spec.py:6` "the dev filter ``< 5``", DESIGN.md:153). Whichever option, add a one-line DECISIONS addendum. Effort XS-S (A: ~20 min incl. test_ep03 touch-ups; B: ~30 min of brief edits now, small code in EP-18/21).

**Verifier notes.** Checked: roadmap/EP-18-stage-loader-buckets.md:48-54,65,72 (constant `DEV_BUCKETS` "lives once in mimicwarehouse.config", plus literal "buckets 0-4" for dev_ready); EP-19:16,46 and EP-21:15,37 (literal `IN (0,1,2,3,4)`); config.py:523 `dev_buckets: list[int] = Field(default_factory=lambda: [0,1,2,3,4])` and validator :579-584 (0..99, sorted set) - the file's line numbers in the finding (506, 551-556) are off but the code exists; .env.example:42-43 exposes `MWH_DEV_BUCKETS`; mimicwarehouse/README.md:84 documents `dev_buckets=[0..4]` as a setting; EP-3 brief :35 specified the field. `grep -rn dev_buckets|DEV_BUCKETS mimicwarehouse/src mimicwarehouse/tests` -> only config.py defines it; no consumer exists yet (tests/conftest.py tier gating uses catalog_path only). DECISIONS.md:491 and README.md:99,379 and DESIGN.md:131,134 fix dev = buckets 0-4 (D-18); fixtures/spec.py:6 and DESIGN.md:153 hard-assume "dev filter < 5". So the two-sources-of-truth problem is real and forward-facing: the EP-18 implementer must either add a constant (leaving a documented, validated MWH_DEV_BUCKETS knob dead) or read the setting (contradicting the brief text and the literal `IN (0,1,2,3,4)` in EP-21:37). Correction to the impact: the finding's scenario "loader imports a constant while the catalog builder reads the setting" is unlikely as written, because EP-18/19/21 all say they import the constant; the concrete failure is a silent no-op override (user sets MWH_DEV_BUCKETS=[0..9] expecting a 10% dev tier; lake, dev views and dev_ready still use 0-4 while README/.env.example/`mwh paths` claim otherwise) plus the fixture generator's `< 5` assumption breaking test_ep11/12 expectations under any override. Correction to the fix: it is not simply "read settings everywhere" - D-18 froze the value, so this needs an owner decision between freezing (recommended) and making it configurable-with-provenance. Severity medium retained: no correctness risk today, but it must be settled before EP-18 starts and touches three pending briefs plus two docs.

### FC-2 — EP-22 wants `demo` in the pytest tier vocabulary; EP-12 shipped a fixed linear ladder that excludes it

**Severity (verified):** medium · **Verdict:** confirmed-with-corrections (high confidence) · **Kind:** design · **When:** now-should · **Effort:** XS · **Retro episode:** EP-168 · **Lens:** forward-compat: pending briefs (EP-13..EP-56) vs shipped P0/P1 code

**Files:** `roadmap/EP-22-stage-demo-tier.md:59-62`, `mimicwarehouse/tests/conftest.py:44-46`, `mimicwarehouse/tests/README.md:24-30`

**Evidence.** EP-22 item 5: "Extend EP-12's tier vocabulary with `demo` (`--tier demo` selects fixture + demo; skipped with a reason when `warehouse\demo.duckdb` is absent)". conftest.py: `TIERS = ("fixture", "dev", "full")` and the docstring "`demo` is a data tier for EP-22, never a test tier"; tests/README.md repeats it. `--tier` is a *maximum* in a linear order, so `demo` has no rank between dev and full.

**Impact.** EP-22 either breaks the linear-maximum semantics (where does `--tier demo` sit relative to dev/full?) or silently contradicts EP-12's documented decision; `poe test-dev` would have to decide whether it also runs demo tests.

**Proposed fix (reviewer).** Amend EP-22: demo tests are opt-in via a separate `--demo` flag (or `tier("demo")` handled as an orthogonal marker, deselected unless `--demo`, skipped when `catalog_path('demo')` is missing); do not insert demo into the ladder. Record in tests/README.md at EP-22.

**Corrected fix (verifier).** Amend roadmap/EP-22-stage-demo-tier.md item 5 (lines 59-62) so it no longer says "`--tier demo` selects fixture + demo". Owner picks one of: (A, recommended, matches four shipped statements) keep the ladder `fixture < dev < full`; add `demo` as an orthogonal opt-in: register `"demo"` as an allowed `tier(...)` marker name in `tier_of()` (today it is a usage error), deselect `tier("demo")` items unless a new `--demo` flag (or `PYTEST_DEMO=1`) is given, and skip them with a reason when `catalog_status("demo")` is absent (that helper already generalises via `catalog_path(tier)`, no new code). Add `poe test-demo = "pytest --demo"`. (B) redefine the ladder as `fixture < demo < dev < full` (`TIERS` in conftest.py:44, choices of `--tier`), accepting that `poe test-dev`/`test-full` also select demo tests (skipped while demo.duckdb is missing). Either way the EP-22 brief must explicitly say that tests/ep/test_ep12.py:810-815 (asserts `PYTEST_TIER=demo` and `--tier demo` are usage errors) and :849 (asserts the README mentions `demo` as excluded) are to be updated, and that tests/README.md §Tiers, DESIGN.md §20 (line 828) and conftest.py docstring (lines 11-13) get an "EP-22 amendment" note. Effort XS (brief edit now; code changes are EP-22 work).

**Verifier notes.** Checked mimicwarehouse/tests/conftest.py lines 1-120: `TIERS = ("fixture", "dev", "full")` (l.44), `--tier` choices=TIERS (l.60), `selected_tier` raises UsageError for PYTEST_TIER outside TIERS (l.79-83), `tier_of` raises UsageError for `tier("demo")` (l.98-101), docstring l.11-13 says demo is "never a test tier". tests/README.md l.24-42 repeats the fixed ladder and the demo exclusion; DESIGN.md l.828 and roadmap/EP-12 brief l.11-12/77 say the same; pyproject.toml l.105-107 defines only test/test-dev/test-full. roadmap/EP-22-stage-demo-tier.md l.59-62 verbatim: "Extend EP-12's tier vocabulary with `demo` (`--tier demo` selects fixture + demo; skipped with a reason when `warehouse\demo.duckdb` is absent)". config.py l.67: `Tier = Literal["fixture", "demo", "dev", "full"]`. Crucially the finding under-states it: EP-12 shipped pytester tests (tests/ep/test_ep12.py l.810-815) that assert `PYTEST_TIER=demo` and `--tier demo` ARE usage errors, and l.849 asserts the README still names `demo` among excluded terms — so an EP-22 session implementing item 5 literally would turn EP-12's acceptance suite red, or would have to silently rewrite EP-12 tests/docs. The finding's phrasing "no rank between dev and full" is imprecise (demo could sit between fixture and dev, which is one of the owner options); the contradiction and the need for an owner decision are real. No other brief besides EP-22 uses `tier("demo")`/`--tier demo` for pytest (grep over roadmap/*.md; the other hits are `mwh build/app --tier demo`, a data-tier usage that is fine). Severity medium is fair: not blocking until EP-22, but it is a documented-decision vs pending-brief conflict that an autonomous session cannot resolve without a judgment call.

**Owner question (as posed by the reviewer).** Confirm demo stays outside the fixture<dev<full ladder (recommended) vs redefining the ladder as fixture<demo<dev<full.

### FC-3 — Contract sort_keys / load_class disagree with the sort keys EP-20/23/24/25/26/27 expect — decide once, not per brief

**Severity (verified):** medium · **Verdict:** confirmed-with-corrections (high confidence) · **Kind:** design · **When:** now-should · **Effort:** S · **Retro episode:** EP-169 · **Lens:** forward-compat: pending briefs (EP-13..EP-56) vs shipped P0/P1 code

**Files:** `mimicwarehouse/src/mimicwarehouse/schema/tables/mimiciv_hosp.yaml`, `mimicwarehouse/src/mimicwarehouse/schema/tables/mimiciv_icu.yaml`, `roadmap/EP-20-stage-small-tables.md:26-33`, `roadmap/EP-23-stage-labevents.md:26-28`, `roadmap/EP-24-stage-emar.md:28-30`, `roadmap/EP-25-stage-hosp-rest.md:26-30`, `roadmap/EP-26-stage-chartevents.md:27-29`, `roadmap/EP-27-stage-icu-events.md:24-27`, `roadmap/EP-16-replan-p1.md:73-79`

**Evidence.** Shipped contract (load_contract()): transfers ('subject_id','intime'); procedures_icd ('subject_id','hadm_id','seq_num'); hcpcsevents ('subject_id','chartdate'); procedureevents ('subject_id','starttime'); outputevents ('subject_id','charttime'); labevents ('subject_id','charttime'); emar ('subject_id','charttime'); emar_detail ('subject_id','emar_id','parent_field_ordinal'); pharmacy/prescriptions ('subject_id','starttime'); poe ('subject_id','ordertime'); microbiologyevents load_class **large**, sort ('subject_id','chartdate','charttime'); chartevents ('subject_id','charttime'); inputevents/ingredientevents ('subject_id','starttime'); datetimeevents ('subject_id','charttime'). Briefs expect: EP-20 `transfers [intime, transfer_id]`, `procedures_icd [chartdate, hadm_id, seq_num]`, `hcpcsevents [chartdate, hadm_id, seq_num]`, `procedureevents [starttime, orderid]`, `outputevents [charttime, itemid]`; EP-23 `[subject_id, charttime, itemid]`; EP-24 `[…, charttime, emar_seq]` / `[…, emar_id, emar_seq, parent_field_ordinal]`; EP-25 microbiologyevents `small`, `[chartdate, charttime, microevent_id]`, poe `[ordertime, poe_seq]`, pharmacy `[starttime, pharmacy_id]`; EP-26 `[…, charttime, itemid]`; EP-27 `[starttime, orderid]`, `[charttime, itemid]`. Each brief says 'fix the contract with a dated note if it disagrees'. EP-16 item 6 lists only EP-17…EP-22 for pickup notes.

**Impact.** Six briefs will each edit the contract YAML separately; every edit changes `Contract.content_hash()` (see FC-4) and forces a fixture regeneration commit each time; sort-key tie-breakers also change fixture CSV order (fixtures/check.py asserts sortedness by `sort_keys`), so the 31 committed CSVs may be rewritten up to six times. Two briefs (EP-25 microbiologyevents small vs large) also disagree on size class.

**Proposed fix (reviewer).** Settle the sort_keys/load_class table once, before EP-17: EP-16 (as part of item 6, extended to EP-23…EP-27) or a tiny pre-EP-17 hotfix updates the YAML with a dated header note, regenerates fixtures with `mwh fixtures build`, and each staging brief gets a pickup note 'contract already carries the tie-break keys; no edit needed'. If the EP-9 `[subject_id, time]` form is deliberate (DESIGN §5 says exactly that), amend the briefs instead and drop the tie-break columns.

**Corrected fix (verifier).** Owner decides two things now, then ONE contract edit before EP-17 (fold into EP-16 item 6, extended to EP-23..EP-27, or a tiny pre-EP-17 hotfix; effort S):
(1) Tie-break sort keys: adopt the brief-form keys (transfers +transfer_id; hcpcsevents chartdate,hadm_id,seq_num; procedureevents/inputevents/ingredientevents +orderid; outputevents/labevents/chartevents/datetimeevents +itemid; emar +emar_seq; emar_detail subject_id,emar_id,emar_seq,parent_field_ordinal; pharmacy/prescriptions +pharmacy_id; poe +poe_seq; microbiologyevents +microevent_id) OR keep EP-9's deliberate `[subject_id, time_column]` rule (roadmap/EP-9 brief line 59, transcribe.py:365) and strike the tie-break columns from EP-20/23/24/25/26/27. Recommended: adopt tie-breaks (deterministic Parquet, itemid row-group pruning), since Table validators (contract.py:200-207, 224-225) already accept them.
(2) microbiologyevents load_class: raw inventory (docs/resources/raw-inventory.md line 80) shows the CSV is 909,240,343 bytes, i.e. < 1 GB, so EP-25's rule says `small` while EP-9 brief lines 60-61 and the shipped YAML (mimiciv_hosp.yaml:223) say `large`. Pick one and record it in DECISIONS addenda.
Then: edit mimiciv_hosp.yaml / mimiciv_icu.yaml once with a dated header note; run `uv run --group dev mwh fixtures build` and commit the regenerated tests/fixtures once (manifest.json contract_hash + any CSVs whose tie order changed); amend DESIGN §5 line 177 ("sorted `(subject_id, <time column>)`") to "sorted by the contract `sort_keys`"; add `> **EP-16 pickup note**` blocks to EP-20/23/24/25/26/27: "contract already carries the final sort_keys/load_class; do not edit". Batch into the same hotfix the other planned contract edits (EP-23/EP-25 `free_text: true` flags — note `free_text` is not a Column field today, extra="forbid" at contract.py:94, so it needs a model change too), because every YAML edit changes `content_hash()` and forces a fixture regeneration regardless of sort keys.

**Verifier notes.** Checked mimicwarehouse/src/mimicwarehouse/schema/tables/mimiciv_hosp.yaml (sort_keys/load_class at lines 36-407) and mimiciv_icu.yaml (lines 19-169): every contract value quoted in the finding is accurate (transfers [subject_id,intime]; hcpcsevents [subject_id,chartdate]; labevents/emar/chartevents/datetimeevents/outputevents [subject_id,charttime]; pharmacy/prescriptions/inputevents/ingredientevents/procedureevents [subject_id,starttime]; poe [subject_id,ordertime]; emar_detail [subject_id,emar_id,parent_field_ordinal]; microbiologyevents large [subject_id,chartdate,charttime]). Checked briefs: EP-20 lines 22-33, EP-23 26-28, EP-24 25-27, EP-25 23-28, EP-26 29-30, EP-27 24-27 expect the tie-break forms quoted; EP-25 says microbiologyevents small vs EP-9 brief lines 60-61 + contract large; raw-inventory.md line 80 gives microbiologyevents.csv = 909 MB (< 1 GB) so EP-25's own rule yields small -> genuine inter-brief conflict needing an owner decision. EP-16 item 6 (lines 73-74) indeed lists only EP-17..EP-22. DESIGN §5 line 177 says sorted (subject_id, <time column>); EP-9 brief line 59 makes [subject_id,time_column] the deliberate rule; transcribe.py:365 defaults the same. Impact mechanics verified: Contract.content_hash() (contract.py:559-563) hashes the full model dump; fixtures/write.py:361 stamps it into manifest.json; tests/ep/test_ep11.py:624 and test_ep12.py:566 assert manifest contract_hash == load_contract().content_hash() and byte-compare committed CSVs/manifest/README, so any YAML edit forces `mwh fixtures build` + a fixture commit; fixtures/check.py:187-191 asserts sortedness by sort_keys and hosp.py:116-117 sorts by them, so tie-break keys reorder tied rows. Corrections: (a) severity over-rated - no correctness failure, the loader (EP-17/18) simply reads whatever the contract says; the cost is churn plus inconsistent per-session judgment (EP-20 has an explicit "not deliberate" escape hatch, EP-23..27 do not); (b) "31 CSVs rewritten up to six times" is overstated - each table's CSV changes at most once (when its own keys change), only manifest.json/README churn each time; (c) settling sort keys alone does not remove per-brief regeneration because EP-23/EP-25 also plan `free_text` contract flags (and `free_text` is not a Column field, extra="forbid" at contract.py:94) - batch them; (d) procedureevents/outputevents small and datetimeevents large agree between contract and briefs (no issue there). No files modified; only read-only greps/sed.

**Owner question (as posed by the reviewer).** Adopt tie-break sort keys (deterministic Parquet, better row-group pruning on itemid; recommended) or keep DESIGN §5's `[subject_id, time]` (no fixture churn)? And is microbiologyevents small (0.9 GB, EP-25 rule) or large (contract)?

### FC-4 — tests/fixtures/manifest.json pins the contract hash — every contract YAML edit in P2/P3 forces a fixture regeneration commit

**Severity (verified):** medium · **Verdict:** confirmed-with-corrections (high confidence) · **Kind:** forward-compat · **When:** now-should · **Effort:** XS · **Retro episode:** EP-169 · **Lens:** forward-compat: pending briefs (EP-13..EP-56) vs shipped P0/P1 code

**Files:** `mimicwarehouse/tests/fixtures/manifest.json:43`, `mimicwarehouse/tests/ep/test_ep11.py:624`, `mimicwarehouse/tests/ep/test_ep12.py:566`, `mimicwarehouse/src/mimicwarehouse/schema/contract.py:559-563`, `roadmap/EP-23-stage-labevents.md:29-31`, `roadmap/EP-29-catalog-data-dictionary.md:26-31`

**Evidence.** manifest.json: `"contract_hash": "e4cd5aa908d1…"`; test_ep11/test_ep12 `assert manifest["contract_hash"] == contract.content_hash()`; `content_hash()` = sha256 of the full model dump (comments, flags, sort_keys, everything). EP-23 adds `free_text: true`, EP-24/25/27 add identifier flags, EP-29 adds descriptions — none mention re-running `mwh fixtures build`.

**Impact.** The first contract edit in EP-17/20/23 turns test_ep11/test_ep12 red with an opaque hash mismatch; sessions will either regenerate fixtures ad hoc (31 CSVs + manifest churn in unrelated commits) or be tempted to weaken the assertion.

**Proposed fix (reviewer).** Add a standing rule to EP-16's pickup notes for EP-17..EP-29: 'any contract change ⇒ `uv run --group dev mwh fixtures build` in the same commit; CSV bytes must stay identical unless sort_keys changed'. Optionally make `content_hash()` exclude documentation-only fields (comment/description/flags) so only structural edits re-hash — a D-27 addendum decision.

**Corrected fix (verifier).** Keep `content_hash()` as-is (it is the provenance id GOVERNANCE §12 / EP-35 / EP-51 rely on). Add a standing rule in three places: (a) EP-16 pickup notes for EP-17..EP-29 and any brief that says "fix it in EP-9's YAML": "any edit under `src/mimicwarehouse/schema/tables/*.yaml` or to the contract model ⇒ run `uv run --group dev mwh fixtures build` and commit `tests/fixtures/manifest.json` in the same commit; only manifest.json's `contract_hash` line should change unless column order/types/sort_keys changed (then all 31 CSVs + README legitimately change and `GENERATOR_VERSION` may need a bump); also re-run `mwh inventory reconcile` so docs/resources/raw-inventory.md:11's contract hash follows"; (b) one sentence in mimicwarehouse/tests/fixtures/README.md after line 27; (c) a dated line in DESIGN.md's EP-11 note (line ~155). Owner question (optional): should `content_hash()` exclude documentation-only fields (comment/description/flags) so doc edits do not re-hash? Options: keep full hash (default; stable provenance, one-line manifest churn per contract edit) vs. exclude doc fields (less churn; two differently-documented contracts share an id — needs a D-27/GOVERNANCE §12 addendum). Effort XS.

**Verifier notes.** Checked: mimicwarehouse/tests/fixtures/manifest.json:43 pins `contract_hash` e4cd5aa9…; tests/ep/test_ep11.py:624 and tests/ep/test_ep12.py:566 both `assert manifest["contract_hash"] == contract.content_hash()` and additionally assert the regenerated manifest.json bytes equal the committed file (so a hash change breaks two assertions in each test); src/mimicwarehouse/schema/contract.py:559-563 hashes the full `model_dump(mode="json", by_alias=True)`; `Column` (contract.py:98-109) carries `comment` and has `extra="forbid"` (line 94), so EP-23/EP-29's `free_text`/`identifier`/`description` fields require model additions and YAML edits, both of which re-hash. Empirically verified with `uv run --group dev python -c`: current hash matches the manifest, and a comment-only edit on one column changes `content_hash()`. Fixture CSV bytes depend only on contract column order/types/sort_keys (fixtures/hosp.py:107,116; icu.py:6; write.py:16), so a doc-only edit changes only manifest.json (and README.md is unaffected: no hash there). Searched roadmap/EP-16..EP-35 briefs, tests/README.md, tests/fixtures/README.md, DESIGN.md: none says "re-run `mwh fixtures build` after a contract edit"; EP-23 items 1-2 and EP-29 item 1 edit the contract without mentioning fixtures; EP-16's acceptance only says pre-commit must not modify tests/fixtures/. Corrections: (1) another committed pin exists — docs/resources/raw-inventory.md:11 embeds the same hash (from EP-10's `raw_snapshot.json`; inventory.py:617/770/1008) — it is not test-asserted (test_ep10:412 compares a fresh snapshot only) but will silently go stale on the first contract edit and EP-16/EP-28 `mwh inventory reconcile` regenerates it; add it to the same rule. (2) The proposed `content_hash()` exclusion of doc-only fields conflicts with GOVERNANCE §12 / DESIGN.md:568-609 / EP-35, EP-51 which use the hash as the run-manifest provenance identity; a description-excluded hash would let two differently-documented contracts share an id — offer this only as an owner option with that trade-off stated, default to keeping the full-content hash and adopting the "rebuild fixtures in the same commit" rule. Impact stands: first contract edit (EP-17 item 5 / EP-23 / EP-29) turns test_ep11+test_ep12 red with a manifest-bytes/hash mismatch. Effort XS is right; when = should-fix-now (a pickup note in EP-16 plus one sentence in tests/fixtures/README.md and DESIGN.md's EP-11 note).

### FC-5 — Identifier list and free_text/identifier column flags assumed by EP-23..EP-30 do not exist (Column is extra=forbid; keys.yaml has no identifiers section)

**Severity (verified):** medium · **Verdict:** confirmed (high confidence) · **Kind:** forward-compat · **When:** now-should · **Effort:** S · **Retro episode:** EP-170 · **Lens:** forward-compat: pending briefs (EP-13..EP-56) vs shipped P0/P1 code

**Files:** `mimicwarehouse/src/mimicwarehouse/schema/contract.py:92-131`, `mimicwarehouse/src/mimicwarehouse/schema/tables/keys.yaml:16-20`, `roadmap/EP-23-stage-labevents.md:29-31`, `roadmap/EP-24-stage-emar.md:30-32`, `roadmap/EP-25-stage-hosp-rest.md:30-32`, `roadmap/EP-27-stage-icu-events.md:30-32`, `roadmap/EP-29-catalog-data-dictionary.md:26-31`, `roadmap/EP-30-safe-query-audit.md:14-16`

**Evidence.** Column model fields: name, type, nullable, comment, unit_of, upstream_type, upstream_nullable with `model_config = ConfigDict(frozen=True, extra="forbid", …)` — a YAML `free_text: true` raises. keys.yaml top-level keys: version_note, primary_keys, uniqueness_hints, foreign_keys (loader uses `.get`, no identifiers). EP-23: "mark `labevents.comments` `free_text: true` and confirm `order_provider_id` is in `keys.yaml`'s identifier list"; EP-24: "add `emar_id` … to the identifier list in `keys.yaml`"; EP-29: "ensure `identifier: true` / `free_text: true` flags exist per column (identifier set = `keys.yaml` list …)"; EP-30 relies on "identifier and free-text flags in the schema contract".

**Impact.** Five briefs each 'add to a list' that has no schema or loader support; the first (EP-23) must design it, and EP-30's governance tests depend on the shape. Risk of two competing representations (per-column flag vs keys.yaml list).

**Proposed fix (reviewer).** Decide the shape once (EP-16 pickup note or EP-17): add `identifier: bool = False` and `free_text: bool = False` to `Column`, an `identifiers:` section in keys.yaml that the loader stamps onto columns (like units.yaml stamps `unit_of`), expose `Table.identifier_columns()/free_text_columns()`; regenerate the fixture manifest (FC-4). Name EP-17 as the owner and turn EP-23..27/29 items into 'verify the flag is set'.

**Verifier notes.** Checked: (1) mimicwarehouse/src/mimicwarehouse/schema/contract.py lines 92-131 — `_Frozen` has `extra="forbid"`; `Column` fields are exactly name/type/nullable/comment/unit_of/upstream_type/upstream_nullable; no identifier or free_text field. A YAML `free_text: true` would raise a pydantic extra-forbidden error at load. (2) keys.yaml top-level keys (grep `^[a-z_]*:`) = version_note, primary_keys, uniqueness_hints, foreign_keys only; loader at contract.py:606-656 reads only those via `.get`; no `identifiers` section, and the only stamping loop is unit_of from units.yaml (contract.py:664-673). (3) `grep -rn identifier|free_text` over schema/*.py and tables/*.yaml hits only column comments — no flag anywhere in the package (guard.py mentions identifiers only in a docstring). (4) Briefs quoted verbatim: EP-23:29-31 ("mark labevents.comments free_text: true and confirm order_provider_id is in keys.yaml's identifier list"), EP-24:30-32 + acceptance line 60 ("keys.yaml lists emar_id as an identifier (checked by a test)"), EP-25:29-31, EP-27:31, EP-29:28-31 ("ensure identifier: true / free_text: true flags exist per column (identifier set = keys.yaml list ...)"), EP-30:14-15 which attributes the flags to "(EP-9/EP-23/EP-24/EP-25/EP-29)" — but EP-9's brief (In scope item 1: `Column(name, duckdb_type, nullable, comment, unit_of)`) never included them, and DESIGN §7/§12 mentions no such flags either. So the flag is assumed to exist by six briefs and owned by none. (5) Corroborating detail: EP-16 item 6 (EP-16-replan-p1.md:73-75) only says "read EP-17…EP-22 (and skim EP-28/29/30)" for pickup notes — EP-23..27 are outside its scan list, so the existing pickup mechanism may not catch this unless the owner names it explicitly. (6) The fix's "regenerate the fixture manifest" part is necessary, not optional: tests/fixtures/manifest.json:43 stores `contract_hash` and tests/ep/test_ep12.py:566 / test_ep11.py:624 / test_ep10.py:412 assert `manifest["contract_hash"] == load_contract().content_hash()`; `content_hash()` (contract.py:559-563) is a sha256 of `model_dump(mode="json")`, so adding two Column fields with defaults changes the hash and turns those tests red until the fixture manifest is regenerated. Severity medium and effort S are appropriate; the proposed fix (two bool fields on Column, an `identifiers:` section in keys.yaml stamped like units.yaml, Table.identifier_columns()/free_text_columns(), regenerate manifest, name EP-17 or the EP-16 pickup note as owner and demote EP-23..27/29 items to "verify flag is set") is the right shape. Minor addition to the fix: EP-30's item (e) also uses a name-pattern heuristic, so EP-30 would not hard-fail without the flag, but EP-24's acceptance test and EP-29's meta.columns `is_identifier/is_free_text` columns do require it — the decision point (per-column flag vs keys.yaml list, or both with stamping) should be recorded as a DECISIONS.md addendum when made.

### FC-6 — `os.replace(dest.new → dest)` for directories fails on Windows when dest exists — EP-17/18/22 swap recipe breaks on --force / dev→full restage

**Severity (verified):** medium · **Verdict:** confirmed-with-corrections (high confidence) · **Kind:** bug · **When:** now-should · **Effort:** XS · **Retro episode:** EP-170 · **Lens:** forward-compat: pending briefs (EP-13..EP-56) vs shipped P0/P1 code

**Files:** `roadmap/EP-17-stage-loader-core.md:52-56`, `roadmap/EP-18-stage-loader-buckets.md:26-32`, `roadmap/EP-18-stage-loader-buckets.md:46-52`, `roadmap/EP-22-stage-demo-tier.md:37-44`

**Evidence.** Verified in the scratchpad with the project interpreter: `os.replace('dest.new','dest')` with an existing non-empty `dest` → `PermissionError [WinError 5] Access is denied`. EP-17 item 3: "swaps `dest_dir.new` → `dest_dir` atomically (`os.replace`; a stale `.new` is deleted first)"; EP-18 item 3: "A later **full** request on a table whose status is `tier_complete = "dev"` restages the whole table"; EP-19 `--force` reruns steps.

**Impact.** Every rerun into an existing table directory (`--force`, dev→full restage, EP-22 demo rebuild, EP-38 patched concepts) raises after a full COPY; sessions will 'fix' it inconsistently.

**Proposed fix (reviewer).** Specify the directory swap as: rename `dest` → `dest.old` (os.replace works when the target does not exist), rename `.new` → `dest`, `shutil.rmtree(dest.old)`; on startup delete stale `.old`/`.new`. Put it in one helper in `loader/paths.py` and cite it from EP-17/18/22/37.

**Corrected fix (verifier).** Add one helper `swap_dir(new: Path, dest: Path) -> None` in src/mimicwarehouse/loader/paths.py (EP-17 creates it, EP-18 extends): 1) if `dest.old` exists and `dest` does not → restore (`os.replace(dest.old, dest)`), else rmtree stale `dest.old`; 2) if `dest` exists → `os.replace(dest, dest.old)`; 3) `os.replace(new, dest)`; 4) `shutil.rmtree(dest.old)` with a short PermissionError retry loop (reuse the pattern of inventory.py `_atomic_write_text`). Document it as crash-safe (not atomic) and add a fixture test that stages the same table twice into the same dest and one that simulates a crash after step 2. Then edit the briefs: EP-17-stage-loader-core.md:54-55 (replace "atomically (`os.replace`; a stale `.new` is deleted first)" with "via `paths.swap_dir` (crash-safe; handles stale `.new`/`.old` and an existing `dest` — needed for `--force`, dev→full restage and demo rebuilds)"); EP-18-stage-loader-buckets.md:31-32 and 37-38 ("swap via `paths.swap_dir`"); EP-19-stage-dag-runner.md:79-84 (note the dev-then-full patients smoke test exercises the existing-dest path); EP-37-concept-runner.md:39-41 (concept sinks use the same helper). EP-22 needs no text change beyond a pointer if desired. Effort S. When: now-should (before EP-17 starts).

**Verifier notes.** Checked: (1) Re-ran the experiment with the project interpreter (uv run python, CPython 3.13.15, scratchpad script fc6_check.py): os.replace('dest.new','dest') with an existing NON-EMPTY dir → PermissionError [WinError 5]; with an existing EMPTY dir → same PermissionError; os.rename → FileExistsError [WinError 183]; the .old dance (replace dest→dest.old, replace .new→dest, rmtree .old) succeeds. So the technical claim holds and is even broader than stated (empty dest fails too). (2) roadmap/EP-17-stage-loader-core.md:51-55 literally says "swaps `dest_dir.new` → `dest_dir` atomically (`os.replace`; a stale `.new` is deleted first)" — only the stale .new case is handled, not an existing dest. (3) roadmap/EP-18-stage-loader-buckets.md:31-32, 37-38 repeat "swap `.new` → `dest`" for both partitioned paths, and item 3 (lines 50-52) mandates "A later **full** request on a table whose status is `tier_complete = "dev"` restages the whole table" — that is a rerun into an existing dest. (4) The most concrete tripwire is roadmap/EP-19-stage-dag-runner.md:79-84: the brief's own tier("dev") test stages patients with --tier dev into lake/core/mimiciv_hosp/patients/subject_bucket=0…4/ and THEN --tier full on the same table — with the brief-as-written recipe that second run raises WinError 5 after the COPY finishes; EP-19 line 79 also specifies "--force reruns". (5) No shipped code implements a directory swap yet (grep os.replace|rmtree over src: only inventory.py:503-515 `_atomic_write_text`, a FILE swap with a PermissionError retry loop, and concepts/vendoring.py:636 rmtree) — so this is purely a pending-brief defect, no P0/P1 regression. Corrections to the finding: (a) roadmap/EP-22-stage-demo-tier.md:37-44 does not mention .new/os.replace at all — it is only an implicit consumer (a demo rebuild reruns every stage step); cite it as downstream, not as a location of the stale text. (b) EP-38 "patched concepts" is likewise indirect: EP-37 (roadmap/EP-37-concept-runner.md:39-41) sinks concepts to Parquet dirs under lake/derived/<tier>/concepts/<concept>/ (partitioned dirs for the >5 M-row concepts), so re-running a patched concept hits the same problem only if EP-37 reuses the stage swap — worth citing EP-37 rather than EP-38. (c) EP-21's `<tier>.duckdb.new` swap is a file replace and already documents the Windows reader-lock case; not affected. (d) The word "atomically" is wrong on Windows for directories under any recipe: the .old dance has a window where dest is absent, so the helper must also define startup recovery (dest missing + dest.old present → restore .old; stale .new/.old otherwise deleted) and the brief should drop "atomically" for "crash-safe". (e) shutil.rmtree of .old should get the same PermissionError retry as inventory.py `_atomic_write_text` (Defender/Malwarebytes briefly lock freshly written files, D-42/Risk). Effort is closer to S than XS once the helper, its recovery cases, a fixture test (stage twice into the same dest; crash-between-renames recovery) and the four brief edits are counted.

### FXT-1 — All fixture id spaces share the 90_000_000 floor, so wrong-key joins still match

**Severity (verified):** medium · **Verdict:** confirmed-with-corrections (high confidence) · **Kind:** design · **When:** owner-decision · **Effort:** S · **Retro episode:** EP-169 · **Lens:** fixture-generator (EP-11/12 synthetic fixture as the test substrate for every later brief)

**Files:** `mimicwarehouse/src/mimicwarehouse/fixtures/spec.py:76`, `mimicwarehouse/src/mimicwarehouse/fixtures/spec.py:79`, `mimicwarehouse/src/mimicwarehouse/fixtures/icu.py:266`, `mimicwarehouse/src/mimicwarehouse/fixtures/hosp.py:916`

**Evidence.** spec.py: `first_subject_id`, `first_hadm_id`, `first_stay_id`, `first_event_id` all default to FIXTURE_ID_FLOOR = 90_000_000; icu.py `caregivers` = range(first_event_id, ...); hosp.transfers `next_id = spec.first_event_id`. Probe over build_frames(): subject_id ∩ hadm_id = 120 (all), subject ∩ stay = 75, hadm ∩ stay = 75, subject ∩ caregiver_id = 15, labevent_id ∩ subject = 120, transfer_id ∩ hadm = 186, inputevents.orderid ∩ stay = 75.

**Impact.** Real MIMIC keeps subject/hadm/stay in disjoint bands precisely so a join on the wrong key yields nothing. On the fixture, `chartevents.hadm_id = icustays.stay_id`, `caregiver_id = subject_id` or `transfer_id = hadm_id` mistakes in concept SQL (EP-37/38), phenotype SQL (EP-41/42), the timeline API (EP-49), the events spine (EP-50) or loader tests (EP-17/18) produce plausible non-empty results and pass; the bug surfaces only on dev/full.

**Proposed fix (reviewer).** Give each id space its own floor in FixtureSpec defaults (e.g. subject 90_000_000, hadm 91_000_000, stay 92_000_000, event ids 93_000_000+, caregiver 90_900_000; all still ≥ FIXTURE_ID_FLOOR and never 8-digit-starting-1/2/3), keep `check.ID_COLUMNS ≥ FIXTURE_ID_FLOOR`, regenerate once (bundle with FXT-6/7/9/10), bump GENERATOR_VERSION. Update the two hardcoded 90 000 000 mentions in tests/fixtures/README.md and DESIGN §4.

**Corrected fix (verifier).** Give subject / hadm / stay (and caregiver) distinct default floors in FixtureSpec (spec.py:74-82, e.g. first_subject_id 90_000_000, first_hadm_id 91_000_000, first_stay_id 92_000_000, first_event_id 93_000_000; caregiver ids could branch off first_event_id + an offset or get their own knob) - all still >= FIXTURE_ID_FLOOR, none an 8-digit number starting 1/2/3, so guard G4 and check.ID_COLUMNS stay unchanged. Event-row ids (labevent_id, specimen_id, transfer_id, pharmacy_id, orderid...) can stay in one shared band: real MIMIC event ids are small integers that already overlap the real subject band, so only subject/hadm/stay (which MIMIC keeps disjoint by design) and caregiver_id need discriminating floors. Regenerate once (bundle with the other FXT regen items), bump GENERATOR_VERSION (write.py:45, "0.1.0"), and update: tests/ep/test_ep11.py:173 (asserts first_subject_id == first_hadm_id == first_stay_id == FIXTURE_ID_FLOOR - must be rewritten, otherwise the test fails), DESIGN.md:640 (lists the four defaults), roadmap/EP-11-fixtures-hosp.md:43 (brief In-scope text mandates all three at 90_000_000 - add a completion-note addendum rather than rewriting), EP-11 completion note lines 137-138/167, and tests/fixtures/README.md line 6 (auto-rendered from spec.first_subject_id, so it regenerates itself). Add a DECISIONS addendum under D-27 recording the band layout. Effort S (~30-45 min incl. regen and test edit).

**Verifier notes.** Checked: src/mimicwarehouse/fixtures/spec.py:41-82 (FIXTURE_ID_FLOOR = 90_000_000; first_subject_id/first_hadm_id/first_stay_id/first_event_id all default to it), spec.py:498-501 (hadm/stay counters start at those floors), icu.py:266/784/1064 and hosp.py:413/647/774/916 (caregiver, orderid, pharmacy, labevent/specimen, microevent, transfer ids all start at first_event_id), check.py:38-54 (ID_COLUMNS only enforces >= floor, no disjointness). Ran a scratch probe over build_frames() (in-memory synthetic frames, aggregates only): subject n=120 [90000000..90000119], hadm n=186 [90000000..90000185], stay n=75 [90000000..90000074]; subject&hadm=120, subject&stay=75, hadm&stay=75; caregiver_id (15) fully inside the subject set; transfer_id&hadm=186; labevent_id&subject=120; inputevents.orderid&stay=75; and the concrete wrong-key join chartevents.hadm_id IN icustays.stay_id matches 9325 of 20125 rows (right key: 20125). So the evidence in the finding is exactly reproduced. Corrections: (1) the shared floor is not a session quick-fix - roadmap/EP-11-fixtures-hosp.md:43 explicitly specifies first_subject_id=first_hadm_id=first_stay_id=90_000_000, so this is a planning-level design choice and genuinely an owner decision; (2) test_ep11.py:173 hard-asserts the equality and is not listed in the finding's fix; (3) the "real MIMIC keeps disjoint bands" argument holds for subject/hadm/stay only - real labevent_id/transfer_id etc. are small integers that overlap the real subject band, so distinct floors for event ids are optional; (4) mitigation not stated in the finding: nearly every downstream brief (EP-17, 37, 38, 49, 50) requires fixture AND dev green in its DoD, so a wrong-key join would fail on dev within the same EP if the test asserts non-empty/count results - the risk is false-green fixture iteration and dev tests that only check "runs", not a bug that reaches full unnoticed by construction. Severity medium is defensible (cheap fix, best before EP-17 depends on fixture counts/hashes) but is on the low side of medium given the dev-tier safety net; the D-27 wording (ids >= 90 000 000) does not preclude distinct floors. No repository files were modified; the probe script lives only in the scratchpad.

**Owner question (as posed by the reviewer).** Regenerate the fixture now (before EP-17) with disjoint id bands, or accept the shared floor and rely on dev-tier tests to catch wrong-key joins? Options: (a) distinct floors now, one regen + version bump 0.2.0 [recommended: cheapest before any EP depends on counts]; (b) keep as is, add a DESIGN caveat that fixture joins are not key-discriminating.

### FXT-10 — EP-19's fixture-tier build has no segregated lake root; catalog_path('fixture') points into the real data root

**Severity (verified):** medium · **Verdict:** confirmed-with-corrections (high confidence) · **Kind:** design · **When:** owner-decision · **Effort:** S · **Retro episode:** EP-167 · **Lens:** fixture-generator (EP-11/12 synthetic fixture as the test substrate for every later brief)

**Files:** `mimicwarehouse/src/mimicwarehouse/config.py:622`, `mimicwarehouse/DESIGN.md:106`, `roadmap/EP-19-stage-dag-runner.md:43`, `roadmap/EP-20-stage-small-tables.md:43`

**Evidence.** config.catalog_path → `<data_root>/warehouse/<tier>.duckdb` (probe: `...\warehouse\fixture.duckdb`); DESIGN §3 layout lists `warehouse\ {demo,dev,full}.duckdb` and one `lake\core` (dev is a bucket filter over the same lake). EP-19 resolves `fixture → tests/fixtures/` as raw root but says nothing about a fixture lake root; only EP-20/21 add `--data-root <tmp>` by convention.

**Impact.** `uv run mwh build --tier fixture` without `--data-root` (a likely reflex in EP-19..22 sessions) writes synthetic Parquet into `C:\mimicdata\lake\core\<schema>\<table>\`, overwriting or interleaving with real dev/full partitions and corrupting status.json/snapshot ids.

**Proposed fix (reviewer).** Decide before EP-19: (a) Settings gains a `fixture_root` (default `<workspace>/.mwh-fixture/` or the pytest tmp root) and `catalog_path('fixture')`/lake_root resolve there; or (b) `mwh build --tier fixture` refuses to run without an explicit `--data-root` that is not the configured data root. Note it in EP-19's amendment block and DESIGN §3.

**Corrected fix (verifier).** Decide before EP-19 (owner decision) and record it in EP-19's amendment block, DESIGN §3/§4 and config.py. Options: (a) [recommended] give the fixture tier a first-class, persistent home: either a `Settings.fixture_root` (default `<workspace>/.mwh-fixture/` — already covered by the root .gitignore rules `*.parquet`, `*.duckdb`, `lake/`) or, symmetric with EP-22's demo tier, `<data_root>/lake/fixture/` + `<data_root>/warehouse/fixture.duckdb`; make the runner resolve `lake_root` per tier (`fixture` → that root, `demo` → `lake/demo`, `dev`/`full` → `lake/core`) so `mwh build --tier fixture` can never target `lake/core` even without `--data-root`; (b) additionally/alternatively, `mwh build --tier fixture` refuses to run when the resolved lake root equals the configured `lake_core`. Whatever is chosen: (1) EP-17's `status.json` `tier_complete: null|dev|full` enum needs a `fixture`/`demo` value (or per-root status files) — EP-21 line 32 already assumes fixture/demo are 'complete on their own lake roots' but nothing defines it; (2) EP-21's 'fixture under a temp root' / session-scoped `--data-root <tmp>` conftest design must be reconciled with EP-139:58 (`mwh link validate … --tier fixture`) and EP-141:57 (`mwh sql … --tier fixture`), which resolve the catalog by tier name alone and therefore need a persistent `catalog_path('fixture')` — today that resolves to `C:\mimicdata\warehouse\fixture.duckdb`, which the temp-root convention never creates; (3) EP-19's fixture-tier smoke and DESIGN §3's `warehouse\ {demo,dev,full}.duckdb` line should list the fixture catalog location. Effort S (config field + per-tier lake-root resolution + doc notes) — but it must land in/before EP-19, not later.

**Verifier notes.** Checked: mimicwarehouse/src/mimicwarehouse/config.py:67 (`Tier` Literal includes "fixture") and :622-624 (`catalog_path` = `layout["warehouse"] / f"{tier}.duckdb"` for every tier; `layout` :598-620 has one `lake_core`, no fixture key) — evidence holds. DESIGN.md:99-105 layout lists `warehouse\ {demo,dev,full}.duckdb` and a single `lake\core`; DESIGN §4 fixture row says the fixture is committed CSVs in `tests/fixtures/` (repo) with no lake/catalog location; DESIGN:167-170 says EP-21 'builds a real fixture.duckdb' without saying where. roadmap/EP-19-stage-dag-runner.md:43-46 resolves only the fixture *raw* root (`tests/fixtures/`), the StepContext carries a `lake_root` with no per-tier rule; EP-22-stage-demo-tier.md:36-38 explicitly gives demo its own `lake\demo\` (separate lake), so fixture is the odd tier out; EP-21-stage-catalog-builder.md:8 ('fixture under a temp root'), :32-33 ('fixture and demo require complete on their own lake roots'), :60-66 (session temp root via `--data-root <tmp>`); EP-20:43 likewise. EP-17:57-63 defines `status.json tier_complete: null|dev|full` (no fixture value); EP-17 §3 and EP-18 §1 swap `<dest>.new → dest` per table dir, so a fixture build aimed at the real `lake/core` REPLACES a staged real table wholesale (not 'interleaves'), and EP-19's skip rule ('complete for that tier') would not protect it because the real status says `full`, not `fixture`. Also EP-139:58 and EP-141:57 invoke CLI commands with `--tier fixture` and no `--data-root`, requiring a persistent fixture catalog that the temp-root convention never produces — an extra downstream trip the finding missed. Corrections: impact is loss/replacement of staged real tables + status/snapshot pollution (recoverable by restaging, no license exposure); GOVERNANCE.md:36-41 does not actually mandate a fixture root outside the data root (the location table is silent), so 'matches GOVERNANCE' is a hygiene argument, not a license one; a repo-local root is already gitignored (root .gitignore: `*.parquet`, `*.duckdb`, `lake/`). No `mwh build` exists yet (cli.py:9 lists it as EP-19), so nothing is broken today — the finding is a genuine pre-EP-19 design gap; medium + owner-decision is the right rating.

**Owner question (as posed by the reviewer).** Where does the fixture-tier lake/catalog live? Options: (a) a dedicated fixture root outside the data root (recommended: keeps synthetic and real bytes physically apart, matches GOVERNANCE); (b) `<data_root>/fixture/` subtree; (c) refuse fixture builds without `--data-root`.

**Severity (verified):** medium · **Verdict:** confirmed-with-corrections (high confidence) · **Kind:** forward-compat · **When:** owner-decision · **Effort:** S · **Retro episode:** EP-168 · **Lens:** fixture-generator (EP-11/12 synthetic fixture as the test substrate for every later brief)
