EP-23..27 briefs each declare `tiers: [fixture, dev, full, demo]` (grep lines 26/24/21/29/22), so fixture-tier staging of the large tables is planned but only lands after EP-21 (roadmap/README.md lines 83-89: EP-21 precedes EP-23..27; EP-28 depends on EP-20..27). mimicwarehouse/tests/conftest.py lines 197-206: `fixture_catalog` = in-memory `build_fixture_catalog` over all 31 CSVs. mimicwarehouse/tests/ep/test_ep12.py: line 572 test iterates every `mimiciv_icu` contract table (9, incl. chartevents/inputevents/ingredientevents/datetimeevents) through `fixture_catalog`; line 605 test queries mimiciv_icu.chartevents/inputevents/procedureevents (via ICU_EVENT_TIMES) plus mimiciv_hosp.microbiologyevents and prescriptions; line 722 uses it too. grep shows `fixture_catalog` is currently used only in test_ep12 (three tests) — blast radius today is those tests plus whatever EP-13..20 add. src/mimicwarehouse/fixtures/catalog.py docstring lines 9-11 and DESIGN.md line 167-169 also state the in-memory catalog is the fixture tier "until EP-21 builds a real fixture.duckdb from the same CSVs" — reinforcing that EP-21 as written expects a 31-table lake it cannot have. Verdict: the contradiction inside EP-21 (omit unstaged tables + 20-table build + 31-table green promise) is real and would force mid-EP improvisation; severity medium and effort S stand; the proposed option (a) needs correcting because it collides with EP-20's coverage assertion and EP-23..27's step ownership, and the finding under-counts the affected tests.

**Owner question (as posed by the reviewer).** Should the fixture tier stage all 31 tables from EP-20 (option a, recommended: one code path, catalog tests exercise the real loader) or should the in-memory catalog remain the test substrate until EP-27 (option b)?

### FXT-4 — Several vendored concepts are empty or degraded on the fixture and this is nowhere documented

**Severity (verified):** medium · **Verdict:** confirmed-with-corrections (high confidence) · **Kind:** test · **When:** next-replan · **Effort:** M · **Retro episode:** EP-169 · **Lens:** fixture-generator (EP-11/12 synthetic fixture as the test substrate for every later brief)

**Files:** `mimicwarehouse/src/mimicwarehouse/fixtures/vocab/d_labitems.yaml:33`, `mimicwarehouse/src/mimicwarehouse/fixtures/vocab/d_items.yaml:60`, `mimicwarehouse/src/mimicwarehouse/concepts/vendor/mimic-code/mimic-iv/concepts_duckdb/measurement/bg.sql:10`, `mimicwarehouse/src/mimicwarehouse/concepts/vendor/mimic-code/mimic-iv/concepts_duckdb/organfailure/kdigo_stages.sql:71`, `roadmap/EP-37-concept-runner.md:76`

(2) In the bundled regen: add 52033 'Specimen Type' (value 'ART.') to the `bg` panel, add the CRRT chartevents settings itemids (224144/224145/224149/224150/224151/224152/224153/224154/225183 or the minimum crrt.sql needs) inside the planted CRRT windows, and optionally 220621/226537 (glucose) and 223834 (O2 flow).

**Corrected fix (verifier).** (1) Now, docs-only (S, ~20 min): add a "Concept coverage on the fixture" section to mimicwarehouse/tests/fixtures/README.md (or a fixtures/COVERAGE.md the README links) listing (a) expected-EMPTY derived tables: icp, rhythm, dobutamine, milrinone, neuroblock, code_status, crrt, bg_art-dependent tables (first_day_bg_art; and the PaO2/FiO2 branch of sofa / first_day_sofa / apsiii / sapsii / lods is NULL/0 because labevents has no itemid 52033 'Specimen Type' so bg.specimen is never 'ART.'); (b) expected-PARTIAL: invasive_line (1/24 itemids), urine_output (2/12), ventilator_setting (4/15), vitalsign (12/19), bg (8/27), enzyme (5/11), blood_differential (3/23), oxygen_delivery (1/4); (c) kdigo_stages: the planted AKI+CRRT story never yields aki_stage 3 through the CRRT route (crrt.sql reads only chartevents itemids and kdigo_stages takes stage 3 only from rows with crrt_mode = itemid 227290 NOT NULL; the fixture plants CRRT solely as procedureevents 225802) and the planted creatinine 0.9 -> 2.1 -> 2.6 stays under both the 3x and >=4.0 stage-3 thresholds, so stage 3 is reachable only stochastically via the urine-output rule. Also amend the EP-12 completion note (roadmap/EP-12-fixtures-icu-markers.md line ~117), which lists `crrt` / `rrt` among the concepts whose itemid lookups were consulted, with a one-line FYI that crrt.sql itself has zero fixture hits. Then EP-37 item 6 can assert "every concept table non-empty except <documented list>" instead of only "sofa / sepsis3 succeed". (2) At the next re-plan / bundled regen (M): add d_labitems 52033 'Specimen Type' (fluid Blood, category Blood Gas, text value 'ART.', no valuenum) to the `bg` panel; add chartevents 227290 'CRRT mode' (text, e.g. 'CVVHDF' -- this is the ONE item kdigo_stages needs; the pressure/flow items 224144/224145/224149-224154/225183 are optional realism) written 4-hourly inside each planted CRRT window with linksto chartevents; optionally 220621/226537 (glucose) and 223834 (O2 flow). Corrections to the finding: (a) oasis does not read bg/PaO2-FiO2 (grep of score/oasis.sql) -- replace with lods / first_day_sofa; (b) the fix's CRRT list omits the only itemid that matters for kdigo stage 3 (227290 crrt_mode) -- adding 224144.. alone would still leave aki_stage_crrt NULL; (c) impact is over-stated for pending acceptance: EP-37 item 4 pins counts on the demo tier, EP-42 item 3 tests on crafted synthetic sepsis3/kdigo_stages frames, and EP-55 item 6 fixture tests do not assert respiration non-null, so no listed brief will actually trip -- the real cost is silent degeneracy, a fixture that cannot serve as regression substrate for those branches, and a misleading EP-12 note. Severity medium retained (documentation gap + misleading note + cheap regen add); doc part should be done now, regen at next re-plan because it moves hosp bytes and forces a GENERATOR_VERSION bump.

### GOV-2 — Parked GOV-1 (PreToolUse hook) is now cheap and is the only construct that can express the needed rule; recommend un-parking as a command-string filter, not an output scanner

**Severity (verified):** medium · **Verdict:** confirmed-with-corrections (medium confidence) · **Kind:** design · **When:** owner-decision · **Effort:** S · **Retro episode:** EP-165 · **Lens:** governance enforcement layers (deny rules, mwh guard, .gitignore/.gitattributes, hooks, GOVERNANCE text vs reality)

**Files:** `roadmap/final-roadmap.md:142`, `mimicwarehouse/DECISIONS.md:397-400`, `mimicwarehouse/DECISIONS.md:505-524`

**Evidence.** final-roadmap.md:142: `PreToolUse output-scanning hook for Claude sessions | if deny rules + wrapper prove insufficient | false positives | v2 GOV-1`. D-39: 'A PreToolUse output-scanning hook is parked.' GOV-1 (this review) shows the deny rules ARE insufficient for python -c / cp / nested-shell paths, and D-42 (heredoc/stdin AV kills) constrains how a hook may be implemented. The venv python.exe is on the Malwarebytes allow list (D-38 seven paths).

**Impact.** Without a hook the project relies on the harness's auto-mode classifier (external, undocumented, mode-dependent) for the bypass class in GOV-1. An output-scanning (PostToolUse) hook cannot help — the tool result has already been produced and is transmitted regardless — which is probably why GOV-1 was parked with 'false positives'.

**Proposed fix (reviewer).** Unpark GOV-1 as a *PreToolUse* hook (matcher `Bash|PowerShell|Read|Grep|Glob`) implemented as `mimicwarehouse/scripts/claude_pretool_guard.py` run by the allow-listed `.venv\Scripts\python.exe <script>` (a file script reading the hook JSON from stdin — not `python -`, not a heredoc; verify once against Malwarebytes/Defender per D-42 (4)). Logic: deny when the command/file_path string matches `(?i)mimicdata|source material[/\\](?!README\.md)|\.csv(\.gz)?\b|\.parquet\b|\.duckdb` AND the command does not start with an allow-list (`uv run mwh`, `uv run --project mimicwarehouse * mwh`, `uv run pytest`, `uv run poe`, `git `); deny `sh -c`/`bash -c`/`python -c`/`uv run python -c` when the argument mentions those tokens; log decisions to a scratch file, never the data. Cost ≈ 100–200 ms per matched tool call. Add a selfcheck row that the hook is registered in .claude/settings.json. Effort S; owner must approve the settings.json change (CLAUDE.md §6).

**Corrected fix (verifier).** Unpark v2 GOV-1 as a PreToolUse *command-string* deny hook, with these corrections to the proposed design:

1. Matcher: `Bash|PowerShell` only (optionally `Read`). The Read/Grep/Glob tools are already covered by the explicit absolute-path deny rules (`Read(//C:/mimicdata/**)`, `Read(./source material/...)`, `Grep(...)`, `Glob(...)` in `.claude/settings.json` lines 5-21); the only Read gap EP-0 recorded is the project-relative `Read(**/*.csv)` (EP-0-baseline.md:103-110), which concerns non-repo CSVs, not the real data. Putting the hook on every Read/Grep/Glob call buys little and charges the ~0.1-0.3 s interpreter start-up on the most frequent tool calls of a session. The real bypass class (no deny rule mentions `python`, `cp`, `Copy-Item`, `sh -c`, `bash -c`, `pwsh -c`, `[IO.File]::ReadAllText`, `Get-Item`, `ConvertFrom-Csv`; only `Import-Csv` and the cat/head/tail/sed/awk/grep/rg/cut/sort family are listed) is entirely on the shell tools.

2. Implementation: `mimicwarehouse/scripts/claude_pretool_guard.py` (stdlib only: json/re/sys), invoked as the allow-listed `<repo>\mimicwarehouse\.venv\Scripts\python.exe <repo>\mimicwarehouse\scripts\claude_pretool_guard.py`; the script reads the hook JSON from stdin (data, not code — this is not `python -`, no heredoc, so D-42 (1) is not violated) and returns `{"hookSpecificOutput":{"hookEventName":"PreToolUse","permissionDecision":"deny","permissionDecisionReason":"..."}}` (or exit 2). Use an absolute path via `$CLAUDE_PROJECT_DIR` so the hook works from every cwd. Verify once that Malwarebytes/Defender do not flag the `bash -c "<python.exe> <script>"` launch line (D-42 (4)) and record the result in a D-42 addendum.

3. Rule shape: deny when the command string matches `(?i)mimicdata|source material[/\\](?!.*\.md\b)|\.csv(\.gz)?\b|\.parquet\b|\.duckdb\b|\bduckdb(\.exe)?\b` AND the command does not start with an allow-list prefix. The allow-list must include every legitimate launcher form actually used in the briefs, not only `uv run mwh`: `uv run [--project mimicwarehouse] [--group <g>] mwh`, `uv run [...] pytest`, `uv run [...] poe`, `uv run [...] pre-commit`, `pre-commit`, `git `. Expect false positives on `grep -rn "\.duckdb" src/` / `ls tests/fixtures/*.csv` — document "use the Grep/Glob tools for source searches" in CLAUDE.md §2 and treat the deny reason text as the hint. Log only timestamp + tool + verdict + the first 200 chars of the command to a gitignored file under the scratchpad or `%LOCALAPPDATA%` (one append per call, so D-42 (2) is not triggered); never log tool output.

4. Docs: (a) the parked item's name is a contradiction in terms — a PreToolUse hook has no output to scan; the phrase "PreToolUse output-scanning hook" appears in final-roadmap.md:142, DECISIONS.md:399-400 (D-39) and :427-428 (D-39 addendum), EP-4-guard-precommit.md:71-72 and EP-133-disclosure-review-tool.md:57,71 — fix the pending brief EP-133 and the roadmap row in place, and correct D-39 by a dated addendum (not by rewriting) that says the hook is a *pre-execution command-string filter* and that a PostToolUse scanner cannot prevent transmission; (b) GOVERNANCE.md §4 item 3 (lines 80-83) lists the enforcement layers — add the hook as a layer if adopted; (c) add a `mwh guard --selfcheck` row `claude-hook-registered` (settings.json parses, hooks.PreToolUse contains the script path, script file exists) so the layer is verified like `hook-installed`; (d) note explicitly that this is a mitigation, not a guarantee (a string filter is trivially evaded by `'mimic'+'data'`), so CLAUDE.md prose stays the primary control.

5. Effort S-M rather than S once the AV verification, the settings.json approval (CLAUDE.md §6 — owner must approve), the selfcheck row and the four doc touch-points are counted; owner decision stays as posed. Suggested owner options: (A) unpark now as above; (B) unpark but Bash|PowerShell only and no selfcheck row (smallest change); (C) keep parked and instead add the cheap deny rules that ARE expressible today (`Bash(*python -c*mimicdata*)`, `Bash(*python -c*source material*)`, `Bash(cp *mimicdata*)`, `Bash(cp *source material*)`, `PowerShell(Copy-Item *mimicdata*)`, `PowerShell(Copy-Item *source material*)`, `Bash(*sh -c*mimicdata*)`) — cheaper than a hook but cannot express the allow-list exception and grows without bound. Recommendation unchanged: (A) before EP-17.

**Verifier notes.** Checked: `.claude/settings.json` (67 deny rules, full listing) — no rule matches `python`, `python -c`, `cp`, `Copy-Item`, `sh -c`/`bash -c`/`pwsh -c`, `.NET` file APIs; the finding's premise (deny rules cannot express "deny X unless the command is an allow-listed launcher") holds because Claude Code deny rules have no exception clause and are prefix/glob string matches. `roadmap/final-roadmap.md:142` quoted verbatim ("PreToolUse output-scanning hook … false positives … v2 GOV-1"); DECISIONS.md:397-400 (D-39) and 424-428 (D-39 addendum: hook "stays parked (final-roadmap GOV-1)"); DECISIONS.md:505-524 (D-42: no heredocs, no stdin code, allow-listed `.venv` python for long writers) and :375 (seven Malwarebytes paths include the workspace `.venv`), so the venv `python.exe` claim is correct. EP-0-baseline.md:100-110 and roadmap/README.md Risk 8 confirm the only tested deny gap is `Read(**/*.csv)` being project-relative, i.e. Read/Grep/Glob are otherwise covered by absolute-path rules — hence the matcher correction. GOVERNANCE.md:76-83 lists the enforcement layers (doc → CLAUDE.md → deny rules → safe module) with no hook. Also found the same "PreToolUse output-scanning" phrase in EP-4-guard-precommit.md:71-72 and EP-133-disclosure-review-tool.md:57,71 (extra doc touch-points). Not verified empirically (read-only review, no settings change allowed): that this host's Claude Code build honours `permissionDecision: deny` from a project-settings PreToolUse hook and that the hook launch line passes Malwarebytes/Defender — both are documented harness features / an explicit D-42 (4) verification step, so the finding's "verify once" caveat is right and my confidence is medium. Impact assessment (medium) stands: it is a real license-enforcement gap in the tooling layer, mitigated today by CLAUDE.md prose and the mode-dependent classifier; the parked hazard "false positives" is real for `.duckdb`/`.csv` tokens in source-search commands and is why the allow-list prefix + "use Grep/Glob tools" note matter. Not refuted; corrected on matcher scope, allow-list completeness, doc touch-points, selfcheck row and effort.

**Owner question (as posed by the reviewer).** Unpark GOV-1 now (S) as a PreToolUse *command-string* deny hook, accepting ~0.2 s per tool call and one D-42 AV verification, or keep relying on prose + the mode-dependent classifier? Recommendation: unpark; the loader EPs (EP-17..EP-21) are where ad-hoc python against the data root becomes tempting.

### GOV-4 — G4 misses float-rendered ids (`2xxxxxxx.0`), underscore-adjacent ids in file names (`stay_3xxxxxxx.parquet`), and comma-grouped ids

**Severity (verified):** medium · **Verdict:** confirmed-with-corrections (high confidence) · **Kind:** bug · **When:** now-should · **Effort:** S · **Retro episode:** EP-165 · **Lens:** governance enforcement layers (deny rules, mwh guard, .gitignore/.gitattributes, hooks, GOVERNANCE text vs reality)

**Files:** `mimicwarehouse/src/mimicwarehouse/guard.py:118`, `mimicwarehouse/tests/ep/test_ep04.py:243-244`

**Evidence.** `ID_TOKEN = re.compile(r"(?<![\w.])[123]\d{7}(?![\w.])", re.ASCII)`. Verified with synthetic tokens via `uv run python -c` calling `id_band_hits`: `hadm_id: <2-band>.0` → no hit; `stay_<3-band>.parquet` → no hit; `<1-band>` written with thousands commas → no hit; `subject_id=<id>`, `<id>-DS-12`, `<id>-4` (note_id / emar_id shapes) → hits. test_ep04.py:243-244 asserts `decimal {SUBJECT_ID}.5` and `_{SUBJECT_ID}` are clean, locking in the gap. G4 also never scans file *names* — only content of TEXT_EXTENSIONS files.

**Impact.** The float form is the realistic leak: DuckDB→pandas of a nullable BIGINT column (or any pandas int column with NaN) renders `20000123.0`; a session that pastes such a frame into a markdown/JSON aggregate table commits real ids past the guard. Per-stay artifact names (`figures/stay_<id>.png` referenced in a manifest.json) pass because `_` is `\w`. Rich/tabulate tables with `{:,}` formatting pass.

**Proposed fix (reviewer).** S: change the token to `(?<![A-Za-z0-9.])[123]\d{7}(?:\.0+)?(?![A-Za-z0-9.])` (allow `_` as a boundary; band constants written `10_000_000` still never match; hex-hash safety preserved by keeping letters as boundaries), add an optional comma-grouped form `[123]\d,\d{3},\d{3}` gated on band, and apply ID_TOKEN to the *path* of every entry as well (`stay_3xxxxxxx.png`). Update test_ep04 lines 243-244 (`decimal .5` stays clean; `.0` and `_`-bordered become hits) and rerun `mwh guard --all-tracked` (must stay clean).

**Corrected fix (verifier).** Keep the S-effort regex change but correct three parts of the proposed fix:

1. Content regex (guard.py:125, not :118): `ID_TOKEN = re.compile(r"(?<![A-Za-z0-9.])[123]\d{7}(?:\.0+)?(?![A-Za-z0-9.])", re.ASCII)`. Verified against every tracked blob (421 entries via guard.tracked_paths/index_entries): 0 new hits, so `mwh guard --all-tracked` stays clean and the vendored mimic-code SQL (`concepts/vendoring.py:79` derives `_ID_TOKEN_BYTES` from `guard.ID_TOKEN.pattern`; its `redact` callback replaces the whole match, so a `.0` suffix is redacted too) is unaffected. `id_band_hits` must strip the `.0` before `int()` (`int(token.split('.')[0])`), and `mask()` should mask only the digit part.

2. Path scanning: the proposed regex does NOT catch `stay_3xxxxxxx.png` / `stay-3xxxxxxx.png` — the trailing `.png` fails the `(?![A-Za-z0-9.])` lookahead (verified: `stay-<3-band>.png` is a miss under the current regex too, so the underscore is not the only blocker). Add a separate path rule in `check_entry` using `PATH_TOKEN = re.compile(r"(?<![0-9])[123]\d{7}(?![0-9])")` over `entry.rel` (0 hits on the current tracked tree, verified). Because a filename cannot carry the line pragma, the owner must decide the escape hatch: (a) none — nothing in git may be named after a band id, demo tier included (recommended; sessions name per-patient demo artefacts by hash/ordinal), or (b) a repo-root allow-list file.

3. Drop the comma-grouped form `[123]\d,\d{3},\d{3}`: no default formatter (Rich, tabulate, pandas to_markdown/to_csv, DuckDB) comma-groups ids, while many MIMIC-IV table row counts sit in 10_000_000–39_999_999 and docs written with `{:,}` would trip constantly (the guard already needed row-count pragmas in the vendored `validate.sql`). If the owner wants it anyway, gate it behind an opt-in.

Also required (omitted by the finding): update guard.py docstring lines 23-26 and the EP-4 brief/DESIGN §15 sentence "longer digit runs, hex hashes and decimals never match by construction" (roadmap/EP-4-guard-precommit.md:37) via a dated addendum — the `.0` exclusion was a documented design choice, not an accident; update test_ep04.py:242-243 (`.5` and `0.{HADM_ID}` stay clean; `{HADM_ID}.0` and `_{SUBJECT_ID}` become hits; add a path-hit test); rerun `uv run pytest tests/ep/test_ep04.py` and `mwh guard --all-tracked`. Effort stays S (~30 min).

**Verifier notes.** Checked mimicwarehouse/src/mimicwarehouse/guard.py (ID_TOKEN is at line 125; docstring lines 19-26 explicitly document "decimals never match by construction"), tests/ep/test_ep04.py:235-248 (the clean-list is at lines 242-243, not 243-244), roadmap/EP-4-guard-precommit.md:31-37, DESIGN.md:280-292 and 540-552, concepts/vendoring.py:79/246/293 and fixtures/write.py:36/119 (both downstream consumers of the guard regex). Ran a scratch script (uv run python, no stdin) calling id_band_hits with synthetic in-band tokens: `.0` float, `stay_<id>.parquet`, `figures/stay_<id>.png`, comma-grouped, `stay-<id>.png` (hyphen), `<id>.5`, e-notation, pandas repr → no hit; `=`, `-DS-12`, `-4`, `/`-separated path components, JSON, CSV/tab, parens, quotes, markdown table → hit. The core claim (float-rendered and underscore/extension-adjacent ids evade G4) is confirmed; the leak channel is real (DuckDB→pandas nullable BIGINT renders `NNNNNNNN.0`; to_csv/to_markdown/DataFrame repr keep fixed notation at 8 digits). Ran a second scratch script applying the candidate regex and a digits-only path regex to all 421 tracked entries: 0 content hits, 0 path hits, so the fix would not break --all-tracked or the vendored tree. Corrections: (a) the finding's proposed regex still misses filenames with an extension (`.png` after the digits) — the trailing dot, not only `_`, is the blocker, so path scanning needs its own regex and an escape-hatch decision; (b) comma-grouped detection would generate false positives on human-readable MIMIC row counts in the 10M-39M range and no default formatter emits ids that way — recommend dropping; (c) the fix must revise the documented "decimals never match" design statement in guard.py/EP-4/DESIGN. Severity: kept at medium — no data-access module (safe.py) exists yet so there is no live channel today, but the gap is locked in by tests and cheap to fix before EP-3x safe_query / EP-59 export / EP-73 screenshots create the channel; license stakes justify fixing now.

### GOV-6 — .gitignore unanchored `data/` will silently drop EP-39's `src/mimicwarehouse/data/item_units.yaml`; `models/`, `studies/`, `exports/`, `notes/`, `runs/` are equally unanchored

**Severity (verified):** medium · **Verdict:** confirmed-with-corrections (high confidence) · **Kind:** forward-compat · **When:** now-should · **Effort:** XS · **Retro episode:** EP-165 · **Lens:** governance enforcement layers (deny rules, mwh guard, .gitignore/.gitattributes, hooks, GOVERNANCE text vs reality)

**Files:** `.gitignore:39-47`, `roadmap/EP-39-itemid-units.md:23`, `mimicwarehouse/src/mimicwarehouse/guard.py:141-155`

**Evidence.** `git check-ignore -v mimicwarehouse/src/mimicwarehouse/data/item_units.yaml` → `.gitignore:39:data/`; likewise `mimicwarehouse/docs/studies/x.md` → `studies/`, `mimicwarehouse/app/models/x.py` → `models/`, `mimicwarehouse/docs/exports/fig.png` → `exports/`. EP-39 brief line 23 names `src/mimicwarehouse/data/item_units.yaml` as package data. TRACKED_PROBES in guard.py contains no probe under `src/`.

**Impact.** EP-39's session will `git add -A`, tests pass locally (file present in the worktree), the commit omits the YAML, and the next clone / EP-44 / EP-55 fail with a missing package resource. Same trap for any future `app/models/`, `docs/exports/`, `docs/notes/` directory.

**Proposed fix (reviewer).** XS: anchor the data-root-shaped directory patterns to where a mis-set root could land — `/data/`, `/mimicwarehouse/data/`, `/mimicwarehouse/lake/` … (or add explicit negations `!mimicwarehouse/src/**/data/`, `!mimicwarehouse/docs/**`, `!mimicwarehouse/app/**` after the patterns; note negation cannot re-include a file whose parent dir is excluded, so anchor rather than negate). Add `mimicwarehouse/src/mimicwarehouse/data/item_units.yaml` and `mimicwarehouse/app/models/x.py` to TRACKED_PROBES; keep `mimicwarehouse/data/x.parquet` in IGNORED_PROBES. Do it before EP-39 (P3); trivial now.

**Corrected fix (verifier).** Anchor the directory patterns in .gitignore lines 39-47 to the two places a data root can actually land in the tree (repo root and the uv project root), e.g. replace `data/` with `/data/` + `/mimicwarehouse/data/` and likewise for `lake/`, `warehouse/`, `runs/`, `models/`, `notes/`, `studies/`, `exports/`, `mimicdata/`. Prefer anchoring over a `!mimicwarehouse/src/**`-style negation — not because negation cannot re-include (line 48 shows a `**` negation does re-include an ignored dir: `mimicwarehouse/tests/fixtures/notes/x.txt` is un-ignored despite `notes/`), but because such a negation would also lift the `*.csv`/`*.parquet`/`*.duckdb` extension guards under `src/` (exactly what line 48/49 deliberately does for fixtures). Then extend guard.py: add `mimicwarehouse/src/mimicwarehouse/data/item_units.yaml` (and e.g. `mimicwarehouse/docs/studies/x.md`) to TRACKED_PROBES; keep `mimicwarehouse/data/x.parquet`/`mimicwarehouse/runs/audit.jsonl` in IGNORED_PROBES and add `mimicwarehouse/models/x.pkl`; re-run `mwh guard selfcheck` (or the EP-4 test). Optionally note in the .gitignore comment block that hatchling wheel builds also honour .gitignore, so an unanchored `data/` would drop the YAML from any non-editable install too. XS effort; do before EP-39.

**Verifier notes.** Checked .gitignore:39-47 (unanchored `data/ lake/ warehouse/ runs/ models/ notes/ studies/ exports/ mimicdata/`) and ran `git check-ignore -v --no-index` on the probes: `mimicwarehouse/src/mimicwarehouse/data/item_units.yaml` -> `.gitignore:39:data/`; `mimicwarehouse/docs/studies/x.md` -> :45; `mimicwarehouse/app/models/x.py` -> :43; `mimicwarehouse/docs/exports/fig.png` -> :46; `mimicwarehouse/docs/notes/x.md` and root `docs/notes/x.md` -> :44 — all reproduce. Confirmed roadmap/EP-39-itemid-units.md:22-23 names `src/mimicwarehouse/data/item_units.yaml` as package data, and guard.py:152-157 TRACKED_PROBES has nothing under `src/` (`mwh guard selfcheck`, guard.py:593-605, therefore cannot detect the trap; the pre-commit hook only sees staged files and ignored files never appear in `git status`). No such directory exists today (`git ls-files` shows no data/models/studies/exports/notes/runs paths), so nothing is currently broken — it is purely forward-compat. Corrections: (a) the finding's stated reason to prefer anchoring ("negation cannot re-include a file whose parent dir is excluded") is inaccurate for `**` negations — line 48 already re-includes `tests/fixtures/notes/x.txt` under the `notes/` rule; the correct reason is that a broad negation would also un-ignore data-shaped extensions under `src/`. (b) Extra impact the finding missed: pyproject.toml uses hatchling (`[tool.hatch.build.targets.wheel] packages = ["src/mimicwarehouse"]`), and hatchling excludes VCS-ignored files by default, so a non-editable wheel/`uv build` would also omit the YAML; the current editable install masks it, matching the "tests pass locally" scenario. (c) Scope check: EP-148's `tests/fixtures/notes/` is safe (re-included by line 48/49); EP-52/EP-125 `models/`, `studies/` refer to the data root, not the repo tree, so the blast radius is EP-39 plus any future in-repo `docs/{studies,exports,notes}` or `app/models` dirs. Severity medium and XS effort stand.

### GOV-8 — GOVERNANCE §2 'the repository is not [excluded]' and §4(3) layer list are stale versus D-38/D-42 (Malwarebytes exclusions include `source material/` inside the repo; telemetry; guard layer; project-relative *.csv rule)

**Severity (verified):** medium · **Verdict:** confirmed-with-corrections (high confidence) · **Kind:** docs-drift · **When:** now-should · **Effort:** XS · **Retro episode:** EP-165 · **Lens:** governance enforcement layers (deny rules, mwh guard, .gitignore/.gitattributes, hooks, GOVERNANCE text vs reality)

**Files:** `mimicwarehouse/GOVERNANCE.md:45-47`, `mimicwarehouse/GOVERNANCE.md:76-80`, `mimicwarehouse/DECISIONS.md:334-395`, `mimicwarehouse/DECISIONS.md:505-524`, `roadmap/README.md:394`

D-38 addendum (DECISIONS.md:375): Malwarebytes allow list includes `source material\` and the workspace `.venv` — both inside the repository directory. D-42 (DECISIONS.md:521-523): 'the data-location exclusions are also a GOVERNANCE §2 disclosure control' — but §2 says nothing about Malwarebytes, telemetry, or sample submission. GOVERNANCE.md:76-80 §4(3): 'Enforcement is layered: this document → CLAUDE.md → repo-shared .claude/settings.json deny rules on reading source material/** (except *.md), C:\mimicdata\**, *.csv/*.parquet/*.duckdb, and the duckdb executable → the safe module itself' — omits the mwh guard hook (§3) and the EP-0 finding that `*.csv/*.parquet/*.duckdb` Read rules are project-relative (Risk 8, D-39 addendum). GOVERNANCE has no mention of Malwarebytes anywhere.

**Impact.** GOVERNANCE 'overrides everything' yet a reader (or the EP-163 release sweep, or a collaborator under GOV-2) gets a wrong picture of which scanner may see which files and which controls actually enforce; D-42 cites a §2 control that §2 does not state.

**Proposed fix (reviewer).** XS (owner approval required — CLAUDE.md §6): dated addendum block under §2: 'both real-time products; Defender: C:\mimicdata excluded; Malwarebytes: seven D-38 paths incl. source material/ (in the repo) and the venv; both products' telemetry/sample submission off; these exclusions are a disclosure control'; amend §4(3) to list five layers (GOVERNANCE → CLAUDE.md → deny rules [project-relative for *.csv…; absolute for the two data locations] → mwh guard/pre-commit → safe_query) and cross-reference Risk 8. Do not rewrite history; append.

**Corrected fix (verifier).** XS, owner approval required (CLAUDE.md §6 lists GOVERNANCE.md as ask-before). Append (do not rewrite) a dated addendum under GOVERNANCE §2 after the sentence at lines 43-45: "Addendum (2026-08-17/18, D-38 addenda + D-42): two real-time products run on the host. Defender: `C:\mimicdata` excluded, the repository is not. Malwarebytes 5.1 Premium: seven allow-list paths (D-38), two of which are inside the repository directory — `source material\` and the workspace `.venv` — plus `C:\mimicdata` and four toolchain paths; Ransomware Protection stays on; both products' usage/threat statistics and sample submission are off. The data-root and source-material exclusions are a disclosure control (a scanner that uploads detected objects must never have reason to open MIMIC files); neither exclusion list is readable non-elevated, so `mwh doctor antivirus` reports product presence only (Risk 12, final-roadmap DOC-1)." Then amend §4(3) (lines 80-83) to read five layers and be precise about scope: GOVERNANCE → CLAUDE.md → repo-shared `.claude/settings.json` deny rules (Read/Grep on the three named `source material/<dataset>/**` subfolders and the absolute `C:/mimicdata/**`; extension rules `**/*.csv|csv.gz|parquet|duckdb|duckdb.wal` are project-relative and do not fire outside the repo — Risk 8, D-39 addendum) → `mwh guard` pre-commit hook G1–G5 (git-side layer, D-39 addendum, §3) → `safe_query` (EP-30, not yet built; until then no session may query the data at all). Cross-reference Risk 8 and Risk 12. Optionally add "(§2 addendum)" next to D-42's phrase "GOVERNANCE §2 disclosure control" so the citation resolves.

**Verifier notes.** Checked: mimicwarehouse/GOVERNANCE.md in full (168 lines); DECISIONS.md lines 325-420 (D-38 addenda EP-6/EP-7/EP-164, D-39 + EP-7 addendum) and 500-524 (D-42); roadmap/README.md Risks 8 and 12 (lines 390, 394); .claude/settings.json deny list; `git log -- mimicwarehouse/GOVERNANCE.md`; `grep -i malwarebytes|guard|Risk 8|project-relative` over GOVERNANCE.md. Findings: (1) GOVERNANCE.md has zero occurrences of "Malwarebytes" and has not been touched since 795a044 (EP-0 follow-up), while D-38 addendum (DECISIONS.md:355 "Governance reading (GOVERNANCE §2/§4)"), D-42 (DECISIONS.md:521-523 "a GOVERNANCE §2 disclosure control") and Risk 12 (README.md:394 "also a GOVERNANCE §2 control") all cite §2 as the home of a control §2 does not state — confirmed. (2) The §2 sentence is at GOVERNANCE.md:43-45, not 45-47, and §4(3) is at lines 80-83, not 76-80 — line refs corrected. (3) Nuance: "the repository is not" is literally true for Defender (the sentence names only Defender), so it is incomplete/misleading rather than false; the real drift is that Malwarebytes exclusions for `source material\` and `mimicwarehouse\.venv` (both inside the repo dir) exist and the telemetry-off condition is undocumented in the authoritative file. (4) §4(3) omission of `mwh guard` is partly mitigated by §3 line 58 which names guard as git-side enforcement; still, D-39's EP-7 addendum calls guard "the git-side layer of D-39" and Risk 8 says "guard and EP-30 safe-query remain necessary layers", so listing it in §4(3) is justified. (5) Additional precision the fix should carry: settings.json denies Read/Grep only on the three named `source material/<dataset>/**` subfolders (lines 5-7, 15-17), not `source material/**` as GOVERNANCE:81 says; the `**/*.csv...` rules are project-relative (Risk 8, verified at EP-0); the "safe module itself" layer (line 83) does not exist yet — src/mimicwarehouse/ has no safe.py (EP-30 pending). Impact stands: GOVERNANCE is declared to override everything and is what a collaborator or the EP-163 release sweep reads; medium severity retained (docs-drift in the authoritative safety file, XS fix, owner-gated).

### INV-1 — A no-op resume `build` (0 to process) overwrites snapshot started/finished/git_sha/contract_hash/duckdb_version — breaks EP-16's verification recipe and the 'timestamp-only diff' promise

**Severity (verified):** medium · **Verdict:** confirmed-with-corrections (high confidence) · **Kind:** bug · **When:** now-should · **Effort:** S · **Retro episode:** EP-167 · **Lens:** raw inventory / provenance module (EP-10)

**Files:** `mimicwarehouse/src/mimicwarehouse/inventory.py:741`, `mimicwarehouse/src/mimicwarehouse/inventory.py:765-771`, `mimicwarehouse/src/mimicwarehouse/inventory.py:779-804`, `mimicwarehouse/src/mimicwarehouse/inventory.py:858-880`, `mimicwarehouse/src/mimicwarehouse/inventory.py:1002-1009`, `roadmap/EP-10-raw-inventory.md (Verification recipe for EP-16 block)`, `roadmap/EP-16-replan-p1.md:33-47`

**Evidence.** `versions = {"duckdb_version": ..., "git_sha": _git_sha(), ..., "contract_hash": contract.content_hash()}` is computed unconditionally (765-771) and passed to every `flush()`; `job = {"started": result.started, "finished": None, ...}` (779-788) is written by `flush()` at 804 before any file is processed, and `write_snapshot` takes `job.get("started", prev.get("started"))` (628-629), so a run with `todo == []` still replaces the 2026-08-18T03:48:36Z/03:50:04Z pair and the EP-9-tick git sha with the current ones. `render_docs` then prints `snap.get("git_sha")` and `Last build: started …, finished …` (1004-1009). The EP-10 verification recipe tells EP-16 to run `mwh inventory build` (must say `0 to process, 41 up to date`) AND `show` must print `finished 2026-08-18T03:50:04+00:00`, AND `reconcile` must rewrite the docs page with 'a timestamp-only diff'.

**Impact.** EP-16 runs the three commands in the natural order (build → show → reconcile): `show` no longer prints the recorded finish time, and `git diff docs/resources/raw-inventory.md` changes the git-sha and Last-build lines, so the acceptance bullet fails or the session 'fixes' the docs by committing a page whose git sha no longer matches the run that produced the hashes. Provenance semantics also blur: `git_sha` is supposed to identify the code that computed the manifest, not the last code that looked at it.

**Proposed fix (reviewer).** In `build_inventory`, when `todo` is empty (and no `--force`), keep `previous` values for started/finished/last_file and the versions block (pass `versions=None` so `write_snapshot` re-uses `prev`), and only append the `runs` entry; alternatively store the versions per FileRecord (`recorded_git_sha`, `recorded_contract_hash`). Also re-order the EP-16 recipe (show/reconcile first, build last) and drop the stale `%MWH_DATA_ROOT%\runs\jobs` wording in EP-16-replan-p1.md:34.

**Corrected fix (verifier).** Code (S, should fix now): in `build_inventory` (mimicwarehouse/src/mimicwarehouse/inventory.py:765-804, 858-878), when `todo` is empty and `force` is False, do not present the no-op run as the job: copy `started`, `finished`, `last_file`, `pid`, `hostname`, `options` from `previous` into `job` explicitly (note `job.get(k, prev.get(k))` in `write_snapshot` never falls back because the keys are always present, so `versions=None` alone is not enough for the job fields) and pass `versions=None` to `write_snapshot` so `duckdb_version/python_version/git_sha/mimic_code_sha/contract_hash` are re-used from `prev`; still append the `runs` entry (`processed=0, skipped=41`) so the history records the check. Add a regression test in tests/ep/test_ep10.py: build twice (second with `_git_sha` monkeypatched), assert `started/finished/last_file/git_sha` unchanged, `runs` grows by one, `raw_snapshot_id` identical. Docs (XS): in the EP-10 "Verification recipe for EP-16" block (roadmap/EP-10-raw-inventory.md:234-238) add "run `build` LAST; until the no-op fix lands a resume build rewrites the job/provenance block, and re-running `reconcile` afterwards would change the git-sha/Last-build lines of the docs page". Do NOT change EP-16-replan-p1.md:34 — the `%MWH_DATA_ROOT%\runs\jobs\ep10-raw-inventory.log` wording is correct (EP-10 brief lines 95/124/152/157; `runs_jobs` layout key at config.py:612), so that part of the proposed fix should be dropped.

**Verifier notes.** Checked inventory.py 599-644 (write_snapshot: `job.get("started", prev.get("started"))` etc., `versions or {prev...}`), 741 (`result.started=_now()`), 765-771 (versions computed unconditionally), 779-804 (job dict with started/finished=None/last_file=None, first flush() before the loop), 858-878 (finally: finished=_now(), runs appended, flush()), 1179-1200 (_job_lines prints started/finished/last_file/git), 1003-1009 (render_docs prints git sha and Last build). Reproduced empirically on the synthetic fixture (scratch pytest in the scratchpad importing test_ep10's fixtures, `_git_sha` monkeypatched for the second run): after a 0-to-process resume build the snapshot's `started` changed, `git_sha` became the monkeypatched value, `last_file` went from a real rel_path to None, `finished` was rewritten to now (same second in the test), `raw_snapshot_id` unchanged, `runs` 1 -> 2 (the original run's started/finished/processed=41 survive in runs[0], a mitigation the finding omits; visible via `show --json`). Committed docs page docs/resources/raw-inventory.md lines 8-11 do carry `git sha 5e27a154...`, `Last build: started 2026-08-18T03:48:36+00:00, finished 2026-08-18T03:50:04+00:00`, so a `reconcile` run after any `build` produces a non-timestamp-only diff and the page's git sha diverges from the on-disk snapshot. Corrections: (1) the EP-10 recipe (EP-10-raw-inventory.md:234-238) already lists show -> reconcile -> build in that order, so a session following it literally passes on the first pass; the failure needs `build` first or a re-run of show/reconcile afterwards (plausible: completion-note capture, EP-17/18/19 regenerating the page) - the headline "breaks the recipe" is over-stated, the provenance drift is the certain harm; (2) `last_file` reset to None is an additional symptom; (3) `versions=None` alone does not restore started/finished/last_file because the job keys are always present; (4) the EP-16-replan-p1.md:34 log-path wording is not stale (checked EP-10 brief launch recipe and completion note, config.py runs_jobs layout key); (5) `--resume/--force` flag exists (inventory.py:1135-1142). Severity kept at medium (low end): raw_snapshot_id (D-26) is content-derived and unaffected, but the top-level provenance the docs page displays becomes wrong after the very first no-op build the recipe mandates.

### SCH-2 — content_hash() is documentation-sensitive and is pinned by the committed fixture manifest and raw snapshot; every YAML wording change breaks tests until fixtures are regenerated

**Severity (verified):** medium · **Verdict:** confirmed-with-corrections (high confidence) · **Kind:** design · **When:** now-should · **Effort:** S · **Retro episode:** EP-169 · **Lens:** schema-contract (EP-9) as the spine of later briefs

**Files:** `mimicwarehouse/src/mimicwarehouse/schema/contract.py:559`, `mimicwarehouse/tests/ep/test_ep11.py:624`, `mimicwarehouse/tests/ep/test_ep12.py:566`, `mimicwarehouse/src/mimicwarehouse/fixtures/write.py:361`, `mimicwarehouse/src/mimicwarehouse/inventory.py:770`, `mimicwarehouse/docs/resources/raw-inventory.md`

**Evidence.** `content_hash()` = sha256 of `self.model_dump(mode="json", by_alias=True)` -> includes every `Column.comment`, `Table.comment`, `ValueUnitPair.note`, `FixedUnit.note`, `ColumnMap.derivation`, `version_note`. test_ep11.py:624 `assert manifest["contract_hash"] == contract.content_hash()` (same in test_ep12.py:566) against the committed tests/fixtures/manifest.json; inventory.py records `"contract_hash": contract.content_hash()` in raw_snapshot.json and the docs page prints `contract hash: e4cd5aa908d1...`. The hash is formatting- and platform-independent (parsed values only, sort_keys=True), so this is purely a scope problem, not a stability bug.

**Impact.** EP-29 ('extend EP-9's YAML with description ... identifier: true / free_text: true flags'), SCH-4/SCH-7 renames, or a one-word typo fix in a comment all change the hash: `poe test` goes red on ep_11/ep_12 until `mwh fixtures build` is re-run (fixture CSVs stay byte-identical, only manifest.json/README change), and the raw-inventory page/raw_snapshot then cite a hash no longer reproducible from HEAD. Sessions will either avoid improving comments or churn the fixture manifest on every doc edit.

**Proposed fix (reviewer).** Add `Contract.structural_hash()` covering only load-relevant facts (schema, table, csv_path, ordered (name, type, nullable), primary_key, uniqueness_hint, sort_keys, time_column, partitioned, load_class, FK tuples, column-map mechanics) and have fixtures/write.py and inventory.py cite that; keep `content_hash()` for `mwh schema list/check` output. One-time regeneration of tests/fixtures/manifest.json and a dated DESIGN section 7 note; document the regen recipe in tests/fixtures/README.md.

**Corrected fix (verifier).** Keep the core fix (a load-relevant `Contract.structural_hash()` cited by fixtures/write.py:361 and asserted in test_ep11.py:624 / test_ep12.py:566, one-time regen of tests/fixtures/manifest.json, dated DESIGN section 7 note) with these corrections: (1) tests/fixtures/README.md already documents the regen recipe (lines 23-27, `uv run --group dev mwh fixtures build`, REGENERATE_COMMAND in write.py:57) — do not re-document it; instead add one sentence there saying which contract fields are hash-relevant so a session knows a comment edit no longer requires regen. (2) Do NOT switch inventory.py:770 / raw_snapshot.json / docs/resources/raw-inventory.md to the structural hash: the raw snapshot is time-of-run provenance (recorded next to `git_sha`, which is equally non-reproducible from HEAD by design, EP-10 completion note); at most record both `contract_hash` and `contract_structural_hash` there. (3) Scope decision for the owner: EP-29's `identifier: true` / `free_text: true` flags are governance-relevant (safe_query/disclose key on them, EP-29 brief lines 26-29), so if a structural hash exists it must be explicit whether those flags are in it; the EP-35 run-ledger manifests (GOVERNANCE section 12, DESIGN.md:586 'EP-35 cites content_hash()') should keep citing the full `content_hash()` so any contract change, including comment/flag edits, is visible in run provenance. (4) Also strengthen the finding: the fixture manifest covers only hosp+icu, yet `content_hash()` also covers mimiciv_ed.yaml, mimiciv_note.yaml, keys.yaml, units.yaml, versions.yaml and column_maps/demo_2_2_to_3_1.yaml — an ED/Note comment or a column-map `derivation` edit also breaks ep_11/ep_12 although nothing about the fixtures changes; a structural hash for the fixture manifest should be restricted to the schemas the fixture ships (or the manifest could simply drop the hash from the byte-for-byte comparison). Effort S (about 30-45 min including regen and DESIGN/README notes).

**Verifier notes.** Checked: src/mimicwarehouse/schema/contract.py:559-563 (content_hash = sha256 of model_dump(mode='json', by_alias=True), sort_keys) and model fields at lines 109/173/397/408/417/423/449/457 (Column.comment, Table.comment, ValueUnitPair/FixedUnit note, version_note, ColumnMap.derivation at 325) — all in the dump. Callers: fixtures/write.py:361, inventory.py:770 and 1008, schema/cli.py:87/106/207; assertions test_ep09.py:349-352, test_ep10.py:412, test_ep11.py:624, test_ep12.py:566, both ep11/ep12 also compare regenerated manifest.json byte-for-byte to the committed one (so removing the direct assert alone would not help). tests/fixtures/manifest.json:43 and docs/resources/raw-inventory.md:11 carry e4cd5aa908d1... which equals HEAD's content_hash (verified via uv run python scratch script). Empirical proof: copied tables_root() to a temp dir, appended one word inside an existing quoted `comment:` in mimiciv_hosp.yaml, load_contract_from -> hash changed to 47f924a5e71d...; so a pure documentation edit red-lines ep_11/ep_12 until `mwh fixtures build` regenerates manifest.json/README.md. Downstream trip is real and scheduled: roadmap/EP-29 lines 26-29 add `description` for all columns and identifier/free_text flags. Corrections: regen recipe already exists in tests/fixtures/README.md:23-27; the raw-snapshot citation is time-of-run provenance like git_sha and is not a reproducibility defect; hash scope also spans ED/Note/column-map YAML that fixtures never touch; the EP-35 run manifests should arguably keep the full content hash. Severity medium retained: failure is loud and has a one-command remedy, but it is guaranteed to recur on every YAML wording change and at EP-29.

### SCH-3 — Downstream briefs describe a contract that does not match what shipped (EP-17 item 3, EP-22 items 2-3, EP-29 item 1)

**Severity (verified):** medium · **Verdict:** confirmed-with-corrections (high confidence) · **Kind:** docs-drift · **When:** next-replan · **Effort:** S · **Retro episode:** EP-170 · **Lens:** schema-contract (EP-9) as the spine of later briefs

**Files:** `roadmap/EP-17-stage-loader-core.md:52`, `roadmap/EP-22-stage-demo-tier.md:12`, `roadmap/EP-22-stage-demo-tier.md:39`, `roadmap/EP-22-stage-demo-tier.md:45`, `roadmap/EP-29-catalog-data-dictionary.md:26`, `mimicwarehouse/src/mimicwarehouse/schema/tables/keys.yaml:39`, `mimicwarehouse/src/mimicwarehouse/schema/contract.py:100`

**Evidence.** EP-17 item 3: `ORDER BY <primary key from keys.yaml>` for the unpartitioned (dimension) path, but keys.yaml has `mimiciv_hosp.provider: null` and `mimiciv_icu.caregiver: null` (upstream declares none) - the contract's `sort_keys` ([provider_id] / [caregiver_id]) exist for every dim and are what the brief must use. EP-22: 'the demo is **v2.2 schema** (drift vs 3.1 -> EP-9's demo 2.2 -> 3.1 column map)', item 2 'applies ... (rename / `add_null` / `drop`)', item 3 '**Lossy-map handling** - where the 2.2 -> 3.1 map cannot fill a 3.1 column (`add_null`)' - but the shipped map is the identity (D-27 addendum) and the field is `added_in_3_1`, not `add_null`. EP-29 item 1: 'extend EP-9's YAML with `description` for all 31 tables ... (others may stay empty; report the count of undescribed columns)' - the contract has 41 tables and every one of the 421 columns already carries a `comment` (test_ep09::test_metadata_rules asserts it); `Column` is `extra="forbid"`, so `identifier`/`free_text` flags need model fields, not just YAML keys.

**Impact.** An autonomous EP-17 session will look for a PK on provider/caregiver and improvise; EP-22 will spend its budget on a lossy-map feature that has no input; EP-29 may add a parallel `description` field beside `comment` (two documentation fields, both in the hash) and count 'undescribed columns' that do not exist.

**Proposed fix (reviewer).** At the EP-16 re-plan amend the three briefs: EP-17 item 3 'ORDER BY the contract's sort_keys (dims have no PK)'; EP-22 items 2-3 -> 'map is identity; validate headers with ColumnMap.check(); the add/rename/drop path is exercised only by the EP-17 unit test'; EP-29 item 1 -> 'comment already exists for every table/column; add `identifier: bool` / `free_text: bool` fields to Column (model + YAML), regenerate the fixture manifest (SCH-2)'.

**Corrected fix (verifier).** At EP-16 (item 6, already in that brief) write `> **EP-16 pickup note (date).**` blocks: EP-17 item 3 -> 'ORDER BY the contract's `Table.sort_keys` (provider/caregiver have `primary_key: null` upstream; sort_keys = provider_id / caregiver_id)'; EP-22 Context + items 2, 3 and 5 -> 'the demo_2_2 map is the identity (D-27 addendum, `tables: {}`); the loader validates headers with `ColumnMap.check()` (never printed) and there is nothing to record in `map_notes` — drop item 3 or reduce it to "log ColumnMap.check() problems"; item 5's column-map test uses a synthetic non-identity map (fields `added_in_3_1` / `renamed` / `dropped_in_3_1`, not `add_null`) or is moved to EP-17's unit tests'; EP-29 item 1 -> 'every table/column already carries `comment` (test_ep09::test_metadata_rules); do not add a parallel `description` field — reuse `comment` (or rename it in one PR); `Column` is extra="forbid", so `identifier: bool` / `free_text: bool` need model fields (or a top-level `identifiers:` list in keys.yaml — keys.yaml has no such list today); adding fields changes `Contract.content_hash()` — record for EP-35; the fixture manifest schema hash may need regenerating (SCH-2)'. Effort S (about 30 min of brief edits inside EP-16).

**Verifier notes.** Checked (read-only): roadmap/EP-17-stage-loader-core.md:47-52 (item 2 already says `added_in_3_1`; item 3 line 52 says `ORDER BY <primary key from keys.yaml>` and line 56 names provider/caregiver as unpartitioned-path tables); roadmap/EP-22-stage-demo-tier.md:12 (2.2->3.1 drift), :39 (`rename / add_null / drop`), :45-47 (Lossy-map handling, `map_notes`), :59 (test expects `map_notes`); roadmap/EP-29-catalog-data-dictionary.md:14,26-32; mimicwarehouse/src/mimicwarehouse/schema/tables/keys.yaml:39,43 (`provider: null`, `caregiver: null`), :69-70 (only uniqueness_hints, no `sort_keys` and no identifier list in keys.yaml — the top-level keys are primary_keys / uniqueness_hints / foreign_keys); schema/contract.py:91-94 (`_Frozen` extra="forbid"), :96-131 (Column fields = name/type/nullable/comment/unit_of/upstream_type/upstream_nullable — no description/identifier/free_text), :303-316 (TableMap fields `added_in_3_1`/`renamed`/`dropped_in_3_1`), :353-390 (ColumnMap.apply/missing/check); schema/tables/column_maps/demo_2_2_to_3_1.yaml (`tables: {}`, 37 identity tables); tests/ep/test_ep09.py:197-199 asserts every table and column has a comment; DECISIONS.md D-27 addendum (lines 211-222) records identity map + 'ColumnMap.check only'; EP-9 completion note items 1-2. Ran `uv run python` over `load_contract()`: 41 tables, 421 columns, 0 tables and 0 columns without comment; dims provider/caregiver have primary_key=None and sort_keys=('provider_id',)/('caregiver_id',); demo_2_2 map `tables` is empty. `git log` shows the three briefs are untouched since planning commit cd67743, so none has a pickup note yet. All factual claims in the finding hold. Corrections: (1) this is not an un-planned gap — roadmap/EP-16-replan-p1.md item 6 (lines 73-79) already mandates `> **EP-16 pickup note**` blocks on EP-17..EP-22 (skim EP-28/29/30) for exactly `sort_keys` and `Contract.column_map("demo_2_2")`; the finding's value is the concrete wording for those notes, so the fix should be framed as input to EP-16 item 6, not a new task. (2) EP-17 item 2 (line 47) already uses the shipped `added_in_3_1`; only EP-22 uses the stale `add_null`, and EP-22 item 5's fixture test (line 59) also expects `map_notes` and a 2.2-shaped header 'built from the map's declared differences' — with an identity map that test has no declared differences, so item 5 needs amending too (or the add/rename/drop path is unit-tested with a synthetic non-identity map). (3) EP-29 '31 tables' is the P2 hosp+icu staging subset (consistent with EP-22 'all 31 tables'), not an error, but any new Column fields land in the YAML for all 41 tables. (4) EP-29 says the identifier set is 'the keys.yaml list' — keys.yaml has no identifier list (only primary_keys/uniqueness_hints/foreign_keys); guard.py:68-70 has only the three banded ids. The pickup note should say the identifier list must be curated in EP-29 (a new `identifier: bool` Column field or a top-level `identifiers:` block in keys.yaml), and that adding fields to `Column` (extra="forbid") changes `Contract.content_hash()` — note that for EP-35 manifests. Severity stays medium: real drift, autonomous sessions would waste budget, but the process (EP-16 item 6) exists and the fix is S.

### VT-1 — Dev-tier skip gate is keyed to dev.duckdb, but EP-17..EP-20 (and EP-23..25) dev tests need raw data / a lake / dev_ready — they will be skipped, making '--tier dev green' vacuous

**Severity (verified):** medium · **Verdict:** confirmed-with-corrections (high confidence) · **Kind:** design · **When:** owner-decision · **Effort:** S · **Retro episode:** EP-168 · **Lens:** verification tooling & test infrastructure (verify.py, roadmap_check, conftest tiers, tests/ep/*, pyproject test/lint config)

**Files:** `mimicwarehouse/tests/conftest.py:107`, `mimicwarehouse/tests/conftest.py:139`, `mimicwarehouse/tests/conftest.py:155`, `mimicwarehouse/tests/conftest.py:176`, `roadmap/EP-17-stage-loader-core.md:82`, `roadmap/EP-17-stage-loader-core.md:97`, `roadmap/EP-23-stage-labevents.md:47`, `roadmap/EP-24-stage-emar.md:43`, `roadmap/EP-12-fixtures-icu-markers.md:198`

**Evidence.** conftest.py catalog_status(): `path = get_settings().catalog_path(tier)` ... `return path.is_file(), str(path)`; pytest_collection_modifyitems adds `pytest.mark.skip(reason=f"{tier} tier: catalog not found ({where}); EP-21 builds it")` for every dev/full item. EP-17 brief §Tests: "`@pytest.mark.tier("dev")` ... stage real `mimiciv_hosp.d_labitems` and `mimiciv_hosp.patients` into a temporary lake root under `<data_root>\tmp\ep17\`" and acceptance "`uv run poe test -m ep_17 --tier dev` green". EP-23/24/25: "Dev-marked test (skips until `dev_ready`)" i.e. a status.json predicate, not the catalog file. EP-12 hand-off admits it: "the first `@pytest.mark.tier("dev")` tests go in `test_ep17.py` and skip cleanly until EP-21 writes `dev.duckdb`". Also the `tier` session fixture returns the *max* tier, so a dev test under `--tier full` receives "full" and must hard-code "dev" (test_ep12.py:867 does).

**Impact.** EP-17, EP-18, EP-19, EP-20 dev-tier acceptance runs are skipped (green with skips) until EP-21; either the EP-17 session edits conftest ad hoc (mechanism drift) or four briefs' dev acceptance is never actually executed. EP-23..25 need a per-test readiness predicate the current collection-time gate cannot express.

**Proposed fix (reviewer).** Split the two concerns: keep the collection hook for tier *deselection* only (plus a coarse skip when settings are unusable), and move readiness into fixtures the test requests: `dev_catalog` (skips unless catalog_path('dev') exists), `raw_root` (skips unless the raw dataset root/EP-10 manifest is present), later `dev_ready(step)` (skips unless status.json says so). Alternatively a marker kwarg `tier("dev", needs="raw"|"catalog"|"status:<step>")` resolved in the hook. Add an `item_tier`/`catalog_path` fixture so dev tests never hard-code the tier name. Record in DESIGN §20 and tests/README; EP-17 is the first consumer.

**Corrected fix (verifier).** Keep the collection hook (mimicwarehouse/tests/conftest.py:139-163) for tier deselection only and move readiness into requestable session fixtures: `dev_catalog` / `catalog_path(tier)` (skip unless `get_settings().catalog_path(tier).is_file()` — the current gate, kept for EP-21+ tests), `raw_root` (skip unless the EP-10 raw dataset root / raw manifest is present — what EP-17/18/19/20 dev tests actually need), and later `dev_ready(step)` (skip unless `lake/manifests/status.json` says so — EP-23/24/25). Add an `item_tier` fixture (from `item.get_closest_marker("tier")`) so dev tests stop hard-coding "dev" when the session `tier` fixture reports the max tier (test_ep12.py:867-868). Update conftest docstring, `--tier` help text (conftest.py:66-70), the marker description (conftest.py:124-130), tests/README.md:24-34, and DESIGN.md §20 (2026-08-18 note) which all state "skipped while dev.duckdb is missing". Landing spot: EP-16 (docs-only) cannot change conftest, so either a tiny pre-EP-17 hotfix commit or the first numbered item of EP-17 (with an EP-16 pickup note on EP-17..EP-20 per EP-16 item 6, roadmap/EP-16-replan-p1.md:73-78). Owner decision unchanged: (a) keep the dev.duckdb gate and explicitly document that EP-17..20 "--tier dev green" means "skipped" until EP-21 (and EP-21 re-runs `poe test-dev` for ep_17..ep_20), (b) fixture-level readiness (recommended, S), or (c) marker kwargs. Note EP-23..25 do not strictly need a hook change — after EP-21 the catalog exists and an in-test `pytest.skip` on status.json suffices — but a shared `dev_ready` fixture avoids five copies.

**Verifier notes.** Checked: mimicwarehouse/tests/conftest.py:107-118 (`catalog_status` = `get_settings().catalog_path(tier).is_file()`), :139-163 (hook adds `pytest.mark.skip(... "EP-21 builds it")` for every dev/full item when the catalog file is absent), :175-178 (`tier` fixture returns the session max tier); test_ep12.py:857-870 hard-codes `catalog_path("dev")` and asserts `tier in ("dev","full")`. Ran `uv run pytest tests/ep/test_ep12.py --tier dev -k catalog_opens -rs` → "SKIPPED ... dev tier: catalog not found (C:\mimicdata\warehouse\dev.duckdb); EP-21 builds it" (skip is real, green). Briefs: EP-17-stage-loader-core.md:82-85 dev test stages real raw `d_labitems`/`patients` into `<data_root>\tmp\ep17\` and :97 requires "`poe test -m ep_17 --tier dev` green"; EP-18:70-73 (real `admissions` into tmp lake, `--tier dev` green at :87); EP-19:81-84 (`mwh build --tier dev` into the real lake, :98 "dev-marked tests green"); EP-20:55-57 (dev partitions exist, :74); EP-21:80 is the first dev test that actually needs dev.duckdb; EP-23:47, EP-24:43, EP-25:47 gate on `dev_ready` in status.json. None of EP-17..20 depends on EP-21, so under the current gate all their dev tests are collected-then-skipped: the "--tier dev green" acceptance is satisfied vacuously. Corrections to the finding: (1) the dev.duckdb gate was prescribed by the EP-12 brief itself (roadmap/EP-12-fixtures-icu-markers.md:36-37, 79-80, 103) and echoed in DESIGN.md:817-826 and tests/README.md:27-33 — this is a roadmap-level inconsistency between EP-12 and EP-17..20 briefs, not an EP-12 session judgment call, and the hand-off at EP-12:202-203 openly states the skip; (2) severity downgraded high→medium: nothing fails or leaks, the worst outcome is deferred/vacuous dev coverage for four EPs plus an ad-hoc conftest edit by the EP-17 session — a small (S) conftest change; (3) EP-23..25 can be handled by an in-test skip since the catalog gate is already satisfied post-EP-21, so the "cannot express" claim is over-stated (a shared fixture is convenience, not necessity); (4) EP-16 is docs-only, so the code fix cannot land there — hotfix or EP-17 item 0. Line references in the finding otherwise verified accurate.

**Owner question (as posed by the reviewer).** Which readiness gate should tier('dev') mean? (a) keep 'dev.duckdb exists' (EP-17..20 dev tests stay skipped until EP-21; document that their '--tier dev green' is vacuous); (b) fixture-level readiness (`dev_catalog` / `raw_root` / `dev_ready`) with the hook only deselecting — recommended, small conftest change before EP-17; (c) marker kwargs. Recommendation: (b), landed as a tiny pre-EP-17 hotfix or inside EP-16 re-plan.

**Severity (verified):** medium · **Verdict:** confirmed-with-corrections (high confidence) · **Kind:** process · **When:** now-should · **Effort:** XS · **Retro episode:** EP-168 · **Lens:** verification tooling & test infrastructure (verify.py, roadmap_check, conftest tiers, tests/ep/*, pyproject test/lint config)

(The function-level variant test_verify_code_brief_without_test_module_is_2 already does this.) Alternatively compute the probe: first brief with tier != 'n/a' whose ep_test_module() is missing, skip if none.

**Corrected fix (verifier).** Replace mimicwarehouse/tests/ep/test_ep06.py:343-346 (the `verify EP-17` literal inside test_mwh_verify_usage_errors) with a crafted-roadmap probe: `rm = _make_roadmap(tmp_path); ws = tmp_path / "ws"; (ws / "tests" / "ep").mkdir(parents=True); monkeypatch.setattr(verify, "roadmap_dir", lambda: rm); monkeypatch.setattr(verify, "workspace_root", lambda: ws); result = runner.invoke(app, ["verify", "EP-1"]); assert result.exit_code == 2 and "test_ep01.py" in result.output and "code brief" in result.output` (add tmp_path + monkeypatch params to the test, or split into its own test to keep the other four usage-error asserts against the real roadmap). Prefer the crafted-roadmap variant (deterministic, no dependency on roadmap state). Effort XS (~10 min). Optionally add one line to roadmap/EP-17-stage-loader-core.md noting the probe was made roadmap-independent so EP-17 need not touch test_ep06.

EP-17 — the loader — is next in line" is present verbatim; the function-level sibling test_verify_code_brief_without_test_module_is_2 (lines 277-297) already uses _make_roadmap + tmp workspace, so the CLI-level probe is the only roadmap-state-dependent one. `git log --oneline -- tests/ep/test_ep06.py` lists 7 commits (0d1807d EP-6, 582bbd7 EP-164, 2702df1 EP-8, a6d4939 EP-9, 8418a53 EP-10, f1fccf1 EP-11, ac4f51e EP-12); `git show <sha> -- tests/ep/test_ep06.py | grep 'verify", "EP-'` confirms the probe literal was re-pointed in EP-8 (8->9), EP-9 (9->10), EP-10 (10->11), EP-11 (11->12), EP-12 (12->17). ### ARCH-9 — Fixture-tier builds through the DAG runner inherit the >= 100 GB free-space guard and write ~2 300 small files per test session; contradicts 'CI-like runs without credentials'

**Severity (verified):** low · **Verdict:** downgraded (high confidence) · **Kind:** forward-compat · **When:** now-should · **Effort:** S · **Retro episode:** EP-167 · **Lens:** data architecture parity/uniformity (lake / catalog / tier / DAG design vs EP-3/9/10/11/12 code)

**Files:** `roadmap/EP-19-stage-dag-runner.md:31-35`, `roadmap/EP-21-stage-catalog-builder.md:57-64`, `mimicwarehouse/src/mimicwarehouse/config.py:519,742-748,799`, `mimicwarehouse/DESIGN.md:127-129,196-198`, `mimicwarehouse/DECISIONS.md:505-525`

**Evidence.** DESIGN section 4 fixture row: "pytest default; CI-like runs without credentials". EP-19 item 2: `run(...)` "runs the >= 100 GB free-space guard; opens the build connection (EP-17)" for every tier including `fixture`; EP-21 item 4 re-points the session `fixture_catalog` at a real fixture lake built "into a session temp root ... via the runner API". config.py `min_free_gb: int = 100`. 100 buckets x 23 partitioned tables ~ 2 300 Parquet files + manifests written and deleted under `%TEMP%` per session; D-42 (2): "no burst copy / `sed -i` / delete loops over hundreds of files in scratch directories from an unsigned process" (python.exe is allow-listed by path, so this is a residual risk, not a blocker).

**Impact.** On any machine with < 100 GB free (a laptop, a future CI runner, the owner's own disk late in P7 when models + notes land) the whole fixture test tier fails at the guard before a single test runs; the 100-bucket layout makes the smallest possible fixture lake still a few thousand files per session.

**Proposed fix (reviewer).** Apply `require_free_space` only when the build's lake root is the credentialed lake (dev/full/demo) or scale it per tier (fixture: 1 GB); allow the fixture build connection to use the `app` memory profile; keep the fixture lake session-scoped (already planned) and consider a `--tier fixture` build cache keyed by fixture manifest hash under `<data_root>/lake/fixture` (ARCH-3) so most sessions do not rebuild it.

**Corrected fix (verifier).** Keep the guard, scope it per tier and document the escape hatch: (1) EP-19 brief item 2 — the runner calls `require_free_space(<effective data_root>, threshold)` where threshold = `settings.min_free_gb` for dev/full/demo and a small fixture floor (e.g. `min(settings.min_free_gb, 5)`) for `--tier fixture` (or a new `Settings.min_free_gb_fixture: int = 5`); one line in the brief + one line in DESIGN section 3 ("fixture builds guard at 5 GB; `MWH_MIN_FREE_GB` overrides everywhere"). (2) EP-21 brief item 4 — the session-scoped `fixture_catalog` builder must either set `MWH_MIN_FREE_GB` on the temp `Settings` it constructs or reuse EP-10's precedent (`tests/ep/test_ep10.py:91` monkeypatches `config.shutil.disk_usage` to 500 GB in the `workspace` fixture) at session scope, so the fixture tier stays green on low-disk laptops/CI without credentials. (3) DESIGN section 4 fixture row: append "set `MWH_MIN_FREE_GB` on machines with < 100 GB free" or, once (1) lands, nothing. Drop the "app memory profile" and "fixture lake cache under <data_root>/lake/fixture" sub-suggestions: DuckDB `memory_limit` is a cap not an allocation, so the 36 GB build profile is harmless for a 12 MB fixture; and a fixture lake under the credentialed data root contradicts DESIGN section 4 (fixture lives in the repo) — that is an owner decision belonging to ARCH-3, not a prerequisite here. The file-count concern is already recorded as a known trade-off in roadmap/EP-18-stage-loader-buckets.md:20 ("100 buckets x ~30 tables ~ 3 000 files vs NTFS/Defender overhead") and python.exe is allow-listed (D-42 (3)); no separate action beyond keeping the fixture lake session-scoped. Effort S (three doc lines now; code in EP-19/EP-21).

**Verifier notes.** Checked: roadmap/EP-19-stage-dag-runner.md:39-46 (runner "runs the >= 100 GB free-space guard" for every tier, fixture raw root = tests/fixtures, `--data-root` override) and :74-82; roadmap/EP-21-stage-catalog-builder.md:60-65 (session fixture_catalog builds the fixture lake into a temp root "via the runner API"); mimicwarehouse/DESIGN.md:120-129 (section 3 "mwh build additionally require min_free_gb (100)" with no tier exception; section 4 fixture row "CI-like runs without credentials"); DESIGN.md:88-91 (the rule is a machine disk-budget rule); DECISIONS.md:505-525 (D-42 items 2-3); config.py:433-440,519 (`require_free_space`, `min_free_gb=100`); inventory.py:732 (EP-10 already guards unconditionally) and tests/ep/test_ep10.py:80-91,168-176,522-527 (tests fake `shutil.disk_usage` at 500 GB — the existing precedent EP-21 can copy); tests/conftest.py:198-202 (current fixture_catalog is in-memory, no guard today); fixtures/spec.py:42,72-74 + tests/fixtures/README.md (120 consecutive subjects from 90_000_000 -> all 100 buckets populated); contract count via `uv run python -c` = 41 tables, 34 partitioned, 24 partitioned within hosp+icu -> worst case ~2 400 partition files (finding's ~2 300 is the right order of magnitude; icu tables cover fewer buckets); roadmap/EP-18:20 already names the file-count trade-off. Verified `MWH_MIN_FREE_GB=1` is honoured (`Settings().min_free_gb == 1`, source `env`), so the "whole fixture tier fails before a single test" scenario only materialises if EP-21 neither patches disk usage nor sets the env — a real design contradiction (fixture tier is defined as credential/machine-independent yet inherits a machine-specific 100 GB guard, undocumented escape hatch), but trivially mitigable and not affecting the owner's machine (~415 GB free), hence low rather than medium. Two of the proposed fixes were wrong or out of scope (memory profile is a cap; fixture cache under the data root contradicts DESIGN section 4).

### CFG-2 — No single DuckDB connection factory: two ad-hoc openers already exist and EP-17/EP-21 will add two more

**Severity (verified):** low · **Verdict:** downgraded (high confidence) · **Kind:** design · **When:** now-should · **Effort:** S · **Retro episode:** EP-167 · **Lens:** settings, CLI contract and host diagnostics (config.py / cli.py / doctor.py / .env.example / .streamlit / tests ep02-ep03-ep164)
