EP-17 item 1: "If EP-3 already ships a connection factory in config.py, wrap it — one implementation only" — it does not; EP-21 item 2 specifies a separate `open_catalog` with extra SET statements (autoinstall/autoload extensions off, disabled_filesystems) that the existing openers do not apply.

**Impact.** EP-17's `open_build_connection` becomes the third implementation, EP-21's `open_catalog` the fourth; the version-pin assertion, free-space guard, temp-dir creation (CFG-3) and the EP-21 hardening (`disabled_filesystems='HTTPFileSystem'`, extension autoload off) will be applied inconsistently — inventory and the fixture catalog will keep opening un-hardened connections.

**Proposed fix (reviewer).** Add `src/mimicwarehouse/engine.py` now (before EP-17): `connect(profile: 'build'|'app', database=':memory:'|Path, *, read_only=False, settings=None, memory_limit=None) -> DuckDBPyConnection` that (1) asserts duckdb.__version__ == pin, (2) mkdir(parents=True) the temp dir, (3) applies duckdb_settings(profile), (4) applies the EP-21 hardening SETs, (5) refuses `.new`/`.tmp` catalog paths; refactor inventory.open_connection, fixtures.catalog and the three tests to call it; amend EP-17 item 1 / EP-21 item 2 to "wrap engine.connect". Record in DESIGN §6/§15.

**Corrected fix (verifier).** Do not add a generic engine.py now. At the EP-16 re-plan (docs-only, ~15 min): (a) amend EP-17 item 1 to state plainly "config.py ships no connection factory (config.py:15); implement `open_build_connection` on a small shared helper `mimicwarehouse/engine.py::connect(profile, database=':memory:', *, read_only=False, settings=None, memory_limit=None)` that only applies `duckdb_settings(profile)` and the EP-21 hardening SETs (`autoinstall_known_extensions=false`, `autoload_known_extensions=false`, `disabled_filesystems='HTTPFileSystem'`), and refuses `.new` paths; keep the version-pin assert and `require_free_space()` in `open_build_connection` itself (build-only; the suite must keep passing on hosts with <100 GB free and must not mkdir under the real data root — CFG-3); refactor `inventory.open_connection` (inventory.py:353-360) and `fixtures/catalog.py:77-78` to call it, and replace the duplicated three lines in tests/ep/test_ep09.py:316-317 and test_ep11.py:154-155"; (b) amend EP-21 item 2 to "open_catalog wraps engine.connect('app', path, read_only=True) and adds the recorded-version check + role"; (c) EP-21 item 6/verify: re-point tests/ep/test_ep12.py:868/883 (and any later dev/full smoke tests) at `open_catalog(tier)` instead of bare `duckdb.connect(str(path), read_only=True)`; (d) one dated line in DESIGN §6/§15 naming engine.py as the single place settings + hardening are applied. Effort: S in EP-16 (doc edits) + S inside EP-17 (code).

**Verifier notes.** Checked: src/mimicwarehouse/config.py:1-40 and 600-660 (docstring line 15 "EP-17/EP-21 apply it — nothing here opens a connection"; duckdb_settings only returns a dict) — evidence holds. inventory.py:353-360 open_connection(build profile, :memory:, config=duckdb_settings) — holds. fixtures/catalog.py:60-95 duckdb.connect(":memory:", config=duckdb_settings("app")) — holds. tests/ep/test_ep09.py:316-317 and test_ep11.py:154-155 repeat the same three lines — holds (connect is on 317/155, dict on 316/154). tests/ep/test_ep12.py:868 and :883 open dev/full catalogs with duckdb.connect(str(path), read_only=True) and no config — holds, but conftest.py:107-118/156 skips these until dev.duckdb/full.duckdb exist (EP-21) and they only run SELECT 1, so the DESIGN §6 breach is nominal; test_ep01.py:94 `duckdb.connect()` (no config) is a further unlisted instance. roadmap/EP-17-stage-loader-core.md:26-34: open_build_connection in loader/engine.py, "If EP-3 already ships a connection factory in config.py, wrap it" — the conditional simply resolves to "it does not; implement it", so no downstream EP trips on it. roadmap/EP-21-stage-catalog-builder.md:45-52 open_catalog in catalog/connect.py with the hardening SETs, and item 4 (:60-66) already re-points the conftest fixture_catalog at open_catalog("fixture") keeping build_fixture_catalog only as fallback — so the roadmap already converges the fixture path. Why downgraded: (1) both existing production openers apply duckdb_settings correctly (DESIGN §6:191-198 satisfied); the only real gap is 3 duplicated lines in tests and no shared home for the EP-21 hardening — inventory reads only local CSVs (HTTPFileSystem/extension autoload moot) and the fixture catalog is synthetic in-memory, so the "un-hardened connections" impact is imaginary today. (2) The roadmap deliberately splits build (writer) vs catalog (read-only) openers into different subpackages; two openers with different semantics is by design, not drift. (3) The proposed generic connect() is over-scoped: version-pin is already asserted globally by tests/ep/test_ep01.py:53-60 (DESIGN §6 note :207-210); require_free_space() and temp-dir mkdir are build-only concerns per config.py:17-24 and must not run for in-memory test connections (config.py header: "the suite passes on any host"; would also touch the real data root from unit tests). (4) Effort S for engine.py + 2 module refactors + 3 test edits + brief/DESIGN amendments + its own tests is optimistic; as a re-plan doc amendment it is genuinely S. Net: real low-severity consolidation item, best handled as an EP-16 re-plan amendment to EP-17 item 1 / EP-21 item 2 rather than new code before EP-13.

### CFG-3 — duckdb_settings() hands out a temp_directory DuckDB will not create when its parent is missing; doctor reports that case as pass

**Severity (verified):** low · **Verdict:** confirmed-with-corrections (high confidence) · **Kind:** bug · **When:** now-should · **Effort:** XS · **Retro episode:** EP-167 · **Lens:** settings, CLI contract and host diagnostics (config.py / cli.py / doctor.py / .env.example / .streamlit / tests ep02-ep03-ep164)

**Files:** `mimicwarehouse/src/mimicwarehouse/config.py:637`, `mimicwarehouse/src/mimicwarehouse/doctor.py:516`, `mimicwarehouse/src/mimicwarehouse/inventory.py:360`, `mimicwarehouse/src/mimicwarehouse/fixtures/catalog.py:78`

**Evidence.** Scratch script against duckdb 1.5.5 with temp_directory=<fresh tmp>/root/tmp/duckdb (parent `tmp` absent), memory_limit 20MB, 3M-row ORDER BY: `IOException IO Error: Failed to create directory "...\root\tmp\duckdb": The system cannot find the path specified.` — connect() succeeds, the failure surfaces mid-query on first spill. doctor.py:516-521 returns CheckResult("temp_dir", "pass", "... missing but creatable — `mwh paths --create` makes it").

**Impact.** Any process that opens a build/app connection on a data root that never had `mwh paths --create` (EP-21's fixture-tier temp roots via `--data-root <tmp>`, a collaborator's first `mwh build`, EP-17's `<data_root>\tmp\ep17` temp lake if tmp/ is absent) runs until memory pressure and then dies with an IO error; the doctor said pass.

**Proposed fix (reviewer).** In the connection factory (CFG-2) — or, minimally, in `Settings.duckdb_settings()` callers — `self.layout['tmp_duckdb'].mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)` before connect; change doctor `check_temp_dir` missing-but-creatable to `warn` ("missing — run mwh paths --create or the first connection creates it"); add a test that connects with a fresh tmp root and forces a small spill.

**Corrected fix (verifier).** Do NOT put the mkdir inside `Settings.duckdb_settings()` (it is a pure accessor also evaluated by `mwh doctor`/`mwh paths` on unchecked, possibly refused roots — `load_settings(checked=False)`); instead call `settings.layout['tmp_duckdb'].mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)` at the connection sites: `inventory.open_connection` (src/mimicwarehouse/inventory.py:353-360), `fixtures/catalog.open_fixture_catalog` (src/mimicwarehouse/fixtures/catalog.py:77-78), and make it a stated requirement of EP-17's `open_build_connection` factory (roadmap/EP-17-stage-loader-core.md:26-33: "wrap EP-3's factory if any" — add "ensure temp_directory exists (parents=True) before connect"). Refine `doctor.check_temp_dir` (src/mimicwarehouse/doctor.py:510-521): DuckDB 1.5.5 creates the *leaf* temp dir itself but not missing parents, so keep `pass` when `temp_dir.parent.is_dir()` (detail: "missing; DuckDB creates it on first spill") and return `warn` when the parent is missing ("missing with missing parent — run `mwh paths --create`, or the first spill fails with IOException"). Update tests/ep/test_ep03.py:495 (currently asserts status == 'pass' and 'creatable' in detail for a fresh tmp root — that is exactly the parent-missing case, so it must become 'warn') and add a spill test: connect with `duckdb_settings` on a fresh `tmp_path` root, memory_limit '20MB', `SELECT count(*) FROM (SELECT * FROM range(3000000) ORDER BY random())` — must succeed after the fix (a few seconds). Effort XS–S (the test edit + 2 mkdir lines + doctor branch). Also add one sentence to DESIGN.md §6 note: "connection factories create `tmp/duckdb` (parents) before connect; DuckDB itself only creates the leaf".

**Verifier notes.** Checked: config.py:599-642 (`layout['tmp_duckdb']`, `duckdb_settings` returns the path string only, no mkdir; `create_layout` at 719-733 is the only place the 15 dirs are made and it is reached solely from `mwh paths --create`, config.py:804); doctor.py:490-521 (`check_temp_dir` returns `pass` "missing but creatable" whenever the nearest existing ancestor is writable — including when `<root>/tmp` itself is absent); inventory.py:353-360 and fixtures/catalog.py:77-78 (both `duckdb.connect(':memory:', config=duckdb_settings(...))` with no mkdir; inventory ensures only `lake/manifests/raw` at 493-496/738); tests/ep/test_ep03.py:490-495 asserts the doctor pass+creatable behaviour on a fresh tmp root. Reproduced the evidence with a scratch script under duckdb 1.5.5 (three cases, memory_limit 20MB, 3M-row ORDER BY): A parent `tmp` missing → connect ok, query fails `IOException IO Error: Failed to create directory "...\rootA\tmp\duckdb": The system cannot find the path specified.`; B parent exists / leaf missing → query ok and DuckDB created the leaf; C exists → ok. So the bug is real, but narrower than stated: DuckDB does create the leaf, only missing parents break, and the failure needs an actual spill. Impact corrections: (1) the EP-17 example (`<data_root>\tmp\ep17`, roadmap/EP-17-stage-loader-core.md:82-85) runs on the owner's real root, which already has `tmp/` from EP-3's `mwh paths --create` (EP-3 completion note), and creating `tmp\ep17` with parents=True would make `tmp` exist anyway → not a trip; (2) EP-21 §4 (`--data-root <tmp>` fixture-tier session root, roadmap/EP-21:60-66) has a fresh root but the 5 MiB fixture never spills under a multi-GB memory_limit → latent, not a failure; (3) the realistic trip is a collaborator/fresh root running a dev/full build before `mwh paths --create` (README quick start does say to run it) — no brief for EP-17/19/21 says the loader/runner creates the layout, so nothing downstream closes the gap. Hence real defect + misleading doctor `pass`, but severity low; fix location must move out of `duckdb_settings()` (accessor used on unchecked roots) into the connect sites/factory, and test_ep03.py:495 must be updated with the doctor change.

### CFG-4 — `--data-root` override is process-local: not seen by `mwh verify`'s pytest child, spawn workers or background jobs

**Severity (verified):** low · **Verdict:** confirmed-with-corrections (high confidence) · **Kind:** bug · **When:** now-should · **Effort:** XS · **Retro episode:** EP-167 · **Lens:** settings, CLI contract and host diagnostics (config.py / cli.py / doctor.py / .env.example / .streamlit / tests ep02-ep03-ep164)

**Files:** `mimicwarehouse/src/mimicwarehouse/config.py:662`, `mimicwarehouse/src/mimicwarehouse/cli.py:107`, `mimicwarehouse/src/mimicwarehouse/verify.py:656`, `mimicwarehouse/tests/conftest.py:107`

**Evidence.** config.py:662-670 `configure()` only fills the module dict `_overrides` and clears the cache; cli.py:107-108 `config.configure(**overrides)`; verify.py:656 `subprocess.run(pytest_argv(n, pytest_args), cwd=workspace, check=False)` passes no env; conftest.py:107-118 `catalog_status` calls `get_settings().catalog_path(tier)` in that child. grep for MWH_DATA_ROOT/environ in verify.py: no hits.

**Impact.** `mwh --data-root <tmp> verify EP-12 -- --tier dev` looks for C:\mimicdata\warehouse\dev.duckdb, not <tmp>; EP-18 spawn workers and EP-19 `mwh build --background` (a re-invoked `mwh`) will silently use the env/default root while the parent used the flag — a build split across two data roots.

**Proposed fix (reviewer).** In `cli.main` (or `configure`), when `--data-root` is given also `os.environ['MWH_DATA_ROOT'] = str(data_root)` so every child (pytest, spawn, background mwh) inherits it; document in cli.py docstring and DESIGN §15; add a test asserting the env var is set after the callback and that verify's child argv/env carries it.

**Corrected fix (verifier).** Keep the mechanism finding, narrow the impact and scope the fix: (1) mimicwarehouse/src/mimicwarehouse/verify.py:656 — when the CLI was invoked with --data-root (available as ctx.obj.data_root_override / CliState in verify_command), run pytest with env={**os.environ, "MWH_DATA_ROOT": str(settings.data_root)} (add an optional `env`/`data_root` parameter to verify()/pytest_argv-adjacent code so test_ep06 can assert it), instead of mutating os.environ globally in cli.main — 22 CliRunner-based test invocations in tests/ep/test_ep{02,03,04,09,164}.py pass --data-root in-process and their fixtures use monkeypatch.delenv("MWH_DATA_ROOT", raising=False), which does NOT restore a variable that was absent, so a callback that sets os.environ would leak the tmp root into later tests. (2) One sentence in cli.py's module docstring and DESIGN.md §15 (dated note): "--data-root is process-local (config._overrides); child interpreters — mwh verify's pytest, EP-19 background `mwh` re-invocations, any spawn worker — only see MWH_DATA_ROOT / .env / mwh.toml, so children must be handed the root explicitly (env or argv)". (3) Add a line to roadmap/EP-19-stage-dag-runner.md In-scope 5/6: `jobs.launch` must forward the effective data root to the detached `uv run mwh …` (repeat the global `--data-root` before the subcommand, or set MWH_DATA_ROOT in the Popen env); note that the brief's `mwh build … [--data-root PATH]` signature places the option after the subcommand while it is a callback-level global option today. Drop the "EP-18 spawn workers" claim (EP-17/18 briefs use DuckDB-internal parallelism only; parallel bucket sorting is parked). Effort XS (~20 min incl. one test).

**Verifier notes.** Checked config.py:649-668 (`_overrides` dict + `configure()` clears cache only), cli.py:107-117 (`config.configure(**overrides)`, CliState.data_root_override), verify.py:613-624 and 656 (`subprocess.run(pytest_argv(...), cwd=workspace, check=False)`; grep for environ/MWH_DATA_ROOT/env= in verify.py: no hits), tests/conftest.py:105-116 (`catalog_status` → `get_settings().catalog_path(tier)` in the child), test_ep12.py:744-748/858-880 (tier dev/full tests). Ran a scratch probe (uv run python …/cfg4_probe.py, no data root touched): after `configure(data_root=A)` the parent's get_settings().data_root == A, MWH_DATA_ROOT stays unset, and a child interpreter with MWH_DATA_ROOT=B resolves B — mechanism confirmed. Corrections: (a) impact today is nil — verify is a DIAGNOSTIC command, dev.duckdb exists at no root yet (EP-21), the tests' own precedent (test_ep12 isolated_root, EP-19/21 briefs) uses MWH_DATA_ROOT env, and cli.py's docstring says only "process-wide" (accurate, not a false promise) — so medium is over-rated; (b) the EP-18 spawn-worker scenario is not in the EP-17/18 briefs (only `parallel=true` inside DuckDB read_csv; parallel bucket sorting parked at EP-18 line 96); (c) the EP-19 hazard is real (`launch()` re-invokes `uv run mwh …` and the brief lists `--data-root PATH` on `mwh build`), but it is a future-brief note, not a present bug; (d) the proposed fix (set os.environ in cli.main) would leak across in-process CliRunner tests because monkeypatch.delenv on an absent var records nothing to restore — hence forward env at the subprocess call site in verify.py instead.

### CFG-6 — Console encoding handled three ways; piped output is cp1252 while every consumer decodes UTF-8; Risk 13 wording inaccurate

**Severity (verified):** low · **Verdict:** confirmed-with-corrections (high confidence) · **Kind:** environment · **When:** owner-decision · **Effort:** S · **Retro episode:** EP-167 · **Lens:** settings, CLI contract and host diagnostics (config.py / cli.py / doctor.py / .env.example / .streamlit / tests ep02-ep03-ep164)

**Files:** `mimicwarehouse/src/mimicwarehouse/cli.py:55`, `mimicwarehouse/src/mimicwarehouse/doctor.py:845`, `mimicwarehouse/src/mimicwarehouse/verify.py:673`, `mimicwarehouse/src/mimicwarehouse/config.py:820`, `mimicwarehouse/src/mimicwarehouse/inventory.py:1100`, `mimicwarehouse/src/mimicwarehouse/schema/cli.py:32`, `mimicwarehouse/src/mimicwarehouse/fixtures/cli.py:33`, `roadmap/README.md:395`, `mimicwarehouse/DESIGN.md:65`

**Evidence.** `uv run python -c 'import sys; print(sys.stdout.encoding)'` under the Bash tool -> cp1252 (piped); `mwh --help` renders "mimicwarehouse � local", `mwh doctor` summary "8 pass � 1 warn � 0 fail � 5 info � OK", callback errors "GOVERNANCE �2" — the bytes are valid cp1252 (0xB7/0x97/0xA7) that the reader decodes as UTF-8. Mechanisms: verify.py:673 `_console_safe` (sys.stdout.encoding), doctor.py:845 `utf = console.encoding...startswith('utf')` glyph switch, cli/config/inventory/schema/fixtures none; five separate `Console()` instances (cli:55, inventory:1100-1101, schema/cli:32-33, fixtures/cli:33-34, verify:822). Scan of src for non-cp1252 glyphs in string literals: only doctor.py:797 (guarded) and verify.py:293-549 (guarded); `·`/`—`/`§`/`…` are cp1252-encodable, so Risk 13's "`·`, `—` raise UnicodeEncodeError" (roadmap/README.md:395) is inaccurate — the EP-3/EP-6 crashers were `≥`, `☑`, `⏱`.

**Impact.** Every log line the Bash tool, VS Code, git hooks and EP-19 job logs capture shows replacement characters; each new module re-invents (or forgets) the guard; a future `≥`/`→` in an unguarded module crashes rich on a piped stdout (roadmap Risk 13 already recorded two incidents).

**Proposed fix (reviewer).** Owner decision (below). Recommended: (1) pyproject `mwh = "mimicwarehouse.cli:main"` where `main()` does `for s in (sys.stdout, sys.stderr): s.reconfigure(encoding='utf-8', errors='replace')` (only affects piped/redirected streams; a real Windows console is already UTF-16 via WindowsConsoleIO) then `app()`; (2) one `mimicwarehouse/console.py` exporting `console`, `err_console`, `console_safe()` used by every command module; (3) reword Risk 13 / DESIGN §2 note ("`≥ ☑ ⏱ ✓` crash on cp1252; `· — §` render as � only when the reader decodes UTF-8"); (4) note in README that PowerShell users who capture output set `[Console]::OutputEncoding = [Text.Encoding]::UTF8`.

**Corrected fix (verifier).** Owner decision still needed, but with these corrections: (0) Scope the problem correctly — the Claude Code PowerShell tool already exports PYTHONIOENCODING=utf-8:surrogateescape (verified: `Get-ChildItem env:` shows it; `uv run mwh doctor` under the PowerShell tool prints "8 pass · 1 warn · 0 fail · 5 info — OK" cleanly), so only the Bash tool, Git-for-Windows hooks launched from bash, plain cmd/pwsh without the profile line, and `> file` redirects see cp1252 (`sys.stdout.encoding` = cp1252, errors=strict; `print('≥')` raises UnicodeEncodeError; `mwh --help` emits byte 0x97 for `—`). Reword the impact to that set. (1) If option A: `main` already exists at cli.py:86 as the typer root callback, so name the entry wrapper differently (e.g. `def run(): for s in (sys.stdout, sys.stderr): s.reconfigure(encoding="utf-8", errors="replace"); app()` and pyproject.toml:37 `mwh = "mimicwarehouse.cli:run"`), and do the reconfigure before any `Console()` is created. (2) Shared console module: today there are 9 instances at 5 sites (cli.py:55; inventory.py:1100-1101, schema/cli.py:32-33, fixtures/cli.py:33-34 each `console`+`err_console`; verify.py:822 ad hoc) — consolidate into one `mimicwarehouse/console.py`. (3) Risk 13 (roadmap/README.md:395) reword: "`≥ ☑ ⏱ ✓ ✗ → ↔` raise UnicodeEncodeError on a cp1252 stream (EP-3 `≥` in mwh paths, EP-6 `⏱`/`☑` in verify); `· — § …` are cp1252-encodable and merely render as U+FFFD when the reader (Bash tool, VS Code log viewer, Read tool) decodes UTF-8; the PowerShell tool is unaffected because it sets PYTHONIOENCODING". Mirror in DESIGN.md:65-66 and mention doctor.py:845's `utf` glyph switch as the third mechanism. (4) Add option D to the owner question: set `env.PYTHONIOENCODING=utf-8` in .claude/settings.json (CLAUDE.md §6 ask-before file) so the Bash tool matches the PowerShell tool — zero code change, but does not help git hooks/pwsh outside Claude Code. (5) Note PEP 686: Python 3.15 defaults to UTF-8 mode; pyproject pins `>=3.13,<3.14`, so whichever option is chosen is interim. (6) Low-priority: schema/contract.py:304 and :309 carry `→` in pydantic Field descriptions — harmless today (schema/cli.py never prints descriptions) but would crash a future `schema show --json-schema` on cp1252; keep in the ASCII-or-guard rule.

**Verifier notes.** Checked: cli.py:55-62 (Console(), help string with `—`), cli.py:86 (`main` is the typer callback), pyproject.toml:37 (`mwh = "mimicwarehouse.cli:app"`, requires-python >=3.13,<3.14); doctor.py:797-846 (_GLYPHS_UTF/_ASCII, `utf` switch keyed on console.encoding, summary_line uses `·` and `—`); verify.py:673-676 (_console_safe on sys.stdout.encoding), verify.py:822 (ad-hoc Console()); inventory.py:1100-1101, schema/cli.py:32-33, fixtures/cli.py:33-34 (console + err_console each, no guard); guard.py:717-794 (uses cli.console, ASCII messages). Ran under the Bash tool: `uv run python -c` -> stdout/stderr cp1252, `≥` raised UnicodeEncodeError, `· — § …` encode to 0xB7/0x97/0xA7/0x85; `mwh --help | od -c` shows byte 227(octal)=0x97 for `—`; `mwh doctor` summary shows U+FFFD without PYTHONIOENCODING and clean with PYTHONIOENCODING=utf-8; Bash tool env has no PYTHON*/LANG vars. Ran under the PowerShell tool: [Console]::OutputEncoding utf-8, pwsh 7.6.5, env PYTHONIOENCODING=utf-8:surrogateescape present, sys.stdout.encoding utf-8 (piped, utf8_mode 0, locale cp1252), `mwh doctor` summary renders correctly. Scratch scan of src/**/*.py for non-cp1252 chars: only doctor.py:797 (guarded), verify.py:293/522-549 (guarded via _console_safe), schema/contract.py:304,309 Field descriptions (unprinted), rest docstrings/comments. Docs: roadmap/README.md:395 Risk 13 says `·`, `—` raise UnicodeEncodeError (inaccurate; verified encodable); DESIGN.md:65-66 says ASCII-or-_console_safe (omits doctor's utf switch); EP-3 note line 159 (`≥` crasher) and EP-6 note line 112 (`⏱`/`☑`) are accurate. Verdict: the mechanics, inconsistency and Risk 13 wording drift are real; severity lowered because the PowerShell tool is already clean, current code has no unguarded crasher glyphs, and the visible effect is cosmetic replacement characters in Bash-tool/redirected output; the fix needs the name-collision and option-D corrections above.

**Owner question (as posed by the reviewer).** Which single strategy for CLI text output? (A) reconfigure stdout/stderr to UTF-8 at the mwh entry point + one shared console module (matches Bash tool, git hooks, VS Code, log files; PowerShell capture needs the profile line Risk 13 already offers); (B) enforce ASCII-only CLI strings with a test; (C) keep per-module guards. Recommendation: A, keeping `console_safe` as belt-and-braces for exotic terminals.

### ENV-1 — 'uv not on tool PATH' is a stale process environment (VS Code predates the uv install), not a permanent machine fact

**Severity (verified):** low · **Verdict:** confirmed-with-corrections (high confidence) · **Kind:** environment · **When:** now-should · **Effort:** XS · **Retro episode:** owner · **Lens:** Local development / session environment as it really is vs. what the roadmap assumed

**Files:** `mimicwarehouse/README.md:33`, `roadmap/EP-8-mimic-code-vendoring.md:122`, `.pre-commit-config.yaml:16`

**Evidence.** Bash tool: `which uv` -> not found; PowerShell tool: `(Get-Command uv).Source` -> empty. But `[Environment]::GetEnvironmentVariable('Path','User')` ends with `C:\Users\willi\AppData\Local\Microsoft\WinGet\Links` ("USER has WinGet Links: True"). Process ancestry: `pwsh (pid 40776) started 08/18/26 02:29:50 <- claude (pid 6416) started 02:02:26 <- Code (pid 44932) started 08/17/26 04:46:21 PM`; `uv.exe LastWriteTime 08/17/2026 17:30:56`. EP-8 note: "`uv` was reached via `%LOCALAPPDATA%\Microsoft\WinGet\Links`"; memory note: "uv not on tool PATH (export before git commit too)".

**Impact.** Every session re-discovers the workaround; any `git commit` from a tool shell without the export dies inside the pre-commit hook because the local hooks are `entry: uv run --project mimicwarehouse ...` (uv resolved from the git process PATH). A future session that forgets the prefix will misreport 'uv missing' or fail a commit mid-EP.

**Proposed fix (reviewer).** Owner action (XS): quit and relaunch VS Code (or reboot) so the Claude Code extension and its tool shells inherit the current User PATH; verify with `which uv` in the Bash tool. Repo edit (XS): add one line to CLAUDE.md §3: 'If `uv` is not found in the tool shell, prefix `export PATH="$LOCALAPPDATA/Microsoft/WinGet/Links:$PATH"` (Bash) / `$env:PATH="$env:LOCALAPPDATA\Microsoft\WinGet\Links;$env:PATH"` (PowerShell) — this is a stale-process symptom; restart VS Code to clear it.' Update the memory note afterwards.

**Corrected fix (verifier).** Owner action (XS): close and relaunch VS Code from Explorer/Start (a reboot is not needed; Explorer already received the PATH-change broadcast, so any newly launched VS Code inherits the User PATH that now ends with %LOCALAPPDATA%\Microsoft\WinGet\Links). Verify in a fresh session with `which uv` (Bash tool) / `(Get-Command uv).Source` (PowerShell tool). Repo edit (XS, one line in CLAUDE.md section 3 next to the `uv run --project ...` bullet): "If the tool shell reports `uv` not found, prefix `export PATH=\"$LOCALAPPDATA/Microsoft/WinGet/Links:$PATH\"` (Bash) or `$env:PATH=\"$env:LOCALAPPDATA\Microsoft\WinGet\Links;$env:PATH\"` (PowerShell) for that call and for `git commit` (the pre-commit local hooks resolve `uv` from the git process PATH). This is a stale-process symptom of a VS Code launched before uv was installed; restarting VS Code clears it." Then trim the memory note (MEMORY.md line 4 and project-mimicwarehouse-status.md line 34) from "uv is NOT on the tool shells' PATH" to "may be missing after a stale VS Code launch; see CLAUDE.md section 3". Do NOT touch mimicwarehouse/README.md:31-33 -- it already says "one-time; then reopen the shell", which is the correct instruction, not drift.

**Verifier notes.** Checked: Bash tool `which uv` -> not found, PATH has no WinGet\Links entry; PowerShell: User PATH contains WinGet\Links = True, process PATH = False, Get-Command uv empty. Process chain: pwsh <- claude pid 6416 (08/18 02:02) <- Code pid 44932 (08/17 16:46:21) <- explorer (08/15). `%LOCALAPPDATA%\Microsoft\WinGet\Links\` directory itself and uv.exe/uvx.exe/uvw.exe symlinks are all timestamped 2026-08-17 17:30 (the Links dir was created by the uv winget install, i.e. the User PATH entry was added 44 min after VS Code started), so the stale-process diagnosis is correct and no other cause (e.g. missing User PATH entry) applies. Pre-commit is installed in .git/hooks/pre-commit and .pre-commit-config.yaml lines 16/22/27 use `entry: uv run --project mimicwarehouse ...` with `language: system`, so a `git commit` from a tool shell without the prefix does fail in the hook -- the memory note "export before git commit too" corroborates this happened. Corrections: (1) impact "every session re-discovers the workaround" is overstated -- MEMORY.md line 4 and project-mimicwarehouse-status.md line 34 already persist the exact prefix, and EP-8..EP-12 all completed with it, so no EP is at real risk; hence severity low, not medium. (2) mimicwarehouse/README.md:33 is not stale ("then reopen the shell" is exactly right); citing it as a file to fix is wrong. (3) EP-1 completion note lines 170-171 ("no further PATH change was needed") is accurate for a fresh shell. (4) Reboot unnecessary; VS Code relaunch from Explorer/Start suffices because winget broadcasts WM_SETTINGCHANGE. Restart recommended plus the durable CLAUDE.md fallback line, as the finding proposes.

**Owner question (as posed by the reviewer).** Restart VS Code now (recommended, zero cost) or keep documenting the PATH prefix as permanent? Recommendation: restart, then keep the prefix line in CLAUDE.md as the fallback.

### FC-10 — EP-19 job launcher: `uv run` is not on the tool PATH, `--data-root PATH` duplicates the global option, and the fallback recipe writes to the drive root when MWH_DATA_ROOT is unset

**Severity (verified):** low · **Verdict:** confirmed-with-corrections (high confidence) · **Kind:** bug · **When:** now-should · **Effort:** S · **Retro episode:** EP-170 · **Lens:** forward-compat: pending briefs (EP-13..EP-56) vs shipped P0/P1 code

**Files:** `roadmap/EP-19-stage-dag-runner.md:64-74`, `roadmap/EP-19-stage-dag-runner.md:75`, `mimicwarehouse/src/mimicwarehouse/cli.py:88-100`, `roadmap/EP-16-replan-p1.md:20-23`

**Evidence.** EP-19 item 5: `launch(argv, job)` "spawns `uv run --group dev mwh …` detached" and the documented fallback `Start-Process pwsh … 'uv run --group dev mwh build … *> $env:MWH_DATA_ROOT\runs\jobs\<name>.log'`. EP-16 amendment (3): "No `MWH_DATA_ROOT` env var exists on this machine" (so `$env:MWH_DATA_ROOT\runs\…` expands to `\runs\jobs\…` = C:\runs). Persistent memory: uv is not on the tool PATH (sessions `export PATH="$LOCALAPPDATA/Microsoft/WinGet/Links:$PATH"`). cli.py already has a global `--data-root` on the app callback; EP-19 adds `[--data-root PATH]` to `mwh build`.

**Impact.** A detached child that execs `uv` by name fails when the parent shell lacked the PATH export; the pwsh fallback silently logs to `C:\runs\jobs`; typer will expose two `--data-root` options with different semantics.

**Proposed fix (reviewer).** Launch the child as `[sys.executable, "-m", "mimicwarehouse.cli", …]` (the allow-listed .venv python, D-42) or resolve uv via `shutil.which` with the WinGet Links fallback; write the fallback recipe with `(uv run mwh paths --json)`/`settings.layout['runs_jobs']` instead of `$env:MWH_DATA_ROOT`; drop the per-command `--data-root` and document `mwh --data-root X build …`.

**Corrected fix (verifier).** Amend roadmap/EP-19-stage-dag-runner.md (S, at EP-19 start or the P2 re-plan): (1) item 5, line 65-66: launch the child as `[sys.executable, "-m", "mimicwarehouse.cli", *argv]` (cli.py:130-131 has `if __name__ == "__main__": app()`, and sys.executable is the allow-listed .venv python.exe of D-42(3)) or `shutil.which("mwh")` (the .venv Scripts entry point is on PATH inside any `uv run` process even when `uv` itself is not) — never `["uv", "run", ...]` by bare name; bonus: JobInfo.pid becomes the real python pid instead of a uv wrapper pid, so psutil liveness/RSS are accurate. (2) line 73: replace the `*> $env:MWH_DATA_ROOT\runs\jobs\<name>.log` recipe with the already-amended EP-10 recipe (roadmap/EP-10-*.md lines 93-97: `$paths = uv run --group dev mwh paths --json | ConvertFrom-Json; $jobs = ($paths.layout | Where-Object key -eq 'runs_jobs').path; Start-Process uv -ArgumentList @('run','--group','dev','mwh','build',...) -RedirectStandardOutput "$jobs\<name>.out" -RedirectStandardError "$jobs\<name>.err"`), which is proven on this host (EP-10 completion note lines 156-160); also fix line 15 and line 100 `%MWH_DATA_ROOT%` to `<data_root>` per the EP-16 amendment (3) wording. (3) `--data-root`: do NOT simply drop the per-command option — EP-20:43, EP-21:62 and EP-23:43 all spell `mwh build --tier fixture … --data-root <tmp>` (trailing form). Owner decision: (a) global-only form `mwh --data-root <tmp> build …` (consistent with EP-2/EP-3/DESIGN.md:401; edit EP-19:75 + those three briefs, S), or (b) keep the per-command option as an alias that calls `config.configure(data_root=…)`/`load_settings` and note that the app callback has already loaded+validated the default root (`C:\mimicdata`) before `build`'s option is parsed. Recommend (a).

**Verifier notes.** Checked roadmap/EP-19-stage-dag-runner.md lines 1-30, 50-80, 100; roadmap/EP-16-replan-p1.md lines 10-35; roadmap/EP-10-*.md amendment lines 5-14 and recipe lines 93-97, completion note 151-160; mimicwarehouse/src/mimicwarehouse/cli.py 60-131 (global --data-root on app callback, __main__ guard); DECISIONS.md D-42; grepped all briefs for --data-root and MWH_DATA_ROOT. Ran read-only probes: `which uv` in the tool shell = not on PATH; invoking uv by absolute WinGet path and asking the child `shutil.which('uv')` -> None while `shutil.which('mwh')` -> .venv\Scripts\mwh.EXE and sys.executable -> .venv python — so a `Popen(["uv", ...])` from a `mwh build --background` process fails ONLY when the launching uv was itself invoked by absolute path / a shell without WinGet Links; with the documented `export PATH=…` the child inherits uv, so the PATH sub-claim is a robustness edge, not a certain failure. `$env:MWH_DATA_ROOT` is empty on this host and `C:\runs` does not exist; a pwsh `*>` redirect into a nonexistent directory throws `Out-File: Could not find a part of the path` before the command runs — so the fallback recipe silently does nothing (in a Start-Process pwsh window that closes), it does not 'silently log to C:\runs\jobs' as the finding states. This exact bug was already fixed in the EP-10 brief at the EP-7 re-plan (and generically covered by EP-16 amendment (3)), but EP-19 lines 15/73/100 were never amended — real doc drift. The --data-root duplication is real (typer will expose both), but the proposed fix (drop per-command form) would break EP-20/21/23 which use the trailing form, so it needs an owner choice and multi-brief edit. Net: all three sub-issues exist, two are over-stated in impact, none block work (each surfaces immediately when tried); severity low, fix at EP-19 start / P2 re-plan, effort S.

### FC-12 — EP-20/EP-28 full-tier reconciliation assumes a validate.sql count for every table — provider, caregiver, ingredientevents have none; '3.1 delta' clauses are moot

**Severity (verified):** low · **Verdict:** downgraded (high confidence) · **Kind:** docs-drift · **When:** now-should · **Effort:** XS · **Retro episode:** EP-170 · **Lens:** forward-compat: pending briefs (EP-13..EP-56) vs shipped P0/P1 code

**Files:** `roadmap/EP-20-stage-small-tables.md:49-56`, `roadmap/EP-28-stage-verify-full.md:27-33`, `roadmap/EP-10-raw-inventory.md:175-181`, `mimicwarehouse/DESIGN.md:622-624`

**Evidence.** EP-20 item 4: "for each of the 20 tables `status.json.rows` equals the `validate.sql` expected count (`mimicwarehouse.inventory.expected_counts()`, EP-10) … If `validate.sql` predates 3.1 for a table (EP-10's completion note says which), record the delta". EP-10 completion note: "34 match · 0 mismatch · 7 no-expectation … the seven without an upstream expectation are `provider` 42,244, `caregiver` 17,984, `ingredientevents` 14,253,480, …" and validate.sql "targets 3.1, so the 3.0-vs-3.1 orphan-subject deltas … do not arise". DESIGN: `expected_counts(dataset)` → "MIMIC-IV 3.1 → 28 tables".

**Impact.** EP-20's `tier("full")` test as written KeyErrors/fails on provider and caregiver (both in its 20); EP-27/EP-28 same for ingredientevents; sessions waste time looking for a '3.1 delta' that EP-10 proved does not exist.

**Proposed fix (reviewer).** EP-16 pickup note on EP-20/27/28: reconcile against validate.sql where `expected_counts()` has an entry, else against EP-10's raw manifest rows only; delete the 3.1-delta clauses (EP-10: 0 mismatches).

**Corrected fix (verifier).** EP-16 item 6 pickup note on EP-20 (item 4, lines 49-57) and EP-28 (item 3, lines 30-31), optionally EP-27: "Reconcile `status.json.rows` against `expected_counts()` only where the contract table has `expected_rows_source` set (28 hosp/icu tables); `provider`, `caregiver`, `ingredientevents` are `expected_rows_source: null` in the contract and `no-expectation` in EP-10's reconcile — for those compare against the EP-10 raw manifest rows only (or reuse `mimicwarehouse.inventory.reconcile()`'s status semantics). EP-10 found 34 match / 0 mismatch against the 3.1 validate.sql, so the 'validate.sql predates 3.1 / record the 3.1 delta' clauses are void — strike them." Effort XS (two short pickup-note blocks); do it in EP-16 alongside the other P2 pickup notes.

**Verifier notes.** Checked: roadmap/EP-20-stage-small-tables.md:49-57 (item 4 literally says "for each of the 20 tables status.json.rows equals the validate.sql expected count (expected_counts(), EP-10)" with an escape clause only for "validate.sql predates 3.1 ... record the delta"); EP-20 item 1 lists provider and caregiver among its 20; roadmap/EP-28-stage-verify-full.md:30-31 ("validate.sql expected (or the recorded 3.1 delta from EP-10)") covers all 31 tables. Vendored src/mimicwarehouse/concepts/vendor/mimic-code/mimic-iv/buildmimic/postgres/validate.sql: 28 table literals, no provider/caregiver/ingredientevents. inventory.py:912-927 expected_counts() returns a plain dict of those 28, so a naive `expected[table]` would KeyError for provider/caregiver (EP-20) and ingredientevents (EP-27/28); reconcile() at 930-960 already models this as status "no-expectation". EP-10 completion note (EP-10-raw-inventory.md:175-181) and DECISIONS.md:198-205 (D-26 addendum) both name the seven no-expectation tables and state 34 match / 0 mismatch against 3.1, so the "3.1 delta" clauses in EP-20/EP-28 are indeed moot. Contract YAML: mimiciv_hosp.yaml:382-387 provider `expected_rows_source: null`; mimiciv_icu.yaml caregiver and ingredientevents likewise null. So the drift is real. Why downgraded: the impact is overstated — no test exists yet ("test as written KeyErrors" is hypothetical), the brief already routes the session to EP-10's completion note which names the seven no-expectation tables explicitly, the contract field and reconcile() already encode the branch, and EP-16 item 6 (EP-16-replan-p1.md:73-80) is the designated place to add P2 pickup notes. A session would lose a few minutes at most, not fail. Real but low-severity doc drift; the proposed XS fix stands, tightened to cite the contract's `expected_rows_source` as the branch condition and to strike the delta clauses in both briefs.

(2) Standing pickup rule (XS, "should fix now" — one line in roadmap/README.md "How to use this roadmap" or the EP-13+ pickup notes): "new Settings field ⇒ same commit adds the `# MWH_<NAME>=<default>` line to mimicwarehouse/.env.example (test_ep03.py:584-589 asserts parity) and, if user-facing, the doctor `settings` row (doctor.py:389-419 currently prints only tier/k/allow_remote)". Applies to EP-17 `loader_reject_max`, EP-21/30 `role`, EP-52 `backup_target`, EP-56 `marts_hourly_max_hours`. (3) Drop the "MWH_ROLE=owner in the shell breaks every mwh command" hazard from the finding — it is false; replace with the accurate, narrower note in EP-21: "an `MWH_ROLE=` line in `mimicwarehouse/.env` or `[settings]` in `mwh.toml` before EP-21 ships makes every `Settings()` raise (extra='forbid' applies to the file sources, not to process environment variables); shell env is safe."

All quotes are accurate. Additional occurrence the finding missed: roadmap/EP-57-ui-app-shell.md:32-33 also uses `MWH_APP_TIER`, `MWH_APP_MEMORY_LIMIT`, `MWH_APP_THREADS`, `MWH_APP_ROLE`, `MWH_APP_RECORD_LATENCY`, `settings.app_dir` — none shipped. Impact claim tested and REFUTED in part: ran `MWH_ROLE=owner uv run mwh paths` and `MWH_ROLE=owner uv run python -c "from mimicwarehouse.config import load_settings; ..."` — both succeed (exit 0, "ok dev"). pydantic-settings' EnvSettingsSource ignores unknown MWH_* process env vars; extra="forbid" only rejects unknown keys in `.env`/`mwh.toml` (test_ep03.py:167-168 proves this with an `.env` line `MWH_DATA_ROOTT`, and EP-12:5-9 / DESIGN.md:826-827 already document exactly this file-source hazard for MWH_TEST_TIER). The .env.example parity trip is real but self-catching (the EP's own test run goes red at test_ep03 with the field name in the assertion message) and the parity requirement is already recorded in EP-3:41, EP-12:9/102 and DESIGN.md:827; a standing pickup note is still useful but this is low-severity doc hygiene, not medium. Net: naming drift in EP-56/57 confirmed (re-plan doc fix + owner decision on app-threads), parity rule confirmed as low, MWH_ROLE shell hazard refuted and corrected to the file-source form.

### FC-16 — Generated Markdown with full-tier row counts (EP-29 DATA-DICTIONARY.md, EP-32 benchmark note, EP-30 mwh sql output) will trip guard G4 unless thousands-separated

**Severity (verified):** low · **Verdict:** downgraded (high confidence) · **Kind:** forward-compat · **When:** now-should · **Effort:** XS · **Retro episode:** EP-170 · **Lens:** forward-compat: pending briefs (EP-13..EP-56) vs shipped P0/P1 code

**Files:** `roadmap/EP-29-catalog-data-dictionary.md:57-63`, `roadmap/EP-29-catalog-data-dictionary.md:76-79`, `roadmap/EP-32-capstone-0-staging.md:37-45`, `roadmap/EP-30-safe-query-audit.md:56-60`, `roadmap/EP-10-raw-inventory.md:210-216`, `mimicwarehouse/src/mimicwarehouse/guard.py:118`

**Evidence.** guard.py `ID_TOKEN = (?<![\w.])[123]\d{7}(?![\w.])`, `.md` in TEXT_EXTENSIONS. EP-10 completion note delta 4: "upstream counts like `pharmacy`, `prescriptions`, `inputevents` and several byte sizes are 8-digit tokens starting with 1–3 … Every human-readable surface … is thousands-separated". EP-29 item 4: dictionary shows "rows (full), Parquet MB" and acceptance "passes the guard hook"; EP-32 table "rows, CSV GB, Parquet GB …"; neither says how to format.

**Impact.** `mwh catalog dictionary` and `mwh runs benchmarks --format md` will emit raw integers that the pre-commit guard refuses; sessions then reach for `mwh-guard: allow` pragmas on data-derived numbers.

**Proposed fix (reviewer).** Amend EP-29/EP-32/EP-30: all human-readable integers via `inventory.fmt_int` (thousands separators); JSON keeps raw ints and is never pasted into tracked files (EP-10 rule).

**Corrected fix (verifier).** Do not amend three briefs individually. Add ONE standing convention sentence to roadmap/README.md § "How to use this roadmap" (and mirror it in CLAUDE.md §5 Docs discipline): "Every integer in tracked Markdown, completion notes and console tables is thousands-separated via `mimicwarehouse.inventory.fmt_int` (guard G4 refuses bare 8-digit tokens starting 1-3); `--json`/`--format json|csv` output keeps raw ints and is never pasted into tracked files (EP-10 completion note delta 4). Never use `mwh-guard: allow` on data-derived numbers." Optionally, at the P1/P2 re-plan (EP-16), have EP-29 item 4 / EP-32 item 2 cite that convention in one clause ("counts via fmt_int"), and add a note to EP-43 item 3(b): the band scanner must NOT be applied to count-like numeric columns/cells (rule from item 1) — otherwise a full-tier aggregate table exported as CSV/Parquet with an 8-digit count (pharmacy/prescriptions/inputevents-scale rows) fails `mwh disclose check` even though it carries no id. Effort XS (two sentences now, three clauses at re-plan).

**Verifier notes.** Checked: mimicwarehouse/src/mimicwarehouse/guard.py:106-127 (TEXT_EXTENSIONS includes .md; ID_TOKEN = (?<![\w.])[123]\d{7}(?![\w.]) so a decimal like 12345678.5 or a comma-separated 12,345,678 does NOT match; ALLOW_PRAGMA exempts a line in id_band_hits, guard.py:297-323); inventory.py:249-252 fmt_int exists and is exported (line 1380), used for every human-readable integer in the raw-inventory doc/table; DESIGN.md:624-628 records that convention. Briefs: EP-29 item 4 (roadmap/EP-29-catalog-data-dictionary.md:52-58) lists "rows (full), Parquet MB ... approx distinct" with no format rule -- BUT item 6 (lines 65-72) already mandates a fixture test "the generated Markdown contains ... no 8-digit number in the real id bands (reuse EP-4's guard scanner) ... regeneration is byte-stable" and acceptance line 84 requires "passes the guard hook". EP-32 item 5 (EP-32-capstone-0-staging.md:51-55) likewise requires the two docs to "pass the guard scanner" and acceptance line 66 "the guard hook passes on commit". EP-30 (EP-30-safe-query-audit.md:63-68) `mwh sql` prints to console/rich, not to a tracked file; no mwh sql exists yet (EP-21 adds it; grep of src shows no sql/count code), so the only exposure is a session pasting console counts into a completion note -- EP-16 line 49 and EP-10 lines 82-83 already state "all integers with thousands separators (guard rule G4)" for completion notes. So the factual core holds (raw full-tier row counts for pharmacy/prescriptions/inputevents-scale tables and multi-million approx-distinct values are 8-digit tokens starting 1-3 and would trip G4 in DATA-DICTIONARY.md / 00-staging-benchmark.md), but the impact is over-rated: both briefs' own acceptance tests catch the raw-int output before commit, the sanctioned helper and precedent exist, and the guard's own violation text (guard.py:126, 379) says the pragma is "for documented examples only". Residual risk is a session choosing the pragma over fmt_int -- real but low, XS to preempt with one standing convention rather than three brief edits. Related sharpening found while verifying: EP-43 item 3(b) (EP-43-disclose-primitives.md:38-40) applies the same band scanner to numeric columns and Markdown cells, so a full-tier count column can trip `mwh disclose check` (EP-44 acceptance line 82, EP-45 line 68) unless count-like columns are exempted -- worth a clause at the P2 re-plan. No files modified; no data read.

### FC-7 — EP-17 'if EP-3 ships a connection factory' — it does not, but `inventory.open_connection()` does; EP-21 re-specifies `catalog_path` that Settings already has

**Severity (verified):** low · **Verdict:** confirmed-with-corrections (high confidence) · **Kind:** design · **When:** now-should · **Effort:** XS · **Retro episode:** EP-170 · **Lens:** forward-compat: pending briefs (EP-13..EP-56) vs shipped P0/P1 code

**Files:** `roadmap/EP-17-stage-loader-core.md:34-36`, `mimicwarehouse/src/mimicwarehouse/inventory.py:353-360`, `mimicwarehouse/src/mimicwarehouse/config.py:611-613`, `roadmap/EP-21-stage-catalog-builder.md:53`

**Evidence.** EP-17: "If EP-3 already ships a connection factory in `config.py`, wrap it — one implementation only." config.py ships only `Settings.duckdb_settings(profile)`; inventory.py ships `open_connection(settings) -> duckdb.connect(database=':memory:', config=duckdb_settings('build'))`. EP-21 item 2 ends "Also `catalog_path(tier)`" while `Settings.catalog_path(tier)` exists and conftest.py already calls it.

**Impact.** Two build-connection factories (inventory vs loader/engine) with the same settings but different assertions (EP-17 adds the DuckDB pin + free-space guard); `catalog_path` duplicated in catalog/connect.py vs config.

**Proposed fix (reviewer).** EP-16 pickup notes: EP-17's `open_build_connection` becomes the one factory and `inventory.open_connection` delegates to it (keep the name as a thin alias); EP-21 uses `settings.catalog_path(tier)` and does not define another.

**Corrected fix (verifier).** Two edits, both XS, best done as EP-16 item 6 pickup notes (EP-16 is the designated mechanism, but its enumerated amend-list at roadmap/EP-16-replan-p1.md:75-80 currently omits `inventory.open_connection`, so add it there too): (1) In roadmap/EP-17-stage-loader-core.md:34-36 replace "If EP-3 already ships a connection factory in `config.py`, wrap it — one implementation only." with "EP-3 ships no factory; EP-10 ships `mimicwarehouse.inventory.open_connection(settings)` (inventory.py:353-360, `duckdb.connect(':memory:', config=duckdb_settings('build'))`, no version/free-space checks — the inventory run calls `require_free_space` separately at inventory.py:732). Make `loader.engine.open_build_connection` the single build factory (adds the DuckDB pin assert + `require_free_space`) and turn `inventory.open_connection` into a thin alias that delegates to it, keeping the exported name (`__all__` inventory.py:1386, callers at :453 and :810)." Optionally note that `fixtures/catalog.py:77-78` opens a third inline `app`-profile connection; EP-21's `open_catalog` may absorb it later but that is not required. (2) In roadmap/EP-21-stage-catalog-builder.md:53 strike "Also `catalog_path(tier)`" and write "Use `Settings.catalog_path(tier)` (config.py:622, EP-3; already used by tests/conftest.py:115 and test_ep12) — do not define a second one in `catalog/connect.py`."

**Verifier notes.** Checked: roadmap/EP-17-stage-loader-core.md lines 26-36 (quoted sentence present verbatim at 34-36); mimicwarehouse/src/mimicwarehouse/config.py 590-640 (Settings has `catalog_path` at 622 and `duckdb_settings` at 626; no connection factory; grep for `duckdb.connect` in src hits only inventory.py:360 and fixtures/catalog.py:78); inventory.py 353-360 (`open_connection` exactly as the finding describes, `require_free_space` called separately at 732, `duckdb.__version__` returned at 311 but not asserted against a pin); roadmap/EP-21-stage-catalog-builder.md:53 ("Also `catalog_path(tier)`" present); roadmap/EP-3-config-data-root.md:38 already planned `catalog_path` on Settings, so EP-21's line is an original planning inconsistency, not later drift; tests/conftest.py:115, test_ep03.py:201, test_ep12.py:867/882 use `get_settings().catalog_path`. roadmap/EP-16-replan-p1.md item 6 (lines 75-80) lists the names EP-16 must reconcile into P2 pickup notes and does not include `inventory.open_connection`, so without this finding the duplication would slip through the designed catch mechanism. Evidence fully holds; severity downgraded from medium to low because the impact is code duplication/maintenance (two build openers with slightly different guards, one redundant path helper) rather than a data-safety, correctness or blocking failure — inventory already enforces the free-space guard at run level, and a duplicate `catalog_path` would compute the same path. Fix is XS and belongs in the EP-16 pickup notes, as the finding says, plus the EP-16 item-6 list itself.

### FC-8 — 'source manifest id' is ambiguous: EP-17 says per-file sha256, DESIGN §5 note / EP-16 say `raw_snapshot_id()`

**Severity (verified):** low · **Verdict:** confirmed-with-corrections (high confidence) · **Kind:** docs-drift · **When:** now-should · **Effort:** XS · **Retro episode:** EP-166 · **Lens:** forward-compat: pending briefs (EP-13..EP-56) vs shipped P0/P1 code

**Files:** `roadmap/EP-17-stage-loader-core.md:58-63`, `mimicwarehouse/DESIGN.md:633-635`, `roadmap/EP-16-replan-p1.md:75-77`

**Evidence.** EP-17 item 4: "`source_manifest_id` (the sha256 EP-10 recorded for the source file in `lake/manifests/raw/<dataset>.jsonl`, or `"fixture"`)". DESIGN.md EP-10 note: "EP-17/18/19 cite `raw_snapshot_id()` as the `source manifest id`"; EP-16 item 6: "`mimicwarehouse.inventory.raw_snapshot_id()` as the loader manifest's `source manifest id`".

**Impact.** EP-19's `layer_snapshot` and EP-35 run manifests need to know whether the field ties a Parquet file to one raw file or to the whole raw snapshot; a per-file sha cannot be reconciled with EP-10's snapshot id later.

**Proposed fix (reviewer).** ManifestLine carries both: `source_sha256` (per-file, from the raw manifest; `null` for fixture) and `raw_snapshot_id` (whole snapshot; `"fixture"` for the fixture tier). Amend EP-17 item 4 and the DESIGN §5 sentence at EP-16.

**Corrected fix (verifier).** Do not amend EP-17 out of band; the reconciliation is already scheduled by EP-16 item 6 (roadmap/EP-16-replan-p1.md:75-77), which instructs the EP-16 session to add an "EP-16 pickup note" to EP-17 naming raw_snapshot_id() as the source manifest id. Correct EP-16 item 6 (one sentence, XS) so the pickup note is precise instead of a bare rename: ManifestLine carries (a) `raw_snapshot_id: str | None` = `mimicwarehouse.inventory.raw_snapshot_id()` (whole raw snapshot; `None`/`"fixture"` on the fixture tier -- pick one and say so, because raw_snapshot_id() returns None when no raw manifest exists, so EP-17's literal `"fixture"` sentinel is not what the helper yields), and (b) `source_sha256: str | None` = the per-file sha256 from the EP-10 raw manifest record for the source CSV (`None` on fixture) -- keep it, it is the per-file lineage EP-17 item 4 asked for and RAW-1/EP-137 can use it. Also tighten DESIGN.md:181 (§5 core text still says only "source manifest id") to "raw snapshot id (+ per-file source sha256)" at the same time. Skip claiming EP-19/EP-35 impact: EP-19 `layer_snapshot` hashes whole manifest lines and EP-35 takes snapshot ids from EP-19, so neither parses this field.

**Verifier notes.** Checked: roadmap/EP-17-stage-loader-core.md:58-63 (item 4 says source_manifest_id = per-file sha256 from lake/manifests/raw/<dataset>.jsonl or "fixture"; brief dated 2026-08-16, before EP-10 shipped 2026-08-17 per git log); DESIGN.md:181 (§5: "source manifest id", undefined) and :633-635 (EP-10 note: EP-17/18/19 cite raw_snapshot_id()); DECISIONS.md:196-206 D-26 addendum ("This id is the source manifest id every lake manifest (EP-17+) cites"); roadmap/EP-10-raw-inventory.md:34,174 (same); src/mimicwarehouse/inventory.py:6 docstring and :576-579 (raw_snapshot_id() returns None until 41 files present / no manifest -> fixture tier gets None, not "fixture"); roadmap/EP-16-replan-p1.md:75-77 item 6 (already schedules a pickup note to EP-17 renaming to raw_snapshot_id()); roadmap/EP-19-*.md:53-57 (layer_snapshot = sha256 over sorted manifest lines, does not interpret source_manifest_id) and EP-35-run-ledger.md:27,36 (snapshot_ids from EP-19 helpers). **Files:** `mimicwarehouse/pyproject.toml:14-31`, `roadmap/EP-19-stage-dag-runner.md:19-20`, `roadmap/EP-35-run-ledger.md`, `roadmap/EP-36-seeds-resource-log.md`, `roadmap/EP-48-attrition-diagram.md:28`

### FXT-2 — Fixture byte-identity silently depends on unpinned numpy/polars versions and on the whole contract hash

**Severity (verified):** low · **Verdict:** confirmed-with-corrections (high confidence) · **Kind:** forward-compat · **When:** now-should · **Effort:** S · **Retro episode:** EP-169 · **Lens:** fixture-generator (EP-11/12 synthetic fixture as the test substrate for every later brief)

**Files:** `mimicwarehouse/pyproject.toml:18`, `mimicwarehouse/pyproject.toml:21`, `mimicwarehouse/src/mimicwarehouse/fixtures/spec.py:249`, `mimicwarehouse/src/mimicwarehouse/fixtures/write.py:105`, `mimicwarehouse/tests/ep/test_ep11.py:623`

**Evidence.** pyproject lists `"polars"` and `"numpy"` unpinned (uv.lock: numpy 2.5.2, polars 1.43.2). NumPy's Generator has no cross-version stream guarantee (NEP 19: distribution methods like integers/dirichlet/lognormal may change), and Polars has changed float text formatting before; both feed `frame_to_csv_bytes`. test_fixture_drift also asserts `manifest["contract_hash"] == contract.content_hash()` where content_hash covers every column comment, so any contract YAML edit (comment fix, EP-17 column-map note) invalidates manifest.json even though no CSV changes.

**Impact.** A routine `uv lock --upgrade` (as EP-164 did) or a one-line contract comment edit turns test_ep11/test_ep12 red with 31 'sha drift' failures in an unrelated EP session; the session either regenerates (churning 5 MB of binaries and every downstream count) or pins ad hoc.

**Proposed fix (reviewer).** (1) Pin numpy and polars minor versions in pyproject (or add a `fixture-regen` note in tests/fixtures/README that an upgrade of either is expected to require regeneration + version bump); (2) record numpy/polars versions in manifest.json (`_manifest_payload`) so a drift failure names its cause; (3) consider hashing only the fixture-relevant part of the contract (ordered (schema, table, name, type, nullable, csv_path)) in the manifest, keeping the full hash in a separate informational key.

**Corrected fix (verifier).** (1) Do NOT pin numpy/polars minors (adds resolver-trap surface for EP-121 torch / EP-93 / EP-148, roadmap Risk 3); instead add `numpy_version`, `polars_version`, `python_version` as informational keys in `_manifest_payload` (write.py:155-169) and put the numpy/polars versions into the drift assertion messages in test_ep11.py:620-621 / test_ep12.py:561-562, so a stream/format change names its cause. Add one paragraph to tests/fixtures/README.md: byte-identity assumes the locked numpy (2.5.2) / polars (1.43.2); a `uv lock --upgrade` that moves either may require `mwh fixtures build` + GENERATOR_VERSION bump + a manifest commit. (2) Split the contract coupling: keep `contract_hash` (full `Contract.content_hash()`, GOVERNANCE §12 provenance) informational, add `contract_schema_hash` = sha256 of the ordered (schema, table, name, type, nullable, csv_path) tuples, and change the two `assert manifest["contract_hash"] == contract.content_hash()` lines (test_ep11.py:624, test_ep12.py:566) to compare the schema hash only — otherwise EP-17 (brief line 74 explicitly anticipates fixing contract YAML) and any doc-comment edit to schema/tables/*.yaml turns test_ep11+test_ep12 red and forces a manifest regeneration in an unrelated EP. Either way this is a one-line manifest.json diff plus a regen commit; effort S (~30 min), when: now-should for (2), fix-at-next-re-plan (EP-16) acceptable for (1).

**Verifier notes.** Checked: mimicwarehouse/pyproject.toml:18,21 (polars, numpy unpinned; uv.lock numpy 2.5.2 l.747, polars 1.43.2 l.881); schema/contract.py:559-563 content_hash = sha256 of full model_dump (comments included, confirmed via `comment` fields at l.109/173/449); fixtures/write.py:81-96 frame_to_csv_bytes (float_scientific=False, no float_precision; hosp.py:60-61 maps DOUBLE/FLOAT to Float64/Float32 so float text depends on polars' formatter) and _manifest_payload l.155-169 (no numpy/polars/python version recorded); spec.py:264-268 table_rng uses default_rng([seed, crc32]) and generators use rng.random/integers/choice/dirichlet (grep over fixtures/*.py) which fall under NEP 19's 'distribution streams may change in feature releases' policy; test_ep11.py:610-627 and test_ep12.py:549-566 assert per-file sha then `manifest["contract_hash"] == contract.content_hash()` and byte-equal manifest.json/README. tests/fixtures/README.md documents regen (`mwh fixtures build`) but says nothing about numpy/polars versions. Corrections to the finding: (a) `git log -- mimicwarehouse/uv.lock` shows the lock changed only in c232142 (EP-1); EP-164 did not run `uv lock --upgrade`, and no pending brief plans one — the numpy/polars risk is real but latent/low-probability (uv preserves locked versions when deps are added). (b) Failure is not silent: it is an explicit assert with a documented regen path; a contract-comment edit changes no CSV byte, so regeneration is a 1-line manifest.json diff (contract_hash at manifest.json:43), not '5 MB of churn'; and it produces 2 failing tests (drift tests stop at first mismatch), not '31 sha drift failures'. (c) Concrete downstream trip that does hold: roadmap/EP-17-stage-loader-core.md:74 says contract mismatches are 'a contract bug to fix in EP-9's YAML with a dated note' — that edit will redden test_ep11/test_ep12 (caught by `poe check`, not by `mwh verify EP-17`). Net: real forward-compat/coupling issue with a cheap, self-explaining failure mode; downgraded from medium to low, fix (2) worth doing now, fix (1) as documentation + manifest metadata rather than pins.

### FXT-3 — GENERATOR_VERSION policy is undocumented and already inconsistent; no written fixture-change protocol

**Severity (verified):** low · **Verdict:** confirmed-with-corrections (high confidence) · **Kind:** docs-drift · **When:** now-should · **Effort:** S · **Retro episode:** EP-169 · **Lens:** fixture-generator (EP-11/12 synthetic fixture as the test substrate for every later brief)

**Files:** `mimicwarehouse/src/mimicwarehouse/fixtures/write.py:44`, `roadmap/EP-11-fixtures-hosp.md:197`, `roadmap/EP-12-fixtures-icu-markers.md:165`, `mimicwarehouse/tests/fixtures/README.md:29`, `roadmap/final-roadmap.md:183`

**Evidence.** write.py:44 `#: Bumped when the generator changes output for the same spec` vs EP-11 hand-off `keep write.GENERATOR_VERSION at 0.1.0 unless a hosp byte changes` vs EP-12 note `GENERATOR_VERSION stays 0.1.0 (hand-off rule: bump only when a hosp byte changes)` although EP-12 added 9 output files and 3 spec keys for the same spec; final-roadmap FIX-1 says `fixture bytes change → GENERATOR_VERSION bump`. tests/fixtures/README only says 'change the generator and rebuild'. Nothing states that a regen is the norm for every realism tweak, that CSV diffs are binary (`*.csv binary` in .gitattributes) so review happens via manifest.json, or that downstream tests must not pin counts (test_ep12.py:295 already hardcodes `== 75`).

**Impact.** Every later EP that touches vocab/generator will guess the rule; version numbers stop meaning anything; sessions may regenerate without bumping (undetectable — test_committed_layout only checks manifest == constant) or bump without regenerating (red). Repeated ad-hoc regens also grow the repo ~5 MB per regen.

**Proposed fix (reviewer).** Add a 'Changing the fixture' section to tests/fixtures/README.md (mirrored in DESIGN §4): any generator/vocab/spec/contract change → `uv run mwh fixtures build` → review `git diff mimicwarehouse/tests/fixtures/manifest.json` (CSV diffs are binary) → bump GENERATOR_VERSION (patch: bytes of existing tables changed; minor: new tables/modules/spec keys) → commit CSVs + manifest + version in one commit; bundle changes to keep regens rare; downstream tests read counts from manifest.json / build_plan(), never literals. Fix the write.py comment to match.

**Corrected fix (verifier).** Do NOT hand-edit mimicwarehouse/tests/fixtures/README.md: it is rendered by write.render_readme (write.py:191-239) and test_ep11::test_fixture_drift / test_ep12::test_icu_fixture_drift assert regenerated README bytes == committed bytes, so a hand-added section turns both drift tests red. Instead: (1) write the protocol once in the hand-maintained mimicwarehouse/tests/README.md (new 'Changing the synthetic fixture' subsection) and a dated line in DESIGN.md §4: any change to fixtures/{spec,vocab/*.yaml,hosp,icu,write}.py or to the EP-9 contract -> `uv run --group dev mwh fixtures build` -> review `git diff mimicwarehouse/tests/fixtures/manifest.json` (CSVs are `binary` in .gitattributes, so the manifest sha256/rows/bytes diff is the review surface) -> bump write.GENERATOR_VERSION when any manifest sha256 changes for the default spec (patch = bytes of existing tables moved; minor = new tables / modules / spec keys; a README-only or manifest-only change needs no bump) -> commit CSVs + manifest.json + README.md + write.py in ONE commit; bundle generator tweaks so regens stay rare (a full regen adds ~0.8 MiB compressed to .git, measured, not ~5 MB). (2) Reword write.py:44 to the per-manifest rule above and add one line to render_readme pointing at tests/README.md (this README change is itself a regen — do it in the same commit as the next real regen, or accept the README-only regen with no version bump). (3) Retire the EP-11/EP-12 'bump only when a hosp byte changes' phrasing with a one-line addendum in EP-12's completion note (or DECISIONS.md addenda) saying it was a hand-off scoped to EP-12 (icu bytes did not exist before), superseded by the manifest rule; do not rewrite the briefs. (4) Optional, low value: change test_ep12.py:295 `== 75` to `== len(plan.icu_segments)` only, or leave it — the fixture's own EP tests are expected to be updated in the regen commit; the real guidance is for later EPs (EP-17/21/37/41/42) to read counts from manifest.json / build_plan(), which belongs in the tests/README.md subsection. Effort S (30 min).

**Verifier notes.** Checked: write.py:44-45 (comment 'Bumped when the generator changes output for the same spec (recorded per file in the manifest)'), EP-11 brief line 197 ('keep write.GENERATOR_VERSION at 0.1.0 unless a hosp byte changes' — a hand-off instruction to EP-12), EP-12 brief line 165 ('hand-off rule: bump only when a hosp byte changes' — the one-off is restated as a rule; literally read it exempts icu-byte changes, which is the genuine ambiguity a later EP can trip on), final-roadmap.md:183 (FIX-1: bytes change -> bump, consistent with write.py), tests/fixtures/README.md (rendered by write.render_readme lines 191-239; only says regenerate/never hand-edit), DESIGN.md §4 notes lines 146-172 and §15 700-712 (say 'regenerated by code changes, never edited by hand'; no bump granularity), tests/README.md and GOVERNANCE.md (no version/regeneration protocol; grep for binary/manifest/regenerat empty in tests/README.md), .gitattributes (`*.csv binary` confirmed). Grep for GENERATOR_VERSION across src/tests/docs/roadmap: the value is only written into the manifest and asserted equal to the constant in test_ep11.py:599/603 and test_ep12.py:565 — there is no functional consumer, so an unbumped regen cannot break anything downstream (drift tests regenerate and compare, so a same-version regen is green and invisible only to the version field, exactly as the finding says; a bump without regen is red via manifest byte comparison). test_ep12.py:295 does pin `== 75` (alongside len(plan.icu_segments)); test_ep11 pins n_subjects == 120 and 22/31 table counts. Overstatements: (a) 'no written fixture-change protocol' — a minimal one exists (regen command + never hand-edit + drift tests); what is missing is the bump granularity and the manifest-as-review-surface hint; (b) 'grow the repo ~5 MB per regen' — measured compressed size of the 31 committed CSV blobs in .git/objects is 0.82 MiB (git count-objects: whole repo 3.25 MiB loose), so a full regen costs <1 MiB and unchanged tables share blobs; (c) 'already inconsistent' is arguable: write.py's 'recorded per file' wording is compatible with EP-12 not bumping since no existing file's bytes moved and the icu files were first written at 0.1.0. Key correction to the proposed fix: the target README is generated and byte-compared, so the protocol must live in tests/README.md / DESIGN.md (and optionally render_readme), not in a hand edit. Net: real, low-severity doc drift with a version field that has no consumers; downgraded medium -> low, still 'should fix now' because it is S and prevents later EPs guessing.

### FXT-5 — EP-41's T2DM phenotype inputs (HbA1c 50852, non-insulin antidiabetics, T1DM exclusion codes) are absent from the fixture vocab

**Severity (verified):** low · **Verdict:** downgraded (high confidence) · **Kind:** test · **When:** next-replan · **Effort:** S · **Retro episode:** EP-169 · **Lens:** fixture-generator (EP-11/12 synthetic fixture as the test substrate for every later brief)

**Files:** `mimicwarehouse/src/mimicwarehouse/fixtures/vocab/d_labitems.yaml:11`, `mimicwarehouse/src/mimicwarehouse/fixtures/vocab/drugs.yaml:8`, `mimicwarehouse/src/mimicwarehouse/fixtures/vocab/icd.yaml:65`, `roadmap/EP-41-phenotype-engine-t2dm.md:44`

**Evidence.** EP-41 item 3: `all( any( medication(noninsulin_antidiabetics@1.0.0, prescriptions), lab(HbA1c ≥ 6.5 %) ), not(diagnosis(t1dm@1.0.0)) )`. d_labitems.yaml has no 50852 (% Hemoglobin A1c); drugs.yaml (36 drugs) has no metformin/glipizide/sitagliptin; icd.yaml has no 25001/25003/E10x T1DM codes; only the diagnosis branch (25000/E119 planted) is exercisable.

**Impact.** EP-41's fixture-tier compile can only exercise one branch of the phenotype tree; the lab/medication branch and the `not()` exclusion get no fixture coverage; EP-40's `noninsulin_antidiabetics` code set matches nothing on fixture.

**Proposed fix (reviewer).** In the bundled regen: add 50852 (HbA1c, %, panel `a1c` or `chem`), metformin + glipizide to drugs.yaml (tag t2dm), T1DM codes 25001/E109 to icd.yaml (tag t1dm), and give planted t2dm admissions one HbA1c ≥ 6.5 draw; plant one T1DM-coded admission as a negative control.

**Corrected fix (verifier).** Treat as an optional fixture-enrichment item for the EP-41 session (or the next fixture regen), not a gap that any brief trips on. If the owner wants `mwh phenotype compile t2dm@1.0.0 --tier fixture` to produce positives from every branch of the tree end-to-end (rather than only from the dx branch, with the med/lab and not(t1dm) branches covered by EP-41's own in-test crafted DuckDB as the brief already specifies), then in one regen: add itemid 50852 (% Hemoglobin A1c, panel `chem`, ref 4.0-6.0) to `fixtures/vocab/d_labitems.yaml`; add metformin (+ glipizide) to `fixtures/vocab/drugs.yaml` with tag `t2dm`; add ICD-9 25001/25003 and ICD-10 E109/E1065 with tag `t1dm` to `fixtures/vocab/icd.yaml`; extend `hosp.py`'s planted t2dm signal (currently 'primary T2DM code + insulin + glucose >= 180', per EP-11 completion note and `hosp.py:17`) with one HbA1c >= 6.5 draw and a metformin order, and plant one T1DM-coded admission with no T2DM code as a negative control; bump `generator_version`, regenerate the 31 CSVs + manifest.json (test_ep11/test_ep12 pin sha256 and row counts, `tests/README.md` catalog numbers), and mirror the new planted-signal description in the EP-11 brief completion note / DESIGN.md §20 note. Effort S-M (the regen + manifest/README/DESIGN drift, not the YAML edits, is the cost). Decision for the owner: (a) leave the bundled fixture as EP-11 specified it and rely on EP-41's crafted in-test subjects (zero cost, brief already covers it); (b) enrich in the EP-41 session so the fixture tier exercises all branches (S-M, gives EP-63 Phenotype Studio and EP-68 prevalence a mixed-branch fixture positive set for screenshots/demos).

**Verifier notes.** Checked (read-only): grep of mimicwarehouse/src/mimicwarehouse/fixtures/vocab/d_labitems.yaml (no 50852 / A1c; only 51222 Hemoglobin), drugs.yaml (38 drug entries, not 36 — DESIGN.md §20 says 36; only Insulin sliding scale + Glargine carry tag t2dm; no metformin/glipizide/sitagliptin), icd.yaml (t2dm-tagged 25000/25002/25040/V5867 and E119/E1165/E1122/Z794; no 250x1/250x3 or E10x T1DM codes). So the factual absence claim holds. The impact claim does NOT hold up as stated: (1) roadmap/EP-41-phenotype-engine-t2dm.md item 5 (lines 53-59) already specifies that branch coverage comes from 'crafted synthetic subjects (ids >= 90 000 000, built in-test into a temp DuckDB using EP-9's schema) cover each branch (dx only; med + lab without dx; T1DM-only excluded; none)' — i.e. EP-41 never intended the bundled fixture to exercise the med/lab branch or the not() exclusion, so 'gets no fixture coverage' is not a defect a brief trips on. (2) roadmap/EP-11-fixtures-hosp.md lines 76-77 defined the planted T2DM signal as 'T2DM codes + insulin + glucose' and its completion note (lines 149-151, 188-190) records exactly that shipped ('so EP-41/42's phenotypes and the T2DM code set find them by number') — the fixture matches its own spec; the dx branch is what was promised. (3) EP-40 item 5 (roadmap/EP-40-codeset-registry-gem.md lines 56-61) tests on fixture only prefix expansion against fixture dictionaries and '>= 1 member per system it declares'; the '>= 90 % dictionary match' requirement is on dev, so `noninsulin_antidiabetics` / `t1dm` matching nothing on the fixture is informational and no different from heart_failure/copd/etc. having partial fixture coverage. (4) EP-39 (roadmap/EP-39-itemid-units.md lines 54-60) verifies HbA1c itemid against real d_labitems via mwh sql and its fixture tests are formula/YAML round-trips, so the missing 50852 in the fixture d_labitems does not break EP-39 either. Real but over-rated: no downstream EP fails; the only consequence is that on the fixture tier the T2DM phenotype's positive set will be dx-branch-only, which is cosmetic for EP-41 and mildly relevant later for EP-63/EP-68 demo/screenshot material. Regen cost noted from tests/ep/test_ep11.py lines 602-640 (manifest sha256/rows pinned; regeneration into tmp_path must reproduce committed bytes) and tests/fixtures/README.md line 23. Minor correction to the finding: drugs.yaml has 38 entries, not 36.

### GOV-3 — Deny rules protect the data root only when it is literally C:\mimicdata; a relocated MWH_DATA_ROOT has zero deny coverage

**Severity (verified):** low · **Verdict:** confirmed-with-corrections (high confidence) · **Kind:** design · **When:** now-should · **Effort:** XS · **Retro episode:** EP-167 · **Lens:** governance enforcement layers (deny rules, mwh guard, .gitignore/.gitattributes, hooks, GOVERNANCE text vs reality)

**Files:** `.claude/settings.json:8-9,17-19`, `mimicwarehouse/GOVERNANCE.md:47`, `mimicwarehouse/DECISIONS.md:397-400`

**Evidence.** Rules `Read(//C:/mimicdata/**)`, `Grep(//C:/mimicdata/**)`, `Glob(C:/mimicdata/**)`, `Bash(cat *mimicdata*)`… all hard-code the default root; GOVERNANCE §2 lists `C:\mimicdata\ (MWH_DATA_ROOT)` as configurable and D-29 refuses only G:/D:/synced/network roots. `.env`/`mwh.toml`/`MWH_DATA_ROOT` can point anywhere on C:.

**Impact.** If the owner ever moves the root (disk pressure — Risk 6 budgets 300+ GB), every Read/Grep/Glob and shell deny rule stops applying and only prose remains; nothing warns.

**Proposed fix (reviewer).** XS: add a `mwh doctor` info/warn row `deny_coverage`: warn when `settings.data_root` (resolved) is not under a prefix that appears in `.claude/settings.json` deny rules (parse the JSON, extract `//C:/…/**` and `C:/…/**` prefixes). Also state in GOVERNANCE §2 that relocating the root requires updating the deny rules first. Add the row to test_ep02/test_ep03 doctor tests.

**Corrected fix (verifier).** Keep the idea, correct the framing and the mechanics.
