### GOV-5 — G1 extension list lacks .tsv/.xlsx/.xls/.zip/.7z/.tar(.gz)/.bz2/.zst/.xz/.sqlite/.db/.orc/.avro/.parquet.gz/.csv.zst/.ndjson; .gitignore/.gitattributes are inconsistent with G1

**Severity (verified):** low · **Verdict:** confirmed-with-corrections (high confidence) · **Kind:** bug · **When:** now-should · **Effort:** XS · **Retro episode:** EP-165 · **Lens:** governance enforcement layers (deny rules, mwh guard, .gitignore/.gitattributes, hooks, GOVERNANCE text vs reality)

**Files:** `mimicwarehouse/src/mimicwarehouse/guard.py:75-95`, `.gitignore:23-53`, `.gitattributes:33-53`

**Evidence.** `data_extension()` uses `lower.endswith(ext)`; verified: `['a.tsv','a.xlsx','a.zip','a.csv.zst','a.parquet.gz','a.sqlite','a.tar.gz','a.orc','a.avro']` → all `None`; only `.npy` matched. `git check-ignore` on `mimicwarehouse/x.sqlite`, `x.db`, `x.xlsx`, `x.npy`, `x.h5`, `x.zip`, `x.orc`, `x.avro`, `x.tsv`, `x.parquet.gz`, `x.csv.zst` → none ignored. `.gitattributes` marks `*.zip`/`*.gz` binary but neither .gitignore nor G1 refuses them; conversely G1 lists `.npy .npz .h5` which .gitignore does not.

**Impact.** A `.tsv` export (`COPY … TO 'x.tsv'`), a `to_excel` dump, a zipped `runs/` folder, or a `.csv.zst` under 20 MB sails past .gitignore, G1, G4 (binary/compressed → not text) and check-added-large-files. EP-58 exports and EP-130/131 report bundles are the first producers of such files.

**Proposed fix (reviewer).** XS: extend DATA_EXTENSIONS with `.tsv .xlsx .xls .zip .7z .tar .tgz .tar.gz .bz2 .zst .xz .gz .sqlite .sqlite3 .db .orc .avro .ndjson .msgpack .hdf5 .sav .dta .rds .mat`; mirror the same list into .gitignore (outside fixtures) and mark them `binary` in .gitattributes; add `x.zip`/`x.tsv` to IGNORED_PROBES and BINARY_PROBES so `--selfcheck` pins it. Note EP-8's VENDOR test is the only `.gz` rule today (EP-8 brief line 86) — `.gz` in G1 makes that explicit.

**Corrected fix (verifier).** XS (~20 min), fine to do now as guard hygiene but low urgency. (a) guard.py DATA_EXTENSIONS: add a short, defensible set — `.tsv .xlsx .xls .zip .7z .tar .tgz .tar.gz .gz .bz2 .zst .xz .sqlite .sqlite3 .db .orc .avro .ndjson .hdf5` (drop the finding's .msgpack/.sav/.dta/.rds/.mat unless the owner wants them; do NOT add .pdf/.png — EP-131/EP-59 promote those into the tree). Because data_extension() is longest-suffix, `.gz`/`.zst` alone cover `.parquet.gz`/`.csv.zst`. (b) Add `.tsv` to TEXT_EXTENSIONS so a `.tsv` in fixtures/elsewhere is also G4-scanned. (c) Mirror the same suffixes into .gitignore:23-38 (also add the already-G1 `.npy .npz .h5`) and add the non-binary-marked ones (`*.tsv *.xlsx *.xls *.7z *.zst *.xz *.bz2 *.sqlite *.db *.orc *.avro *.hdf5 *.npy *.npz *.h5`) to .gitattributes as `binary`. (d) Extend IGNORED_PROBES with `mimicwarehouse/x.tsv`, `mimicwarehouse/x.zip`, `mimicwarehouse/x.npy` and BINARY_PROBES with `x.zip`, `x.tsv` so `mwh guard --selfcheck` pins it; extend the parametrized list in tests/ep/test_ep04.py:131-137. (e) Update the three places that enumerate the G1 list (EP-4 brief lines 25-26 via a dated addendum, DESIGN §15 dated note, DECISIONS D-39 addendum) and note that `.gz` enforcement moves from the EP-8 vendor test into G1 (keep the test). Correct the finding's impact text: no planned EP produces these formats in-tree; the value is catching ad-hoc session dumps and `git add -f`.

**Verifier notes.** CHECKED. (1) guard.py:76-96 DATA_EXTENSIONS and :232-238 data_extension(); ran `uv run python -c` → {'a.tsv','a.xlsx','a.zip','a.csv.zst','a.parquet.gz','a.sqlite','a.tar.gz','a.orc','a.avro','a.gz','a.ndjson','a.xls','a.db'} all None; only .npy/.h5 matched — factual claim holds. (2) `git check-ignore -v` on mimicwarehouse/x.{sqlite,db,xlsx,npy,npz,h5,zip,orc,avro,tsv,parquet.gz,csv.zst,tar.gz,gz,ndjson}: none ignored (only exports/x.tsv via the `exports/` dir rule .gitignore:46) — holds. `.gitignore:23-38` indeed lacks .npy/.npz/.h5 that G1 lists — asymmetry holds, but harmless (guard is the stronger second layer and catches them). (3) `git check-attr binary`: x.zip/x.gz/x.tar.gz set, x.tsv/x.xlsx/x.npy/x.h5/x.sqlite/x.7z/x.zst unspecified. Calling `.gitattributes` "inconsistent with G1" is a stretch: `.gitattributes:26-51` also marks png/jpg/pdf/woff binary — `binary` is a normalise/diff attribute, not a refusal list, and its own comment says .gitignore + guard do the refusing. (4) Extra gap the finding under-states: `.tsv` is also absent from TEXT_EXTENSIONS (guard.py:106-123), so a `.tsv` with real-band ids passes G1 AND G4 (G4 skips it because of the suffix, not because it is binary as the finding says); a `.txt` or extensionless dump would still be G4-scanned. (5) `git ls-files` → 0 tracked files with .gz/.zip/.tsv/.xlsx/.npy/.h5/.pdf/.png today. IMPACT REFUTED AS STATED: none of the cited producers writes such files into the repo. EP-58 brief lines 49-52: exports are CSV/PNG under `%MWH_DATA_ROOT%\runs\exports\` (outside the tree; CSV already covered). EP-59 lines 16-24, 40: promotable formats are md/json/png/svg/vl.json only, `.csv` promotion refused. EP-130/131: bundle formats md/html/pdf (`reports/<slug>/`, docs/analyses); no tsv/xlsx/zip. Only sqlite mention in the roadmap is EP-148's `notes_fts.sqlite` fallback, which lives in the warehouse dir (data root; `warehouse/` is gitignored anyway). No `xlsx`/`to_excel`/`.tsv`/`zip` producer anywhere in roadmap/*.md, DESIGN.md or src/. So this is a defense-in-depth gap for ad-hoc session output, not a concrete downstream trip → severity low. DESIGN NOTE: the guard docstring (guard.py:4-5), final-roadmap.md:179 (GOV-3) and D-39 addendum (DECISIONS.md:402-406) all describe G1 as an explicit, enumerated list ("shape-and-id filter"), so a non-exhaustive list is documented intent, not drift; the enumerated list is repeated in EP-4 brief lines 25-26, DESIGN §15 (~line 443) and D-39 — any extension change must touch those or create new drift. EP-7 re-plan (EP-7 brief lines 203-204) and EP-8 (brief line 86, completion note line 144) knowingly left bare `.gz` to the EP-8 vendor test; adding `.gz` to G1 is safe (no tracked .gz; upstream load_gz/7z are `excluded`).

### INV-2 — Resume key is (bytes, mtime_ns) only: header status is never re-evaluated when the contract changes, while the snapshot's `contract_hash` claims the current contract

**Severity (verified):** low · **Verdict:** downgraded (high confidence) · **Kind:** bug · **When:** now-should · **Effort:** S · **Retro episode:** EP-167 · **Lens:** raw inventory / provenance module (EP-10)

**Files:** `mimicwarehouse/src/mimicwarehouse/inventory.py:149-151`, `mimicwarehouse/src/mimicwarehouse/inventory.py:756-763`, `mimicwarehouse/src/mimicwarehouse/inventory.py:770`, `mimicwarehouse/tests/ep/test_ep10.py:452-472`

**Evidence.** `if prev is not None and not force and prev.matches_stat(p.bytes, p.mtime_ns): if prev.rows is not None or not rowcount: result.skipped.append(...); continue` — the stored `header_matches_contract/missing_columns/extra_columns` are carried forward untouched, yet `versions["contract_hash"] = contract.content_hash()` is refreshed on every build. `test_build_resume_skips_unchanged_and_force_recomputes` only exercises byte changes, never a contract change.

**Impact.** EP-9 amendments (e.g. a column added/renamed in a `<schema>.yaml`, or the demo column map work in EP-22 touching a table) leave `mwh inventory show` reporting `41/41 … 0 header mismatch` and the docs page `header ok` against the OLD contract while `raw_snapshot.json.contract_hash` says NEW; EP-17's SchemaMismatchError then fires on a file the manifest calls `ok`, or worse, nobody notices because everyone trusts the manifest.

**Proposed fix (reviewer).** Cheap and correct: for every skipped file re-run `read_header` + `compare_header` (first line only, microseconds) and rewrite the record if the header fields differ (do not touch sha256/rows/timings); or store `contract_hash` per FileRecord and treat a mismatch as a header-only todo. Add a test: change one column name in a copied contract → resume flips that file to `mismatch` without re-hashing.

**Corrected fix (verifier).** Re-evaluate header status for skipped files without touching hash/rows: in build_inventory (inventory.py ~757-760), for a record that matches_stat, recompute `matches, missing, extra = compare_header(prev.header, contract.table(p.table_qn))` — the record already stores the full `header` list, so NO file I/O is needed (cheaper than the finding's read_header proposal and works identically); if the three fields differ, `manifest.records[rel] = prev.model_copy(update={...})` (FileRecord is frozen), rewrite that dataset's JSONL, and count it in a new `BuildResult.refreshed` bucket (keep it out of `processed` so the EP-16 recipe "0 to process, 41 up to date" and raw_snapshot_id stay stable — the id canonical tuple excludes header, verified). Optionally also store `contract_hash` per FileRecord. Test: build on the fixture, then build again with `load_contract().model_copy(...)` renaming one column of `mimiciv_hosp.services` → that record flips to `mismatch` with missing/extra populated, `processed == []`, `sha256`/`seconds_hash`/`recorded_at` unchanged, `raw_snapshot_id` unchanged, snapshot `datasets[...]['header_ok']` drops by one. Until then, one sentence in DESIGN §(inventory) / mimicwarehouse/README: "after any change to schema/tables/*.yaml column names, run `mwh inventory build --force` (~2 min on the full tree per the EP-10 completion note) so header status is re-evaluated". Effort S (~30 min incl. test). Timing: before EP-17 lands, not urgent now.

**Verifier notes.** Checked mimicwarehouse/src/mimicwarehouse/inventory.py: FileRecord.matches_stat (149-151) is (bytes, mtime_ns) only; build_inventory 756-763 skips such files and never touches header_matches_contract/missing/extra; versions['contract_hash'] = contract.content_hash() at 770 is written by flush() at 804 even when todo is empty; show (1277-1280) and the docs page (1008 contract hash, 1046 per-file header status) derive from the stale records. tests/ep/test_ep10.py:444-467 only changes bytes, never the contract. Reproduced with a scratch pytest (scratchpad/test_inv2_repro.py, importing the EP-10 fixtures, run via `uv run pytest ... -o addopts=""` on the synthetic fixture only): after renaming one column of mimiciv_hosp.services in a model_copy of the contract, the second build reports processed=[] skipped=41, snapshot contract_hash == NEW hash, the services record still says header_status 'ok' with empty missing/extra, snapshot header_ok stays 31, raw_snapshot_id unchanged; `force=True` then yields 'mismatch' missing=['curr_service_renamed'] extra=['curr_service']. So the mechanism is CONFIRMED. Downgraded medium→low because the impact is over-stated: (a) EP-17's brief (roadmap/EP-17-stage-loader-core.md:41-44, 80, 98) has the loader validate the raw header itself before reading and raise SchemaMismatchError, so a real mismatch cannot load silently — the manifest is only a stale report, not a safety gate; (b) the workaround is `mwh inventory build --force`, which per the EP-10 completion note re-hashes+recounts the whole 98 GB tree in ~93 s, so the cost of the bug is a 2-minute rerun plus confusion; (c) header-affecting contract edits are rare — the tables are transcribed from the pinned mimic-code DDL and the finding's EP-22 example is wrong: column maps live in Contract.column_maps and change content_hash() but not Table.column_names, so header status would not actually be affected by them (only the hash would move, harmlessly); (d) raw_snapshot_id excludes header, so provenance ids are unaffected either way. Corrections to the fix: the record already stores `header`, so re-evaluation needs no read_header/file I/O; FileRecord is frozen (use model_copy); count refreshed files separately from `processed` so the EP-16 verification recipe stays valid; add a doc line about --force after contract edits. Nothing in DESIGN/DECISIONS/roadmap documents this limitation today (grepped contract_hash/resume/matches_stat).

### INV-3 — `source_manifest_id` means two different things: D-26 addendum/DESIGN §15 say `raw_snapshot_id`, EP-17 brief says the per-file sha256 (and the demo tier has neither)

**Severity (verified):** low · **Verdict:** confirmed-with-corrections (high confidence) · **Kind:** docs-drift · **When:** owner-decision · **Effort:** S · **Retro episode:** EP-166 · **Lens:** raw inventory / provenance module (EP-10)

**Files:** `mimicwarehouse/DECISIONS.md:196-207`, `mimicwarehouse/DESIGN.md:174-182`, `mimicwarehouse/DESIGN.md:634-635`, `roadmap/EP-17-stage-loader-core.md:58-63`, `roadmap/EP-22-stage-demo-tier.md:24-40`

**Evidence.** DECISIONS D-26 addendum: 'This id [raw_snapshot_id] is the `source manifest id` every lake manifest (EP-17+) cites'. DESIGN §15 EP-10 note: 'EP-17/18/19 cite `raw_snapshot_id()` as the `source manifest id`'. EP-17 brief item 4: '`source_manifest_id` (the sha256 EP-10 recorded for the source file in `lake/manifests/raw/<dataset>.jsonl`, or `"fixture"`)'. EP-22 stages `.csv.gz` from `ext\demo\…` with its own `source.yaml`; `read_header` (inventory.py:333-340) reads raw bytes so it cannot inventory a `.csv.gz`, and no brief says what a demo-tier manifest line cites.

**Impact.** EP-17 will implement one reading, EP-19/35 (snapshot ids, run ledger) the other; a manifest line carrying only a per-file sha256 cannot be joined back to a raw snapshot without the raw JSONL, and one carrying only the snapshot id cannot prove which file version was read. Demo-tier lines get an undefined value.

**Proposed fix (reviewer).** Owner decision at EP-16: (a) `source_manifest_id` = raw_snapshot_id (snapshot-level) + a new `source_sha256` per line; (b) keep EP-17's per-file sha256 and add `raw_snapshot_id` as a second field; (c) per-file only. Recommend (b): both fields, `raw_snapshot_id` nullable for fixture/demo, and for demo use the `source.yaml` file sha256 as `source_sha256`. Then align the D-26 addendum, DESIGN §5/§15 and EP-17 item 4 to the chosen wording.

**Corrected fix (verifier).** Handle at EP-16 (re-plan P1), item 6, which already instructs the session to add an "EP-16 pickup note" to EP-17 saying `mimicwarehouse.inventory.raw_snapshot_id()` is the loader manifest's `source manifest id` (roadmap/EP-16-replan-p1.md:73-76). Make that pickup note explicit rather than a bare rename: keep EP-17's per-file field (rename to `source_sha256`: the sha256 from `lake/manifests/raw/<dataset>.jsonl` via `load_raw_manifest().records[rel_path]`, or the staged file's own sha256 for fixture/demo) and add `raw_snapshot_id: str | None` (from `raw_snapshot_id()`, `None` for fixture/demo). For the demo tier, EP-22 item 1 already writes `ext\demo\source.yaml` with per-file `{path, sha256, bytes}` from PhysioNet's SHA256SUMS — cite that sha256 as `source_sha256` and add one sentence to EP-22 item 2/3 saying so. Then align the wording in DECISIONS.md:204-205 (D-26 addendum), DESIGN.md:181 (§5) and DESIGN.md:634-635 (§15), inventory.py:6 docstring, and EP-17 item 4 to "`raw_snapshot_id` (snapshot) + `source_sha256` (per file)". Effort S (30 min of doc edits inside EP-16; no code change until EP-17). No separate owner decision is strictly required — the both-fields default is cheap and loses nothing — but the owner can veto if they prefer a single field.

**Verifier notes.** Checked: DECISIONS.md:196-207 (D-26 addendum, "This id is the `source manifest id` every lake manifest (EP-17+) cites"), DESIGN.md:174-182 (§5 lists "source manifest id" without defining it — planning-era text, commit cd67743) and DESIGN.md:634-635 (§15 EP-10 note "EP-17/18/19 cite `raw_snapshot_id()` as the `source manifest id`"), inventory.py:1-20 docstring (same snapshot-level wording), roadmap/EP-10-raw-inventory.md:34,174, roadmap/EP-16-replan-p1.md:73-76, roadmap/EP-17-stage-loader-core.md:58-63 (item 4: `source_manifest_id` = "the sha256 EP-10 recorded for the source file in lake/manifests/raw/<dataset>.jsonl, or "fixture""), roadmap/EP-22-stage-demo-tier.md:24-60, roadmap/EP-18/19/35 briefs (grep for manifest/snapshot/source_manifest), inventory.py:325-340 (`read_header`), and `git log --follow` on EP-17 (unchanged since planning commit cd67743, so the per-file reading is the original planning intent and EP-10/EP-16 later re-declared it snapshot-level). The two-meanings drift is REAL and the fixture value ("fixture") is defined while the demo-tier value is genuinely unspecified. Corrections: (1) the claim that "EP-19/35 implement the other reading" does not hold — neither EP-19 (layer snapshot id = sha256 over lake manifest lines, EP-19:53-57) nor EP-35 (records `snapshot_ids` per layer, EP-35:27,36) reads or defines `source_manifest_id`; no downstream brief consumes the field, so the join-back impact is theoretical provenance hygiene, not a concrete tripwire; (2) the `read_header` cannot-read-`.csv.gz` point is irrelevant: no brief runs the raw inventory over the demo tree; the loader itself stages `.csv.gz` via DuckDB (EP-17 test item, line 79) and EP-22's `source.yaml` already carries per-file PhysioNet sha256s, which is the natural demo value; (3) EP-16 item 6 (line 76) already schedules exactly this alignment as a pickup note into EP-17, so this is "fix at the next re-plan", not a blocking owner decision — the only substantive risk is that EP-16's wording ("raw_snapshot_id() as the source manifest id") would silently drop the per-file sha256 that EP-17 planned, so the pickup note should keep both. Severity therefore low (docs drift with a scheduled fix point), not medium.

**Owner question (as posed by the reviewer).** What does a lake manifest line's `source_manifest_id` cite — the raw_snapshot_id (D-26 addendum, DESIGN), the per-file raw sha256 (EP-17 brief), or both as two fields? Recommendation: both (`source_sha256` per file + `raw_snapshot_id`), nullable for fixture/demo tiers.

### INV-4 — No table→FileRecord / table→source-path lookup: the `<dataset-dir>/<csv_path>` key formula is duplicated four times and EP-17/20 will re-derive it

**Severity (verified):** low · **Verdict:** downgraded (high confidence) · **Kind:** forward-compat · **When:** next-replan · **Effort:** S · **Retro episode:** EP-167 · **Lens:** raw inventory / provenance module (EP-10)

**Files:** `mimicwarehouse/src/mimicwarehouse/inventory.py:462`, `mimicwarehouse/src/mimicwarehouse/inventory.py:661-663`, `mimicwarehouse/src/mimicwarehouse/inventory.py:937`, `mimicwarehouse/tests/ep/test_ep10.py:449`, `roadmap/EP-17-stage-loader-core.md:50-63`, `roadmap/EP-20-stage-small-tables.md:50-53`

**Evidence.** `rel_path=rel_path or f"{ds_dir}/{table.csv_path}"` (462), `rel = f"{ds_dir}/{t.csv_path}"` (663), `manifest.records.get(f"{dataset_dir(t.dataset)}/{t.csv_path}")` (937), and the test's `manifest.records[f"{DATASET_DIRS[t.dataset]}/{t.csv_path}"]`. `RawManifest` exposes only `records: dict[str, FileRecord]` and `by_dataset`. EP-17 needs 'the sha256 EP-10 recorded for the source file' per `table_spec` and the file's absolute path under `settings.source_root`; EP-20 must compare `status.json.rows` with 'EP-10's raw manifest rows (`lake/manifests/raw/mimic-iv-3.1.jsonl`)'.

**Impact.** EP-17 either imports `DATASET_DIRS`/`dataset_dir` and rebuilds the string (a fifth copy) or hard-codes directory names; a later rename of a PhysioNet directory or of `csv_path` breaks the join silently (record not found → `source_manifest_id` None) instead of at one helper.

**Proposed fix (reviewer).** Add to inventory.py: `rel_path_for(table: Table) -> str`, `source_path(table, settings=None) -> Path` (= `settings.source_root / dataset_dir / csv_path`), and `RawManifest.for_table(table_or_qualified_name) -> FileRecord | None`; use them in `inventory_file`, `plan_files`, `reconcile` and the tests; mention them in the EP-17 brief's item 4 and EP-20 item 4.

**Corrected fix (verifier).** Add `rel_path_for(table: Table) -> str` and `RawManifest.for_table(table: Table) -> FileRecord | None` (join on `(schema_name, table)` or `rel_path_for`) in inventory.py; use them at inventory.py:462, :663, :937 and tests/ep/test_ep10.py:430; do not add `source_path()` — `plan_files(contract, settings.source_root)` indexed by `table_qn` already resolves table→absolute source path; record both helpers in the EP-16 pickup note for EP-17 item 4 and EP-20 item 4, and in the same note resolve whether `source_manifest_id` is the per-file sha256 (EP-17 wording) or `raw_snapshot_id()` (EP-10/EP-16 wording), and correct `lake/manifests/raw/<dataset>.jsonl` to `<dataset-dir>.jsonl`. Effort S.

**Verifier notes.** Checked mimicwarehouse/src/mimicwarehouse/inventory.py (DATASET_DIRS 76-80, FileRecord 100-115, PlannedFile 155-167, RawManifest 170-188, dataset_dir 259-264, inventory_file 425-470, plan_files 652-687, load_raw_manifest 544-557, reconcile 930-940, dataset_manifest_path 499-500, __all__ 1358-1400), tests/ep/test_ep10.py (lines 150, 430, 449, 647), roadmap/EP-17-stage-loader-core.md 12-20, 50-63, 80-86, roadmap/EP-20-stage-small-tables.md 50-56, roadmap/EP-16-replan-p1.md 72-80, roadmap/EP-10-raw-inventory.md 62, 174.

Facts that hold: the `f"{dataset_dir}/{csv_path}"` key is indeed built independently at inventory.py:462, :663, :937 and in the test (the test occurrence is line 430, not 449; 449-450 hard-codes "mimic-iv-3.1/hosp/services.csv"). RawManifest exposes only `records` (keyed by rel_path), `by_dataset`, `files_done`, `raw_snapshot_id`; there is no `for_table` and no `rel_path_for/source_path` helper; `__all__` exports `dataset_dir` and `DATASET_DIRS` so a downstream module can rebuild the key. EP-17 item 4 and EP-20 item 4 do require a per-table join against the raw manifest.

Why the severity is over-rated: (1) `FileRecord` already carries `schema_name` and `table` (inventory.py:108-109), so EP-17/20 can join on `(schema_name, table)` == `Table.schema_name/name` without ever touching the path formula — the "fifth copy or hard-coded directory names" dilemma is false. (2) `plan_files(contract, source_root)` (652-687) already IS the table→absolute-source-path resolver: `PlannedFile.table_qn`, `.rel_path`, `.path` — EP-17 can index it by `table_qn`; the proposed `source_path()` largely exists. (3) The writer (`inventory_file`) and the reader (`reconcile`) both go through `dataset_dir()`, so a rename of a PhysioNet directory does not "break the join silently": manifest lines are re-derived from the same helper on the next inventory run and `dataset_manifest_path` uses it too; a silent None only arises if EP-17 hard-codes strings, which is a hypothetical authoring mistake, not a present defect. (4) EP-16 item 6 (replan-p1.md:73-77) already mandates amending EP-17..EP-22 with the shipped P1 API names, so the "mention them in the EP-17/EP-20 brief" part of the fix has an existing home. Net: real DRY/ergonomics gap, no current failure scenario, low.

Corrected fix (S, at EP-16 re-plan or the first EP-17 session): add `rel_path_for(table) -> str` and `RawManifest.for_table(table) -> FileRecord | None` (lookup by `(schema_name, table)` or by `rel_path_for`) to inventory.py and use them at :462, :663, :937 and test_ep10.py:430; do NOT add a separate `source_path()` — document that `plan_files(contract, settings.source_root)` keyed by `table_qn` is the table→source-path resolver, and say so in EP-17 item 1/4 via the EP-16 pickup note. Also worth flagging to the owner in the same pickup note (adjacent inconsistency found while verifying): EP-17 item 4 defines `source_manifest_id` as the per-file sha256 from EP-10, whereas EP-10 item 62/completion note 174 and EP-16 item 6 say the loader's `source manifest id` is `raw_snapshot_id()` (whole-snapshot id) — decide which (options: per-file sha256 in `source_manifest_id` plus snapshot id in a separate `raw_snapshot_id` field, or snapshot id only). Also EP-17 line 62 says `lake/manifests/raw/<dataset>.jsonl` while the file is named by directory (`<dataset-dir>.jsonl`, e.g. the note dataset file is the long PhysioNet dir name) — cosmetic, fix in the same pickup note.

### SCH-1 — Contract has no CSV parsing hints; upstream TIMESTAMP(3) precision is discarded and the CSV dialect is duplicated outside the contract

**Severity (verified):** low · **Verdict:** confirmed-with-corrections (high confidence) · **Kind:** design · **When:** now-should · **Effort:** M · **Retro episode:** EP-169 · **Lens:** schema-contract (EP-9) as the spine of later briefs

**Files:** `mimicwarehouse/src/mimicwarehouse/schema/contract.py:274`, `mimicwarehouse/src/mimicwarehouse/schema/transcribe.py:19`, `mimicwarehouse/src/mimicwarehouse/concepts/vendor/mimic-code/mimic-iv/buildmimic/postgres/create.sql:244`, `mimicwarehouse/src/mimicwarehouse/concepts/vendor/mimic-code/mimic-iv/buildmimic/postgres/create.sql:487`, `mimicwarehouse/src/mimicwarehouse/inventory.py:88`, `mimicwarehouse/src/mimicwarehouse/fixtures/write.py:54`, `mimicwarehouse/src/mimicwarehouse/fixtures/catalog.py:34`, `roadmap/EP-17-stage-loader-core.md:39`

**Evidence.** transcribe.py maps `TIMESTAMP(n) / TIMESTAMP -> TIMESTAMP (naive, as shipped)` and the drift check compares mapped DuckDB types, so create.sql's `starttime TIMESTAMP(3)`, `stoptime TIMESTAMP(3)`, `entertime TIMESTAMP(3) NOT NULL`, `verifiedtime TIMESTAMP(3)`, `expirationdate TIMESTAMP(3)` (pharmacy), `starttime/stoptime TIMESTAMP(3)` (prescriptions) and `charttime/storetime TIMESTAMP(3) NOT NULL` (outputevents) leave no trace in the YAML. The only loader-facing helper is `read_csv_columns()` -> `{name: duckdb_type}`. The dialect already lives in inventory.py `CSV_READ_OPTIONS = "header=true, all_varchar=true, delim=',', quote='\"', escape='\"'"`, fixtures/write.py `TIMESTAMP_FORMAT = "%Y-%m-%d %H:%M:%S"` / `DATE_FORMAT`, fixtures/catalog.py `READ_CSV_SQL`, and EP-17 item 2 hard-codes `dateformat='%Y-%m-%d', timestampformat='%Y-%m-%d %H:%M:%S', quote='"', escape='"', nullstr=''` a fourth time. Upstream's own DuckDB build (build_mimic.sh) uses `COPY ... (HEADER, DELIM ',', QUOTE '"', ESCAPE '"')` with no timestampformat. Nothing in the contract states the quoted-empty-string policy (`allow_quoted_nulls`, DuckDB default true) that the two nullability relaxations (spec_type_desc, drug: 'zero-length strings load as NULL') depend on.

**Impact.** EP-17 will read every timestamp with one fixed strptime format; if any of the nine TIMESTAMP(3) columns carries fractional seconds in the raw CSV (upstream declared millisecond precision for a reason), the whole file is rejected under `loader_reject_max=0` and the session has to discover per-table formats ad hoc; if EP-17 sets `allow_quoted_nulls=false` (a legitimate choice to preserve upstream NOT NULL) the two recorded relaxations become wrong. Three copies of the dialect will drift (fixtures currently generate with one, EP-17 will read with another).

**Proposed fix (reviewer).** Add a `csv_dialect` block to the contract (schema-level default in each `<schema>.yaml` header: `delim, quote, escape, header, nullstr, allow_quoted_nulls, dateformat, timestampformat, encoding`) exposed as `Table.read_csv_options()`; keep upstream precision as `upstream_type: "TIMESTAMP(3)"`-style notes or a `csv_format` per column when it differs; make fixtures/write.py, fixtures/catalog.py, inventory.py and EP-17 import the one constant. In EP-17 add a dev-tier aggregate probe (`SELECT max(length(<col>)) FROM read_csv(..., all_varchar=true)` on pharmacy/prescriptions/outputevents) before fixing timestampformat, or leave timestampformat unset and rely on DuckDB's ISO cast which accepts optional fractional seconds. State the quoted-null policy in D-17 next to the two relaxations.

**Corrected fix (verifier).** Keep the finding but shrink it. (1) In roadmap/EP-17-stage-loader-core.md item 2 (line 38-39) drop the hard-coded `timestampformat='%Y-%m-%d %H:%M:%S'` (and `dateformat`) or make them a single shared constant; prefer DuckDB's default ISO cast for TIMESTAMP columns, which accepts both `HH:MM:SS` and `HH:MM:SS.fff` (verified with duckdb 1.5.5: `try_strptime('…12:00:00.123','%Y-%m-%d %H:%M:%S')` -> NULL, `try_cast(... as timestamp)` -> ok). Add a one-line dev-tier aggregate probe to EP-17/EP-20 (`SELECT max(length(col)) FROM read_csv(..., all_varchar=true)` on pharmacy/prescriptions/outputevents) so the fractional-seconds question is answered by counts, not by a full-tier failure under `loader_reject_max=0`. (2) Introduce ONE dialect constant (e.g. `mimicwarehouse/schema/csv_dialect.py`: `delim, quote, escape, header, nullstr, allow_quoted_nulls=True`, plus the fixture writer's `TIMESTAMP_FORMAT`/`DATE_FORMAT`) and import it from fixtures/write.py:54, fixtures/catalog.py:34-36 and the future loader/csv.py; inventory.py:88 may stay separate (deliberately `all_varchar` counting) but should reference the same delim/quote/escape. A full `csv_dialect` YAML block + `Table.read_csv_options()` is heavier than needed and not required by any later brief. (3) Do NOT add `upstream_type: "TIMESTAMP(3)"` to the nine columns: transcribe.py:19 already documents the mapping, the checker compares type classes, and DuckDB TIMESTAMP is microsecond so no precision is lost on load; a YAML note would only add noise. (4) Add one sentence to D-17 addendum / DESIGN §7 stating the quoted-empty-string policy: loader uses DuckDB defaults (`nullstr=''`, `allow_quoted_nulls=true`), matching build_mimic.sh; the two `upstream_nullable` relaxations depend on it. Effort S (30 min), timing: before EP-17 starts (brief edit + constant module); owner decision only on the quoted-null policy (recommend keeping DuckDB default = upstream behaviour).

**Verifier notes.** Checked: mimicwarehouse/src/mimicwarehouse/schema/transcribe.py:13-27 (TIMESTAMP(n) -> TIMESTAMP, deviations only via upstream_type/upstream_nullable); contract.py:274-276 (`read_csv_columns` is the only loader-facing helper; no dialect); vendored create.sql lines 244-250, 264, 305-306, 487-488 (nine TIMESTAMP(3) columns) vs mimiciv_hosp.yaml lines 291-306 etc. (plain TIMESTAMP, no trace) — precision claim holds; build_mimic.sh:66-79 and 113 (`sed 's/TIMESTAMP\([0-9]+\)/TIMESTAMP/'`, COPY with HEADER/DELIM/QUOTE/ESCAPE only, no timestampformat, relies on DuckDB default allow_quoted_nulls for the two NOT NULL relaxations) — so upstream reference behaviour is the ISO cast; inventory.py:88 CSV_READ_OPTIONS, fixtures/write.py:54-55 TIMESTAMP_FORMAT/DATE_FORMAT, fixtures/catalog.py:34-36 READ_CSV_SQL, tests/ep/test_ep11.py:91 (literal duplicate), roadmap/EP-17-stage-loader-core.md:38-39 (hard-coded timestampformat, nullstr='') and :72-73 (loader_reject_max default 0) — duplication claim holds (four places, though inventory's all_varchar variant is intentionally different and write.py is a writer format). DECISIONS.md D-17 addendum lines 123-135 and mimiciv_hosp.yaml:233,356: quoted-null policy is only implicit ("under DuckDB's CSV reader"). Ran `uv run python -c` with the pinned duckdb: strict strptime rejects fractional seconds, ISO cast accepts them, confirming the failure mechanism IF the raw CSVs contain fractional seconds. Could not and did not verify whether they do (no data access in this review; EP-17 dev tests only stage d_labitems/patients, so a mismatch would surface later, e.g. EP-20+, as an obvious parse error fixed by dropping timestampformat). Because the premise is unverified, the fix is a one-line brief change, and DuckDB TIMESTAMP loses no precision, severity is over-rated at medium and the proposed contract-level `csv_dialect` block plus `upstream_type` annotations is more machinery than the risk warrants; the shared-constant consolidation and D-17 policy sentence are still worth doing now (S).

**Owner question (as posed by the reviewer).** Which quoted-empty-string policy should the loader adopt: (a) DuckDB default `allow_quoted_nulls=true` (matches upstream build_mimic.sh; keeps the two contract relaxations) or (b) `allow_quoted_nulls=false` (preserves upstream NOT NULL for spec_type_desc/drug and PK completeness for prescriptions)? Recommendation: (a), recorded in D-17, because the vendored DuckDB build is the reference behaviour and (b) would leave '' values inside PK columns.

## Low/info findings — detail (unverified)

### ARCH-11 — DuckDB memory/thread budget is coherent for one process but not for the concurrent shape P2 creates: a 36 GB background build + dev-tier tests opening a second 36 GB build connection + the 12 GB app, 12+12 threads on 16 cores

**Severity:** low · **Kind:** design · **When:** now-should · **Effort:** S · **Retro episode:** EP-166 · **Lens:** data architecture parity/uniformity (lake / catalog / tier / DAG design vs EP-3/9/10/11/12 code)

**Files:** `mimicwarehouse/src/mimicwarehouse/config.py:511-519,626-643`, `mimicwarehouse/DESIGN.md:28-33,191-200`, `roadmap/EP-17-stage-loader-core.md:24-31,73-77`, `roadmap/EP-18-stage-loader-buckets.md:64-67`, `roadmap/EP-19-stage-dag-runner.md:31-35`

**Evidence.** config.py: `duckdb_memory_limit = "36GB"`, `duckdb_app_memory_limit = "12GB"`, one `duckdb_threads = 12` for both profiles. EP-17/18 `tier("dev")` tests stage real tables into `<data_root>\tmp\ep17\` with `open_build_connection` (build profile) while, per the P2 ordering rationale, ⏱ jobs (EP-23..27, each a build-profile process) run in the background for hours; the app (EP-57) adds a 12 GB / 12-thread reader. Machine: 64 GB RAM, 16 cores. Temp budget itself is coherent: `max_temp_directory_size` 150 GB + lake ~25 GB against 414 GB free leaves > 100 GB, and `min_free_gb` is checked at build start.

**Impact.** Two build-profile processes = 72 GB nominal on 64 GB (DuckDB's limit bounds the buffer pool only; CSV reader/sort overhead sits on top) - a dev-tier test run during a chartevents ⏱ job can OOM the job (which is resumable, but costs hours) or the test; 24 DuckDB threads + Streamlit + Polars on 16 cores thrash.

**Proposed fix.** Add `duckdb_app_threads` (default 4-6) to Settings/`duckdb_settings('app')`; have EP-17's `open_build_connection(memory_limit=...)` default to a smaller limit (e.g. 8GB) when called from tests/`tier('dev')` helpers, and state in DESIGN section 6 'one build-profile connection per machine at a time; the build lock covers `mwh build`, tests use the app profile'.

### ARCH-15 — Contract has no marker for DATE-grain time columns (patients.dod, procedures_icd/hcpcsevents/omr.chartdate, microbiologyevents chartdate fallback); EP-34/49/50 treat time as TIMESTAMP

**Severity:** low · **Kind:** forward-compat · **When:** next-replan · **Effort:** XS · **Retro episode:** EP-166 · **Lens:** data architecture parity/uniformity (lake / catalog / tier / DAG design vs EP-3/9/10/11/12 code)

**Files:** `mimicwarehouse/src/mimicwarehouse/schema/tables/mimiciv_hosp.yaml:191,258,274,378`, `mimicwarehouse/src/mimicwarehouse/schema/contract.py:60-62,161-163`, `roadmap/EP-34-time-semantics-grains.md:34-40`, `mimicwarehouse/DESIGN.md:212-236,300-308`

**Evidence.** Contract: `hcpcsevents.chartdate DATE` (nullable) is that table's `time_column`; `procedures_icd.chartdate DATE NOT NULL`, `omr.chartdate DATE NOT NULL`, `patients.dod DATE`; `TIME_TYPES = {TIMESTAMP, DATE}` so both are legal time columns. EP-34 item 2: `sql_hours_since(anchor, event)` = `date_diff('second', anchor, event) / 3600.0`; DESIGN section 10 spine column `time` (MEDS-shaped) with no resolution flag. Types otherwise agree: naive TIMESTAMP, DOUBLE for NUMERIC/FLOAT, DuckDB FLOAT for REAL, no DECIMAL in the contract.

**Impact.** Hours-since computed from a DATE (midnight) silently mixes day- and second-resolution events in timelines, spine and hour bins; nothing forces EP-49/50 to say so. Not a loader problem now, but the loader is the last cheap place to record it.

**Proposed fix.** Add an optional `time_resolution: day|second` (derived: DATE -> day) to `Table`/`Column` or at least a DESIGN section 7 sentence 'DATE-grain time columns are day-resolution; relative-time helpers must not derive hours from them; the spine carries `time_resolution`'; EP-34 implements the guard.

### CFG-10 — catalog_path("fixture") and the fixture-tier build path contradict DESIGN §3 and will hit the 100 GB free-space guard on temp roots

**Severity:** low · **Kind:** forward-compat · **When:** next-replan · **Effort:** S · **Retro episode:** EP-167 · **Lens:** settings, CLI contract and host diagnostics (config.py / cli.py / doctor.py / .env.example / .streamlit / tests ep02-ep03-ep164)

**Evidence.** config.py:622-624 `catalog_path(tier)` -> `<data_root>/warehouse/<tier>.duckdb` for any Tier incl. fixture; DESIGN.md:105 tree: "warehouse\ {demo,dev,full}.duckdb catalogs (EP-21)"; DESIGN.md:169 "until EP-21 builds a real fixture.duckdb from the same CSVs"; conftest.py:110-111 hard-codes fixture = "in-memory fixture catalog"; EP-21 item 4 builds the fixture lake+catalog into a session temp root via `mwh build --tier fixture --tag small --data-root <tmp>` — which runs the D-29 validators and (EP-19) `require_free_space(min_free_gb=100)` on the tmp volume.

**Impact.** After EP-21 the fixture tier — promised as "always available; a fresh checkout is never red for lack of data" (DESIGN §20) — needs >= 100 GB free and an NTFS temp volume; the DESIGN §3 tree and the conftest short-circuit go stale.

**Proposed fix.** DESIGN §3 tree: add `fixture.duckdb (temp roots only)`; EP-19/EP-21 amendment: the fixture-tier build uses a size-based guard (`Settings(min_free_gb=1)` for tmp roots or `require_free_space(path, min(min_free_gb, 2 * expected_bytes))`); conftest `catalog_status` to switch from the in-memory short-circuit to the built catalog at EP-21 (note the TODO now).

### CFG-11 — workspace_root()/repo_root() fall back to CWD for a wheel install — every workspace-relative path silently moves

**Severity:** low · **Kind:** forward-compat · **When:** next-replan · **Effort:** S · **Retro episode:** EP-167 · **Lens:** settings, CLI contract and host diagnostics (config.py / cli.py / doctor.py / .env.example / .streamlit / tests ep02-ep03-ep164)

**Files:** `mimicwarehouse/src/mimicwarehouse/config.py:151`, `mimicwarehouse/src/mimicwarehouse/config.py:162`, `mimicwarehouse/src/mimicwarehouse/fixtures/write.py:69`, `mimicwarehouse/src/mimicwarehouse/inventory.py:971`, `mimicwarehouse/src/mimicwarehouse/theme.py:427`, `mimicwarehouse/src/mimicwarehouse/verify.py:118`

**Evidence.** config.py:151-158 `candidate = Path(__file__).resolve().parents[2]; if (candidate/'pyproject.toml').is_file(): return candidate; return Path.cwd()`; config.py:162-167 `repo_root()` -> None without a `.git` ancestor, so `_default_source_root` becomes `cwd.parent / 'source material'`; fixtures/write.py:69, inventory.py:971, theme.py:427, verify.py:118-126 all derive `.env`/`mwh.toml`, tests/fixtures, docs/, .streamlit and tests/ep paths from it.

**Impact.** EP-158 (`mwh init` bootstrap/cloner) or EP-160 (docs site) installing the wheel would read `.env` from wherever the shell happens to be, write fixtures under `<cwd>/tests/fixtures`, and probe `<cwd>/../source material` — with no warning.

**Proposed fix.** Either declare editable-checkout-only support (raise `ConfigError('mimicwarehouse must run from its checkout (uv run …)')` when the src layout is absent) or add an explicit `MWH_WORKSPACE` setting/`--workspace` option and make the fallback log a warning; decide in EP-158's brief; a doctor `settings` line "workspace: <path> (from package|cwd)" would make it visible now.

**Owner question.** Is a non-editable (wheel) install ever a supported mode (EP-158 cloner, EP-160 docs)? Options: editable-only with a hard error / explicit MWH_WORKSPACE. Recommendation: editable-only until a brief needs otherwise.

### CFG-12 — DuckDB reads "36GB"/"150GB" as decimal (33.5 / 139.6 GiB) while the project's GB means GiB — units undocumented

**Severity:** low · **Kind:** docs-drift · **When:** next-replan · **Effort:** XS · **Retro episode:** EP-167 · **Lens:** settings, CLI contract and host diagnostics (config.py / cli.py / doctor.py / .env.example / .streamlit / tests ep02-ep03-ep164)

**Files:** `mimicwarehouse/src/mimicwarehouse/config.py:70`, `mimicwarehouse/src/mimicwarehouse/config.py:512`, `mimicwarehouse/.env.example:22`, `mimicwarehouse/DESIGN.md:193`

**Evidence.** Scratch connect with `duckdb_settings('build')` then `select current_setting(...)`: memory_limit '36GB' -> `33.5 GiB`, max_temp_directory_size '150GB' -> `139.6 GiB`, app '12GB' -> `11.1 GiB` (duckdb 1.5.5). config.py:70 `GB = 2**30  # GiB, labelled "GB"`; min_free_gb=100 and doctor disk figures are GiB; DESIGN §6 "36–40 GB builds; 8–16 GB app; e.g. 150 GB" and §3 budget arithmetic mix the two. Both connections open at once (background build + app) = 44.7 GiB of 64 GiB, with 12 threads each.

**Impact.** Not a bug, but the free-space/temp arithmetic in DESIGN §3 (100 GB guard vs 150 GB temp cap) is off by ~7 %, and a future author may 'fix' the mismatch in either direction; concurrent build+app memory (44.7 GiB + Polars + Streamlit) is close to the 64 GB ceiling and undocumented.

**Proposed fix.** One sentence in .env.example ("DuckDB parses GB as 10^9 bytes; write GiB for binary") and DESIGN §6; optionally switch defaults to '36GiB'/'12GiB'/'150GiB' if the owner wants binary units everywhere; add a DESIGN §6 line on the concurrent build+app budget (and EP-57's app threads 8).

### CFG-15 — Dead members and duplicated helpers around Settings/CLI/doctor

**Severity:** low · **Kind:** design · **When:** next-replan · **Effort:** XS · **Retro episode:** EP-167 · **Lens:** settings, CLI contract and host diagnostics (config.py / cli.py / doctor.py / .env.example / .streamlit / tests ep02-ep03-ep164)

**Files:** `mimicwarehouse/src/mimicwarehouse/cli.py:72`, `mimicwarehouse/src/mimicwarehouse/config.py:495`, `mimicwarehouse/src/mimicwarehouse/doctor.py:325`, `mimicwarehouse/src/mimicwarehouse/doctor.py:71`, `mimicwarehouse/src/mimicwarehouse/config.py:598`

**Evidence.** cli.py:72-76 `CliState.data_root_override` and `.data_root` have no readers (grep: only the constructor); config.py:495/498 `env_file=".env"`, `toml_file="mwh.toml"` in model_config are dead because `settings_customise_sources` builds explicit sources at workspace_root (config.py:479-487); doctor.py:325-338 `_drive_of`/`_mount_of` duplicate `config.drive_letter`/`volume_root`; doctor.py:71 `DISK_FAIL_GB = 100` duplicates the `min_free_gb` default; `Settings.sources()` re-instantiates and re-reads .env/mwh.toml once per field (13 x 3 reads per doctor/paths call, config.py:551-576).

**Impact.** Harmless today; the two workspace-relative model_config entries invite a future author to 'fix' the wrong place, and the seam duplication (doctor._drive_of vs config.drive_letter) means tests patch two different functions for one concept.

**Proposed fix.** Remove the unused CliState members and the two model_config keys (keep a comment pointing at named_sources); have doctor use `config.drive_letter`/`volume_root`; compute `sources()` from one pass over the three sources; fold `DISK_FAIL_GB` into `Settings.model_fields['min_free_gb'].default`.

### CFG-16 — doctor `uv` check depends on uv being on PATH although `uv run` exports UV=<exe>; doctor costs ~6 s per run (three PowerShell spawns) and EP-35 embeds it in every run manifest

**Severity:** low · **Kind:** environment · **When:** next-replan · **Effort:** XS · **Retro episode:** EP-167 · **Lens:** settings, CLI contract and host diagnostics (config.py / cli.py / doctor.py / .env.example / .streamlit / tests ep02-ep03-ep164)

**Files:** `mimicwarehouse/src/mimicwarehouse/doctor.py:170`, `mimicwarehouse/src/mimicwarehouse/doctor.py:363`, `mimicwarehouse/src/mimicwarehouse/doctor.py:145`, `roadmap/EP-35-run-ledger.md:22`

**Evidence.** `uv run python -c "import os; print(os.environ.get('UV'))"` -> `C:\Users\willi\AppData\Local\Microsoft\WinGet\Links\uv.exe`; doctor.py:170-174 runs `["uv", "--version"]` from PATH; persistent memory: uv is not on the tool PATH by default (needed `export PATH=...` here). EP-164 note: bitlocker/defender/antivirus PowerShell probes ~0.8-2 s each, whole doctor 6.4 s; check_data_root also writes a `.mwh-doctor-*.tmp` file into the data root each run.

**Impact.** In pre-commit/tool shells `check_uv` warns spuriously; once EP-35 embeds `run_checks()` in every manifest, each run pays ~6 s and three PowerShell processes (Malwarebytes watches process bursts, D-42).

**Proposed fix.** `_uv_version` -> `_run([os.environ.get('UV', 'uv'), '--version'])`; add `run_checks(settings, quick=True)` (skip PowerShell/nvidia-smi probes, or batch the three PowerShell scripts into one call) for the manifest path; document in EP-35.

### CFG-17 — mimicwarehouse/mwh.toml is not gitignored — a local settings file would silently retarget every checkout's data root if committed

**Severity:** low · **Kind:** governance · **When:** owner-decision · **Effort:** XS · **Retro episode:** EP-167 · **Lens:** settings, CLI contract and host diagnostics (config.py / cli.py / doctor.py / .env.example / .streamlit / tests ep02-ep03-ep164)

**Evidence.** `git check-ignore -v mimicwarehouse/mwh.toml mimicwarehouse/.env` -> only `.gitignore:74 .env mimicwarehouse/.env`; .gitignore has `.env`, `.env.*`, `!.env.example` and no `mwh.toml`; config.py:486 reads `<workspace>/mwh.toml [settings]` on every invocation.

**Impact.** No data-leak risk (paths only), but an accidentally committed `mwh.toml` with `data_root`/`duckdb_memory_limit` becomes a repo-wide default for a collaborator or the EP-158 cloner, bypassing the per-machine intent of .env.

**Proposed fix.** Owner decision (CLAUDE.md §6 requires asking before touching .gitignore): either add `mimicwarehouse/mwh.toml` to .gitignore and say 'local overrides only' in .env.example:5, or document mwh.toml as the committed project-defaults file (then move DuckDB defaults there and keep .env personal).

**Owner question.** Is mwh.toml a local (gitignored) file or a committed project-defaults file? Recommendation: gitignore it; project defaults already live in the Settings class.

### CFG-7 — JSON outputs end in \r\r\n and settings errors go to stdout, mixing with JSON

**Severity:** low · **Kind:** bug · **When:** now-should · **Effort:** XS · **Retro episode:** EP-167 · **Lens:** settings, CLI contract and host diagnostics (config.py / cli.py / doctor.py / .env.example / .streamlit / tests ep02-ep03-ep164)

**Files:** `mimicwarehouse/src/mimicwarehouse/config.py:808`, `mimicwarehouse/src/mimicwarehouse/doctor.py:841`, `mimicwarehouse/src/mimicwarehouse/guard.py:682`, `mimicwarehouse/src/mimicwarehouse/cli.py:115`

**Evidence.** `uv run mwh paths --json | tail -c 12 | od -c` -> `]  \r  \n   }  \r  \r  \n`; same for guard --json; schema list --json ends `\r\n` cleanly. Cause: `sys.stdout.write(json.dumps(...) + os.linesep)` on a text-mode stdout that already translates \n -> \r\n. cli.py:115 prints settings failures via `console` (stdout), while schema/fixtures/inventory use `err_console` (stderr); `mwh doctor --json` with a bad .env therefore emits a non-JSON line on stdout with exit 2.

**Impact.** Cosmetic for parsers, but a `--json > file` committed as an aggregate carries a stray CR; a JSON consumer of `mwh doctor --json` (EP-35 embedding, PowerShell ConvertFrom-Json in briefs) gets prose on stdout when settings fail.

**Proposed fix.** Replace `os.linesep` with `"\n"` (or `print(json.dumps(...))`) in the three writers; route the callback's settings error and `refused:` messages through `err_console`; add a byte-level test on the trailing newline.

Adding a field is mechanically fine (extra=forbid does not block model growth) but test_ep03:584-589 requires an `.env.example` line per field and only checks presence — not stale keys or default drift.

### CMP-4 — Malwarebytes seven-path allow list has been 'owner's word to be re-confirmed at hand-off' since EP-164 and was never re-confirmed; `mwh doctor antivirus` is a permanent warn on this host with no acknowledged state — EP-16 item 6 and every EP-17+ full-tier writer depend on both

**Severity:** low · **Kind:** environment · **When:** owner-decision · **Effort:** S · **Retro episode:** EP-166 · **Lens:** critic

**Files:** `roadmap/EP-164-toolchain-remediation-p1.md:126-129`, `roadmap/EP-16-replan-p1.md:78-83`, `mimicwarehouse/src/mimicwarehouse/doctor.py:604-620`, `mimicwarehouse/DECISIONS.md:505-524`, `roadmap/README.md:394`

**Evidence.** EP-164 note: "**Seven-path allow list still in place: owner's word to be re-confirmed at hand-off** — last confirmed by the owner on 2026-08-17 evening … this session was autonomous and could not ask." No later brief, addendum or memory entry records a re-confirmation (EP-8..EP-12 notes are silent; EP-10 only observes 'neither product interfered'). Doctor summary is now permanently "8 pass · 1 warn · 0 fail · 5 info" (EP-164/EP-12/baseline) because the warn keys on product *presence*; EP-16 item 6 says "Confirm P2 readiness … `mwh doctor` green (exit 0 — no `fail`)" and "Malwarebytes seven-path allow list recorded on the owner's word".

**Impact.** If the owner's Malwarebytes allow list was reset (product update, profile change), the first EP-17 full-tier Parquet write from the unsigned managed `python.exe` is the ARW heuristic's likeliest kill (Risk 12); nobody will notice until a job dies. A warn that is always present trains sessions to ignore `warn`, so a genuinely new warn (e.g. `allow_remote=true`, `longpaths` unset on a fresh clone) is lost in the noise.

**Proposed fix.** Owner re-confirms the seven paths now (and after each Malwarebytes update); record the date as a D-38 addendum. For the doctor: either keep the presence rule and add an `MWH_ACKNOWLEDGED_AV`-style setting/`.env.example` line (or a `--known-warn antivirus` flag) that downgrades the row to `info` with 'acknowledged <date>' text, or revert to the bit-only rule as EP-164 offered — one condition in `check_antivirus`.

**Owner question.** (1) Please re-confirm the seven Malwarebytes exclusions are still in place (date it). (2) Doctor row: keep permanent warn (status quo), add an 'acknowledged' state that shows info (recommended — keeps the reminder in --json for EP-35 manifests but restores warn's signal), or switch to the bit-only rule (silent on this host).

### CMP-8 — `mwh doctor` has no `git` probe although guard/verify/inventory/doctor shell out to git and rely on version ≥ 2.40, repo-local `core.longpaths`, and an installed hook — a fresh clone (EP-158 cloner, collaborator) fails late and diffusely

**Severity:** low · **Kind:** environment · **When:** next-replan · **Effort:** S · **Retro episode:** EP-166 · **Lens:** critic

**Files:** `mimicwarehouse/src/mimicwarehouse/doctor.py:54-69`, `mimicwarehouse/src/mimicwarehouse/guard.py:405`, `mimicwarehouse/src/mimicwarehouse/verify.py:383-400`, `mimicwarehouse/src/mimicwarehouse/inventory.py:286`, `roadmap/EP-4-guard-precommit.md:122-125`, `roadmap/EP-158-bootstrap-init-cloner.md`

**Evidence.** `CHECK_IDS` = python, uv, duckdb, settings, disk_free, data_root, temp_dir, cloud_mounts, defender, antivirus, bitlocker, power_scheme, gpu, longpaths — no `git`. EP-4 note gotcha (3): "`git cat-file --batch -z` (NUL-delimited input) needs git ≥ 2.40; the machine has 2.55." `core.longpaths=true` is repo-local (`git config --get core.longpaths` → true here) and `.git/hooks/pre-commit` is per clone (`ls .git/hooks` → pre-commit); the doctor's `longpaths` check reads the repo config but nothing reports git version or hook presence except `mwh guard --selfcheck` (hook-installed, warn-level).

**Impact.** On a fresh clone the first symptom is a guard traceback inside a pre-commit run or `roadmap_check` failing to resolve hashes, not a doctor row; EP-158's cloner smoke test and any collaborator setup will rediscover this.

**Proposed fix.** Add a `git` check to doctor (version ≥ 2.40 via `git --version`, `core.longpaths` already covered, `.git/hooks/pre-commit` present → warn if not) behind the existing `_run` seam; extend the three fake runners (or the shared helper CFG-13 proposes).

**Proposed fix.** Show both shell forms in tests/README (bash and `$env:` PowerShell); make the skip reason generic ('run `mwh build --tier <t>` / `--select catalog` to create it') so no EP number is baked in.

EP-10 note: "`show [--timing] [--json]` is the only window a Claude session has on the job (deny rules cover the log)".

DuckDB `count(*)` in a test?). Governance gap disguised as a forward reference.

Owner to confirm that P2 briefs already say this (EP-17..EP-29 acceptance) or add it at EP-16.

**Owner question.** What may a pre-EP-30 session run against the data root? Options: (a) only package commands/tests that emit counts+hashes+schema (recommended; matches EP-10 precedent); (b) allow ad-hoc `count(*)`/`DESCRIBE` in a session (needs a written allow-list and would be un-audited); (c) pull EP-30 forward before EP-17.

### ENV-4 — pre-commit local hooks resolve `uv` from the git process PATH; hook and Malwarebytes exclusion are hard-wired to the current .venv path

**Severity:** low · **Kind:** environment · **When:** next-replan · **Effort:** S · **Retro episode:** EP-165 · **Lens:** Local development / session environment as it really is vs. what the roadmap assumed

**Files:** `.pre-commit-config.yaml:16`, `.pre-commit-config.yaml:22`, `.pre-commit-config.yaml:27`, `mimicwarehouse/README.md:109`

**Evidence.** `.git/hooks/pre-commit`: `INSTALL_PYTHON='C:\Users\willi\Documents\DATA\mimicwarehouse\mimicwarehouse\.venv\Scripts\python.exe'` (absolute) then `exec "$INSTALL_PYTHON" -mpre_commit`; the three `language: system` hooks are `entry: uv run --project mimicwarehouse --group dev ...`. `.venv/Scripts` contains `mwh.exe ruff.exe pre-commit.exe poe.exe pytest.exe` but no `uv.exe`. D-38 Malwarebytes path 3 is 'the workspace .venv' (path-based); pyright `venvPath = "."`.

**Impact.** (a) With the stale PATH (ENV-1) a commit from the tool shell fails at hook time with 'uv: command not found' unless the session exports PATH first — the memory note exists precisely because this happened. (b) If the repo folder is ever moved/renamed (e.g. the v1.0 public-repo step, D-41) the hook, the Malwarebytes exclusion and pyright all silently point at the old path; the hook falls back to `pre-commit` on PATH (absent) and every commit fails until `pre-commit install` is re-run.

**Proposed fix.** Keep `uv run` entries (they guarantee the locked toolchain) — ENV-1's restart is the real fix. Add to mimicwarehouse/README.md § Contributing and the D-38 owner list one sentence: 'the hook, the Malwarebytes .venv exclusion and pyright are path-bound; after moving the clone re-run `uv run --group dev pre-commit install` and update the Malwarebytes entry'. Optional hardening (S, next re-plan): give each local hook a PATH-independent fallback by making the entry a tiny repo script (`scripts/hook.ps1`/`.sh`) that prefers `uv` and falls back to `mimicwarehouse/.venv/Scripts/<tool>.exe`; `mwh doctor precommit` already warns when the hook file is missing.

### ENV-5 — No permission allow-list anywhere: every read-only project command prompts outside auto mode

**Severity:** low · **Kind:** process · **When:** owner-decision · **Effort:** S · **Retro episode:** EP-165 · **Lens:** Local development / session environment as it really is vs. what the roadmap assumed

**Files:** `.claude/settings.json:3`

**Evidence.** Repo `.claude/settings.json` has only `permissions.deny` (66 rules); no `.claude/settings.local.json` exists; `~/.claude/settings.json` keys = ['effortLevel','agentPushNotifEnabled','model'] (no permissions, no env). Sessions currently run in auto mode (harness banner 'While auto mode is active').

**Impact.** Any session run without auto mode (owner supervising, a reviewer, another machine) prompts on `uv run mwh doctor`, `uv run pytest ...`, `git log`, `ls`; owners tend to answer 'always allow' ad hoc, which lands unreviewed rules in settings.local.json outside D-39.

**Proposed fix.** Owner decision (CLAUDE.md §6): add a reviewed `permissions.allow` block to the shared settings.json (deny still wins): `Bash(uv run mwh *)`, `Bash(uv run --project mimicwarehouse * mwh *)`, `Bash(uv run pytest *)`, `Bash(uv run poe *)`, `Bash(uv run ruff *)`, `Bash(uv run pyright*)`, `Bash(git status*)`, `Bash(git log *)`, `Bash(git diff *)`, `Bash(git show *)`, `Bash(git ls-files*)`, `Bash(ls *)`, `Read(./mimicwarehouse/src/**)`, `Read(./mimicwarehouse/tests/**/*.py)`, `Read(./roadmap/**/*.md)`, `Read(./**/*.yaml)`, `Read(./**/*.toml)`; keep `mwh sql` inside the `mwh *` rule (it is the sanctioned aggregate path). Record as a D-39 addendum ('allow rules are convenience only; deny rules remain the control').

**Owner question.** Add a shared allow-list (recommended: fewer prompts, reviewed in git, deny still precedes) vs. leave prompting to auto mode / settings.local.json? Recommendation: shared, minimal, read-only commands only.

### ENV-6 — Malwarebytes coverage is process-based; two writer binaries outside the seven D-38 paths are worth naming (claude.exe native binary, uv cache) — info for the owner list, no incident yet

**Severity:** low · **Kind:** environment · **When:** next-replan · **Effort:** XS · **Retro episode:** EP-165 · **Lens:** Local development / session environment as it really is vs. what the roadmap assumed

**Files:** `mimicwarehouse/DECISIONS.md:334`, `roadmap/README.md:394`

**Evidence.** D-38 seven paths: Git, %APPDATA%\uv\python, .venv, C:\mimicdata, source material\, WinGet\Packages\astral-sh.uv_*, ~/.cache/pre-commit. Burst-write locations not on the list: `%LOCALAPPDATA%\uv\cache` (`du` on it timed out at 2 min — large), the Claude scratchpad under %TEMP%, `%TEMP%\mimic-code` (present, HEAD 8bcbd190…), `.git/objects`, `mimicwarehouse/.pytest_cache`, `.ruff_cache` (repo root and workspace). Their writers are uv.exe / bash.exe / git.exe / python.exe / ruff.exe — all inside allow-listed folders. The Write/Edit tools' writer is `CLAUDE_CODE_EXECPATH=C:\Users\willi\.vscode\extensions\anthropic.claude-code-2.1.233-win32-x64\resources\native-binary\claude.exe` — a version-suffixed path that is not and cannot stably be allow-listed.

**Impact.** If ARW ever judges claude.exe (it writes many files quickly during an EP), the symptom is 'Write/Edit tool fails / file vanished' rather than a shell error, and D-42 (4) tells sessions to check Malwarebytes quarantine only for shell processes. Real-time malware scanning (not ARW) of the uv cache on `uv sync` is a performance cost only.

**Proposed fix.** Owner action (XS): no new exclusion required now; extend the D-42 (4) sentence and Risk 12 with 'the same applies to the Write/Edit tools (claude.exe under %USERPROFILE%\.vscode\extensions\anthropic.claude-code-*); if Write/Edit fails or a just-written file vanishes, check quarantine first'. Optional: add `%LOCALAPPDATA%\uv\cache` as an eighth Malwarebytes path if `uv sync` of the gpu/text groups (EP-121/148) is slow.

### ENV-7 — doctor.check_uv depends on `uv` being on PATH although `uv run` exports the `UV` env var with the binary path

**Proposed fix.** Use `os.environ.get("UV") or shutil.which("uv") or the WinGet Links path` as the probe executable; keep the warn text. Add one test in test_ep02/test_ep164 that monkeypatches PATH empty and UV set.

**Evidence.** `git ls-files | grep -i licen` -> only `mimicwarehouse/src/mimicwarehouse/concepts/vendor/mimic-code/LICENSE` (upstream MIT) and root `NOTICE`. `uv build --wheel` (1.1 s, scratchpad) -> 186 files, `dist-info/METADATA` present, no `licenses/LICENSE` entry (hatchling auto-includes only LICENSE* found in the project root `mimicwarehouse/`).

**Impact.** The v1.0 public-repo step (D-41) ships a package whose METADATA says MIT with no license text; the vendored mimic-code MIT notice is present but the project's own is not — a licensing-hygiene gap the guard sweep will not catch.

**Proposed fix.** Add `LICENSE` (MIT, owner name/year) at the repo root and either a copy/symlink-free duplicate in `mimicwarehouse/` or `[tool.hatch.build.targets.wheel] ... ` plus `license-files = ["../LICENSE"]` in `[project]`; extend test_ep01/test_ep04 to assert the wheel contains it (optional).

**Owner question.** Confirm MIT + copyright line ('W. Taylor Farrington 2026') for the LICENSE file; recommendation: add now so every later commit is under an explicit license.

### FC-22 — MWH_ALLOW_REMOTE is documented as the remote gate but only for text modules; EP-22 (physionet), EP-40 (CMS GEM zip), EP-138 (concept_map) and P1 web research are ungated

**Severity:** low · **Kind:** governance · **When:** owner-decision · **Effort:** XS · **Retro episode:** EP-170 · **Lens:** forward-compat: pending briefs (EP-13..EP-56) vs shipped P0/P1 code

**Files:** `mimicwarehouse/GOVERNANCE.md:122-128`, `mimicwarehouse/.env.example`, `roadmap/EP-22-stage-demo-tier.md:19-21`, `roadmap/EP-40-codeset-registry-gem.md:50`, `CLAUDE.md`

**Evidence.** GOVERNANCE §9: "Text analysis runs entirely on this machine (local models only; `MWH_ALLOW_REMOTE=false` is the default and text modules refuse to run when it is true)". EP-22: "`mwh demo fetch` is the only network-touching command in P2 (physionet.org only; not a text module, so the `MWH_ALLOW_REMOTE` gate does not apply)". CLAUDE.md §6: ask before "enabling remote/network calls from text modules (MWH_ALLOW_REMOTE)". EP-40 downloads the GEM zip; EP-138 fetches concept_map.

**Impact.** Sessions cannot tell whether a new download command needs an owner ask; the doctor warns on `allow_remote=true` but nothing lists which commands egress.

**Proposed fix.** Owner clarifies in a D-n addendum + DESIGN note: allow_remote gates *data-bearing* remote calls (text/LLM); explicit fetchers (demo, GEM, concept_map) are allowed to named public hosts and must log the URL + sha256 in a source.yaml. Add the sentence to EP-22/EP-40/EP-138.

**Owner question.** Is MWH_ALLOW_REMOTE meant to gate all network egress (then EP-22/40/138 must check it) or only text-module remote inference (then document the allow-listed fetch hosts)? Recommendation: the latter, with an explicit host list.

### FXT-12 — validate()/write_fixture()/build_frames() are hard-wired to hosp+icu; ED/notes modules will need signature changes

**Severity:** low · **Kind:** forward-compat · **When:** next-replan · **Effort:** S · **Retro episode:** EP-169 · **Lens:** fixture-generator (EP-11/12 synthetic fixture as the test substrate for every later brief)

**Evidence.** `validate(frames, contract, plan, *, icu=None)` takes one kwarg per module; `write_fixture(..., dataset_dir=DATASET_DIR)` applies a single dataset dir to every module (ED lives under `mimic-iv-ed-2.2/ed/`, notes under `mimic-iv-note-.../note/`); `build_frames` returns exactly `{hosp, icu}`; `MODULE_SCHEMAS`/`FIXTURE_SCHEMAS` are hosp/icu; render_readme hardcodes 'EP-11 hosp, EP-12 icu' and 'test_ep11.py (hosp) and test_ep12.py (icu)'. EP-148 puts notes under `tests/fixtures/notes/` (not the raw layout).

**Impact.** EP-142/EP-148 either fork a second writer/validator or change these signatures late; the manifest/drift discipline may not cover the new modules.

**Proposed fix.** Generalise now or at re-plan: `validate(frames_by_module: Mapping[str, Mapping[str, DataFrame]], contract, plan)`, `write_fixture` accepting `{(dataset_dir, module): {table: frame}}` or a per-module dataset map, README template driven by MODULE_SCHEMAS; decide EP-148's notes layout (raw-layout `mimic-iv-note.../note/` recommended for the same loader path).

### FXT-13 — hosp.py/icu.py duplication and size; brief's '≤ ~30 lines per generator' not honoured or noted

**Severity:** low · **Kind:** design · **When:** next-replan · **Effort:** M · **Retro episode:** EP-169 · **Lens:** fixture-generator (EP-11/12 synthetic fixture as the test substrate for every later brief)

**Files:** `mimicwarehouse/src/mimicwarehouse/fixtures/hosp.py:187`, `mimicwarehouse/src/mimicwarehouse/fixtures/icu.py:244`, `mimicwarehouse/src/mimicwarehouse/fixtures/icu.py:269`, `roadmap/EP-11-fixtures-hosp.md:63`

**Evidence.** hosp.py 1510 lines, icu.py 1255; `_build_orders` ≈130 lines, `_build_labs` ≈125, `_build_inputs` ≈170, `procedureevents` ≈120 vs brief item 3 'one function per table, ≤ ~30 lines each' (completion note silent). HospContext and IcuContext duplicate `rng/table/_cached`; two GENERATORS dicts + two build_*_frames with the same 'no generator for table' check; `IcuContext.norepinephrine_starts` rebuilds a whole HospContext (`hosp.orders`) inside the icu module — a hidden hosp→icu coupling that the test named `..._independent_of_hosp` does not test (its only independence assertion is `assert icu_mod.table_rng is not None`).

**Impact.** EP-142 (ED) and EP-148 (notes) will copy the pattern a third and fourth time; a hosp `orders` change moves icu inputevents bytes, contradicting the 'icu cannot perturb / is independent of hosp' wording.

**Proposed fix.** Before EP-142: extract a `ModuleContext` base (rng/table/_cached/provider helpers) and a `build_module_frames(schema, GENERATORS, ctx)` helper; expose `norepinephrine_starts` from the plan/trait_times stage instead of rebuilding HospContext; replace the vacuous assertion with a real one (hosp frames equal with/without icu build) or delete it; add one line to the EP-11 completion note acknowledging the line-count deviation.

### FXT-14 — `mwh fixtures` has only `build`, which overwrites the checkout; no drift check / diff command

**Severity:** low · **Kind:** design · **When:** next-replan · **Effort:** S · **Retro episode:** EP-169 · **Lens:** fixture-generator (EP-11/12 synthetic fixture as the test substrate for every later brief)

**Files:** `mimicwarehouse/src/mimicwarehouse/fixtures/cli.py:44`, `mimicwarehouse/src/mimicwarehouse/fixtures/write.py:296`

**Evidence.** cli.py registers a single `build` command writing directly into `default_out_dir()` (tests/fixtures/); write_fixture writes CSVs one by one and the manifest last (a mid-run kill leaves a half-new tree); the only way to learn whether the generator drifted is running the tests.

**Impact.** A session that runs `mwh fixtures build` to 'see' the effect of a change has already mutated 31 binary files in the working tree; a Malwarebytes kill mid-write (Risk 12) leaves manifest/CSVs inconsistent with no diagnostic.

**Proposed fix.** Add `mwh fixtures check [--json]` (regenerate into a temp dir, compare sha256s with the committed manifest, print per-file same/changed and the version), and write CSVs to `<out>.new` then swap; XS if only the check command.

### FXT-15 — check_bytes forbids trailing whitespace on physical lines, which constrains future free-text last columns (ED triage.chiefcomplaint)

**Severity:** low · **Kind:** forward-compat · **When:** next-replan · **Effort:** XS · **Retro episode:** EP-169 · **Lens:** fixture-generator (EP-11/12 synthetic fixture as the test substrate for every later brief)

**Files:** `mimicwarehouse/src/mimicwarehouse/fixtures/write.py:107`, `mimicwarehouse/src/mimicwarehouse/fixtures/vocab/d_items.yaml:37`

**Evidence.** check_bytes flags any line not equal to `line.rstrip(' \t')`; the fixture already writes real-shaped values with trailing blanks ('Bipap mask ' — 7 chartevents rows, needed because oxygen_delivery.sql matches that exact string) — safe only because `value` is not chartevents' last column. `quote_style="necessary"` will not quote a trailing space. mimiciv_ed.triage's last column is `chiefcomplaint` (free text).

**Impact.** EP-142's ED generator will hit WriteError the first time a chief complaint or last-column text ends in a space and either strip realism or patch the writer under time pressure.

**Proposed fix.** Document the constraint next to check_bytes and in the fixture-change protocol; when the ED module arrives, force-quote last-column text values (per-column `quote_style` or wrap in quotes) or strip trailing blanks by design.

### FXT-17 — test_ep12 pins the plan count (`== 75`) and test_ep11 pins seed/subject literals; downstream EPs will copy the habit

**Impact.** Every bundled regeneration (FXT-1/4/5/6/7) breaks these and any EP-17+ test that copies the pattern (EP-20 'rows equal fixture rows', EP-55 'row count == count(*) of icustays' are fine if they compare to the manifest/catalog, not literals).

**Proposed fix.** Replace literals with `len(plan.icu_segments)` / `FixtureSpec().seed` / manifest values; add the 'no literal counts' rule to the fixture-change protocol (FXT-3).

**Files:** `mimicwarehouse/src/mimicwarehouse/fixtures/spec.py:263`, `mimicwarehouse/src/mimicwarehouse/fixtures/hosp.py:588`, `mimicwarehouse/src/mimicwarehouse/fixtures/icu.py:474`

**Evidence.** spec.minute() truncates to the minute and every generator uses MINUTE steps; probe: `second(storetime)<>0` = 0 rows in labevents, emar.charttime = 0, chartevents.storetime = 0. Real MIMIC-IV storetime / emar.charttime / poe.ordertime / pharmacy.entertime / chartevents.storetime carry seconds.

**Impact.** A loader (EP-17) or timeline (EP-49) bug that truncates or mis-parses seconds (e.g. `%Y-%m-%d %H:%M` format, cast to minute) passes on the fixture; the loader's `timestampformat='%Y-%m-%d %H:%M:%S'` path is never exercised with non-zero seconds.

**Proposed fix.** In the bundled regen, add a seconds component (0–59) to storetime/ordertime/entertime/emar.charttime/chartevents.storetime draws (keep charttime minute-level like MIMIC).

### FXT-7 — At most one ICU segment per admission, first_careunit == last_careunit always, and almost no 30-day readmissions

**Severity:** low · **Kind:** test · **When:** next-replan · **Effort:** M · **Retro episode:** EP-169 · **Lens:** fixture-generator (EP-11/12 synthetic fixture as the test substrate for every later brief)

**Files:** `mimicwarehouse/src/mimicwarehouse/fixtures/spec.py:335`, `mimicwarehouse/src/mimicwarehouse/fixtures/spec.py:435`, `roadmap/EP-31-tracer-bullet.md:62`

**Evidence.** spec._segments shapes are [icu][ward] / [ward][icu][ward] / [ward][icu] / [icu] — never two ICU segments; probe: max icustays per hadm = 1, first<>last careunit = 0; inter-admission gap is `20–500 days` (spec.py:435) → only 2 admissions within 30 days of a prior discharge. EP-11 completion note lists 'at most one ICU segment per admission' as a simplification.

**Impact.** `icustay_detail.first_icu_stay` is always true, so a 'first ICU stay' cohort filter bug (EP-31 tracer bullet, EP-47 cohorts) is invisible; readmission outcomes (EP-111, EP-68 event rates) have ~1 % positives on fixture, all suppressed at k = 11.

**Proposed fix.** Bundled regen: allow a [ward][icu][ward][icu] shape for ~10 % of ICU admissions (two IcuSegments per admission — AdmissionPlan.icu becomes a tuple, icustays derives from all), a careunit change inside a stay for a few, and a `readmit_30d_fraction` spec knob that draws the next gap in 1–29 days.

### FXT-8 — Planted phenotype signal never lands in the dev buckets (subject_id % 100 < 5)

**Severity:** low · **Kind:** test · **When:** next-replan · **Effort:** XS · **Retro episode:** EP-169 · **Lens:** fixture-generator (EP-11/12 synthetic fixture as the test substrate for every later brief)
